# Backend subsystems

## 1. `bootstrap` - Process bootstrap, configuration and app topology

Builds the two processes that run from one image — the FastAPI API (`main:app`) and the durable ingestion worker (`workers.ingestion_worker`) — from a single pydantic-settings object loaded at import time. Owns the middleware stack and its ordering, CORS, global/named rate-limit wiring, the OWASP header layer, the pre-auth body-size ceiling, the operations-monitor thread lifecycle, the runtime database schema contract, and the `/health` `/health/worker` `/ready` probes. `config.py` is also where the paper's frozen RAG constants live as `Literal` types, and where `validate_production_services` refuses to construct a production configuration that is missing Redis, MFA, ClamAV or a guest token budget.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/main.py` (336 lines) - API entrypoint: warning filters, logging, LangSmith env priming, operations-monitor thread, lifespan, app object, 5 user middlewares, 10 router includes, schema-contract cache, /health, /health/worker, /ready
- `rag-thesis-backend/config.py` (266 lines) - Single pydantic-settings `Settings` class (76 fields, 3 required), 5 computed properties, one `mode='after'` model validator, module-level `settings = Settings()` singleton
- `rag-thesis-backend/models.py` (300 lines) - All request/response pydantic schemas plus `CCSICT_TRACKS`. No DB models — persistence is raw Supabase/RPC dicts
- `rag-thesis-backend/warning_filters.py` (34 lines) - One function that registers a message-scoped `UserWarning` filter for langchain_core's pydantic.v1 shim on Python 3.14
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/rate_limiting.py` (45 lines) - `limiter` (slowapi `Limiter`, default 120/minute) plus `rate_limit_key` (HS256-verified sub → IP+guest-id hash → IP) and `ip_rate_limit_key`
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/request_limits.py` (161 lines) - `BodySizeLimitMiddleware`, a raw ASGI middleware that bounds request bodies before FastAPI spools multipart to disk
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/safe_logging.py` (54 lines) - `PrivacyFilter` / `redact_log_text` / `configure_safe_logging` — root-logger and root-handler redaction of bearer tokens, JWTs, key=value secrets and URL query strings
- `rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py` (361 lines) - Second process from the same image: same warning-filter-then-import preamble, its own `create_client`, `_Housekeeping` cadence, optional thread pool, SIGINT/SIGTERM drain
- `rag-thesis-backend/Dockerfile` (85 lines) - Two-stage Chainguard build, digest-pinned, exact-3.14.7 interpreter assertion, hash-verified lock install, tesserocr/tessdata verification, tiktoken cache bake, pip/setuptools removal, explicit COPY manifest, uid 65532, HEALTHCHECK on /health
- `rag-thesis-backend/.env.example` (197 lines) - Documented env surface; 10 of the 76 settings fields are undocumented here
- `docker-compose.operations.yml` (68 lines) - Four-service production topology (redis, clamav, api, worker, frontend); api+worker share `build: ./rag-thesis-backend` and both force APP_ENVIRONMENT=production
- `rag-thesis-backend/requirements.txt` (40 lines) - 38 human-readable pins; `requirements.lock` (98 packages, Linux-resolved, hashed) is what actually installs
- `rag-thesis-backend/pytest.ini` (9 lines) - testpaths=tests, pythonpath=., blanket DeprecationWarning ignore, `integration` marker
- `rag-thesis-backend/.pylintrc` (29 lines) - py-version 3.14, ignores venv/.venv/tests, disables 13 checks, max-line-length 120, max-args 10
- `rag-thesis-backend/.coveragerc` (7 lines) - `branch = false` — the CI --cov-fail-under=85 gate is line coverage only
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py` (88 lines) - Forces 20 env vars before `config` is imported, warns when sys.prefix is not `.venv`
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py` (72 lines) - AST-resolves first-party top-level imports of both entrypoints and asserts each is covered by a build-stage COPY
- `.github/workflows/quality.yml` (~200+ lines) - Pins python-version 3.14.7, installs `--require-hashes -r requirements.lock`, pip check, pip-audit, pytest --cov-fail-under=85, pylint over 7 targets

### Control flow

Import-time sequence for the API is load-bearing and deliberately non-idiomatic. `main.py` imports `warning_filters` first and calls `silence_known_third_party_warnings()` at module scope (main.py:13-16) before any FastAPI/slowapi/langchain import, because `langchain_core` pulls `pydantic.v1` at module scope and that shim emits an unconditional `UserWarning` on Python 3.14 that cannot be retracted once fired. The filter is message-scoped (a regex on the exact text), not a blanket `UserWarning` suppression (warning_filters.py:23-34). Pylint's `wrong-import-order` and `wrong-import-position` are disabled around this (main.py:20-28, .pylintrc:20). `logging.basicConfig(level=INFO, format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s')` then `configure_safe_logging()` run next (main.py:35-39), which attaches a `PrivacyFilter` to the root logger *and* to each root handler and pins httpx/httpcore to WARNING. Only then does `from config import settings` execute, which constructs the `Settings()` singleton and can hard-fail the process. LangSmith env priming happens after settings but **before** the router imports (main.py:43-52): if `effective_langsmith_tracing` is truthy, seven `os.environ.setdefault` calls seed both the modern `LANGSMITH_*` and legacy `LANGCHAIN_*` names. The routers are imported afterwards (main.py:54-55) precisely so LangChain sees those vars at its own import; `routers.settings` is aliased to `settings_router` to avoid shadowing the config singleton.

Middleware is registered in five `add_middleware` calls and Starlette's `add_middleware` does `user_middleware.insert(0, ...)` (starlette/applications.py:101) while `build_middleware_stack` wraps in `reversed(...)` order — so **last registered is outermost**. Registration order is SlowAPIMiddleware (main.py:155), GZipMiddleware(minimum_size=1024) (main.py:158), `security_headers` as a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` dispatch (main.py:161), `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` (main.py:180), `CORSMiddleware` (main.py:187). The resulting runtime chain outer→inner is: ServerErrorMiddleware → CORS → BodySizeLimit → security_headers → GZip → SlowAPI → ExceptionMiddleware → router. That placement is intentional and documented in-file: BodySizeLimit must sit outside everything that touches the request because FastAPI reads and spools the whole multipart body before it resolves a single dependency (so `require_upload_access` can never stop an oversized upload), and inside CORS so a 413 is readable by the browser rather than reported as an opaque network failure (main.py:174-186). Consequence nobody states: a 413 emitted by BodySizeLimit gets CORS headers but **not** the OWASP headers, because `security_headers` is inner.

`BodySizeLimitMiddleware` is a raw ASGI class, not a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` dispatch, because only the raw form can interpose on the receive channel before the body is consumed. It short-circuits non-http scopes and non-body methods, computes `limit_for_path`, refuses a declared `Content-Length` over the ceiling with a synthesized 413 before reading a byte, and otherwise wraps `receive` with a counter that raises `BodyTooLarge` at the ceiling. Because FastAPI wraps its own body read in a bare `except Exception` and rewrites it to `400 There was an error parsing the body`, a chunked overrun surfaces as 400 rather than 413 — the read still stopped (request_limits.py:95-161).

Rate limiting has two layers. `SlowAPIMiddleware` resolves the matched route handler via `_find_route_handler` (which takes the **last** `Match.FULL` route, not the first) and calls `_should_exempt`: a handler whose `module.name` is already in `limiter._route_limits` — i.e. anything carrying an explicit `@limiter.limit` decorator — is skipped entirely, so decorated routes do **not** also pay the global `120/minute`. Everything undecorated does, keyed by `rate_limit_key`. Decorated routes today: POST /chat (30/min user + 300/min IP stacked, chat.py:2008-2009), POST /duplication/scan (5/min), POST /duplication/chat and the two history DELETEs (20/min), the five upload POST/cancel routes (10/min), and four public reads on 30/min (`/analytics/summary`, `/catalog/departments`, `/catalog/departments/legacy`, `/departments/`, `/upload/tracks`). `RateLimitExceeded` is registered as an app exception handler (main.py:154) and slowapi's middleware looks it up itself via `app.exception_handlers.get(type(e), _rate_limit_exceeded_handler)`, returning the 429 from the innermost user-middleware position so it flows back out through GZip, headers and CORS.

Health/readiness diverge deliberately. `/health` goes through `_cached_database_contract()`: `time.monotonic()` diff against `_CONTRACT_CACHE['checked_at']`, TTL 15 s; on miss it runs `_verify_database_contract()` (four `.select().limit(1).execute()` reads against `dependencies.auth.sb`, imported lazily inside the function so the module can be imported without a live client) and caches the boolean. The rationale is amplification: the endpoint is unauthenticated, the frontend polls it every 30 s per tab, and 120 req/min × 4 reads = 480 DB reads/min/caller. `/ready` calls `_verify_database_contract()` directly every time (main.py:325) so a stale answer cannot route traffic into a broken process, and adds two cheap checks on `gemini_api_key` and the rate-limit store URI.

The operations monitor is a daemon thread owned by `lifespan`. Each generation gets its **own** `threading.Event` passed as an argument rather than read from module scope — the prior shared-event design let a restart `clear()` the event a still-running thread was watching and then start a second thread, and the two raced on `upsert_alert`/`resolve_alert` making alerts flap (main.py:74-93). `stop_operations_monitor` sets the event, joins for 2 s, and if the thread is still inside `evaluate_operations` it **keeps the handle** and returns, so a later `start()` declines instead of racing (main.py:96-121). `_operations_monitor` evaluates first and sleeps second, with a broad `except Exception` logging only the exception type name.

The worker is the same image with a different `CMD`. It repeats the warning-filter-then-import preamble (ingestion_worker.py:16-19), builds its **own** `create_client(settings.supabase_url, settings.supabase_key)` rather than importing `dependencies.auth.sb`, and calls `configure_safe_logging()` only inside `main()` — `run_worker()` invoked programmatically gets no redaction. `run_worker` uses concurrency 1 (inline, the evaluated shape) or a `ThreadPoolExecutor` above 1, forces 1 under `--once`, ticks `_Housekeeping` for registry heartbeat / cleanup / `expire_terminal_jobs` / `evaluate_operations`, refuses to claim while `scanner_status() == 'unavailable'`, and on shutdown drains in-flight futures with `executor.shutdown(wait=True)` before `stop_worker`.

### Public surface

- GET /health → `{status, checks:{api,database}, version}` (main.py:278). Always HTTP 200, even when `database == 'unavailable_or_incompatible'`; unauthenticated; subject only to the global 120/minute default limit; schema check memoised for 15 s.
- GET /health/worker → `JSONResponse({'status': 'healthy'|'degraded'}, 200|503)` (main.py:289). Reads `ingestion_workers` filtered by `last_seen_at >= now - operations_worker_stale_seconds` and `state != 'stopping'`, healthy only if `scanner_status != 'unavailable'`. Unauthenticated, uncached, one DB read per request.
- GET /ready → `JSONResponse({'status':'ready'|'not_ready', 'checks':{database,ai_configuration,rate_limit_store}, version}, 200|503)` (main.py:311). Deliberately bypasses the 15 s cache and pays for a fresh 4-read contract check every call.
- FastAPI defaults, never overridden: GET /docs, GET /redoc, GET /openapi.json. No `docs_url=None` anywhere in the repo — the interactive schema is public in every environment, production included.
- `main.app` — FastAPI(title='ISU Thesis AI Library API', version='2.1.0', lifespan=lifespan) (main.py:142). `app.state.limiter = limiter`.
- `main.lifespan(_app)` — async context manager; `start_operations_monitor()` on enter, `stop_operations_monitor()` in `finally`.
- `main.start_operations_monitor()` / `main.stop_operations_monitor()` / `main._operations_monitor(stop: threading.Event)` — generation-scoped thread lifecycle over `_OPERATIONS_STATE = {'thread','stop'}`.
- `main.security_headers(request, call_next)` — `@app.middleware('http')`, i.e. a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` dispatch. `setdefault`s X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options: DENY, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, Permissions-Policy, and HSTS only when `request.url.scheme == 'https'`.
- `main._verify_database_contract()` — four `select(...).limit(1).execute()` reads against `profiles(id,status,role,department)`, `departments(id,name)`, `papers(id,department,ingestion_status,active_index_version)`, `paper_index_versions(paper_id,index_version,embedding_model,embedding_dimensions,preprocessing_version,chunking_version,provenance_status)`.
- `main._cached_database_contract()` / `main.reset_contract_cache()` — 15 s memo (`_CONTRACT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 15.0`, `_CONTRACT_CACHE = {'checked_at': 0.0, 'ok': False}`). `reset_contract_cache` is a documented test hook.
- `config.Settings` — 76 fields; required: `gemini_api_key`, `supabase_url`, `supabase_key`. `model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_file='.env', extra='ignore')`.
- `config.Settings` properties: `cors_origin_list`, `gemini_reserve_key_list` (dedupes and excludes the primary), `turnstile_allowed_hostname_list` (lowercased), `effective_langsmith_tracing` (LANGSMITH_TRACING wins over LANGCHAIN_TRACING_V2 including when explicitly False), `effective_langsmith_api_key`, `effective_langsmith_project` (default 'isu-thesis-library').
- `config.Settings.validate_production_services()` — `@model_validator(mode='after')`; 7 rules (see invariants).
- `config.settings` — module-level singleton constructed at import (config.py:266). Any validation failure is an ImportError-time crash for both processes.
- `warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings()` — idempotent; must run before the first `langchain*` import.
- `services.rate_limiting.limiter` — `Limiter(key_func=rate_limit_key, default_limits=['120/minute'], storage_uri=settings.rate_limit_storage_uri)`.
- `services.rate_limiting.rate_limit_key(request)` → `user:<sub>` (only when `supabase_jwt_secret` is set and HS256 verification with `audience='authenticated'` succeeds), else `guest:<sha256(ip:guest_id)[:32]>` when `X-Guest-ID` is 16–128 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, else `ip:<addr>`.
- `services.rate_limiting.ip_rate_limit_key(request)` → `ip:<addr>`; used as the second, un-bypassable ceiling on POST /chat.
- `services.request_limits.BodySizeLimitMiddleware`, `limit_for_path(path)`, `BodyTooLarge`. `_BODY_METHODS = {POST, PUT, PATCH}`; `_BATCH_PATHS = {'/upload/batch', '/upload/batch/extract-metadata'}`; single limit = `max_upload_mb*1MB + 2MB`, batch limit = `max_batch_files*max_upload_mb*1MB + 8MB` (27 MB / 508 MB at defaults).
- `services.safe_logging.configure_safe_logging()`, `PrivacyFilter`, `redact_log_text(value)`.
- `workers.ingestion_worker.run_worker(*, once=False, stop_event=None, client=None) -> int` and `main()` (`--once` flag). `python -m workers.ingestion_worker` is the compose command.
- models.py exported schemas: ChatRequest, DuplicationAlert, ChatResponse, MetadataExtractionResponse, SessionCreate, SessionUpdate, PaperOut, UploadAccepted, UploadJobStatus, UploadCancelRequest, UploadCancelResponse, BatchRow, BatchExtractedFile, BatchExtractResponse, BatchFileResult, BatchUploadAccepted, UploadJobList, ScanHistoryOut, RoleUpdate, ProfileUpdate, CatalogEntityCreate, CatalogEntityUpdate, UserUpdate, DepartmentOut, DepartmentCreate, DepartmentUpdate, plus the `CCSICT_TRACKS` list.
- Container entrypoint: `ENTRYPOINT []` + `CMD ["uvicorn","main:app","--host","0.0.0.0","--port","8000","--proxy-headers"]`, `EXPOSE 8000`, `USER 65532:65532`, HEALTHCHECK hitting `http://localhost:8000/health` every 30 s.

### Invariants

- `silence_known_third_party_warnings()` must be the first thing both entrypoints do, before any `langchain*` import. Also called at the top of tests/conftest.py before it touches config.
- Starlette registration order is reverse of execution order. The comments at main.py:174-186 encode the required chain: CORS outermost, BodySizeLimit next, security_headers, GZip, SlowAPI innermost. Re-ordering any `add_middleware` call silently changes which layers a 413 / 429 / 5xx response passes through.
- `Literal`-frozen settings, changing any of which is a paper change: `embedding_dimensions: Literal[768]` (config.py:21 — pgvector column is `vector(768)` under a CHECK constraint, so an env-only change is impossible by design), `chunk_size_tokens: Literal[800]` / `chunk_overlap_tokens: Literal[100]` (config.py:74-75), `gemini_thinking_level: Literal['minimal','low','medium','high']`, `app_environment: Literal['development','test','production']`, `malware_scan_mode: Literal['disabled','clamav']`.
- Non-Literal but numerically frozen and pinned by tests/test_hardening_fixes.py: `retrieval_threshold == 0.30`, `retrieval_match_count == 5`, `duplication_threshold == 0.85`.
- `validate_production_services` (config.py:242-263) enforces, in order: (1) `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds * 2 < ingestion_lease_seconds` — the ONLY rule that applies in every environment; (2) production ⇒ `rate_limit_storage_uri` must not start with `memory://`; (3) production ⇒ `require_privileged_mfa` true; (4) production ⇒ `malware_scan_mode == 'clamav'`; (5) production ⇒ `guest_daily_token_budget > 0`; (6) the operations webhook URL and secret must be set together; (7) webhook URL must be `https://` and the secret ≥ 32 chars.
- Embeddings are never routed through `LLM_BASE_URL` — `match_chunks` filters on the recorded embedding model, so a different embedding source returns zero rows until every paper is reindexed (config.py:64-70).
- `SUPABASE_KEY` is the service-role key; the backend bypasses RLS and every boundary is enforced in Python (config.py:13).
- Dockerfile COPY manifest must name every root-level module either entrypoint imports. Currently `main.py config.py models.py warning_filters.py` plus `routers/*.py services/*.py dependencies/*.py workers/*.py scripts/gemini_release_smoke.py` (Dockerfile:56-61). tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py AST-resolves the direct imports of `main.py` and `workers/ingestion_worker.py` and asserts coverage; it also hard-asserts the literal string `warning_filters.py`.
- Interpreter is asserted to be exactly `(3, 14, 7)` at build time (Dockerfile:27); `.github/workflows/quality.yml` pins `python-version: '3.14.7'` to match.
- Both base images are digest-pinned, not tag-pinned; Chainguard's free tier retains only the newest build so a stale digest fails loudly with `manifest unknown`.
- `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock` in both the image and CI — `requirements.txt` is intent, `requirements.lock` (98 hashed packages, resolved for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` / py3.14) is what installs. The lock does not install on Windows.
- `pip` and `setuptools` are uninstalled before the runtime stage copies `/opt/venv` (Dockerfile:51) — nothing may `pip install` inside the running container.
- `TIKTOKEN_CACHE_DIR=/opt/tiktoken-cache` is baked with `cl100k_base` at build time so chunking never makes a network call at runtime (Dockerfile:44-47).
- `.dockerignore` excludes `.env`, `.venv*/`, `tmp-*/`, `tmp/`, coverage artifacts and `evaluation/results/` from the build context — the `tmp-*/` line exists because pytest leaves read-only working directories that make `docker build` fail with 'Access is denied' locally.
- Guest identifier accepted by `rate_limit_key` is 16–128 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; anything else silently degrades to the plain IP bucket.
- `X-Guest-Verification` is the only exposed response header; the allowed request headers are exactly `Authorization, Content-Type, X-Guest-ID, Idempotency-Key, X-Turnstile-Token`; allowed methods include PATCH specifically because `routers/catalog.py` serves two PATCH routes and omitting it made them unreachable from any browser.

### Data model

- `config.Settings` — 76 fields in 8 groups. Required secrets: `gemini_api_key`, `supabase_url`, `supabase_key` (service-role).
- Models: `gemini_chat_model='gemini-3.6-flash'`, `gemini_verdict_model='gemini-3.5-flash-lite'`, `gemini_embed_model='models/gemini-embedding-001'`, `embedding_dimensions: Literal[768]`, `gemini_timeout_seconds=60.0`, `gemini_max_retries=1`, `gemini_max_output_tokens=2000` (shared by the reasoning trace and the visible answer — the comment records a measured 609-of-696-token reasoning spend returning finish_reason=MAX_TOKENS), `gemini_thinking_level='low'`, `llm_gateway_max_output_tokens=6000`, `gemini_capacity_cooldown_seconds=60`, `gemini_api_keys=''`, `gemini_key_cooldown_seconds=60 (5..3600)`, `llm_base_url=''`, `llm_api_key=''`.
- RAG contract: `chunk_size_tokens: Literal[800]`, `chunk_overlap_tokens: Literal[100]`, `retrieval_threshold=0.30 (0..1)`, `retrieval_match_count=5 (1..20)`, `duplication_threshold=0.85 (0..1)`, `thesis_evaluation_department='CCSICT'`, `retrieval_candidate_pool=15 (5..20)`, `retrieval_per_paper_cap=3 (1..5)`. No cross-field validator ties pool ≥ match_count; `services/retriever.py:785` defends with `max(pool, final_count)`.
- Hardening: `app_environment='development'`, `cors_origins='http://localhost:5173,http://127.0.0.1:5173'`, `rate_limit_chat='30/minute'`, `rate_limit_chat_ip='300/minute'`, `rate_limit_upload='10/minute'`, `rate_limit_scan='5/minute'`, `rate_limit_followup='20/minute'`, `rate_limit_public='30/minute'`, `rate_limit_storage_uri='memory://'`, `guest_daily_token_budget=0 (ge=0, 0=unlimited)`, `require_privileged_mfa=False`, `max_upload_mb=25` (unbounded), `max_pdf_pages=500 (1..2000)`, `require_ocr_for_scanned_pages=True`.
- Ingestion: `ingestion_poll_seconds=2.0 (0.2..60)`, `ingestion_lease_seconds=120 (30..900)`, `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds=30 (5..300)`, `ingestion_max_attempts=3 (1..10)`, `ingestion_maintenance_seconds=300 (30..3600)`, `ingestion_concurrency=1 (1..8)`, `max_batch_files=20 (1..50)`.
- Operations/DR: `operations_monitor_enabled=False`, `operations_monitor_seconds=60 (15..3600)`, `operations_worker_stale_seconds=90 (30..900)`, `operations_queue_age_seconds=300`, `operations_queue_depth_threshold=10`, `operations_cleanup_age_seconds=600`, `backup_rpo_hours=0 (0 disables the staleness alert)`, `backup_rto_hours=4` (recorded, never enforced), `operations_alert_webhook_url/_secret/_timeout_seconds=5.0`, `retention_enforcement_enabled=False`.
- Scanning/guest: `malware_scan_mode='disabled'|'clamav'`, `clamav_host='127.0.0.1'`, `clamav_port=3310`, `clamav_timeout_seconds=20.0`, `turnstile_secret_key=''` (empty = guard off), `turnstile_verify_timeout_seconds=5.0`, `turnstile_guest_ttl_seconds=3600 (60..86400)`, `turnstile_expected_action='guest_chat'`, `turnstile_allowed_hostnames=''` (empty disables the hostname check), `supabase_jwt_secret=''`.
- LangSmith: `langchain_tracing_v2=False`, `langchain_api_key`, `langchain_project`, `langsmith_tracing: bool|None = None` (tri-state — `None` means defer to the legacy flag, `False` explicitly wins), `langsmith_api_key`, `langsmith_project`, `langsmith_hide_inputs=True`, `langsmith_hide_outputs=True`.
- `main._OPERATIONS_STATE: dict[str, object] = {'thread': None, 'stop': None}` — module-level mutable singleton, no lock; mutated only from lifespan and tests.
- `main._CONTRACT_CACHE: dict[str, float | bool] = {'checked_at': 0.0, 'ok': False}` — module-level, not thread-safe (benign: worst case a duplicate schema read).
- Tables the runtime contract asserts: `profiles`, `departments`, `papers`, `paper_index_versions` (all in `supabase_setup.sql`), plus `ingestion_workers` read by `/health/worker` (defined only in `migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql`, so `/health/worker` depends on a migration the base schema does not contain).
- models.py request/response shapes — notable details: `ChatRequest` sets `extra='ignore'` so legacy `match_count`/`match_threshold` are silently dropped (retrieval policy is server-owned); `guest_history` max 5 items ×4000 chars, `guest_source_ids` max 10, `conversation_replies` max 30 ×500. `DuplicationAlert` is metadata-only by construction — `matched_abstract`/`matched_excerpt` are not fields and are dropped on model_dump (pinned by tests/test_api.py:17). `ChatResponse.kind: Literal['answer','notice']` duplicates `chat_notices.KIND_*` by literal rather than import, deliberately, to keep models.py free of service imports. `BatchRow._blank_is_absent` coerces '' → None so a blank form field falls back to the request-level program_id.

### Config knobs

- All 76 `Settings` fields are environment-settable with `extra='ignore'` (unknown vars are silently dropped, so a typo'd env var is never reported).
- APP_ENVIRONMENT — the only value that activates the four production refusals; `test` is forced by tests/conftest.py.
- RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI — `memory://` default; anything else (redis://) makes limits shared across replicas and is mandatory in production.
- SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET — presence flips rate-limit keying from IP to verified `sub`. Unset means everyone behind one campus NAT shares a bucket.
- LLM_BASE_URL / LLM_API_KEY — routes chat/extract/verdict to an OpenAI-compatible gateway; never embeddings. `.env.example:38-43` warns the no-training guarantee the paper relies on does not extend to a third-party operator, and the release fingerprint records `gateway_enabled` and `gateway_host` (never the URL or credential).
- GEMINI_API_KEYS — comma-separated reserve pool; `gemini_reserve_key_list` dedupes and drops the primary.
- OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED — gates both the API monitor thread and the worker's per-maintenance `evaluate_operations` call.
- REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES — default true; the comment records a measured 2026-09-07 run where a host without the tesserocr wheel silently dropped 94 of 835 pages (11.3%) across 11 of 12 manuscripts.
- INGESTION_CONCURRENCY — 1 keeps the evaluated inline pipeline; >1 switches `run_worker` onto a ThreadPoolExecutor. `--once` forces 1 regardless.
- GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET — 0 = unlimited (dev/test/evaluation); production must set >0 or the process refuses to start.
- TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY — empty disables the guest-chat guard entirely; TURNSTILE_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES empty disables the hostname check.
- BACKUP_RPO_HOURS — 0 disables the `backup_stale` alert; deliberately not enforced at startup.
- FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS — read by uvicorn, not by `Settings`; documented in `.env.example:160` but absent from the compose `api` service.
- TIKTOKEN_CACHE_DIR — set in both Dockerfile stages to `/opt/tiktoken-cache`.
- Compose-only: `PUBLIC_API_ORIGIN`, `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_WS_ORIGIN`, and `DOLLAR: $$` (nginx template escaping).

### Error handling

- Settings validation failure → `pydantic.ValidationError` at `import config`, i.e. before uvicorn can bind. No log line, just a traceback.
- `RateLimitExceeded` → registered on the app (main.py:154); slowapi's middleware resolves it itself and returns 429 from the innermost user-middleware position, so the response still collects GZip, security headers and CORS on the way out.
- Body over ceiling with a declared Content-Length → synthesized `413 {'detail': 'Request body exceeds the N MB limit'}` written directly to the ASGI `send`, before the app runs.
- Body over ceiling with no/false Content-Length → `BodyTooLarge` raised through the app; FastAPI's own `except Exception` around the body read rewrites it to `400 There was an error parsing the body`. The middleware only emits its own 413 if the response had not already started.
- `_verify_database_contract()` raising anywhere → caught broadly. `/health`: logs `type(error).__name__` only at WARNING, caches `ok=False`, returns 200 with `status: 'degraded'`. `/ready`: logs, sets `database: 'unavailable_or_incompatible'`, returns 503.
- `/health/worker` wraps the whole Supabase read in a bare `except Exception: healthy = False` with no logging at all (main.py:306) — a broken query is indistinguishable from a stale worker.
- `_operations_monitor` catches every exception per cycle and logs `type(error).__name__` at WARNING, then sleeps. It never exits on error.
- `stop_operations_monitor` degrades rather than blocks: 2 s join, then a WARNING and an early return that keeps the handle so no second monitor can start.
- Worker: `claim_job` failures are `logger.exception`'d and retried after a poll interval (or return under `--once`); registry heartbeat and maintenance failures are swallowed with a WARNING; `process_claimed_job` is documented as never raising, so completed futures are only counted.
- Logging errors are pre-rendered: `PrivacyFilter.filter` calls `record.getMessage()` inside `try/except (TypeError, ValueError)`, then blanks `record.args` and pre-formats `exc_info` into `exc_text` so tracebacks and frame locals pass through the same redaction.
- No global handler for `HTTPException`, `RequestValidationError` or `Exception` — FastAPI/Starlette defaults apply, so an unhandled error is a bare 500 from `ServerErrorMiddleware` (outermost, therefore without CORS headers).

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_api.py:33-120 — `TestHealth` pins that `/health` re-reads the schema at most once per 15 s window, recovers when TTL is monkeypatched to 0, reports `degraded` without raising, and that `/ready` deliberately pays twice for two calls. Also pins `/ready`'s check key set to exactly `{database, ai_configuration, rate_limit_store}`.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_api.py:17-31 — `DuplicationAlert` drops `matched_abstract`/`matched_excerpt` on model_dump.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:85-92 — `app.router.lifespan_context is not None` and the lifespan owns the monitor's start/stop.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:113-140 — importing `config` emits no class-based-Config Pydantic deprecation; `Settings.model_config` is still `env_file='.env'`, `extra='ignore'`; the frozen RAG contract (800/100/0.30/5/0.85/768) is asserted numerically.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_rag_controls.py:435-485 — `Settings(**BASE, ...)` raises ValidationError matching 'Redis', 'MFA' and the guest-budget rule; also pins the LangSmith tri-state (`langsmith_tracing=False` beats `langchain_tracing_v2=True`) and the `isu-thesis-library` project default.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py — every first-party top-level import of `main.py` and `workers/ingestion_worker.py` is covered by a build-stage COPY; `warning_filters.py` is named literally.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_request_limits.py — declared-Content-Length refusal, chunked-overrun stop, lifespan/non-http passthrough, and that the application never runs for an over-ceiling body.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_operations_security.py:653-663, 842-890 — monitor start/stop lifecycle, one bounded evaluation per cycle, and that a restart does not race a second thread; :198-212 pins `PrivacyFilter` behaviour.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py — the isolation contract itself: 20 forced env vars including `GEMINI_API_KEYS=''`, `LLM_BASE_URL=''`, `LLM_API_KEY=''` (added after a developer's real gateway config made twelve tests bypass their monkeypatched fakes and make live calls), plus a `.venv` interpreter warning.
- Not covered: the ClamAV production rule, the heartbeat-vs-lease rule, and all three webhook rules in `validate_production_services`; the middleware *ordering* itself (no test asserts the stack order); `/health/worker`.

### Gotchas

- **The container HEALTHCHECK cannot fail on a database problem.** `/health` returns HTTP 200 unconditionally (main.py:278-286) and the Dockerfile healthcheck asserts `status == 200` (Dockerfile:81). A process whose schema contract is broken reports `status: 'degraded'` in the JSON body but Docker/compose still marks the container healthy. `/ready` is the probe that actually returns 503 and nothing in the compose file or Dockerfile uses it.
- **`/ready`'s `rate_limit_store: 'misconfigured'` branch is unreachable.** main.py:317-322 reports misconfigured when `app_environment == 'production'` and the URI starts with `memory://` — but `validate_production_services` (config.py:246) refuses to construct exactly that configuration, so the process would have died at import. Dead defensive code.
- **`docker-compose.operations.yml` forces `APP_ENVIRONMENT: production` on both api and worker but only injects 2 of the 4 production preconditions.** It sets `RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI` and `MALWARE_SCAN_MODE` (lines 24-26, 40-42) but not `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` or `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET`. Both default to values the validator rejects (`False` and `0`), so `docker compose up` dies at import with a pydantic ValidationError unless the operator's `.env` sets both — and `.env.example` has both commented out (lines 112, 158).
- **Both processes run the operations monitor in that compose topology.** `OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED: "true"` is set on api *and* worker (compose lines 27, 43). The API runs `_operations_monitor` on a thread; the worker calls `evaluate_operations(self.client)` every maintenance interval (ingestion_worker.py:239-240). The careful single-generation guard in main.py:74-93 only prevents two threads inside one process — nothing prevents API and worker racing on `upsert_alert`/`resolve_alert`, which is the same alert-flapping failure that guard was written for.
- **uvicorn access logs bypass `PrivacyFilter` entirely.** `configure_safe_logging()` attaches the filter to the root logger and root's handlers, but uvicorn's `LOGGING_CONFIG` gives `uvicorn.access` its own handler with `propagate: False` (uvicorn/config.py:110). Since `redact_log_text` explicitly strips URL query strings, the concern is acknowledged — yet the one logger that prints request lines is not covered.
- **`PrivacyFilter` on the root *logger* is near-useless on its own.** Python applies logger-level filters only to records logged directly to that logger, not to records propagated from children. Redaction works here solely because `configure_safe_logging` also adds the filter to root's *handlers* — and only to handlers that exist at call time. Any handler added later is unfiltered.
- **`env_file='.env'` is relative to the process CWD, not the module directory** (config.py:202). Running `python -m workers.ingestion_worker` from anywhere other than `rag-thesis-backend/` silently loads no `.env` and falls back to defaults or crashes on the three required fields. In the image this is benign: WORKDIR is `/app`, no `.env` is copied, and compose injects everything via `env_file:`/`environment:`.
- **`settings = Settings()` runs at import (config.py:266).** Any misconfiguration is an import-time crash with a pydantic traceback, not a startup log line — including inside `pytest` collection, which is why conftest.py sets 20 env vars before anything imports config.
- **Two committed scratch files at the backend root.** `rag-thesis-backend/probe7b.py` is tracked (`git ls-files` confirms) — a throwaway regex probe that imports `tests.test_guards_multilingual`, reads `/tmp/probe6.py`, and `exec`s a slice of it. `rag-thesis-backend/tmp-probe/probe.py` is also tracked. Neither is gitignored (`tmp-probe/` matches none of the `tmp/`, `tmp-pytest-*/`, `tmp-*.json`, `tmp_*.py` patterns in .gitignore), neither is in the pylint target list, and neither is in the Dockerfile COPY list. The most recent commits include 'chore: untrack a scratch repro script and ignore the shape' — this pair was missed.
- **Two dead models.** `MetadataExtractionResponse` (models.py:79) and `ScanHistoryOut` (models.py:227) have zero references in `routers/`, `services/`, `workers/`, `dependencies/`, `tests/`, `scripts/` or `evaluation/`. `POST /upload/extract-metadata` (upload.py:1455) declares no `response_model` at all and returns a bare dict; `routers/duplication.py` declares no `response_model` anywhere.
- **`/docs`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json` are exposed in production.** No `docs_url`/`redoc_url`/`openapi_url` override exists anywhere in the repo. They are also undecorated, so they consume the global 120/minute bucket.
- **`_CONTRACT_CACHE['checked_at']` is seeded to `0.0` and compared against `time.monotonic()`.** On Linux and Windows `monotonic()` counts from boot, so the sentinel normally misses. Within the first 15 seconds of machine uptime the comparison succeeds and `/health` returns the cached `ok: False` without ever checking — reporting `degraded` on a perfectly healthy system. `reset_contract_cache()` reintroduces the same window. Use `-inf` or a `None` sentinel.
- **HSTS is emitted only when `request.url.scheme == 'https'`** (main.py:169). With `--proxy-headers`, uvicorn trusts `X-Forwarded-Proto` only from `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` (default `127.0.0.1`). Behind a TLS-terminating proxy on any other address the scheme stays `http` and the API silently serves no HSTS. `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` is documented in `.env.example:160` but is not set for the `api` service in the compose file. The same trust gate also means `get_remote_address` returns the proxy's IP, collapsing every caller into one rate-limit bucket.
- **`security_headers` uses `setdefault`**, so any router that sets these headers wins; and because it sits *inside* `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` and `CORSMiddleware`, the synthesized 413 from the body limiter carries none of them.
- **`max_upload_mb: int = 25` has no `Field` bounds** (config.py:114), and it multiplies into `limit_for_path` alongside `max_batch_files` (≤50). At the documented ceilings that is already a 508 MB allowance on `/upload/batch`; an unvalidated `MAX_UPLOAD_MB` makes it unbounded. `gemini_max_output_tokens`, `gemini_max_retries`, `gemini_timeout_seconds` and `llm_gateway_max_output_tokens` are likewise unbounded ints/floats while nearly every neighbouring field uses `Field(ge=..., le=...)`.
- **`_BATCH_PATHS` is an exact-string set.** `POST /upload/batch/` (trailing slash) is measured against the 27 MB single-file ceiling, and the declared-Content-Length refusal fires before FastAPI's 307 redirect ever runs.
- **slowapi's `_find_route_handler` takes the LAST `Match.FULL` route**, whereas the router dispatches the first. With today's routes each path+method matches one route, so this is latent rather than live — but adding an overlapping path would attribute the default limit to the wrong handler.
- **`pytest.ini` blanket-ignores `DeprecationWarning`** (pytest.ini:6) while `.coveragerc` sets `branch = false` — so the 85% CI gate is line coverage only, and deprecations that the whole `warning_filters` apparatus exists to manage surgically are hidden wholesale in the suite.
- **`validate_production_services` has no direct test.** `tests/test_rag_controls.py:461-485` covers the Redis, MFA and guest-budget rules; nothing exercises the ClamAV rule, the heartbeat-vs-lease rule, or the three webhook rules. The heartbeat rule is the only one that fires outside production and is therefore the easiest to trip accidentally.
- **10 of 76 settings fields are absent from `.env.example`**: EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS, GEMINI_MAX_RETRIES, GEMINI_CAPACITY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS, CHUNK_SIZE_TOKENS, CHUNK_OVERLAP_TOKENS, RETRIEVAL_CANDIDATE_POOL, RETRIEVAL_PER_PAPER_CAP, RATE_LIMIT_UPLOAD, RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC, OPERATIONS_ALERT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS. Four of those are frozen-contract knobs that an operator could set from the environment without any documented warning.
- **`UploadJobStatus.max_attempts: int = 3` is hardcoded** (models.py:136) while `settings.ingestion_max_attempts` is configurable 1–10. Changing the setting leaves the API's default response field lying.
- **`scripts/release_fingerprint.py` is not in the image** — only `scripts/gemini_release_smoke.py` is copied, and `scripts/__init__.py` is not copied either (the package works inside the container only as an implicit namespace package). Evidence generation must run outside the container. Note also that `routers/`, `services/` and `dependencies/` have no `__init__.py` at all; only `scripts/` and `workers/` do.
- **`tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py` only resolves *direct* imports of the two entrypoints.** A new root-level module imported transitively — e.g. by a service — is invisible to the guard and would still fail at container import, which is the exact class of bug the test was written for.
- **`/ready` is not in the Vite dev proxy** (`vite.config.js:93-105` lists all ten router prefixes plus `/health`), so a dev-time fetch of `/ready` hits the SPA history fallback and returns `index.html`. The frontend only calls `/health` (`src/api.js:161`), so this is latent.
- **The two processes log in different formats from one image**: `'%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'` in main.py:37 versus `'%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s'` in ingestion_worker.py. Any log shipper parsing one will not parse the other.
- **LangSmith priming writes empty strings.** If tracing is enabled without a key, `os.environ.setdefault('LANGSMITH_API_KEY', '')` (main.py:45) sets the var to `''` rather than leaving it unset. `services/observability.safe_trace` separately re-checks `effective_langsmith_api_key` and no-ops, so the effect is contained — but the env var is now polluted for any library that only checks presence.

### Open questions

- Is the compose topology actually exercised? It sets APP_ENVIRONMENT=production without MFA or the guest budget, so `docker compose -f docker-compose.operations.yml up` cannot boot from `.env.example` alone. Either a private `.env` supplies both, or the file has never been run as written.
- Should the operations monitor run in the worker at all when the API already runs it? Enabling it in both (compose lines 27 and 43) reintroduces cross-process alert flapping that main.py's own docstring identifies as the failure mode.
- Should the Docker HEALTHCHECK point at `/ready` instead of `/health`? As written, no database fault can ever mark the container unhealthy.
- `probe7b.py` and `tmp-probe/probe.py` are tracked — intentional evidence of the guard-regex derivation, or leftovers the recent untracking commit missed?
- `MetadataExtractionResponse` and `ScanHistoryOut` have no callers. Were the endpoints meant to declare them as `response_model` (which would also document the schema in the public `/openapi.json`), or should they be deleted?
- Is the public `/docs` / `/openapi.json` surface a deliberate defense-demo affordance, or an oversight for a production deployment?

**Depends on:** config.settings (every module), dependencies.auth.sb — the single service-role Supabase client, imported lazily inside main's health functions so the module stays importable without a live client; the worker does NOT reuse it and builds its own, services.rate_limiting.limiter, services.request_limits.BodySizeLimitMiddleware, services.safe_logging.configure_safe_logging, services.operations.evaluate_operations (API monitor thread), warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings, slowapi 0.1.10 (Limiter, SlowAPIMiddleware, _rate_limit_exceeded_handler, RateLimitExceeded), starlette middleware semantics (add_middleware inserts at index 0; build_middleware_stack wraps in reverse), fastapi 0.139.2 / pydantic 2.13.4 / pydantic-settings 2.14.2, supabase 2.31.0 (create_client), uvicorn[standard] 0.51.0 — and its LOGGING_CONFIG, which the redaction layer does not reach

**Used by:** All 10 routers (`/upload /chat /papers /sessions /duplication /analytics /departments /catalog /settings /maintenance`) via `app.include_router`, workers/ingestion_worker.py — same image, different CMD, shares config/services/dependencies, scripts/release_fingerprint.py — reads `settings` for models, generation contract and gateway route into the PI-03 manifest, evaluation/ harness and the JMeter summarizers, rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js — calls `/health` (healthCheck); vite.config.js proxies all ten router prefixes plus `/health` to :8000, docker-compose.operations.yml — api + worker + frontend + redis + clamav, tests/: test_api.py (health/ready contract + cache semantics), test_hardening_fixes.py (lifespan, frozen RAG contract, pydantic config style), test_rag_controls.py (production validators), test_request_limits.py (body ceiling), test_dockerfile_contents.py (COPY manifest), test_operations_security.py (monitor lifecycle, PrivacyFilter)

**Additional notes returned by the reader** (fields outside the schema, preserved verbatim)

- `controlFlowExtra`: 

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 11 correction(s), 6 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "`logging.basicConfig(...)` then `configure_safe_logging()` run next (main.py:35-39) ... Only then does `from config import settings` execute, which constructs the `Settings()` singleton and can hard-fail the process."
  **Actual:** The order is reversed. `from config import settings` is main.py:30, `from services.rate_limiting import limiter` (which constructs the slowapi `Limiter` at import, rate_limiting.py:41-45) is main.py:31, and `from services.safe_logging import configure_safe_logging` is main.py:33. Only afterwards does `logging.basicConfig` run at main.py:35-38 and `configure_safe_logging()` at main.py:39. So the settings singleton is built, and the Limiter connects to `rate_limit_storage_uri`, BEFORE any logging format or redaction filter is installed. Anything those imports log goes out unredacted through the root logger's default lastResort handler. (`rag-thesis-backend/main.py:30-39`)
- **Wrong:** "tests/conftest.py — Forces 20 env vars before `config` is imported" / "the isolation contract itself: 20 forced env vars"
  **Actual:** `_ISOLATED_TEST_ENV` has 16 entries: GEMINI_API_KEY, GEMINI_API_KEYS, LLM_BASE_URL, LLM_API_KEY, SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY, APP_ENVIRONMENT, RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI, REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA, MALWARE_SCAN_MODE, OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED, RETENTION_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED, LANGSMITH_TRACING, LANGCHAIN_TRACING_V2, TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY, REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py:19-44`)
- **Wrong:** "Not covered: ... the middleware *ordering* itself (no test asserts the stack order); `/health/worker`."
  **Actual:** `/health/worker` IS covered. `test_worker_health_is_generic` calls `main.worker_health()` against a fake client and asserts both branches — 200 with `{'status':'healthy'}` for a fresh non-stopping worker with `scanner_status='healthy'`, and 503 with no worker name leaked in the body for an empty `ingestion_workers` table. The middleware-ordering claim is correct (grep for `user_middleware`/`middleware_stack` in tests/ returns nothing). (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_operations_security.py:639-651`)
- **Wrong:** ".pylintrc — disables 13 checks"
  **Actual:** 14 checks are disabled: missing-module-docstring, missing-class-docstring, missing-function-docstring, unused-argument, too-few-public-methods, too-many-locals, too-many-statements, too-many-branches, too-many-arguments, too-many-positional-arguments, broad-exception-caught, duplicate-code, import-outside-toplevel, wrong-import-position. (A comma count gives 13 because the last entry has no trailing comma.) Note also that `wrong-import-order` is NOT in this file — it is only disabled inline at main.py:20/28 and ingestion_worker.py:23/25. (`rag-thesis-backend/.pylintrc:6-20`)
- **Wrong:** "requirements.txt — 38 human-readable pins"
  **Actual:** 27 pins. Count of lines matching `^[A-Za-z]`: 6 web/runtime, 7 RAG, 4 digitization, 7 hardening, 3 testing = 27. The file is 40 lines including comments and blanks. The `requirements.lock` count of 98 is correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/requirements.txt:4-40`)
- **Wrong:** "docker-compose.operations.yml — Four-service production topology (redis, clamav, api, worker, frontend)"
  **Actual:** Five services, and the map's own parenthetical lists five. Only one of them declares a `healthcheck:` block (clamav, line 11). (`docker-compose.operations.yml:2,8,19,34,49`)
- **Wrong:** "and four public reads on 30/min (`/analytics/summary`, `/catalog/departments`, `/catalog/departments/legacy`, `/departments/`, `/upload/tracks`)"
  **Actual:** Five, not four — the map's own list has five entries. Verified decorator sites: analytics.py:137 GET /summary, catalog.py:182 GET /departments, catalog.py:188 GET /departments/legacy, departments.py:61 GET /, upload.py:1275 GET /tracks. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1275`)
- **Wrong:** "Guest identifier accepted by `rate_limit_key` is 16–128 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; anything else silently degrades to the plain IP bucket."
  **Actual:** The check is `all(char.isalnum() or char in '-_' for char in guest_id)`. `str.isalnum()` is Unicode-aware, so 16+ characters of Cyrillic, CJK, Arabic-Indic digits, etc. all pass and mint a distinct `guest:` bucket. The accepted alphabet is Unicode alphanumerics plus `-_`, not ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/rate_limiting.py:30`)
- **Wrong:** "`warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings()` — idempotent"
  **Actual:** It is not idempotent in the literal sense: `warnings.filterwarnings` inserts a new entry at the head of `warnings.filters` on every call, so the three call sites in a test run (conftest.py:17, main.py:16, ingestion_worker.py:19) leave three identical filters stacked. The *effect* is unchanged, which is presumably what was meant, but there is no guard. (`rag-thesis-backend/warning_filters.py:28-34`)
- **Wrong:** "pytest.ini — testpaths=tests, pythonpath=., blanket DeprecationWarning ignore, `integration` marker" (9 lines)
  **Actual:** Incomplete in a way that matters. It also sets `addopts = -v --tb=short`, and the second filterwarnings entry is a narrowly-scoped `ignore:Using .*httpx.* with .*starlette\.testclient.*:starlette.exceptions.StarletteDeprecationWarning`. That second entry names a class pytest must import while parsing the config, which conftest.py documents as the cause of a whole-run `AttributeError` abort on a non-`.venv` interpreter — the reason the interpreter warning exists at all. (`rag-thesis-backend/pytest.ini:4,7`)
- **Wrong:** ".github/workflows/quality.yml — ~200+ lines"
  **Actual:** 342 lines. The substantive claims check out: `python-version: '3.14.7'` (line 47), `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock` (line 55), `--cov-fail-under=85` (line 72), pylint over exactly 7 targets (line 75). (`.github/workflows/quality.yml:47,55,72,75`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- **The worker container is permanently unhealthy in the compose topology.** The image bakes `HEALTHCHECK ... CMD python -c "...urlopen('http://localhost:8000/health')..."` (rag-thesis-backend/Dockerfile:80-81). The `worker` service overrides `command:` to `python -m workers.ingestion_worker` but does NOT override or disable the inherited healthcheck (docker-compose.operations.yml:34-47), and the worker serves no HTTP. Every worker container therefore goes `unhealthy` after 3 retries and stays there forever. The map found the inverse problem (/health can never mark the API unhealthy) but missed this one, which is the same HEALTHCHECK line causing a false negative instead of a false positive.
- **`models.py` is in the Dockerfile COPY list but is NOT protected by the guard test the map credits.** Neither entrypoint imports it: main.py imports only `warning_filters`, `config`, `services.*`, `routers.*` (plus lazy `dependencies.auth`), and workers/ingestion_worker.py imports `warning_filters`, `config`, `services.*`. `first_party_top_level_imports` only walks those two files (tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py:36-48), so `models.py` at Dockerfile:56 is covered by manual care alone. The map states the COPY invariant as though the test enforced the whole list; the only literal string it hard-asserts is `warning_filters.py`.
- **`_operations_monitor` can die on its first tick without the module logging anything**, contradicting the map's "It never exits on error." The two imports `from dependencies.auth import sb` and `from services.operations import evaluate_operations` sit at main.py:63-64, OUTSIDE the `try` (which starts at :67). An ImportError or a failure constructing the Supabase client at that point propagates out of the thread target — handled only by `threading.excepthook`, never by `logger.warning` — while `_OPERATIONS_STATE['thread']` keeps a handle to the dead thread, so `start_operations_monitor` will happily restart it only after `is_alive()` goes False.
- **`Settings` sets neither `frozen=True` nor `validate_assignment=True`** (config.py:202 sets only `env_file` and `extra`). The `Literal[768]` / `Literal[800]` / `Literal[100]` types and every `Field(ge=..., le=...)` bound constrain construction and env parsing only; a plain `settings.chunk_size_tokens = 400` at runtime is accepted silently and `validate_production_services` never re-runs. The whole test suite relies on this (`monkeypatch.setattr(main.settings, ...)`), so the map's "frozen contract" framing is about import-time parsing, not about the object.
- **`ChatResponse.notice_type: Optional[Literal['conversation']] = None`** (models.py:76) is a second presentation-layer field the map's dataModel entry omits while detailing `kind`. It is the hint that separates routine conversation from refusals/outages/no-evidence notices in the live UI; persistence still keys on `kind`.
- Confirmation of several load-bearing claims I checked and could not break: slowapi's `_find_route_handler` does take the LAST `Match.FULL` route (no `break`, .venv/.../slowapi/middleware.py:18-26) and `_should_exempt` returns True for any handler already in `limiter._route_limits` (middleware.py:98-113); `services/retriever.py:785` is exactly `pool_size = max(settings.retrieval_candidate_pool, final_count)`; the 76-field count and the exact 10-field `.env.example` gap list are both right; `ingestion_workers` is defined only in migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql and migrations/20260804_backup_runs.sql, never in supabase_setup.sql; `routers/`, `services/` and `dependencies/` have no `__init__.py` while `scripts/` and `workers/` do; `probe7b.py` and `tmp-probe/probe.py` are both tracked and both escape every `tmp*` pattern in .gitignore:47-69; the frontend polls `/health` at `refetchInterval: 30_000` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/components/AppShell.jsx:36); `/ready` is genuinely absent from the Vite proxy while `/health/worker` is covered by the `/health` prefix entry.

## 2. `chat-router` - Chat router - the RAG request handler

`POST /chat` is the single generation-phase entrypoint: it turns one user message into either a deterministic system notice (no model call), a catalog/metadata answer read straight from Postgres, or a citation-validated RAG answer built from at most 5 chunk blocks. About 1,200 of its 2,906 lines are deterministic routing — regex/exact-set classifiers that divert greetings, identity, capability, provenance, catalog listings, author lookups and positional follow-ups away from retrieval entirely, in English, Filipino and Ilocano. The remaining path does embed -> match_chunks -> rerank -> prompt -> Gemini -> [n] marker validation with a bounded repair ladder -> persist via save_chat_exchange. routers/sessions.py is the separate CRUD surface for saved conversations and is the only place a stored transcript is read back to the browser.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py` (2906 lines) - The whole request handler: ~620 lines of module-level regex/set constants (lines 86-620), ~60 helper functions (621-2005), the route wrapper `chat` (2007-2073), the kind-stamping shim `_chat_impl` (2076-2095) and the pipeline body `_chat_impl_unstamped` (2098-2906).
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/sessions.py` (100 lines) - Session CRUD + transcript read-back. Explicit column lists (`_SESSION_FIELDS`, `_MESSAGE_FIELDS`) replace `select('*')`; derives `notice_type` from stored answer text on read.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py` (281 lines) - Every canonical notice string, the 60 s process-wide capacity cooldown, `response_kind()` (answer vs notice at write time), `is_stored_non_answer()` (text fallback for pre-migration rows), `notice_type()` (presentation subtype), `varied_message()` (deterministic LRU variant picker).
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/question_types.py` (67 lines) - Precedence-ordered regex classifier: AGGREGATE > COMPARISON > ENUMERATION > FACTUAL > DEFAULT. Pure regex, no model call.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/query_translation.py` (123 lines) - Deterministic `looks_non_english()` marker test plus the query-rewrite prompt and `usable_translation()` acceptance gate. Translation feeds the embedding and the classifier only, never the generation prompt.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/guards.py` (606 lines) - `REFUSAL_MESSAGE`, `prohibited_reason()` (prompt_injection | academic_content_generation), `is_ambiguous_followup()`, `fallback_standalone_question()`.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/citations.py` (165 lines) - `normalize_citation_markers`, `validate_citations`, `filter_cited_sources`, `enforce_citation_coverage` (paper-gated structural repair).
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/prompts.py` (513 lines) - `PROMPT_VERSION='iskai-prompt-v4'`, the four generation templates, `QUESTION_TYPE_TASKS`, the `ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT` sentinel and `strip_no_evidence_sentinel`, fencing helpers.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/guest_budget.py` (146 lines) - UTC-day token ceiling in the rate-limit store; `estimate_charge`, `charge`, `is_exhausted`, `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`. Budget 0 = unlimited.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/turnstile.py` (137 lines) - `ensure_guest_chat_verification` — in-process verified-guest cache, action/hostname echo checks, fail-closed.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py` (984 lines) - Everything chat.py calls for evidence and metadata: `search_chunks`, `get_paper_overview_context`, `list_archive_papers`, `find_papers_by_{ids,title,title_fragment,author}`, `check_topic_duplication`, `public_source`.
- `rag-thesis-backend/models.py` (300 lines) - `ChatRequest` (lines 15-44), `DuplicationAlert` (47-55), `ChatResponse` (58-76), `SessionCreate`/`SessionUpdate`.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/messageNotice.js` (35 lines) - Consumer of `no_relevant_thesis` / `notice_type` / `kind`; decides whether a message gets a notice chip.

### Control flow

#### Route wrapper — `chat()` (chat.py:2010-2073)

slowapi checks the per-IP limit (`300/minute`, keyed by `ip_rate_limit_key`) then the per-caller limit (`30/minute`) before the body runs. First statement in the body is `await ensure_guest_chat_verification(request, user)`: no-op for authenticated users or when `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is empty; otherwise it reads `X-Guest-ID`, short-circuits on the in-process verified cache (`turnstile_guest_ttl_seconds`, default 3600), else requires `X-Turnstile-Token`, POSTs it to Cloudflare, additionally checks the echoed `action` against `TURNSTILE_EXPECTED_ACTION` and the echoed `hostname` against `TURNSTILE_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES`, and fails closed (403) on any HTTP/JSON error. On success the guest id is cached. This guard lives only in the wrapper, so the evaluation harness's direct `_chat_impl` call never sees it.

Inside `safe_trace('rag.chat.total')` the wrapper resolves the department **once** — `resolve_effective_department(user, req.department_filter)` in a thread, and only `if user` (guests get `None` here and re-resolve inside the body). For non-superadmins this pins the profile department and 403s a mismatched `department_filter`; for a superadmin it validates the requested name against `departments` (422 unknown, 503 unavailable). It then rejects `edit_from_turn` without `session_id` (400) *before* spending a model call. `_chat_impl` runs, and afterwards, only for an authenticated caller and only when `_client_gone(request)` is False, it (a) deletes the edited turn and everything after it via `_truncate_session_from_turn`, then (b) persists through the `save_chat_exchange` RPC and overwrites `response.session_id` with the returned id and sets `history_saved=True`. `HTTPException` from persistence re-raises; anything else logs a warning and sets `history_saved=False`. Trace metadata gets `source_count`, `no_relevant_thesis`, `history_saved`, `status`.

`_chat_impl` (2076) is a two-line shim that calls `_chat_impl_unstamped` and then unconditionally overwrites `response.kind = chat_notices.response_kind(response)` — so the one branch that sets `kind='notice'` by hand (2670) is re-derived, and every return in the body gets one consistent classification.

#### `_chat_impl_unstamped` in exact order (2098-2906)

**Department + session ownership (2116-2127).** `effective_department = resolved_department or resolve_effective_department(...)`. If `session_id` is set: guests get 401; authenticated callers go through `_ensure_session_owner` (owner row + department equality, 404/409).

**Conversational fast paths, all before the guard, all model-free.** Each logs `chat_query` with a `fast_path` tag and returns immediately.
1. `_is_model_question` (2125) — exact membership of `_MODEL_QUESTIONS` against `re.sub(r'\\s+',' ', _normalize_short_query(q))`. **No politeness/addressee stripping** (unlike every other set below). Returns `_model_response()`, `notice_type='conversation'`.
2. `_is_capability_question` (2141) — `_CAPABILITY_QUESTIONS_ALL` (English + Tagalog/Ilocano), tested against each of `_short_query_candidates` (as typed → minus `po`/`ho` → minus trailing addressee), optionally preceded by any greeting; `len(candidates[0]) > 80` bails. Returns `CAPABILITIES_MESSAGE`. Deliberately ahead of the greeting so "what can you do" is not answered with an introduction.
3. `_is_courtesy_message` (2156) — `_THANKS_ALL | _FAREWELLS_ALL`, cap 40 chars. `_is_farewell_message` then picks `FAREWELL_MESSAGES` vs `COURTESY_MESSAGES`; `chat_notices.varied_message` selects the least-recently-used variant from the client-supplied `conversation_replies` list, deterministically (no RNG).
4. `_is_simple_conversation` (2175) — cap 80; True if any candidate is in `_GREETINGS_ALL` or `_IDENTITY_QUESTIONS_ALL`, or fullmatches one of the two time-of-day regex families (`magandang araw/umaga/tanghali/hapon/gabi`, `naimbag (nga|a) aldaw/bigat/malem/rabii/sardam(yo)`), or is `<longest greeting prefix> + (identity question | addressee)`, or — **English greetings only** — `<greeting> + exactly one trailing word`. `_conversation_response` returns `IDENTITY_MESSAGE` when `_is_identity_question` agrees, else a varied `CONVERSATION_MESSAGES` entry.
5. `_is_system_origin_question` (2193) — `_SELF_ORIGIN_QUESTION` or `_FIL_SELF_ORIGIN_QUESTION`, both `fullmatch`. Returns `SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE` with `notice_type='conversation'`.

**Guard (2207).** `prohibited_reason(req.question)` → `'prompt_injection'` (regex) or `'academic_content_generation'` (a generation verb must *govern* a prohibited artifact **and** the sentence be directed at the assistant or claim ownership — two independent searches previously refused ordinary retrieval questions). On a hit: log `chat_query_blocked`, return `REFUSAL_MESSAGE` with no `notice_type`.

**Guest budget pre-check (2218).** `guest_budget.is_exhausted()` in a thread, guests only. This is the last point before anything can reach Gemini (rewrite, translation, embedding, generation). Returns `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`; logs `chat_query_budget_exhausted` stage `pre_retrieval`.

**History + reference sources (2224-2276).** Authenticated: `_load_chat_history(session_id, user.id, edit_from_turn)` — re-verifies ownership (a *second* query after `_ensure_session_owner`), then either the default path (SQL `kind='answer'`, `order created_at desc`, `limit 5`, reversed) or, for a prompt edit, `_history_before_turn` (ordered ascending, `limit min(before_turn, 500)`, filter `kind=='answer'`, keep last 5). Rows are then filtered again in Python by `prohibited_reason(question)` and `is_stored_non_answer(answer)`. `latest_sources` = the newest history row that has any `sources`; its ids (deduped, first 10) are re-fetched through `find_papers_by_ids` **under the enforced department**, producing `reference_sources` — the server-verified list every positional follow-up indexes. Guests instead get `guest_history[-5:]` as question-only rows (guard-filtered) and `find_papers_by_ids(req.guest_source_ids)`; a failure here logs and continues. `chat_history_str = _format_chat_history(...)` HTML-escapes every turn (`prompts.fence_history`) and strips `[n]` from prior answers.

**Archive catalog (2278-2323).** `listing_cursor = _archive_listing_cursor(history, guest_source_ids)` counts distinct paper ids across every stored answer containing the marker string `'from the live indexed archive'`; for a guest transcript (questions only) it falls back to the deduped `guest_source_ids`. `continues_listing = bool(listing_cursor) and _is_archive_continuation_question(question, _last_turn_was_a_listing(history))` — the strong patterns (`remaining`, `rest of the list`, `next N`, `more titles`, plus anchored Filipino/Ilocano forms) always apply; the weak ones (`the rest`, `show more`, `what else`, `ano pa`, `tuloy`) only when the turn directly above was a listing. Both `_is_archive_continuation_question` and the local catalog matchers first veto on `_FIL_RETRIEVAL_GUARD` (any deictic/possessive/manuscript-part/topic/year word → this is about one manuscript, not the catalog) after `_split_scope_container` removes container phrases like "sa system na ito". If `continues_listing or _is_archive_inventory_question(...)`: `list_archive_papers(dept, category, limit=10, offset)` (exact `count='exact'` total, `order title, id`, so paging is stable); failure → 503. `_archive_inventory_response` renders count-only or a numbered page, with `citation_id` numbering the **page** from 1 so "number 2" stays resolvable. Returns `archive_current=True`, `sources=[]` when count-only, `no_relevant_thesis=not total`.

**Context-dependent self questions (2325-2378).** `_is_ambiguous_system_identity_question` ("ano ba itong system na ito") and `_is_ambiguous_system_origin_question` ("who developed this system", "sino ang nag-develop netong system") both require `not reference_sources` — with an archived source on the table, "this system" means that manuscript's system and stays a retrieval question. Between them sits `_is_self_platform_reference` ("itong system na ginagamit ko", "the app I'm using"), deliberately **ungated**, because the turn that prompts it is usually a wrong retrieval answer whose own sources would block the correction; it answers `SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE` if any of the last 3 questions asked about origin, else `IDENTITY_MESSAGE`.

**Author fast path (2380-2420).** Nested closure `try_author_fast_path(question)`: name from `_extract_author_name` (English `who is X` / `what about X` needing 2-6 parts, or `_FIL_AUTHOR_QUESTION` — `sino/asino/siasino + si/ni/sina/da + name`, with `_trim_name_stopwords` peeling trailing particles and catalog nouns) **or** `_extract_followup_author_token` (one-word `what about <word>`, excluded by `_NON_AUTHOR_TOKENS`). `find_papers_by_author` does `ilike '%name%'` with a surname fallback; a one-word token is additionally confirmed against whole archived name parts (`_author_token_matches`) so `ai` cannot match `Rainier`. If nothing is found and the question is not an explicit `who is` / `sino si <2 parts>`, the path declines and falls through. Lookup failure raises 503 (harsher than the title-fragment lookup, which degrades). Response carries `archive_current=True`.

**Capacity cooldown (2427).** `_capacity_limit_is_active()` — a 60 s process-wide flag set by `mark_capacity_limited` — returns `CAPACITY_MESSAGE` as HTTP 200. Placed *after* the catalog and author paths (which are DB-only) and *before* anything that can call the provider.

**Reference resolution (2429-2557), first match wins.**
- `_extract_explicit_thesis_title` (a quoted title, or `thesis titled X` / `the other paper X`) → `find_papers_by_title`; a unique hit sets `referenced_paper_id`. Failure → 503.
- Else `_extract_thesis_title_fragment` (`what/how about X`, `tell me (more) about X`, 6-300 chars) → `find_papers_by_title_fragment`; a unique hit becomes an **overview** of that paper. Failure degrades silently.
- `_resolve_numbered_thesis_reference` — whole-question `number N` / `the second thesis` against `reference_sources` → overview of that paper.
- `_resolve_inline_thesis_reference` — the same reference inside a longer question (`what are the objectives of number 2`); vetoed by `_IN_DOCUMENT_COUNTABLE` when a count noun precedes (`objective number 2`); rewrites the numeral out of the wording into `the archived thesis titled \"...\"`; **not** an overview.
- `_is_plural_source_followup` (>=2 prior sources and `both/all/these/the two <theses>`, or `their|them`, or a bare "summary" request) → `referenced_paper_id` becomes a **list**.
- Else `is_ambiguous_followup(question, prior_questions)` (explicit reference patterns in EN/FIL/ILO, or — only when the question does not name its own subject — a local possessor/dangling-demonstrative signal, or `<=5 words ending in '?'`). With >=2 prior sources it answers for every shown thesis; with 0-1 it uses `_resolve_referenced_thesis` (overview of source[0]) → `_resolve_specific_paper_followup` (prefix the title) → and only with **no** prior sources at all, the Gemini `_rewrite_followup` call, which for a guest is charged and **refusable** (`_charge_guest_extra`, stage `followup_rewrite`). The rewritten question is re-run through `prohibited_reason` (2544) and, if `effective_question != req.question`, through the author fast path again (2553).

**Cross-lingual query prep (2576-2593).** If `query_translation.looks_non_english(effective_question)`, a guest charge is booked and **can refuse** (stage `query_translation`), then `_translate_for_retrieval` asks Gemini for a one-line English search query; `usable_translation` rejects multi-line output, anything outside 3-400 chars, or a reply starting `answer:|response:|query:|i cannot`. Any exception returns the question unchanged. The result feeds **only** the embedding and the classifier — the generation prompt still receives `req.question` / `effective_question`, which is what keeps `PROMPT_VERSION` frozen and keeps the Objective 2 harness (English) out of this path.

**Classification + retrieval (2595-2641).** `question_type = classify_question(retrieval_question)`. `_retrieve_evidence` has three shapes: (a) `referenced_paper_id` is a list → `asyncio.gather` of `get_paper_overview_context` per paper, with line-initial `[n]` markers renumbered by a running offset and `citation_id` reassigned; similarity is synthesised as `1.0`/`0.0`; **no duplication check**. (b) a single `referenced_paper_id` → one `get_paper_overview_context`; **no duplication check**. (c) the semantic path → `embed_text` in a thread under `safe_trace('rag.embedding')`, then `asyncio.gather(search_chunks, check_topic_duplication)` sharing the one embedding. `search_chunks` calls `match_chunks` at the frozen 0.30 threshold with `retrieval_provenance_params()`, pulls a 15-candidate pool, hybrid-reranks (0.75 cosine / 0.25 lexical), applies the per-paper cap (**1** when `question_type == AGGREGATE`, else `retrieval_per_paper_cap=3`), keeps exactly 5, assigns citation ids in rank order, then `long_context_reorder`. `top_similarity` is the pool max, not the selection max. `evaluation_trace`, when supplied, receives `context`/`sources`/`top_similarity` — never `ChatResponse`. Any exception: `_is_capacity_error` → cooldown + `CAPACITY_MESSAGE` at 200, else 503.

**Empty context (2645-2657).** `alert_data['summary']` was already forced to `''` at 2644. Returns `get_no_relevant_message(dept)` with `no_relevant_thesis=True`, `archive_current=True`, and the duplication alert **with an empty summary** (no generation ran, so nothing summarised it). Note there is **no `log_activity` call on this branch**.

**Opaque single token (2659-2671).** `_is_unsupported_single_token_query(req.question, context, sources)` — exactly one token, >=4 chars, and its compacted form absent from the concatenated context + title/authors/track/section_title of every source → `UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE`, sources cleared.

**Guest generation charge (2673-2691).** `estimate_charge(context, question, effective_question, chat_history_str) = sum(count_tokens) + gemini_max_output_tokens`, booked atomically against `guest-token-budget:<YYYY-MM-DD>`. Refusal returns `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`; a refused charge still increments (documented, avoids a check-then-incr race).

**Generation (2693-2736).** Prompt selection in this order: list → `exact_papers_prompt`; `is_overview_followup` → `overview_prompt`; single id → `exact_paper_prompt`; else `grounded_prompt(dept, question_type)`, which composes an optional `QUESTION_TYPE_TASKS` block on top of the frozen EVIDENCE/CITATION/SYNTHESIS/BOUNDARIES/FORMAT contracts. Input dict differs: the exact-paper paths send only `{context, question: effective_question}` (**no chat history at all**), the grounded path sends `{chat_history, context, question: req.question, resolved_question: effective_question}`. If an alert exists an extra unrefusable guest charge is booked and `_invoke_generation` runs the answer and `_summarize_duplication` as two concurrent Gemini calls; the summary is fenced/escaped because it is built from third-party manuscript text and is read by faculty. Exceptions: capacity → cooldown + 200, else 502.

**Post-processing ladder (2738-2888).**
- `strip_no_evidence_sentinel` runs first, on the raw text, before anything else touches it: a leading own-line `ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT` (optionally bold/fenced) sets `sentinel_fired`; the token anywhere else is removed but never flags, because `<retrieved_context>` is untrusted and would otherwise be an injection primitive.
- `generation_truncated = is_truncated(result)` reads the provider's **stop reason**, never inferred from text.
- `reports_no_evidence = sentinel_fired or _answer_reports_no_evidence(answer)` (a ~28-phrase list). Both computed **once** and never recomputed — `GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX` begins "I could not verify", which is itself in the phrase list.
- `normalize_citation_markers` expands `[1, 2]` → `[1] [2]`.
- Multi-paper coverage repair: only when `referenced_paper_id` is a list, not `reports_no_evidence`, not truncated, and some referenced paper received no citation. The guest charge is **booked, not refused**. Truncated repair raises and the draft is kept.
- `_looks_like_misdirected_greeting` → replace with the grounded fallback.
- Then three mutually exclusive branches: **truncated** → discard entirely for `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` (repairing a fragment would make it *look* complete, which is worse); **reports_no_evidence** → keep the model's own text and whatever it cited, bypassing the repair ladder on purpose (`enforce_citation_coverage` would staple `[1]` onto a negative claim about the archive), falling back to the generic notice with `no_relevant_thesis=True` only if nothing survives; **else** → `validate_citations` + missing-paper check, and on failure one AI `_repair_citations` (charge booked, not refused), then `enforce_citation_coverage`, then `_grounded_retrieval_fallback`. `unique_sources = filter_cited_sources(answer, sources)` on every branch, so uncited sources are dropped.

**Final (2890-2906).** Logs `chat_query` with `sources_cited`, `duplication_flagged`, `citation_repaired`, `department`, `question_type`, then returns `answer`, `unique_sources`, `duplication_alert`, `no_relevant_thesis`, `archive_current=True`.

### Public surface

- POST /chat -> ChatResponse. Decorators bottom-up: @limiter.limit(settings.rate_limit_chat) inner, @limiter.limit(settings.rate_limit_chat_ip, key_func=ip_rate_limit_key) outer, @router.post('', response_model=ChatResponse, responses=errors(401,502,503)) outermost (chat.py:2007-2013). Auth dep: OptionalUser = Annotated[Any, Depends(require_chat_access)] — guests -> None, signed-in roles must have the `chat` feature (dependencies/auth.py:398).
- GET /sessions -> list of {id,user_id,title,department,created_at}, newest first (sessions.py:46).
- POST /sessions {title} -> inserted row; department comes from get_user_scope(user.id), never the client (sessions.py:57).
- PUT /sessions/{session_id} {title} -> updated row; 404 if not owned (sessions.py:68).
- DELETE /sessions/{session_id} -> {deleted:true}; 404 if not owned (sessions.py:76).
- GET /sessions/{session_id}/messages -> rows of {id,session_id,question,answer,sources,duplication_alert,kind,created_at} ascending, each augmented in Python with notice_type (sessions.py:82-100).
- ChatRequest fields (models.py:20-44): question (1..4000, required); session_id (<=64); department_filter (1..100); thesis_category_filter (^(student|faculty)$); guest_history (<=5 strings, each <=4000); guest_source_ids (<=10 strings, each <=64); edit_from_turn (int >=0); conversation_replies (<=30 strings, each <=500). model_config extra='ignore' — legacy match_count/match_threshold are silently dropped, retrieval policy is server-owned.
- ChatResponse fields (models.py:58-76): answer; sources (list[dict], metadata only via retriever.public_source); duplication_alert; session_id; no_relevant_thesis; history_saved; archive_current; kind Literal['answer','notice']; notice_type Optional[Literal['conversation']].
- _chat_impl(req, request, background_tasks, user, evaluation_trace=None, resolved_department=None) — the function the Objective 2 harness calls directly (evaluation/run_comparison.py:272 passes request=None, user=None, and a mutable trace dict that receives context/sources/top_similarity). It is also the entrypoint every unit test in tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py drives.
- _chat_impl_unstamped(...) — the pipeline body; ~19 distinct return statements; pylint disable=too-many-return-statements.
- get_no_relevant_message(department=None) -> str — module-public, also imported by tests.
- Prompt selector aliases (chat.py:1407-1410): get_rag_prompt=prompts.grounded_prompt, get_overview_prompt=prompts.overview_prompt, get_exact_paper_prompt=prompts.exact_paper_prompt, get_exact_papers_prompt=prompts.exact_papers_prompt.
- classify_question(question) -> 'aggregate'|'comparison'|'enumeration'|'factual'|'default' (question_types.py:57).
- query_translation.looks_non_english(text) -> bool; translation_prompt(question) -> str; usable_translation(candidate, original) -> str (query_translation.py:86,113,117).
- chat_notices.response_kind(response) -> 'answer'|'notice'; is_stored_non_answer(answer) -> bool; notice_type(answer) -> 'conversation'|None; varied_message(messages, prior_replies) -> str; is_capacity_error/mark_capacity_limited/capacity_limit_is_active/reset_capacity_limit.
- HTTP error surface raised from this subsystem: 400 'A prompt edit requires a saved session.' (chat.py:2051); 401 'Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.'
