(2122); 403 Turnstile REQUIRED_MESSAGE / FAILED_MESSAGE with X-Guest-Verification: required|failed (turnstile.py:124,133); 404 'Session not found' (chat.py:1509, 1527; sessions.py:44); 409 'This conversation belongs to a different department. Start a new conversation.' (chat.py:1528); 502 'The research AI service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.' (2734); 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.' (2299, 2394, 2440, 2639) and 'Department validation is unavailable.' (auth.py:126).

### Invariants

- Every conversational fast path is exact-set membership or regex `fullmatch` against a normalized string, never a substring `search`. This is the only thing stopping a real research question being intercepted — the file documents a measured case where a `.*`-joined version of `_FIL_WHAT_IS_THIS_SYSTEM` swallowed 7 of 8 ordinary archive questions (chat.py:217-224).
- _GREETINGS_BY_LENGTH and _ADDRESSEES_BY_LENGTH must stay TUPLES sorted longest-first. The prefix scan in `_is_simple_conversation` stops at its first hit, so iterating the sets made the answer depend on PYTHONHASHSEED: measured 2026-09-14, `hi there friend` was True under seeds 0/2/7 and False under 1/3 (chat.py:345-354).
- The politeness/addressee candidate list is ordered as-typed FIRST. A stripped reading must never beat one that matches as written, or `ano ang iskai` reduces to `ano ang` before testing (chat.py:655-661).
- Every conversational set holds exactly ONE canonical entry per phrase — never a `po`/`ho` variant and never with an addressee. `_short_query_candidates` strips those. Writing a phrase twice breaks the stated contract (chat.py:248-259).
- `_FIL_RETRIEVAL_GUARD` must be applied to the body AFTER `_split_scope_container` removes the container phrase, and applied to the raw normalized string for the scope test. Order matters: "anuano ang mga theses na nanandito sa system na ito" tripped the guard on its own `na ito` and was answered from retrieval as though 3 theses were the whole archive (chat.py:474-489).
- `_FIL_SCOPE` deliberately excludes `sistema`, `library`, `aklatan`, `database`, `katalogo` — in a CCSICT archive those are among the commonest words in thesis TITLES (chat.py:412-414). `_FIL_CAT_PLURAL` is pluralized-only because unpluralized `pananaliksik` names a single manuscript (chat.py:408-410).
- Prompt-edit positions: `edit_from_turn` is a position in the session's FULL ordered transcript, not an index into the 5-row answer window. `_history_before_turn` and `_truncate_session_from_turn` must resolve it against the SAME ordered read or they land on different boundaries (chat.py:1468-1499).
- _MAX_TRANSCRIPT_ROWS = 500 bounds both transcript reads by something this code chose. Without it they were bounded by PostgREST's `db-max-rows` (project setting, default 1000) which truncates SILENTLY, so an edit resolved against a partial transcript and deleted from a different boundary than it read from (chat.py:1430-1436, 1543-1548).
- Archive listing pages are consecutive slices of ONE ordering (`order title, id` — title alone is not a total order). The cursor counts distinct titles already shown, not listing turns, so a repeated "list the theses" re-shows page one instead of skipping ahead (chat.py:1015-1022; retriever.py:423-427).
- Inventory `citation_id` numbers the PAGE from 1, never the archive. A continuation page numbered 11-16 would put every "number N" reference out of range (chat.py:1050-1055; retriever.py:465-468).
- `'from the live indexed archive'` must appear in every listing AND count reply. It is the only marker that lets a stored transcript be read back to find which turns were listings (chat.py:557).
- Notices must never become model context. Enforced structurally at write time via `chat_messages.kind` (SQL-filtered in `_load_chat_history`) with `is_stored_non_answer` as a text-based second line of defence for pre-migration rows.
- Every notice string lives in `services/chat_notices.py`, beside the classifier that recognizes it. A reworded copy inside `routers/chat.py` would stop being classified as a notice and start being replayed as conversational context (chat.py:847-849, 862-865).
- `is_stored_non_answer` compares `marker[:60]`, so no two NOTICE_MARKERS may share a 60-character prefix and no notice may be a prefix of a real answer. `tests/test_chat_notices.py:264` asserts this directly.
- `strip_no_evidence_sentinel` must run on the RAW text before anything else: `_looks_like_misdirected_greeting` uses `startswith`, the repair prompts interpolate the draft, and `enforce_citation_coverage` would staple a marker onto the token's own line (chat.py:2741-2752). Its remainder is `lstrip`ped because `response_kind` uses raw `==`/`startswith` with no whitespace normalisation.
- `reports_no_evidence` and `no_relevant_thesis` are computed exactly once, on the model's own output, and never recomputed — `GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX` starts with "I could not verify" and `'could not verify'` is the first phrase in the detector list (chat.py:2738-2747).
- Truncation is read from the provider's stop reason, never inferred from the text. A severed reply is a well-formed prefix no downstream check can distinguish from a short answer; one measured case spent 1,920 of 1,996 output tokens reasoning, announced "two distinct systems", described one, and was served as finished work (chat.py:2755-2761).
- `enforce_citation_coverage` is gated on `len(paper_ids) == 1` and a non-empty id. Mapping an uncited claim onto `allowed[0]` across several theses shows the reader one specific thesis as the authority for a sentence the model never attributed to it — the most damaging thing a citation library can do, and invisible (citations.py:99-119).
- `_substantive_spans` returns positions, not just text. Patching by `str.replace` found the already-patched first occurrence again on a repeated paragraph: unit one got a doubled marker, unit two stayed uncited, validation failed, and a grounded answer was discarded (citations.py:63-71). The patch loop runs back-to-front so earlier offsets stay valid.
- `_format_chat_history` must HTML-escape every turn. Guest history arrives in the request body and is filtered only by `prohibited_reason`, whose injection regex matches nothing XML-shaped; unescaped, one guest turn could close the fence and open a forged `<retrieved_context>` first, and any marker it reused would be in range so `validate_citations` would pass a fabricated claim (chat.py:1620-1632; prompts.py:120-127).
- Cross-lingual translation touches ONLY the embedding and the classifier. The generation prompt receives the question as asked, so `services/prompts.py` is untouched, `PROMPT_VERSION` does not move, and the English-only Objective 2 harness never enters the path (chat.py:2559-2575; query_translation.py:14-30).
- Guest budget: a charge is booked BEFORE the call it pays for, because a ceiling billed afterwards cannot refuse the request that breaches it. A refused request still increments, to avoid the check-then-incr race (guest_budget.py:17-29).
- Provider capacity errors never raise. They trip a 60 s process-wide cooldown and every question inside it returns HTTP 200 carrying `CAPACITY_MESSAGE`. The tests, the JMeter summarizers and the evaluation harness all depend on this shape.
- `_client_gone` counts every ambiguous case as still-connected. A double with no `is_disconnected`, or a transport that cannot answer, must not stop the exchange being recorded — that failure would be silent and would lose real chat history (chat.py:1585-1591).
- `sessions.py` must keep explicit column lists. `select('*')` published whatever columns the table happened to have, so any future column reached the client by default (sessions.py:19-26).

### Data model

- chat_sessions: id (uuid), user_id, title, department, created_at. Department is stamped from get_user_scope at creation (sessions.py:59-63) and compared on every chat turn by _ensure_session_owner (409 on mismatch).
- chat_messages: id, session_id, question, answer, sources (jsonb), duplication_alert (jsonb), kind ('answer'|'notice'), created_at. `kind` is written by save_chat_exchange from chat_notices.response_kind (B14); it is the SQL filter that keeps notices out of model context (chat.py:1503, 1463).
- notice_type is NOT a column. It is a response field on the live path and is re-derived from answer text on read-back (sessions.py:96-100).
- save_chat_exchange RPC params: p_user_id, p_session_id, p_title (question[:40] + '...'), p_question, p_answer, p_sources, p_duplication_alert, p_department, p_kind. Returns the session id as a scalar; it does NOT return the inserted message id, which is why prompt edits are keyed on a transcript position instead (documented at chat.py:1490-1499).
- match_chunks RPC: query_embedding, match_count=retrieval_candidate_pool (15), match_threshold=0.30, p_department, p_thesis_category (key omitted entirely when unset, so the unfiltered call is byte-identical to the evaluated pipeline), plus retrieval_provenance_params() so vectors from another embedding model can never match.
- check_topic_duplication RPC: query_embedding, dup_threshold=0.85, p_department, p_thesis_category, provenance params.
- ChatResponse.sources entries come only from retriever.public_source: id, title, authors, year, track, department, optional thesis_category / program_code / program_name / specialization_code / specialization_name / similarity (x100, rounded) / citation_id / chunk_id / chunk_index / page_start / page_end / section. Never chunk text.
- DuplicationAlert has NO model_config, so pydantic's default extra='ignore' silently drops matched_abstract and matched_excerpt — verified: DuplicationAlert(**alert_data).model_dump() emits only flagged, similarity (percent), threshold (percent), matched_paper, matched_location, summary. That is the only thing keeping 600-char manuscript excerpts out of the API response and out of chat_messages.duplication_alert.
- Guest token budget: a single counter at key 'guest-token-budget:<YYYY-MM-DD>' in settings.rate_limit_storage_uri (memory:// in tests, Redis in production), TTL = seconds to next UTC midnight + 60.

### Config knobs

- GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL (default gemini-3.6-flash) — interpolated verbatim into `_model_response()`, so the model name is user-visible
- GEMINI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS (2000) — also the entire output half of every guest budget estimate
- GEMINI_THINKING_LEVEL (low), GEMINI_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, GEMINI_MAX_RETRIES — baked into the module-level `llm` at import
- GEMINI_CAPACITY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS (60) — the process-wide capacity flag lifetime
- RETRIEVAL_THRESHOLD (0.30, frozen), RETRIEVAL_MATCH_COUNT (5, frozen), RETRIEVAL_CANDIDATE_POOL (15; setting it equal to match_count is the documented rollback lever that disables rerank+diversity), RETRIEVAL_PER_PAPER_CAP (3; overridden to 1 for AGGREGATE questions)
- DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD (0.85, frozen)
- RATE_LIMIT_CHAT (30/minute), RATE_LIMIT_CHAT_IP (300/minute), RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI (also backs the guest budget counter)
- GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET (0 = unlimited; validate_production_services refuses to start production at 0)
- TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY (empty = guard disabled), TURNSTILE_GUEST_TTL_SECONDS (3600), TURNSTILE_EXPECTED_ACTION, TURNSTILE_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES, TURNSTILE_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
- THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT — the department every guest is pinned to
- LLM_BASE_URL — routes chat/extract/verdict through the OpenAI-compatible gateway; embeddings are never routed. Changes `gemini_pool.active_output_ceiling()`, which the truncation log line reports
- GEMINI_RESERVE_API_KEYS — tried only on capacity errors, via `gemini_pool.attempt_chain`
- system_settings.role_features -> the `chat` toggle read by require_chat_access (fallback: routers/settings.py DEFAULT_FEATURES, mirrored in src/lib/permissions.js)
- PROMPT_VERSION ('iskai-prompt-v4') — must be bumped for any wording change in services/prompts.py and recorded in evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md

### Error handling

- Provider capacity (429 / RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED / 'quota exceeded' / 'rate limit' / 'too many requests', with '429' matched only as a labelled HTTP status via network_retry.mentions_http_status): never raises. `mark_capacity_limited()` sets a 60 s process-wide flag and the turn returns HTTP 200 with CAPACITY_MESSAGE. Checked at three points — the pre-flight guard (chat.py:2427), the retrieval except (2635), the generation except (2731).
- Retrieval failure (non-capacity): logged with `logger.exception('Retrieval failed')`, raises 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.'
- Generation failure (non-capacity): `logger.exception('LLM generation failed')`, raises 502 'The research AI service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.'
- Archive metadata lookups: inventory (2295) and author (2390) and explicit-title (2434) failures raise 503; the title-FRAGMENT lookup (2456) and the guest reference lookup (2270) log a warning and continue with empty results, because both are guesses rather than stated references.
- Follow-up rewrite: any exception -> `logger.warning` + `guards.fallback_standalone_question` (a deterministic 'Previous research question: X Follow-up: Y' string, truncated to 4000).
- Query translation: any exception -> `logger.warning` + the question as asked. `usable_translation` additionally rejects multi-line replies, lengths outside 3-400 chars, and prefixes 'answer:|response:|query:|i cannot|i can not'.
- Duplication summary: `logger.exception` + empty string; the alert still ships with `summary=''`.
- Citation repair: `is_truncated` -> raise TruncatedGeneration (a severed repair is a failed repair, since surviving units keep their markers and would pass validation). Caught by the caller -> grounded retrieval fallback. Multi-paper coverage repair raises the same way and the caller keeps the original draft.
- Persistence: HTTPException re-raises (so 404/409 from truncation still surface); anything else logs a warning and sets `history_saved=False`, which the frontend reads at Chat.jsx:1165.
- Ownership/scope: 401 for a guest sending session_id, 404 'Session not found' for a missing or foreign session (wrapped by `db_errors.identifier_not_found` so a mistyped non-uuid becomes 404 rather than an unhandled 500 from Postgres), 409 for a department mismatch, 400 for an edit without a session.
- Turnstile: fail-closed 403 on missing id/token, on Cloudflare `success: false`, on an unexpected echoed action/hostname, and on any httpx/JSON error.
- Guest budget: fails OPEN on storage error (ALLOWED_UNLIMITED / spent 0). Refusals return HTTP 200 with GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE and log `chat_query_budget_exhausted` with a `stage` of pre_retrieval | followup_rewrite | query_translation | pre_generation.
- Observability: every phase is wrapped in `services.observability.safe_trace` — rag.chat.total, rag.embedding, rag.duplication, rag.retrieval, rag.generation, rag.citation_repair — which yields None when LangSmith is off, hence the `if run:` / `if retrieval_run:` guards.

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py (1406 lines) — TestEarlyChatPaths (fast-path ordering and exact strings), TestRetrievalAndGenerationFlow, TestTracingBoundaries, TestChatPersistence (save/truncate/_client_gone), TestReportedTranscriptRegressions (the dated 2026-09-04/09-14 transcript failures the comments cite), TestTheResponseCarriesItsKindLive, TestQuestionTypeReachesRetrievalAndPrompt, TestCapabilityAndCourtesyFastPaths (pins COURTESY_MESSAGE/FAREWELL_MESSAGE as the first variant), TestNumberedReferenceInsideAQuestion, TestCrossLingualRetrieval. All drive `_chat_impl` directly with a `_NoRequest` stub, not through TestClient.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_logic.py (614 lines) — TestCitationFiltering, TestConversationFastPath, TestUnsupportedSingleTokenQuery, TestArchiveInventoryFastPath, TestGroundingGuards, TestDuplicationPercentage, TestSystemProvenanceFastPath, TestBareTitleReferenceCapture, TestFilipinoAndIlocanoConversation, TestGreetingMatchIsDeterministic (the PYTHONHASHSEED regression).
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_notices.py (379 lines) — pins every notice string, response_kind/is_stored_non_answer/notice_type, and asserts no notice marker is a 60-char prefix of another (line 264). Named in CLAUDE.md as the file to run after any notice wording change.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_routing_multilingual.py (421 lines) — the Filipino/Ilocano catalog, origin, identity and continuation routing.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_query_translation.py (89 lines), tests/test_question_types.py (102 lines) — the two deterministic classifiers.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_prompt_contracts.py — pins services/prompts.py (run after any prompt edit). tests/test_auth_authorization.py — pins PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL and the guard matrix. tests/test_request_limits.py — ChatRequest field bounds.
- rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/messageNotice.test.js and transcript.test.js — assert the frontend's reading of kind / notice_type / no_relevant_thesis, including a source-text grep (`assert.match(chat, /notice_type: m\.notice_type/)`) that pins the wiring in Chat.jsx.

### Gotchas

- `looks_non_english` has a broad English false-positive surface. `_WEAK_MARKERS` contains both `'a'` and `'at'`, and two distinct weak markers is enough. Verified by running the function: `'What is a model used at ISU?'` -> True, `'Is there a thesis about OCR at ISU?'` -> True. `_DECISIVE_MARKERS` also contains the bare token `'ar'` (intended for Ilocano `ar-aramaten`, which tokenizes to `ar` + `aramaten`), so `'Which AR applications are in the archive?'` -> True — and augmented reality is an entirely plausible CCSICT topic. Each false positive costs one extra Gemini call per turn, and for a guest a refusable budget charge. The translation prompt says "if already English, repeat unchanged", so the answer is usually unharmed — the cost and the latency are not.
- `_is_model_question` is the ONE fast path that does not use `_short_query_candidates`. Verified: `_is_model_question('what model are you')` -> True, `_is_model_question('what model are you po')` -> False. There is also no `_LOCAL_MODEL_QUESTIONS` set, so no Filipino/Ilocano phrasing reaches it — in a deployment whose entire multilingual apparatus exists because students code-switch.
- `_model_response()` is not in `NOTICE_MARKERS` and not in `notice_type()`'s list. Verified: `notice_type(_model_response())` -> None. The live response sets `notice_type='conversation'` (chat.py:2137) so the browser shows no chip; after a reload `sessions.py` derives `None` while `kind` is `'notice'`, so `messageNoticeLabel` returns SYSTEM_NOTICE_LABEL and the same message suddenly gains a "System message - not a research answer" chip. It also drops out of the `conversation_replies` list the browser builds from `notice_type === 'conversation'` (Chat.jsx:1017,1147), so the `varied_message` rotation differs before and after a reload.
- The same inconsistency runs the other way for the origin reply. The first origin branch (chat.py:2202) sets `notice_type='conversation'`; the ambiguous-origin branch (2376) does NOT, so live it shows a system-notice chip. On reload both become `'conversation'` because `notice_type()` recognizes SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE. Identical text, three different renderings depending on which branch fired and whether the page was reloaded.
- `_FIL_WHAT_IS_THIS_SYSTEM` intercepts plain first-turn nouns. Verified: `ano ang software`, `ano ang sistema`, `ano ang programa`, `anong website` all fullmatch and, with no `reference_sources`, are answered with IDENTITY_MESSAGE instead of retrieval. (`ano ang database` escapes only because `database` is absent from `_FIL_SYSTEM_NOUN` while being present in `_FIL_SCOPE_CONTAINER` — an inconsistency between two noun lists that are otherwise parallel.)
- A count-only inventory reply contains the listing marker but ships zero sources, so `_archive_listing_cursor` sets `listed = True` and returns 0 via the early `if listed: return len(shown)`. Verified end-to-end: cursor after a bare "how many theses" is 0, so `continues_listing` is False and "show me the rest" cannot page — and the `listed` short-circuit also blocks the guest `guest_source_ids` fallback. The request then falls through to vector retrieval, which is precisely the failure the continuation patterns were added to fix (documented at chat.py:2277-2281).
- The `if not context:` early return (chat.py:2645) has no `log_activity` call, unlike all 12 other terminal branches. Every no-evidence turn is therefore missing from `chat_query` analytics, which biases any dashboard computed from it toward success.
- `enforce_citation_coverage` is effectively dead on the ordinary grounded path. It returns the input unchanged unless all sources share one paper id, and the grounded path selects up to 5 chunks across as many papers (per-paper cap 3, or 1 for aggregates). So for a multi-paper context the third rung of the ladder re-runs the same validation on the same text, `structural_valid == repaired_valid == False`, and the grounded fallback is always served. This is the deliberate safe behaviour (citations.py:117-119) but it means the ~40 lines at chat.py:2856-2884 never change an answer outside the overview/exact-paper paths.
- `request` is an unused parameter in both `_chat_impl` and `_chat_impl_unstamped` — verified by grep over the whole body. Only the route wrapper uses it (`_client_gone`). `evaluation/run_comparison.py:272` passes `None` for it.
- Three separate ownership round trips per authenticated edit turn: `_ensure_session_owner` (chat.py:2122), `_load_chat_history`'s own owner `select` (1507), and `_truncate_session_from_turn` -> `_ensure_session_owner` again (1540). The comment at 2110-2115 explains hoisting `resolve_effective_department` to save two round trips while leaving these three in place.
- The department hoist only helps authenticated callers. `chat()` resolves it `if user else None` (2035), and `_chat_impl_unstamped` does `resolved_department or ...`, so a guest always re-resolves — harmless (`resolve_effective_department(None, ...)` returns `settings.thesis_evaluation_department` with no DB hit) but it means `department_filter` is silently ignored for guests.
- A guest can pass `thesis_category_filter` and it is honoured (models.py:23-25 says so explicitly: "not a security boundary"). Combined with `department_filter` being ignored for guests, a guest's scope is exactly THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT x optional category.
- A plural `referenced_paper_id` list always selects `exact_papers_prompt`, because the `isinstance(list)` check precedes the `is_overview_followup` check (chat.py:2695-2698). So the `is_overview_followup=True` set at 2521 (plural overview via `_overview_question_for_sources`) never reaches `overview_prompt`; it only changes whether the question is passed to `get_paper_overview_context` for chunk ranking.
- Neither exact-paper path receives `chat_history` at all (chat.py:2704-2712 sends only `{context, question}`). A follow-up pinned to a paper therefore loses all conversational wording context, which is part of why `_resolve_specific_paper_followup` has to prefix the title into the question text.
- No duplication screen runs on any referenced-paper path (`_retrieve_evidence` returns `..., None` at chat.py:1948 and 1960). The query-time 85% novelty guard the paper describes fires only on the pure semantic branch.
- The duplication summary is a second concurrent Gemini call whose guest charge is booked but the return value discarded (`await _charge_guest_extra(...)` with no `if not ... .allowed` at chat.py:2716) — by design per the docstring, but it means an alert turn can exceed the daily ceiling before the counter catches up. Same for the two repair calls (2777, 2836). `_charge_guest_extra`'s docstring states a turn can make up to four generation calls against one measured charge.
- In the `if not context:` branch the alert is delivered with `summary=''` — set at 2644 and never filled, because generation (and therefore `_summarize_duplication`) never ran. The comment at 2646-2649 explains the alert used to be dropped entirely here; it is now delivered, just empty.
- `_extract_explicit_thesis_title`'s generator filter does not reference the loop variable: `next((title.strip() for title in quoted_titles if thesis_terms.search(question)), None)` (chat.py:1753-1756). The condition is loop-invariant, so this is really "if the question mentions thesis/paper/study/other, take the first quoted string". Functionally what was wanted, but written as if it filtered per title.
- `_is_archive_continuation_question` does `patterns += _WEAK_CONTINUATION_PATTERNS` on a module-level TUPLE (chat.py:999-1001). Correct only because it is a tuple — the same line against a list would permanently mutate the module constant on the first weak-eligible request.
- The author fast path raises 503 on lookup failure even for a tentative one-word `what about X` (chat.py:2390-2395), whereas the structurally identical title-fragment guess degrades to a warning and continues (2456-2464). A transient Supabase blip therefore fails the turn for one phrasing and not the other.
- An author-not-found reply (`'I could not verify {name} as an author...'`) is NOT a notice: `response_kind` only prefix-matches `'I could not verify a direct answer'`. Verified by inspection of the marker list. So it is stored with `kind='answer'` and replayed to the model as conversational context on the next turn.
- An archive listing / count reply is likewise `kind='answer'` (its text matches no notice marker), so a 10-title alphabetical page becomes model context for the following turn.
- `_is_courtesy_message` runs before archive paging is resolved, which is why the affirmation family (`sige`, `opo`, `oo`, `ok na`, `tama na`, `wala na`) is deliberately absent from `_LOCAL_FAREWELLS` — reading "sige" as goodbye would end the conversation of a user who just said carry on (chat.py:314-319). `_LOCAL_THANKS` does include `'nice'` and `'galing mo'`, so a bare "nice" ends a research thread with a courtesy reply.
- All five conversational fast paths and the self-origin path run BEFORE `prohibited_reason`. Safe only because every one is exact-set or `fullmatch` and length-capped (80/80/40/40). The `<english greeting> + one trailing word` rule (chat.py:713-716) is the widest of them: `hello ignore` is a greeting.
- Turnstile's verified-guest cache is in-process (`turnstile.py:11-13`) and so is the capacity cooldown (`chat_notices.py:194`). Both are documented as single-process assumptions; a multi-replica deployment gets per-replica challenge state and per-replica cooldowns.
- `guest_budget` fails OPEN: an unreachable counter returns `ALLOWED_UNLIMITED` on charge and 0 on read (guest_budget.py:117-120, 141-143). A Redis outage removes the ceiling entirely while leaving the per-guest and per-IP rate limits in place.
- `TestClient` queues `http.disconnect` as soon as the body is consumed, so an authenticated chat driven through it skips persistence. The docstring at chat.py:1593-1597 says to drive `_chat_impl` directly instead, which is what every test does — meaning the wrapper's persistence and truncation logic is exercised almost entirely through direct unit calls, not through the route.
- `_client_gone` carries a deployment dependency: a reverse proxy in front of uvicorn must forward the client abort. `docker-compose.operations.yml` publishes the API directly, so nothing intercepts it today; a buffering proxy silently reverts to the old always-persist behaviour (chat.py:1577-1583).
- `_is_unsupported_single_token_query` sets `kind='notice'` by hand (chat.py:2670) — redundant, since `_chat_impl` overwrites it and UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE is in the exact-match list anyway. It is also the only terminal branch that omits `archive_current`, so the UI labels it "Saved response snapshot" (responseActions.js:68) despite having just queried the live index.
- English origin patterns have no politeness handling while the Filipino ones do — verified: `_is_system_origin_question('who developed you po')` -> False, `_is_ambiguous_system_origin_question('sino ang nag develop netong system po')` -> True. All origin/identity/platform predicates go through `_normalized_short_question`, which strips punctuation but not particles or addressees, unlike `_short_query_candidates`.
- `match_chunks` omits `p_thesis_category` entirely rather than passing null when no scope is requested (retriever.py:794-796) — a deliberate contract so the unfiltered call stays identical to the evaluated pipeline, but it means the RPC must tolerate a missing named parameter.
- `top_similarity` is the max over the whole 15-candidate pool, not over the 5 selected blocks (retriever.py:806-807), so the trace value can exceed the similarity of anything actually sent to the model.
- `guest_source_ids` is capped at 10 (models.py:32-34) and `find_papers_by_ids` caps at 10 again, while `_ARCHIVE_INVENTORY_LIMIT` is exactly 10. A guest can therefore resolve positional references into at most one listing page, which is what the comment at chat.py:1036-1040 relies on.

### Open questions

- Is the `looks_non_english` English false-positive rate (any question containing both `a` and `at`, or the token `ar`) known and accepted? It fires an unbudgeted-in-spirit extra Gemini call on a large fraction of ordinary English questions, and `_charge_guest_extra` makes it refusable — so a guest can be turned away at stage `query_translation` for an English question.
- The live-vs-reload `notice_type` divergence for `_model_response()` and for the ambiguous-origin branch: should `_model_response()` join NOTICE_MARKERS and `notice_type()`, and should chat.py:2376 set `notice_type='conversation'` like 2202 does? Both look like omissions rather than decisions; nothing in the comments addresses them.
- Should `_FIL_WHAT_IS_THIS_SYSTEM` require a deictic? As written, `ano ang software` / `ano ang sistema` / `anong website` are answered as IskAI identity questions on a first turn. `database` escapes only by being absent from `_FIL_SYSTEM_NOUN`, which reads like an oversight given it IS in `_FIL_SCOPE_CONTAINER`.
- After a count-only reply the listing cursor is 0 while `listed` is True, so continuations cannot page. Should `_archive_listing_cursor` skip count-only rows (they carry the marker but no sources) rather than letting them set `listed`?
- Should `_is_model_question` use `_short_query_candidates` and gain a `_LOCAL_MODEL_QUESTIONS` set, for parity with the other four conversational paths?
- The `if not context:` branch logs no `chat_query` activity. Intentional, or an omission that skews the analytics dashboards?
- `_ensure_session_owner` + `_load_chat_history`'s own owner check + `_truncate_session_from_turn`'s repeat = three ownership queries per edit turn, in a file that hoisted `resolve_effective_department` specifically to save two round trips. Worth consolidating?
- Should the author-not-found reply be classified as a notice? It currently persists as `kind='answer'` and is replayed to the model as context, which is exactly the class of thing B14 exists to prevent.
- `request` is an unused parameter on `_chat_impl` / `_chat_impl_unstamped`. Kept for the harness/test signature, or removable?

**Depends on:** services/retriever (search_chunks, get_paper_overview_context, list_archive_papers, find_papers_by_ids/title/title_fragment/author, check_topic_duplication, public_source, split_author_names, _author_name_matches), services/embedder.embed_text, services/gemini_pool (arun/CHAT/active_output_ceiling/gateway_enabled — primary key, then reserve keys on capacity only, then the gateway when LLM_BASE_URL is set), services/prompts (the four templates, QUESTION_TYPE_TASKS, the sentinel, fence_history/fence_untrusted/safe_label), services/citations (normalize_citation_markers, validate_citations, filter_cited_sources, enforce_citation_coverage), services/guards (REFUSAL_MESSAGE, prohibited_reason, is_ambiguous_followup, fallback_standalone_question), services/chat_notices, services/guest_budget, services/turnstile, services/query_translation, services/question_types, services/llm_output (coerce_text, is_truncated, TruncatedGeneration), services/observability.safe_trace, services/activity.log_activity, services/rate_limiting (limiter, ip_rate_limit_key), services/db_errors.identifier_not_found, services/chunker.count_tokens (via guest_budget), dependencies/auth (require_chat_access, resolve_effective_department, get_current_user, get_user_scope, sb — the service-role client, so RLS is bypassed and every boundary is Python-side), models (ChatRequest, ChatResponse, DuplicationAlert, SessionCreate, SessionUpdate); routers/openapi_responses.errors, Postgres RPCs: match_chunks, check_topic_duplication, save_chat_exchange

**Used by:** rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx via src/api.js (the only backend transport; attaches the Supabase JWT or a per-session X-Guest-ID, plus X-Turnstile-Token for guests), rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/{messageNotice,responseActions,transcript}.js — consume kind / notice_type / no_relevant_thesis / archive_current / history_saved, rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:272 — calls `_chat_impl` in-process with an `evaluation_trace` dict to capture context/sources/top_similarity without ever attaching raw context to ChatResponse (Objective 2 harness), jmeter/ load profiles and the evaluation summarizers, which depend on capacity errors surfacing as HTTP 200 + CAPACITY_MESSAGE rather than 5xx, vite.config.js — /chat is both a React route and an API prefix, so it uses the HTML-aware proxy bypass

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 10 correction(s), 10 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "Both `_is_archive_continuation_question` and the local catalog matchers first veto on `_FIL_RETRIEVAL_GUARD` ... after `_split_scope_container` removes container phrases like 'sa system na ito'." Restated as an invariant: "`_FIL_RETRIEVAL_GUARD` must be applied to the body AFTER `_split_scope_container` removes the container phrase."
  **Actual:** False for `_is_archive_continuation_question`. It tests the guard against the RAW normalized string and never calls `_split_scope_container` at all. Only `_matches_local_inventory` (chat.py:920-923) and `_matches_local_count` (chat.py:942-943) split the container first. Verified by execution: `_matches_local_inventory('anuano ang mga theses na nanandito sa system na ito')` -> True, but `_is_archive_continuation_question('may iba pa bang tesis sa system na ito', True)` -> False, and `_is_archive_continuation_question('natitirang tesis sa archive na ito', True)` -> False. The exact 2026-09-14 failure class the container-splitting rule was added to fix is therefore still live on the continuation path: a paging request that names the container is vetoed by its own `na ito` and falls through to vector retrieval. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:989-1000 (guard at 996, no split); contrast chat.py:920-923 and 942-943`)
- **Wrong:** The map describes `_is_archive_continuation_question` as having exactly one veto (`_FIL_RETRIEVAL_GUARD`) before the pattern scan.
  **Actual:** There is a second, earlier veto the map never mentions: `_references_a_listed_item(question, normalized)` at chat.py:992, defined at chat.py:973-979. It returns False immediately when the question carries an inline numbered reference (not preceded by `_IN_DOCUMENT_COUNTABLE`), an inline ordinal reference, or `_ABOUT_A_SHOWN_ITEM` (`about it|this|that|these|those|them|the thesis|the study|the paper`). Verified: `_is_archive_continuation_question('tell me more about number 3', True)` -> False. This is the mechanism that keeps 'tell me more about number 3' a retrieval follow-up rather than a page request, and it is load-bearing for the map's own claim that '"more" belongs to both "show me more" and "tell me more about number 3"'. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:992, 973-979, 615-618`)
- **Wrong:** `_is_unsupported_single_token_query` "is also the only terminal branch that omits `archive_current`, so the UI labels it 'Saved response snapshot'."
  **Actual:** Only 4 of the ~23 returns in `_chat_impl_unstamped` set `archive_current=True`: the archive inventory return (2322), the author fast path (2418), the empty-context return (2656) and the final return (2905). Nineteen returns omit it — all five conversational fast paths, the guard refusal, all three guest-budget refusals, all three capacity returns, the ambiguous-identity, self-platform and ambiguous-origin returns, and the unclear-token return. The 'Saved response snapshot' label consequence applies to every one of them, not uniquely to this branch. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2322, 2418, 2656, 2905 (the only four); rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/responseActions.js:68`)
- **Wrong:** "The `if not context:` early return (chat.py:2645) has no `log_activity` call, unlike all 12 other terminal branches. Every no-evidence turn is therefore missing from `chat_query` analytics."
  **Actual:** The premise is right, the exclusivity is not. Three other terminal returns also emit no activity event: the pre-flight capacity return (`if _capacity_limit_is_active(): return _capacity_response(...)`, 2427-2428), the retrieval-except capacity return (2632) and the generation-except capacity return (2736). So a capacity-cooled turn is equally absent from `chat_query`, which matters more for the dashboard skew argument than the no-evidence case, since one tripped cooldown silently swallows 60 s of traffic. There are also more than 12 logging branches (17 distinct `log_activity` calls in the body, counting the closure). (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2427-2428, 2632, 2736 (no log_activity); 2645-2657`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling: "Retrieval failure (non-capacity): logged with `logger.exception('Retrieval failed')`, raises 503" — the map treats the retrieval `except` as covering the whole `asyncio.gather(search_chunks, check_topic_duplication)`.
  **Actual:** The duplication half can never raise. `check_topic_duplication` wraps its own embed + RPC in `try/except Exception`, logs `logger.exception('Topic duplication check failed')` and returns `None` (retriever.py:938-957). So a failed novelty RPC produces `alert_data = None` and the turn proceeds as though the topic were novel, with no alert, no 503, no notice and nothing in `chat_query`. The map documents no failure mode for the query-time 85% guard at all, and this is a silent-degradation path on the one feature the paper's Section 1.3 is about. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:938-960; rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1963-1986, 2628-2637`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: "`'from the live indexed archive'` must appear in every listing AND count reply. It is the only marker that lets a stored transcript be read back to find which turns were listings."
  **Actual:** `_archive_inventory_response` has one branch that omits the marker: `if not total: return f'The {department} archive currently has no indexed theses.'`. An empty-archive reply therefore carries no marker, so `_archive_listing_cursor` and `_last_turn_was_a_listing` cannot see it as a listing turn. Harmless today (the cursor would be 0 anyway), but the invariant as stated is not enforced by the code. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1061-1062, 1003-1042, 557`)
- **Wrong:** "`_chat_impl_unstamped(...)` — the pipeline body; ~19 distinct return statements"; and "~60 helper functions (621-2005)".

And 71 module-level `def`s between 499 and 1989, plus 3 nested closures (`check_duplication` 1965, `generate` 1990, `try_author_fast_path` 2382) — not ~60. Note the file's own docstring at chat.py:2086 says "~12 return statements", which is stale by the same drift. The map also enumerates none of the helpers, which the task explicitly required (`_is_archive_count_question`, `_is_archive_count_confirmation`, `_matches_local_count`, `_references_a_listed_item`, `_is_explicit_author_identity_question`, `_author_lookup_response`, `_format_names`, `_trim_name_stopwords`, `_longest_greeting_prefix`, `_matches_local_greeting`, `_strip_trailing_addressees`, `_asked_about_origin`, `_missing_referenced_papers`, `_overview_question_for_source(s)`, `_notice_response`, `_persist_chat_exchange` and others are never named). Conversely, `overview_prompt` CAN receive `chat_history`: at 2519-2521 `is_overview_followup` is set from `bool(effective_question)` while `referenced_paper_id` is only assigned `reference_sources[0].get('id')`, so a prior source row with no `id` yields `is_overview_followup=True` with a falsy `referenced_paper_id` — selecting `overview_prompt` at 2698 and then the four-key grounded input dict at 2706-2711. Omissions the checker caught:

- The 403 from the department boundary is absent from the map's otherwise exhaustive HTTP error surface: `resolve_effective_department` raises 403 'You can only search your assigned department.' for a non-superadmin whose `department_filter` does not match their profile department. It fires inside the route wrapper's threaded call (chat.py:2019-2023) before `_chat_impl` runs, and again inside the body for a direct `_chat_impl` call with no `resolved_department`. dependencies/auth.py:113-118; rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2019-2023, 2113-2115
- The Objective 2 harness's own `department_filter` is inert, which the map does not connect. `run_comparison.py:272` builds `ChatRequest(question=question, department_filter=department)` and passes `user=None`, so `resolve_effective_department(None, department)` returns `settings.thesis_evaluation_department` on the first line and never looks at the argument. The evaluated pipeline is pinned to THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT no matter what the harness asks for. rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:272-278; dependencies/auth.py:105-108
- `reference_sources` carry no `citation_id` and no `thesis_category`. `find_papers_by_ids` selects `id,title,authors,year,track,department,program_id,specialization_id` and calls `public_source(paper)` with no `citation_id` kwarg, so every positional follow-up (`_resolve_numbered_thesis_reference`, `_resolve_inline_thesis_reference`, `_is_plural_source_followup`) resolves purely by list index into that list — which is why the archive listing's page-relative `citation_id` numbering happens to line up, but only because `list_archive_papers` numbers from 1 in the same order. rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:185-200, 469-472, 44-75
- `find_papers_by_author` likewise emits sources with no `citation_id` (retriever.py:184), while `_author_lookup_response` writes `[1]`, `[2]`, ... positionally (chat.py:1275, 1286) and the author return is terminal — `filter_cited_sources` never runs on it. The one-word branch also caps at 5 results after the whole-name-part confirmation, and the two-part branch caps at 5 before and after the surname fallback, so an author with 6+ archived theses silently loses rows. rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:163-184; rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1246-1287
- `_rewrite_followup`'s acceptance gate is undocumented in the map and is narrower than `usable_translation`'s: `3 <= len(rewritten) <= 1000`, no newline, and not starting with `answer:`/`response:`. Anything else falls to `fallback_standalone_question`, so a long or multi-line rewrite is discarded after the guest has already been charged for it. rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1800-1812
- `routers/sessions.py` GET '' and POST '' carry no `responses=errors(...)` decoration, unlike PUT/DELETE/messages — so the OpenAPI schema documents no error surface for the two routes the browser hits first. And `get_session_messages` performs a function-local `from services.chat_notices import notice_type` inside the handler body rather than at module scope, re-resolving the import on every request. rag-thesis-backend/routers/sessions.py:45, 57, 97
- `_archive_inventory_response` omits the `count_only` marker sentence in the zero-total branch and in the `offset and not sources` branch appends `provenance` but not the count-only wording — three distinct provenance strings ('This count comes from the live indexed archive.' inline, the `provenance` constant, and none) all satisfying the `_ARCHIVE_LISTING_MARKER` substring except the zero-total one. rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1061-1118
- `_is_capability_question` and `_is_identity_question` iterate `_GREETINGS_ALL` (a set) for their greeting-prefix test, not `_GREETINGS_BY_LENGTH`. The map states the tuple-ordering invariant generically; it is only load-bearing for `_is_simple_conversation`'s `_longest_greeting_prefix` scan, and the code comment at chat.py:726-728 explains why the other two are safe (both halves must match, so no greeting can shadow another). rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:675-685, 722-735, 765-779
- `search_chunks` has a rollback branch the map mentions as a config lever but not as code: when `pool_size == final_count` it skips `rerank_candidates` and `apply_paper_diversity` entirely and sorts the pool by raw cosine — meaning the `per_paper_cap=1` the AGGREGATE path passes is silently ignored in that configuration. rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:800-822
- `apply_paper_diversity` backfills over-cap chunks when too few papers qualify, then re-sorts the selection into the incoming rank order — so the AGGREGATE `per_paper_cap=1` does not guarantee 5 distinct theses; it guarantees 5 chunks with distinct-thesis preference. rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:734-762

## 3. `retrieval` - Retrieval, embedding and index provenance

This is the evidence-selection half of the RAG pipeline: it turns a natural-language question into exactly five numbered, metadata-rich context blocks and a parallel `sources` list that carries citation metadata only (never PDF URLs, storage paths, or chunk text). It also owns the deterministic lookup paths that bypass vector search entirely (author, exact title, title fragment, paper-id overview, archive inventory), the query-time 85% duplication guard, the embedding-space provenance contract that stops vectors from one embedding model matching an index built by another, and the structural `[n]` citation validator/repairer that decides whether a generated answer is served or replaced by the grounded fallback. The frozen constants here (0.30 threshold, 5 blocks, 768 dims, 800/100 chunking) are the ones printed in the paper's tables.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py` (984 lines) - Retrieval phase. Module-level `sb = create_client(settings.supabase_url, settings.supabase_key)` (service-role, RLS bypassed) at :25. Holds search_chunks, the rerank/diversity selection stage, long_context_reorder, public_source, all deterministic lookup paths, and check_topic_duplication.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py` (55 lines) - Single GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings instance built at import (:21-25) with output_dimensionality=768; embed_text (query) and embed_texts (batched documents). Every call goes through gemini_pool.run(..., EMBED, ...).
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/index_provenance.py` (43 lines) - Three pure functions and three constants. Defines what gets stamped on a new index (current_index_fingerprint), what gets sent to the RPCs to scope retrieval to one embedding space (retrieval_provenance_params), and the Python-side compatibility predicate used only by scripts/reindex_citations.py.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/citations.py` (165 lines) - Pure-string citation layer: marker normalization, cited-id extraction, source filtering, substantive-unit span detection, deterministic coverage repair, and validation. No I/O, no imports beyond `re`.
- `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` (~1800+ lines) - Base schema. match_chunks at :840-898 (language plpgsql), check_topic_duplication at :925-983, paper_index_versions table at :317-345 with the verified_index_provenance_is_current CHECK, activate_paper_index at :995-1031, commit_upload_ingestion at :1665.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql` (122 lines) - Latest definition of both retrieval RPCs: identical bodies but `language sql stable` instead of plpgsql, both carrying `set hnsw.ef_search = 100`. This is what is actually deployed if migrations were applied in order.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql` (~350 lines) - Creates paper_index_versions, backfills legacy rows as 'models/gemini-embedding-2' / 'legacy_assumed' (:48), adds the chunks_index_provenance_fkey, and rewrites commit_paper_ingestion to hard-validate the provenance payload (:130-140).
- `rag-thesis-backend/config.py` (~200+ lines) - Frozen RAG constants at :21 (Literal[768]), :74-75 (Literal[800]/Literal[100]), :76-78 (threshold 0.30, match_count 5, dup 0.85), :88-89 (candidate_pool 15, per_paper_cap 3).
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py` (~2900 lines) - Sole consumer. _retrieve_evidence at :1909-1984 fans out to get_paper_overview_context or search_chunks+check_topic_duplication concurrently; the citation ladder runs at :2795-2890.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py` (large) - Parses the exact context-block shape retriever emits: _CONTEXT_HEADER at :122, _ranked_contexts at :207-220. Context Precision is computed on the order recovered by sorting the [n] markers.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_retriever.py` (~640 lines) - Pins RPC parameters, the rerank blend, tie-breaks, the diversity cap and public_source's exact key set.

### Control flow

**search_chunks (retriever.py:764-922).** The query vector is either passed in (chat.py:1975 embeds once and reuses it for both retrieval and the duplication check) or computed with `embed_text(question)`. `final_count = settings.retrieval_match_count` (5); `pool_size = max(settings.retrieval_candidate_pool, final_count)` (max(15,5)=15). The RPC payload is built at :786-796: `query_embedding`, `match_count=pool_size` (note: the POOL size, not 5), `match_threshold=settings.retrieval_threshold` (0.30), `p_department=department_filter`, plus `**retrieval_provenance_params()` which splats exactly `p_embedding_model='models/gemini-embedding-001'` and `p_embedding_dimensions=768`. `p_thesis_category` is inserted only when a category was requested (:795-796) — the key is *omitted*, not set to None, so an unfiltered call is byte-identical to the evaluated pipeline (pinned by tests/test_thesis_category.py:108-113). The whole RPC is wrapped in `retry_transient` (3 attempts, 0.25s base, doubling) which re-runs it only for transport/HTTP-status-shaped errors classified by services/network_retry.py:63. Empty pool returns `('', [], 0.0)` immediately (:803-804). `top_similarity = max(similarity over the WHOLE pool)` (:808) — this is the trace value, deliberately not the top of the *selected* set.

**Selection.** If `pool_size == final_count` the rollback lever fires (:810-813): the pool is sorted by pure descending cosine and the rerank/diversity stage is skipped entirely. Otherwise `rerank_candidates(pool, question)` then `apply_paper_diversity(ranked_pool, 5, effective_cap)` where `effective_cap = per_paper_cap if per_paper_cap is not None else settings.retrieval_per_paper_cap` (3; chat.py:2612 passes 1 when `classify_question` returned AGGREGATE).

**rerank_candidates (:705-731).** Stable pre-sort by `(-similarity, id or 0)` — this pre-sort IS the tie-break mechanism. `_search_terms(question)` stems and stopword-filters the query; if it yields nothing, or the pool has ≤1 element, the pre-sorted (pure cosine) list is returned unchanged. Otherwise `lexical = {id(chunk): _lexical_evidence_score(chunk, query_terms)}` keyed on CPython object identity; `max_lexical = max(values) or 1`. `_lexical_evidence_score` (:688-702) = `sum(min(content_terms.count(t), 4) for t in set(query_terms)) + 3 * sum(min(section_terms.count(t), 2) for t in set(query_terms))`, i.e. content hits saturate at 4 per term, section-title hits at 2 per term and count triple. Final score is `0.75 * similarity + 0.25 * (lexical / max_lexical)` (:725-729, weights at :684-685) and the list is `sorted(by_similarity, key=lambda c: -blended(c))`. Because Python's sort is stable and the input was already pre-sorted, equal blended scores resolve to higher cosine, then lower chunk id — that is the whole determinism claim.

**apply_paper_diversity (:734-761).** One forward pass: a chunk is selected if `len(selected) < final_count AND per_paper[paper_id] < per_paper_cap`, else it goes to `deferred`. A second pass drains `deferred` in rank order until `final_count` is reached, so a single-paper archive still yields 5 blocks. Then `selected.sort(key=lambda c: rank_order[id(c)])` restores the incoming rank order, so a backfilled rank-2 chunk still cites as [2].

**Hydration.** If any selected chunk lacks both `page_start` and `section`, a second query fetches `id,metadata` for the final chunks and merges (:822-833). Paper metadata for the ≤5 distinct `paper_id`s is fetched with `thesis_category,program_id,specialization_id` included, falling back to a legacy 6-column select on a 42703 missing-column error (:837-859); `_add_academic_labels` (:361-404) then does up to two more lookups (`programs`, `specializations`) and mutates the paper dicts in place, swallowing failures with a warning.

**Numbering, then reordering.** `cited_chunks = [{**chunk, 'citation_id': i+1} ...]` is assigned over the rank-ordered list (:868) and ONLY THEN is `long_context_reorder(cited_chunks)` applied (:869). long_context_reorder (:28-41) walks `reversed(items)` and alternately `insert(0)` / `append`, so [1,2,3,4,5] becomes [1,3,5,4,2] — best at both edges, worst in the middle. Context blocks are emitted in the reordered sequence as `[n] Title: ... | Authors: ... | Track: ... | Year: ... | Department: ... | Program: CODE - Name | Specialization: CODE - Name | Pages: 12-13 | Section: Methodology\\n<escaped body>` joined by `\\n\\n` (:871-907). A chunk whose paper row is missing from `paper_lookup` is silently dropped from BOTH context and sources, leaving a hole in the numbering. `sources` is built from `cited_chunks` (rank order, NOT reordered) at :910-919, so `sources[0]` is citation [1]. This ordering invariant is what `evaluation/run_comparison.py:207-220` relies on to recover the retrieval ranking by sorting the markers.

**Provenance enforcement.** The Python side only ever sends two scoping parameters. The actual guard is inside the RPC (supabase_setup.sql:884-892, migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:57-63): it joins `paper_index_versions piv ON piv.paper_id = c.paper_id AND piv.index_version = c.index_version`, then requires `c.index_version = p.active_index_version AND p.ingestion_status = 'ready' AND piv.embedding_model = p_embedding_model AND piv.embedding_dimensions = p_embedding_dimensions AND piv.provenance_status in ('verified','legacy_assumed')`. So vectors written by a different embedding model are not down-ranked — they are structurally invisible. The write side is `services/ingestion.py:213` stamping `current_index_fingerprint()` into the payload, which `commit_paper_ingestion` hard-validates field by field (migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:130-140) before inserting the `paper_index_versions` row.

**Embedding.** `embed_text` -> `gemini_pool.run(embeddings_model, EMBED, lambda client: retry_transient(client.embed_query, text, attempts=3, base=0.5s))`. `gemini_pool.attempt_chain` (gemini_pool.py:206-233) explicitly skips the gateway hop when `kind != EMBED` is false, i.e. EMBED is never routed off Google; the chain is `primary` then reserve keys, and `_should_continue` rotates a key only on a capacity error. `embed_texts` slices into batches of 64 (`_BATCH_SIZE`, embedder.py:17) and extends; the lambda uses `chunk=batch` as a default arg to defeat late binding. No L2 normalization anywhere — unnecessary because pgvector's `<=>` is cosine distance and services/novelty.py:332 divides by explicit norms.

**Citation validation ladder (routers/chat.py:2764-2890, functions in citations.py).** The raw answer is `normalize_citation_markers`d once at :2764. Then: truncated generation -> discard and serve `_grounded_retrieval_fallback`; model reported no evidence -> `filter_cited_sources` only, repair ladder deliberately bypassed; otherwise `validate_citations(answer, sources)`. `validate_citations` (:146-165) builds `allowed` from `source_citation_id(source, position)`, flags a surviving `_GROUPED_CITATION` match, flags `used - allowed` as out-of-range, walks `_substantive_units` and flags any unit with no ids or ids not ⊆ allowed, and flags `sources and not used`. On failure chat.py spends one AI repair call (`_repair_citations`), re-validates; on failure again it calls `enforce_citation_coverage` and re-validates; on failure again it serves the grounded fallback. `enforce_citation_coverage` (:93-143) is gated at :132: `if len(paper_ids) != 1 or not next(iter(paper_ids)): return repaired` — across multiple theses it does nothing but normalize, because mapping an uncited sentence onto `allowed[0]` would attribute it to an arbitrary thesis. For a single identified paper it remaps out-of-range markers to `allowed[0]` and appends ` [fallback]` at the end of each uncited substantive span, iterating `reversed(_substantive_spans(...))` so earlier offsets stay valid. Finally `unique_sources = filter_cited_sources(answer, sources)` drops every source the surviving answer never cited, and only those reach `ChatResponse.sources`.

### Public surface

- services.retriever.search_chunks(question, department_filter=None, query_embedding=None, thesis_category=None, per_paper_cap=None) -> tuple[str, list[dict], float] # (context, sources, top_similarity); returns ('', [], 0.0) when the RPC pool is empty. retriever.py:764
- services.retriever.check_topic_duplication(question, threshold=None, query_embedding=None, department_filter=None, thesis_category=None) -> dict | None # swallows every exception and returns None. retriever.py:925
- services.retriever.get_paper_overview_context(paper_id, department_filter=None, question=None) -> tuple[str, list[dict], float] # similarity is hardcoded 1.0 on success, 0.0 on miss. retriever.py:549
- services.retriever.public_source(paper, similarity=None, *, chunk=None, citation_id=None) -> dict # the ONLY shape that reaches the client. retriever.py:44
- services.retriever.long_context_reorder(items: list) -> list # local reimplementation of LangChain's LongContextReorder. retriever.py:28
- services.retriever.rerank_candidates(chunks, question) -> list[dict] # deterministic hybrid rerank. retriever.py:705
- services.retriever.apply_paper_diversity(ranked, final_count, per_paper_cap) -> list[dict] # cap + backfill + re-sort into rank order. retriever.py:734
- services.retriever.rank_paper_chunks(chunks, question, paper) -> list[dict] # within-one-paper lexical rank used only by get_paper_overview_context. retriever.py:514
- services.retriever.safe_chunk_text(content) -> str # html.escape(quote=False) then rewrite line-initial [n] to (n). retriever.py:81
- services.retriever.chunk_location(chunk) -> dict # {page_start, page_end, section}, columns first then legacy JSON metadata. retriever.py:100
- services.retriever.find_papers_by_author(name, department_filter=None) -> list[dict] # retriever.py:163
- services.retriever.find_papers_by_ids(paper_ids, department_filter=None) -> list[dict] # dedupes, caps at 10, preserves caller order. retriever.py:185
- services.retriever.find_papers_by_title(title, department_filter=None) -> list[dict] # requires normalized full-title equality. retriever.py:268
- services.retriever.find_papers_by_title_fragment(fragment, department_filter=None) -> list[dict] # returns ALL matches; caller must require exactly one. retriever.py:319
- services.retriever.normalize_title_fragment(value) -> str # retriever.py:303
- services.retriever.split_author_names(authors) -> list[str] # splits on , ; and the word 'and'. retriever.py:110
- services.retriever.list_archive_papers(department_filter=None, thesis_category=None, limit=10, offset=0) -> tuple[int, list[dict]] # exact count + page; citation_ids number the PAGE from 1. retriever.py:407
- services.retriever.sb # the shared service-role Supabase client; scripts/reindex_citations.py imports it directly (reindex_citations.py:318)
- services.embedder.embed_text(text) -> list[float] # embedder.py:38
- services.embedder.embed_texts(texts) -> list[list[float]] # batches of 64. embedder.py:46
- services.embedder.embeddings_model # module-level client, also reused as gemini_pool.run's `primary`. embedder.py:21
- services.index_provenance.current_index_fingerprint() -> dict # 8 keys: embedding_model, embedding_dimensions, preprocessing_version, chunking_version, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, provenance_status='verified'. index_provenance.py:11
- services.index_provenance.retrieval_provenance_params() -> dict # {'p_embedding_model', 'p_embedding_dimensions'} only. index_provenance.py:25
- services.index_provenance.is_embedding_compatible(index: dict | None) -> bool # index_provenance.py:33
- services.index_provenance.PREPROCESSING_VERSION = 'document-v1', PROVENANCE_STATUS_VERIFIED = 'verified', PROVENANCE_STATUS_LEGACY = 'legacy_assumed'
- services.citations.normalize_citation_markers(answer) -> str # [1, 2] / [1; 2] -> [1] [2]. citations.py:11
- services.citations.cited_ids(answer) -> set[int] # every \[(\d+)\] anywhere in the string. citations.py:20
- services.citations.source_citation_id(source, position) -> int # source['citation_id'] or 1-based position. citations.py:24
- services.citations.filter_cited_sources(answer, sources) -> list[dict] # sorted by citation number. citations.py:29
- services.citations.enforce_citation_coverage(answer, sources) -> str # single-paper-only deterministic repair. citations.py:93
- services.citations.validate_citations(answer, sources) -> tuple[bool, list[str]] # citations.py:146
- RPC public.match_chunks(query_embedding vector(768), match_count integer, match_threshold double precision, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions integer, p_thesis_category text default null) returns (id bigint, paper_id uuid, chunk_index integer, content text, metadata jsonb, page_start integer, page_end integer, section text, similarity float) — service_role only, set hnsw.ef_search = 100
- RPC public.check_topic_duplication(query_embedding vector(768), dup_threshold double precision, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions integer, p_thesis_category text default null) returns 14 columns, limit 1 — service_role only, set hnsw.ef_search = 100

### Invariants

- Citation numbers are assigned in post-rerank quality-rank order BEFORE long_context_reorder (retriever.py:868-869). `sorted(markers)` must always recover the asserted ranking — evaluation/run_comparison.py:207-220 computes Context Precision on exactly that, and retriever.py:771-782 documents it as the contract.
- `sources[i]` corresponds to citation id i+1 on the semantic path, because `sources` is built from `cited_chunks` (rank order), not from `reordered`. citations.source_citation_id falls back to 1-based position when `citation_id` is absent, which is what makes the metadata-only lookup paths (find_papers_by_author etc., which never set citation_id) interoperate with filter_cited_sources.
- Exactly `settings.retrieval_match_count` (5) blocks reach the prompt whenever the pool can supply them — apply_paper_diversity backfills over-cap chunks rather than returning a short context (retriever.py:755-758).
- The 0.30 threshold is applied SQL-side only, as `1 - (c.embedding <=> query_embedding) >= match_threshold`. Nothing in Python re-filters on similarity, so every chunk in the pool already cleared it.
- match_count sent to the RPC is the POOL size, not the final block count. Anything reading the RPC argument as 'how many blocks the model saw' is wrong.
- retrieval_provenance_params() sends only model + dimensions. Chunk layout (chunking_version, tokenizer, chunk sizes) is deliberately NOT part of the retrieval filter — is_embedding_compatible (index_provenance.py:33-43) documents this: compatibility is about the vector space, not the chunk boundaries.
- `p_thesis_category` must be absent from the RPC payload (not None) when no category scope was requested, in both search_chunks (:795) and check_topic_duplication (:948). Pinned by tests/test_thesis_category.py:108-120.
- commit_paper_ingestion rejects any index_provenance payload that is not exactly {embedding_dimensions=768, preprocessing_version='document-v1', chunking_version='token-v1', tokenizer='cl100k_base', chunk_size_tokens=800, chunk_overlap_tokens=100, provenance_status='verified'} with a non-empty embedding_model (migrations/20260720:130-140). The same five values are also a table CHECK. Bumping PREPROCESSING_VERSION or CHUNKING_VERSION in Python without a matching migration makes EVERY new ingestion fail at the commit RPC.
- Embeddings are never routed through the OpenAI-compatible gateway: gemini_pool.attempt_chain:228 gates the gateway hop on `kind != EMBED`, and _build:157 always constructs a GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings for EMBED. A gateway-sourced vector would return zero rows (wrong model name in the piv filter), presenting as an empty archive rather than a misconfiguration.
- No L2 normalization is performed on any embedding. This is correct only because every comparison is cosine: pgvector `<=>` in both RPCs, and explicit norm division in services/novelty.py:332 and :264-266.
- enforce_citation_coverage must never run when the sources span more than one paper, or when the single paper id is falsy (citations.py:132). The docstring records this as an audited 2026-09-12 misattribution defect; relaxing the gate reintroduces it.
- safe_chunk_text must be applied to every archived body before it enters a prompt (retriever.py:667, :906). It both html-escapes (so `</retrieved_context>` cannot close the fence) and rewrites line-initial `[n]` to `(n)` so manuscript text cannot forge a context header — which would otherwise inflate run_comparison's retrieved-context count and corrupt Context Precision.
- find_papers_by_author / find_papers_by_title interpolate their argument into an `ilike '%...%'` pattern with no escaping. Safety depends entirely on the caller: chat.py:1163-1175 `_extract_author_name` restricts to `[A-Za-z'’-]`, and the title paths derive `lookup_token` from `re.findall(r'[a-z0-9]+')`. Any new caller passing raw user text introduces ILIKE wildcard injection.
- Both RPCs carry `set hnsw.ef_search = 100` on the function. Removing it drops the candidate window to 40, below which the six post-filter equality predicates can starve a query of rows even when qualifying chunks exist (migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:1-31).

### Data model

- `chunks` row as returned by match_chunks: {id: bigint, paper_id: uuid, chunk_index: int, content: text, metadata: jsonb, page_start: int, page_end: int, section: text, similarity: float}. `similarity` is computed in SQL as `1 - (c.embedding <=> query_embedding)`.
- `public.paper_index_versions` (supabase_setup.sql:317-345): PK (paper_id, index_version); embedding_model text not null; embedding_dimensions int not null CHECK = 768; preprocessing_version, chunking_version text not null; tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens nullable; provenance_status CHECK in ('verified','legacy_assumed'); CHECK verified_index_provenance_is_current forces verified rows to document-v1 / token-v1 / cl100k_base / 800 / 100. RLS enabled, all privileges revoked from public/anon/authenticated, granted only to service_role.
- `public.chunks` carries FK chunks_index_provenance_fkey (paper_id, index_version) -> paper_index_versions ON DELETE RESTRICT, so a provenance row cannot be deleted while its chunks exist.
- `public.papers.active_index_version uuid` is the single switch that makes an index live; match_chunks filters `c.index_version = p.active_index_version`, so stale index versions kept for the rollback window are invisible to retrieval but still occupy the HNSW index.
- public_source output shape (retriever.py:44-75): always {id, title, authors, year, track, department}; plus 'thesis_category' ONLY when the key was present in the row (null coerced to 'student'); plus any truthy of program_code/program_name/specialization_code/specialization_name; plus 'similarity' = round(cosine*100, 2) when similarity is not None; plus 'citation_id' when given; plus {chunk_id, chunk_index, page_start, page_end, section} when a chunk is given. Never content, pdf_url, storage_path, filename, embedding.
- current_index_fingerprint() dict (index_provenance.py:13-22) — written verbatim as `paper.index_provenance` into commit_paper_ingestion and into scripts/release_fingerprint.py's manifest.
- ChatResponse.sources is `list[dict]` (models.py:60) — untyped, so the public_source contract is enforced only by tests/test_retriever.py:42-47, not by pydantic.

### Config knobs

- EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS — Literal[768]; any other value raises ValidationError at startup (config.py:21, pinned by tests/test_index_provenance.py:54-60). Changing it also needs a DB migration: the pgvector column and the paper_index_versions CHECK both hardcode 768.
- CHUNK_SIZE_TOKENS / CHUNK_OVERLAP_TOKENS — Literal[800]/Literal[100] (config.py:74-75); also hardcoded in the verified_index_provenance_is_current CHECK and in commit_paper_ingestion's validation.
- RETRIEVAL_THRESHOLD — float, ge=0 le=1, default 0.30. Env-overridable despite being a frozen paper constant; only tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:136 pins it.
- RETRIEVAL_MATCH_COUNT — int, ge=1 le=20, default 5. Raising it at or above RETRIEVAL_CANDIDATE_POOL silently disables the rerank + diversity stage.
- RETRIEVAL_CANDIDATE_POOL — int, ge=5 le=20, default 15. Setting it to 5 is the documented rollback lever to the pure-cosine pipeline. Undocumented in .env.example.
- RETRIEVAL_PER_PAPER_CAP — int, ge=1 le=5, default 3; overridden to 1 at chat.py:2612 for AGGREGATE questions. Undocumented in .env.example.
- DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD — float, default 0.85; used by check_topic_duplication, novelty.screen_new_submission and the faculty scan.
- GEMINI_EMBED_MODEL — default 'models/gemini-embedding-001'. This string is the join key for retrieval: changing it makes every existing index invisible until reindexed.
- THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT — default 'CCSICT'; becomes the guest department filter passed as p_department.
- LLM_BASE_URL / LLM_API_KEY — configure the OpenAI-compatible gateway for chat/extract/verdict ONLY; deliberately inert for embeddings.
- hnsw.ef_search = 100 — set on the two retrieval functions themselves, not session-level; there is no per-request GUC hook through PostgREST.

### Error handling

- `retry_transient` (services/network_retry.py:84-111) wraps every Supabase call in retriever.py: 3 attempts, 0.25s base with doubling, retrying only when `is_transient_network_error` matches a WinError/errno in {10035,10053,10054,10060,11001}, a `status_code`/`status` attribute in {408,429,500,502,503,504}, one of 14 text markers, or a *labelled* HTTP status in the message (bare 3-digit substrings are deliberately excluded, network_retry.py:29-42).
- `_is_missing_column_error` (retriever.py:476-482) detects Postgres 42703 by `error.code`, by the literal `'code': '42703'` in the string, or by `'column ' ... ' does not exist'`. It gates a legacy-select fallback in find_papers_by_ids, _ready_title_candidates, get_paper_overview_context (twice), list_archive_papers and search_chunks. The fallback selects fewer columns, which means program/specialization labels and thesis_category silently disappear from the response rather than erroring.
- `_add_academic_labels` catches every exception and logs `Academic label lookup failed; using paper metadata (%s)` (retriever.py:388-392), returning silently — sources lose their program/specialization labels with no user-visible signal.
- `check_topic_duplication` catches everything, logs `Topic duplication check failed`, returns None (retriever.py:955-957).
- `search_chunks` lets exceptions propagate. chat.py:2632-2644 converts them: capacity errors trip `_mark_capacity_limited()` and return HTTP 200 with CAPACITY_MESSAGE; everything else becomes HTTP 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable.'
- `embed_text` failures surface through `gemini_pool.run`, which re-raises the LAST error after exhausting the chain (gemini_pool.py:281). Retries multiply: 3 `retry_transient` attempts per client × (primary + N reserve keys). A capacity error also calls `_mark_exhausted(api_key)` putting that key in a `gemini_key_cooldown_seconds` (default 60s) penalty box.
- `_get_encoder` in services/chunker.py:31-39 fails closed with `RuntimeError('Unable to initialize required tokenizer cl100k_base')` — index_provenance imports TOKENIZER_ENCODING from it at module load, so a missing tiktoken cache breaks provenance stamping at first use, not at import.
- commit_paper_ingestion raises `Verified 768-dimensional index provenance is required` on any fingerprint mismatch; commit_upload_ingestion (supabase_setup.sql:1665) delegates to it after its own lease/cancellation checks, so a provenance drift fails the ingestion job rather than writing a bad index.
- Citation failures never raise: the ladder in chat.py degrades AI repair -> deterministic repair -> `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` (chat.py:1315-1352), which prefixes `chat_notices.GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX` so `response_kind()` stores it as a notice, keeps up to 3 deduplicated source cards with their markers, and is never replayed to the model as history.

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_retriever.py — TestLongContextReorder pins the [1,3,5,4,2] edge placement; TestIndirectAccessModel:32-47 pins public_source's exact key set and similarity==91.23 rounding; TestChunkRetrieval:131-152 pins every match_chunks parameter including match_count == max(pool, match_count), p_embedding_model and p_embedding_dimensions==768, and the escaping of `</retrieved_context>`; TestHybridRerank:473-525 pins the 0.75/0.25 blend arithmetic, the triple section weight, pure-cosine fallback on all-stopword queries, order-independence of ties, and the per-term saturation at 4; TestPaperDiversityCap:528-604 pins cap binding, single-paper backfill, rank-order restoration after backfill, cap=1 distinct sampling, exactly-5 blocks, and the per_paper_cap override.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_index_provenance.py — pins the full 8-key fingerprint, the 2-key retrieval params, that both 'verified' and 'legacy_assumed' are readable, that a different model / 3072 dims / an unknown status are rejected, and that EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS cannot be changed by env.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_thesis_category.py:100-135 — pins the conditional p_thesis_category contract for both RPCs and public_source's thesis_category emission rules (absent key vs null -> 'student').
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_rag_controls.py:280-430 — the citation suite: grouped-marker rejection, normalization, out-of-range detection, uncited-unit detection, single-paper repair, and (at :386-400) the cross-paper case where repair must NOT run.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_prompt_contracts.py:145-215 — asserts enforce_citation_coverage staples `[1]` onto an uncited unit and that validate_citations rejects it beforehand.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py:89-96 whitelists the plpgsql-vs-sql divergence for both retrieval RPCs; :189-205 asserts both copies of both functions still carry `set hnsw.ef_search`.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:91-104 pins the provenance backfill literals including 'models/gemini-embedding-2', the 768 CHECK, the piv.embedding_model filter and the FK ON DELETE RESTRICT.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_release_fingerprint.py:25-28 pins retrieval_threshold, retrieval_match_count, retrieval_candidate_pool and retrieval_per_paper_cap into the manifest's rag_contract.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:136 pins settings.retrieval_threshold == 0.30.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_logic.py:44-62 pins filter_cited_sources ordering, deduplication and out-of-range dropping.

### Gotchas

- **Raising retrieval_match_count above retrieval_candidate_pool silently disables the entire selection stage.** `pool_size = max(candidate_pool, final_count)` (retriever.py:785), and the rollback lever is `if pool_size == final_count` (:810). With candidate_pool=15 and match_count=20 (both in range: pool is ge=5 le=20, match_count is ge=1 le=20), pool_size becomes 20 == final_count, so rerank_candidates and apply_paper_diversity never run and the pipeline reverts to pure cosine order — with no log line, and `release_fingerprint` still records both settings as if selection were active.
- **The legacy provenance backfill hardcodes an embedding model that does not exist in this configuration.** supabase_setup.sql:354 and migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:48 insert `'models/gemini-embedding-2'`, while config.py:18 sets `models/gemini-embedding-001`. Because match_chunks requires `piv.embedding_model = p_embedding_model`, every row created by that backfill is permanently unreachable by retrieval — which makes the `provenance_status in ('verified','legacy_assumed')` clause and is_embedding_compatible's acceptance of `legacy_assumed` effectively dead for exactly the rows they were written for. It also makes scripts/reindex_citations.py:166 raise 'Embedding model change requires --allow-model-change' for every such paper. tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:95 pins the literal `'models/gemini-embedding-2'`, so the two values are pinned apart from each other.
- **The legacy chunk-location lookup at retriever.py:822-833 is redundant work.** match_chunks already returns `c.metadata` in its result set (supabase_setup.sql:853, migrations/20260903:52), so the follow-up `select id,metadata from chunks where id in (...)` can only re-fetch what is already in hand. It fires whenever any selected chunk has neither page_start nor section — which is exactly when the metadata column is the fallback — costing an extra round trip per question on legacy indexes for no new data.
- **activate_paper_index does not check the embedding model.** supabase_setup.sql:1015-1020 only requires `provenance_status in ('verified','legacy_assumed')`. An index built with a different embedding model can therefore be activated successfully and then return zero rows from match_chunks forever; the paper looks 'ready' in the admin UI and is invisible to chat.
- **match_chunks is defined twice with different languages.** supabase_setup.sql:849 uses `language plpgsql`; migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:48 uses `language sql stable`. tests/test_schema_consistency.py:89-96 knowingly whitelists this for both retrieval RPCs. Consequence: a fresh project built from supabase_setup.sql and a migrated project run structurally different functions (the plpgsql one is not `stable` and cannot be inlined either way), and replaying supabase_setup.sql over a migrated database silently reverts the migration's body — the create-or-replace hazard CLAUDE.md warns about, here for the single hottest function in the system.
