  **Actual:** Branch 1 of _GENERATE_OWNED_ARTIFACT interpolates the FULL `_GENERATION_VERB` (guards.py:95), which includes Tier B — not an English-only verb list. Verified: `_GENERATE_OWNED_ARTIFACT.search('gumawa ka ng my thesis')` matches. Only branches 2 and 3 (the Filipino-possessive ones) restrict the verb, and they use `_FIL_REQUEST_VERB | _FIL_TIERB_COMPLEMENT` — not "Tier A only": _FIL_TIERB_COMPLEMENT (guards.py:186) admits `nga (agsurat|agaramid|agbukel|agpartuat)`. The measured behaviour the invariant is protecting does hold (`prohibited_reason('sino ang gumawa ng tesis ko')` → None), but the stated mechanism is wrong. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/guards.py:186-196`)
- **Wrong:** "worst case for one embedding is 2 SDK attempts x 3 retry_transient attempts x (1 + len(reserve_keys)) hops" / "No jitter and three stacked retry layers"
  **Actual:** There is no SDK retry layer on the embedding path. `_build`'s EMBED branch passes only model, google_api_key and output_dimensionality — `max_retries` is passed at gemini_pool.py:113 (gateway), :143 (CHAT/EXTRACT) and :154 (VERDICT) but never for EMBED, and `embedder.embeddings_model` omits it too. So embeddings have exactly two layers (retry_transient × key rotation ⇒ up to 3 × (1+reserves) calls). Conversely the chat path has SDK max_retries and key rotation but no retry_transient — the three layers never stack on any single call. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/gemini_pool.py:157-162, rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:21-25`)
- **Wrong:** "gateway failures log `'Gemini gateway gateway hop failed (%s); falling back to Google'`"
  **Actual:** The literal is `'Gemini gateway hop failed (%s); falling back to Google'` — one "gateway", not two. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/gemini_pool.py:247-250`)
- **Wrong:** "Six spans exist … Every call site guards `if run:` before `add_metadata`."
  **Actual:** Only three of the six bind a run object at all: `rag.chat.total` (chat.py:2017, `as run`), `rag.retrieval` (:2599, `as retrieval_run`), `rag.generation` (:2718, `as generation_run`). `rag.citation_repair` (:1835), `rag.embedding` (:1958) and `rag.duplication` (:1966) are `async with safe_trace(...)` with no `as` clause, so they emit a span and never call add_metadata. The stated `if run:` discipline applies to three sites, not six. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1835,1958,1966`)
- **Wrong:** "a 14-test TestGateway class"
  **Actual:** TestGateway defines 16 test methods (test_gemini_pool.py:217, 220, 223, 230, 234, 240, 246, 253, 267, 275, 285, 292, 297, 313, 330, 340), several of them parametrized, so the collected count is higher still. The list of what they pin is otherwise accurate. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_gemini_pool.py:209-340`)
- **Wrong:** capacity/error anchors: "chat.py:2630, 2734" for mark_capacity_limited; "2633-2637" 503; "2737-2740" 502; "chat.py:2696-2702" prompt selection; "chat.py:2704-2712" generation_input; "`_chat_impl` (2094)"
  **Actual:** All off by one or more. `_mark_capacity_limited()` is at chat.py:2631 and :2735. The 503 raise is 2632-2636, the 502 raise 2736-2740. Prompt selection is 2694-2701 (`if isinstance(referenced_paper_id, list):` at 2695). `generation_input` is 2703-2712. `_chat_impl` is *defined* at 2076; 2094 is the `response.kind = chat_notices.response_kind(response)` restamp inside it. `_chat_impl_unstamped` at 2098 and chat.py's length of 2906 (map says ~2915) are right. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2076,2094,2631,2695,2703,2735`)
- **Wrong:** Test anchors in the invariants section: "test_prompt_contracts.py:253-263" (repair prompts carry CITATION+FORMAT, never EVIDENCE) and ":335-339" (TASK blocks carry no braces)
  **Actual:** Drifted by 8-10 lines. The repair-contract assertions are at 268-272 (test defined at 255); 253-263 lands on the preceding fence test plus its docstring. The no-brace assertions are at 327-329 (test at 325); 335-339 lands on `test_the_other_three_prompts_never_carry_a_type_block` and the aggregate-block test. The 307-309 byte-identical-composition anchor, :82 (prompt_version), :73 (department injection) and :93-113 (history fence) are all correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_prompt_contracts.py:268-272,325-329`)
- **Wrong:** "`resolve_effective_department` (dependencies/auth.py:123) returns `departments.name` verbatim"
  **Actual:** Line 123 is the `sb.table('departments').select('name').eq('name', selected)` existence check. The function returns `assigned` (auth.py:119) for a pinned non-superadmin, or `selected` (auth.py:129) — i.e. the *requested* string after confirming a row with that exact name exists, not the column value read back. The downstream consequence the map draws (an attacker-chosen department name reaches `identity()` unfiltered) still holds, because the name must equal the stored one. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:119-129`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- **A third module sends prompts to a generation model and the map never mentions it.** `services/query_translation.py` owns `_TRANSLATION_PROMPT` (query_translation.py:83-99), exposed as `translation_prompt()` (:101-103), called from `routers/chat.py:1821` and invoked through `gemini_pool.arun(llm, gemini_pool.CHAT, …)` at chat.py:1823-1824, gated by `looks_non_english()` at chat.py:2577 and reached at chat.py:2586. The map's purpose statement ("prompts.py owns every text the system sends to a generation model") is therefore false in a second place, and its gotcha about routers/duplication.py's two inline prompts has an exact twin here. The module docstring (query_translation.py:20-27) and the comment at :79-82 state outright that it is kept out of prompts.py *so PROMPT_VERSION does not move*. `services/query_translation.py` is also absent from `release_fingerprint.build_manifest()`'s input list even though `services/question_types.py` — added for exactly the "selection decides what reaches the prompt" reason — is in it (scripts/release_fingerprint.py:66-67).
- **That translation prompt is the only model-facing prompt in the chat path with unfenced third-party text.** `translation_prompt()` does `_TRANSLATION_PROMPT.format(question=question)` with the raw user question, no `fence_untrusted`, no `<untrusted_*>` tag and no "never instructions" directive — the exact gap `prompts.followup_rewrite_prompt`'s docstring (prompts.py:391-394) says it closed for the last such prompt, and the one `tests/test_untrusted_prompt_framing.py` exists to prevent. (I checked the adjacent theory and it is NOT a bug: braces in the *question* are safe, because `str.format` only parses the template, so `translation_prompt('… {x} …')` returns fine.) Blast radius is bounded: `usable_translation` (query_translation.py:106-123) rejects multi-line output, anything outside 3-400 chars, and `answer:`/`response:`/`query:`/`i cannot` openers, and the result is only embedded — never shown, stored, or put in the generation prompt. So this is query steering / retrieval-quality, not answer injection.
- **Two Gemini call sites silently swallow capacity errors.** The map says `mark_capacity_limited()` is called from exactly two places, which is true, but it does not note the consequence: `_rewrite_followup` (chat.py:1810-1812) and `_translate_for_retrieval` (chat.py:1826-1828) each catch `Exception`, log at WARNING and fall back, with no `_is_capacity_error` test. A 429 on the rewrite or translation call therefore neither trips the cooldown nor surfaces — the turn proceeds to retrieval and generation and only trips the cooldown there, and if those succeed the exhaustion is never recorded at all. Same shape at `_summarize_duplication` (chat.py:1905-1906), which `logger.exception`s and returns ''.
- **`_grounded_retrieval_fallback` can emit NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX text.** chat.py:1316-1317 returns `get_no_relevant_message(department)` when `sources` is empty, so the map's characterisation of GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX as the always-cited fallback is incomplete. In practice all four call sites (chat.py:2794, 2811, 2880, 2887) sit past the `if not context: return …` guard so sources is non-empty, and `response_kind` classifies either text as KIND_NOTICE — but the caller never sets `no_relevant_thesis=True` for the degenerate case.
- **A helper the notice inventory omits:** `_notice_response(message, session_id)` (chat.py:1354-1356) is the shared "never any sources" constructor behind `_capacity_response` (:1360) and `_guest_budget_response` (:1364). Minor, but it is the only place the "a notice carries no sources" rule is enforced structurally rather than by hand.

## 5. `upload-router` - Upload HTTP surface and job queueing

This is the write-side entry point of the thesis archive: it authenticates an uploader against the role-feature matrix, validates and hashes a PDF, resolves the manuscript's department/program/specialization against the server-owned catalog, stages the bytes into the private `pdfs` bucket, and reserves a durable row in `upload_jobs` under a two-phase `reserve_upload_job` -> storage upload -> `queue_upload_job` commit. The API never runs the ingestion pipeline; a separate leased worker does, and this module also exposes the authoritative polling, cancellation, and title-page metadata-autofill endpoints the admin UI drives. The same module additionally carries a large deterministic title-page parser (title / authors / year / department / program / specialization) used to pre-fill the upload form, with Gemini only filling what the local pass leaves blank.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py` (1545 lines) - The whole /upload HTTP surface: 7 endpoints, PDF validation, idempotency, two-phase staging commit, job polling, cancellation, and the local title-page metadata extractor (lines 86-480 are pure parsing; 480-920 are staging; 1053-1310 are polling/cancel/tracks; 1313-1545 are extraction endpoints)
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py` (184 lines) - Service-role RPC wrappers used by the WORKER, not the router: claim/heartbeat/retry/fail/cleanup plus the retry-delay ladder `_RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600)` at line 13 and `retry_at()` at line 111
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/catalog.py` (115 lines) - `normalize_thesis_category` (line 21) and `resolve_academic_selection` (line 53): the only place department/program/specialization UUID ownership and the legacy-track translation are validated
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/catalog.py` (241 lines) - `_nested_catalog` (line 65) with two levels of pre-migration fallback, `active_track_names` (line 118) and `active_programs` (line 145) — the vocabularies the upload extractor and /upload/tracks read; plus superadmin CRUD for programs/specializations
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/filenames.py` (29 lines) - `sanitize_filename` — strips path components, replaces every char outside [A-Za-z0-9._-] with '_', caps the stem at 100 chars, and with `force_suffix='pdf'` pins the extension
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/malware.py` (61 lines) - Fail-closed ClamAV INSTREAM client. NOT called from routers/upload.py at all — only from services/ingestion.py:115 (worker) and routers/duplication.py:196
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql` (473 lines) - Original queue DDL and RPCs: reserve_upload_job (line 89), queue_upload_job (line 156), claim_upload_job (line 175), heartbeat/retry/fail/commit/cleanup/expire
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql` (~560 lines) - Adds the `cancelled` status, cancel_* columns, `upload_job_events` + its trigger, `request_upload_cancellation` (line 157), `heartbeat_upload_job_control` (line 218), `finalize_upload_cancellation` (line 250), and REPLACES claim/retry/commit/cleanup/expire with cancellation-aware bodies (lines 350-482)
- `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` (~1400+ lines) - Base schema. upload_jobs table at line 772 (only 4 statuses in the inline check), then re-applies the whole durable block at lines 1323-1500+ including the 7-status check and the cancel_* columns
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/request_limits.py` (~180 lines) - `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` + `limit_for_path` (line 61): the pre-authentication body ceiling that stops an unauthenticated caller spooling an arbitrary body to disk on the /upload/* multipart routes
- `rag-thesis-backend/models.py` (—) - UploadAccepted (115), UploadJobStatus (122), UploadCancelRequest/Response (144/148), BatchRow (157), BatchExtractedFile (187), BatchFileResult (206), BatchUploadAccepted (217), UploadJobList (223)

### Control flow

POST /upload/paper (routers/upload.py:885) runs three resolution steps in a fixed order the clients' tests depend on: `resolve_effective_department(user, department)` off-thread, then `_validate_metadata(title, authors, year, abstract)`, then `_resolve_staging_context(...)`. That context call internally invokes `_resolve_upload_scope`, which calls `resolve_effective_department` a **second** time on the already-resolved name (idempotent but a redundant profile read), normalises `thesis_category` through `services/catalog.normalize_thesis_category`, and resolves the academic selection via `resolve_academic_selection` with `require_program = (category == 'student')`. Catalog resolution is three sequential single-row reads: department by `name` + `active`, program by `id` + `department_id` + `active`, specialization by `id` + `program_id` + `active`; each miss is a 422 with a distinct message. If no `program_id` is given and the legacy `track` is one of {Data Mining, Web Development, Network Security} it is translated to a (program_code, specialization_code) pair and re-resolved inside the same department; the two `AMBIGUOUS_LEGACY_TRACKS` yield `classification_status='needs_review'` with null ids but only when a program is not required. BSCS/BSIT additionally require a specialization, and every other program code rejects one.

Everything then funnels into `_stage_and_queue_one` (line 764), which is the two-phase commit and is shared verbatim by the batch path. It reads at most `max_upload_mb*1MB + 1` bytes (`_read_limited_upload`, line 515) and validates off-thread (`_validate_pdf_upload`, line 485) in this order: filename ends `.pdf` -> 415; content-type in {application/pdf, application/x-pdf} -> 415; empty -> 400; over 25 MB -> 413; missing `%PDF-` magic -> 422; `fitz.open` raises -> 422 'Malformed or unreadable PDF'; `document.needs_pass` -> 422; page_count < 1 -> 422; page_count > 500 -> 422. It returns the sanitized filename. The idempotency key is parsed at line 781 (`uuid.UUID(value)` or, when the header is absent, a **fresh uuid4**; a malformed key is 400). A candidate `job_id = uuid4()` and `source_path = uploads/{user}/{job}/{name}` are minted, the SHA-256 is computed off-thread, and `request_payload` is assembled.

Phase 1 is `reserve_upload_job` (line 797): it validates the digest against `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` and `max_attempts` in 1..10, `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (owner_id, idempotency_key) DO NOTHING` at status='staging'/stage='store'/progress=5, re-selects the row, and raises SQLSTATE 22000 'Idempotency key was already used for different content' if `content_sha256` differs. The router maps that substring to 409 (line 809) and every other RPC exception to 503; a falsy result is 503 too. Critically, lines 815-817 then **overwrite** the local `job_id`, `source_path`, and `status` with the row the database actually holds, so a replay re-stages into the original path under the original job id. If the row already existed and is no longer 'staging', the handler short-circuits with a 202 carrying that status and 'This submission is already tracked' (line 818).

Phase 2 is the storage upload (line 827, upsert=true). On failure it compensates: `_remove_staged_source` (which on its own failure logs and enqueues `storage_cleanup_queue` via `record_storage_cleanup`), then `_fail_staging_job`, which is a **direct table UPDATE** (not an RPC) guarded by `.eq('status','staging')` that writes status=failed/stage=error/progress=100, a fixed public error, `cleanup_status = pending|not_required` and `source_stored = not removed`, plus completed_at and expires_at = now+30d — then 503. Phase 3 is `queue_upload_job` (line 842), which flips staging -> queued, stage='queued', progress=8, `source_stored=true`, `next_retry_at=now()`. A false return is only treated as an error when a re-read of the authoritative status is not in {queued, processing, retry_wait, completed}; on any exception the same set is re-checked before compensating, precisely so a lost response after a committed transition never deletes the source out from under a worker. The successful return always reports `status='queued'` (line 875) regardless of what the row advanced to.

POST /upload/batch (line 1001) rejects envelope problems first: `_check_batch_size` (empty -> 422, > `max_batch_files`=20 -> 413), `_parse_batch_rows` (JSON list validated by `TypeAdapter(list[BatchRow])`; length must equal len(files); every `idempotency_key` must be a UUID and unique inside the request; a row carrying `specialization_id` without `program_id` is 422). It resolves the scope once, builds a `_ClassificationCache`, and if any row omits `program_id` it resolves the request-level classification up front so a bad shared classification kills the batch before a single byte is read. Then it loops **sequentially** (comment at line 1043: concurrency would hold 20 x 25 MB in memory), and each iteration calls `_stage_batch_file`, which catches `HTTPException` into `BatchFileResult(error, status_code)` and any other exception into a generic 500-shaped row. The envelope returns 202 with accepted/rejected counts.

Polling: `_select_upload_jobs` (line 1066) tries the extended column list and, only if the error text mentions `cancel_requested_at`/`cancelled_at`, retries with `_JOB_FIELDS_LEGACY`. `_job_status_model` (line 1117) derives `cancel_requested` from `cancel_requested_at` and computes `can_cancel = status in {staging, queued, retry_wait} or (status == 'processing' and not cancel_requested)`. `last_event_at` comes from `upload_job_events` — one read per job for `/status`, one `in_()` read for `/jobs`; both failures are logged and degrade to null.

Cancellation: `request_upload_cancellation` takes `FOR UPDATE` on the row, returns not_found / forbidden (non-superadmin and not the owner) / already_terminal ({completed, failed, cancelled}), and otherwise branches on status. For 'processing' it only stamps `cancel_requested_at`/`cancel_requested_by`/`cancel_reason` and a message, returning 'cancellation_requested'; the worker observes it through `heartbeat_upload_job_control` (which returns `{lease_valid, cancel_requested}` and suppresses further message writes once cancellation is pending), raises `CancellationRequested` at the next checkpoint, and `finalize_upload_cancellation` writes the terminal row. For any other non-terminal status it writes status='cancelled' immediately. `commit_upload_ingestion` also hard-aborts if `cancel_requested_at is not null` (operations_security line 431), and the cancellation-aware `claim_upload_job` sweeps a `processing` job whose lease expired while cancellation was pending straight to 'cancelled'.

Retry ladder: `services/upload_queue.retry_at` (line 111) indexes `_RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600)` with `min(attempt_count - 1, 2)`, then takes `max(ladder, min(provider_retry_after, 900))` where the provider value is scraped from a `retry-after` header or a `retry[- ]after...(\d+)` regex over the error text. `schedule_retry` sets status='retry_wait' with `next_retry_at = greatest(p_retry_at, now())` and releases the lease; the worker only calls it when `attempt_count < max_attempts` **and** `is_retryable_ingestion_error` is true (never for `PermanentIngestionError` or `LeaseLostError`; true for transient network errors and for any 408/429/5xx found by walking `__cause__`/`__context__`).

### Public surface

- POST /upload/paper -> 202 UploadAccepted. routers/upload.py:880-903. Guard `require_upload_access`, rate limit `settings.rate_limit_upload` = 10/minute. Multipart: file, title (required Form), authors='', year='', abstract='', track='', department=None, program_id=None, specialization_id=None, thesis_category='student', plus header `Idempotency-Key`. Declared error responses: 400, 409, 413, 415, 422, 503.
- POST /upload/batch -> 202 BatchUploadAccepted. routers/upload.py:996-1050. Same guard and 10/minute limit. Multipart: files[] plus `rows` (a JSON list of BatchRow aligned 1:1 with files), and request-level track/department/program_id/specialization_id/thesis_category. Per-file outcomes are folded into `results[]` with an HTTP-like `status_code`; the envelope is always 202 once it gets past size/alignment checks. Declared: 400, 403, 413, 422, 503.
- GET /upload/status/{job_id} -> UploadJobStatus. routers/upload.py:1142-1161. Guard `require_upload_access`. NO explicit rate limit (falls back to the global 120/minute in services/rate_limiting.py:43). Owner-scoped: `.eq('owner_id', user.id)`, so even a superadmin cannot read another user's job here. 404 on absent OR malformed (non-UUID) id, 503 otherwise.
- GET /upload/jobs?ids=<comma-separated> -> UploadJobList. routers/upload.py:1188-1210. Guard `require_upload_access`, no explicit limit. Max 50 ids (`_MAX_JOB_IDS`, line 1053); non-UUID entries are silently dropped, duplicates collapsed in request order, empty result set -> 422. Owner-scoped. Returns only the ids that matched, in request order.
- POST /upload/jobs/{job_id}/cancel -> UploadCancelResponse. routers/upload.py:1212-1272. Guard `require_upload_access`, 10/minute. Body `{reason?: str<=200}`. Reads `profiles.role/department` directly (not the cached `get_user_role`) to compute `p_is_superadmin`, then calls `request_upload_cancellation`. outcome ∈ {cancelled, cancellation_requested, already_terminal, forbidden, not_found}; forbidden -> 403, not_found -> 404, missing RPC -> 503 'requires the operations migration'.
- GET /upload/tracks -> {tracks: [...]}. routers/upload.py:1274-1310. UNAUTHENTICATED (no user dependency at all) despite living under the /upload prefix; limited to `settings.rate_limit_public` = 30/minute. Returns `active_track_names(settings.thesis_evaluation_department)`, falling back to the frozen `models.CCSICT_TRACKS` on a catalog exception OR on an empty result.
- POST /upload/extract-metadata -> raw dict (NO response_model). routers/upload.py:1455-1472. Guard `require_upload_access`, 10/minute. Validates the PDF, reads pages 1-3, runs the local title-page pass, then a Gemini completion only if any of title/authors/year/department is blank. Any non-HTTPException failure returns `_empty_metadata()` (all six keys blank) with 200.
- POST /upload/batch/extract-metadata -> BatchExtractResponse. routers/upload.py:1483-1545. Guard `require_upload_access`, 10/minute. Reads files sequentially (keeping only title-page text), then fans the incomplete ones out under `asyncio.Semaphore(_EXTRACT_CONCURRENCY=3)`. Per-file validation failures are reported in place with their status_code.
- Module-level helpers other routers import: none. `routers/upload.py` imports `active_programs` and `active_track_names` FROM routers/catalog.py (router -> router import).
- services/upload_queue.py exports (worker-facing only): claim_job, heartbeat_job, heartbeat_job_control, finalize_cancellation, is_retryable_ingestion_error, retry_at, schedule_retry, fail_job, process_one_cleanup, expire_terminal_jobs.
- services/catalog.py exports: LEGACY_CLASSIFICATIONS, AMBIGUOUS_LEGACY_TRACKS, SPECIALIZATION_REQUIRED_PROGRAMS={'BSCS','BSIT'}, THESIS_CATEGORIES={'student','faculty'}, normalize_thesis_category(), AcademicSelection, resolve_academic_selection().
- routers/catalog.py public: GET /catalog/departments (unauthenticated, 30/minute, `{contract_version:'2026-07-25', departments:[...]}`), GET /catalog/departments/legacy (unauthenticated, 30/minute, bare list), POST/PATCH /catalog/programs and /catalog/specializations (require_superadmin, NOT rate-limited).

### Invariants

- `papers.id == upload_jobs.id`. `commit_upload_ingestion` raises 'Paper identifier must match upload job' unless `p_paper->>'id'` equals `p_job_id`, and `hydrate_paper_academic_classification` looks the job up by `new.id`. Minting a separate paper id would silently drop program_id/specialization_id/legacy_track/classification_status/thesis_category from every archived paper.
- Idempotency is scoped per owner: UNIQUE(owner_id, idempotency_key). Two different uploaders may reuse the same key; one uploader reusing it with different bytes gets 409.
- Content is pinned to the key. `reserve_upload_job` compares `content_sha256` and raises 22000; the router recognises it only by the substring 'different content' (routers/upload.py:809).
- A job is never worker-visible until the PDF is durably stored: `claim_upload_job` requires `source_stored = true`, and only `queue_upload_job` sets that flag.
- After `reserve_upload_job` returns, the database's `job_id` and `source_path` are authoritative — the router discards its own minted values (lines 815-816) so a replay cannot create a second storage object.
- Compensation never deletes a source whose job has advanced. Both failure paths re-read the status and only roll back when it is not in {queued, processing, retry_wait, completed}.
- `_fail_staging_job` is filtered on `.eq('status','staging')`, so it can never demote a job another actor already advanced or cancelled.
- Every transactional state change except `_fail_staging_job` goes through a `security definer` RPC that is revoked from public/anon/authenticated and granted only to `service_role`; `upload_jobs` itself is RLS-enabled and revoked from anon/authenticated.
- Department is server-owned. `resolve_effective_department` pins a non-superadmin to their profile department and 403s any other request; a superadmin's choice is validated against `departments.name`.
- Program/specialization ownership is validated by UUID, not by name: program must belong to the resolved department and be active; specialization must belong to that program and be active. BSCS and BSIT require a specialization; every other program refuses one.
- `require_program` follows the manuscript, not the uploader: `thesis_category == 'student'` forces a program, faculty research may stay unclassified (routers/upload.py:670-681).
- Job status reads are owner-scoped at the SQL level (`.eq('owner_id', user.id)`) on both `/status/{id}` and `/jobs`; there is no admin-wide job read in this router.
- The ingestion lease contract: `heartbeat_upload_job*`, `schedule_upload_retry`, `fail_upload_job`, `finalize_upload_cancellation` and `commit_upload_ingestion` all require `status='processing' AND lease_owner = p_worker_id AND lease_expires_at >= now()`. A lost lease makes every one of them a no-op returning false.
- `config.validate_production_services` enforces `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds * 2 < ingestion_lease_seconds` (config.py:244); defaults 30 and 120.
- Body-size ceiling is enforced BEFORE authentication, because FastAPI parses the multipart body (spooling >1MB parts to disk) before resolving `require_upload_access`. `limit_for_path` gives batch paths `max_batch_files * max_upload_mb + 8MB` and everything else `max_upload_mb + 2MB`.
- Filenames reaching Storage contain only [A-Za-z0-9._-], have no path separators, and always end `.pdf` on the upload path (`force_suffix='pdf'`).

### Data model

- `public.upload_jobs` — id uuid PK (deliberately equal to the future papers.id), owner_id uuid FK auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL, department text FK departments(name), status text, stage text, progress int 0-100, message text, paper_id uuid FK papers, chunks int, duplication jsonb, error text, idempotency_key uuid NOT NULL, source_path text, source_stored bool, original_filename text, content_sha256 text, request_payload jsonb, attempt_count int, max_attempts int (1-10), next_retry_at, lease_owner text, lease_expires_at, heartbeat_at, started_at, completed_at, expires_at, failure_category text, cleanup_status text, thesis_category text, cancel_requested_at/cancel_requested_by/cancel_reason/cancelled_at.
- status domain (7 values, `upload_jobs_status_check`, migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:14): staging | queued | processing | retry_wait | completed | failed | cancelled.
- cleanup_status domain (5): not_required | pending | processing | completed | delegated.
- stage values actually written anywhere: 'store' (reserve, progress 5), 'queued' (queue, 8), 'download' (claim, 10; ingestion re-reports 12), 'malware_scan' (16), 'extract' (20), 'chunk' (40), 'embed' (58), 'screen' (72), 'index' (88), 'done' (100), 'error' (100), 'cancelled' (100).
- `request_payload` jsonb written by routers/upload.py:786-796 — title, authors, year, abstract, department_id, program_id, specialization_id, track, legacy_track, classification_status, thesis_category, department, uploader_id. Only program_id/specialization_id/legacy_track/classification_status/thesis_category are read back by the `hydrate_paper_academic_classification` BEFORE-INSERT trigger on `papers` (migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql:47); the rest are carried into `p_paper` by services/ingestion.py:191-212.
- `public.upload_job_events` — append-only lifecycle log written by the `record_upload_job_event` AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE trigger (migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:58-101). event_type ∈ reserved | cancellation_requested | status_changed | stage_changed | cleanup_changed | migration_snapshot. job_id FK is ON DELETE SET NULL so the 90-day event window outlives the 30-day job row.
- Indexes that shape the queue: upload_jobs_owner_idempotency_uidx UNIQUE(owner_id, idempotency_key); upload_jobs_claim_idx(status, next_retry_at, created_at) WHERE status IN (queued, retry_wait, processing); upload_jobs_lease_idx(lease_expires_at) WHERE processing; upload_jobs_cleanup_idx; upload_jobs_expiry_idx; upload_jobs_cancel_idx.
- Private Storage layout: bucket `pdfs`, key `uploads/{owner_id}/{job_id}/{sanitized_filename}` (routers/upload.py:784), uploaded with content-type application/pdf and upsert=true.
- Python-side value objects: `_UploadScope(user_id, department, category)` (line 625), `_StagingContext(user_id, department, category, classification_payload)` (line 638), `_ClassificationCache` memoising resolve_academic_selection per (program_id, specialization_id) pair including its failures (line 706).
- `AcademicSelection(department_id, program_id, specialization_id, display_track, legacy_track, classification_status)`; classification_status ∈ classified | needs_review | unclassified.

### Config knobs

- MAX_UPLOAD_MB (config.py:114, default 25) — per-file byte ceiling in the handler and the basis of the pre-auth body ceiling
- MAX_PDF_PAGES (config.py:115, default 500, 1..2000) — page-bomb limit
- MAX_BATCH_FILES (config.py:140, default 20, 1..50) — batch cardinality and the batch body ceiling
- RATE_LIMIT_UPLOAD (config.py:96, default '10/minute') — shared by /paper, /batch, /jobs/{id}/cancel, /extract-metadata, /batch/extract-metadata
- RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC (config.py:103, default '30/minute') — /upload/tracks only
- default_limits '120/minute' (services/rate_limiting.py:43) — the only ceiling on /upload/status/{id} and /upload/jobs
- INGESTION_MAX_ATTEMPTS (config.py:128, default 3, 1..10) — passed as `p_max_attempts`; determines how much of the retry ladder is reachable
- INGESTION_LEASE_SECONDS (config.py:126, default 120, 30..900) and INGESTION_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS (config.py:127, default 30, 5..300) — validated so heartbeat*2 < lease
- INGESTION_POLL_SECONDS (config.py:125, default 2.0)
- THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT (config.py:79, default 'CCSICT') — guest/fallback department and the scope of /upload/tracks
- MALWARE_SCAN_MODE (config.py:161, 'disabled' | 'clamav'), CLAMAV_HOST/PORT/TIMEOUT_SECONDS — 'clamav' is mandatory under APP_ENVIRONMENT=production (config.py:250); affects the worker and /duplication/scan, not this router
- GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL / GEMINI_TIMEOUT_SECONDS / GEMINI_MAX_RETRIES / GEMINI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS — bound the metadata-extraction completion (`_metadata_llm`, line 1364)
- Module constants that behave as tuning knobs: `_MAX_JOB_IDS = 50`, `_EXTRACT_CONCURRENCY = 3`, `_MIN_CODE_MATCH_LENGTH = 3`, `_TITLE_MAX_LINES = 4`, `_TITLE_MAX_CHARS = 240`, `services/upload_queue._RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600)`, `services/filenames._MAX_STEM = 100`, `services/request_limits._SINGLE_SLACK_BYTES = 2MB` / `_BATCH_SLACK_BYTES = 8MB`

### Error handling

- 415: non-.pdf filename, or content-type outside {application/pdf, application/x-pdf}. 400: empty file, or a non-UUID Idempotency-Key. 413: body over `max_upload_mb` (handler) or over `limit_for_path` (middleware, pre-auth), or more than `max_batch_files` files. 422: bad magic bytes, malformed PDF, encrypted PDF, 0 pages, over `max_pdf_pages`, title outside 5..300 chars, authors > 500, abstract > 10000, year not a 4-digit value in 1978..(UTC year + 1), unknown/archived department, program not in department, specialization not in program, BSCS/BSIT without a specialization, a non-BSCS/BSIT program with one, bad thesis_category, misaligned/duplicate/invalid `rows`.
- 409: 'Idempotency-Key was already used for another file' — derived from the substring 'different content' in the `reserve_upload_job` exception (routers/upload.py:809).
- 503, five distinct messages: 'The durable upload queue is temporarily unavailable' (reserve raised), 'The durable upload queue did not reserve the submission' (falsy result), 'The private manuscript could not be staged safely' (Storage upload failed), 'The private manuscript could not be queued safely' (queue transition unconfirmed), 'Upload status is temporarily unavailable'.
- 404 vs 503 on `/status/{job_id}`: a non-UUID path parameter raises SQLSTATE 22P02 from PostgREST, which `services/db_errors.is_invalid_identifier` recognises and converts to 404 rather than 503, so a client does not keep retrying a mistyped id.
- Batch per-file errors never fail the envelope: `_stage_batch_file` converts `HTTPException` into `BatchFileResult(error=detail, status_code=...)` and any other exception into a generic 'This file could not be staged. Please try it again.' with status_code 500. The response is still 202.
- Metadata extraction is fail-soft everywhere: `_ai_completion` swallows every exception and returns the local pass; `_load_program_records` swallows a catalog failure and returns `[]`; `extract_metadata` swallows non-HTTP exceptions and returns blanks with 200; `list_tracks` falls back to the frozen `CCSICT_TRACKS`.
- Storage deletions that fail are never lost: `_remove_staged_source` and `services/upload_queue.process_one_cleanup` both hand the path to `record_storage_cleanup`, which inserts into `storage_cleanup_queue` and logs CRITICAL if even that fails. Job `cleanup_status` becomes 'delegated' in that case.
- Worker-side classification: `is_retryable_ingestion_error` returns False for `PermanentIngestionError` (which includes `MalwareDetectedIngestionError`) and `LeaseLostError`, True for transient network errors and for any 408/429/5xx status discovered by walking the exception chain. `fail_job` publishes only `error.public_error` when the class deliberately sets one (`DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError`); everything else is replaced by a fixed sentence so no provider text leaks to the uploader.
- `record_security_event` failure during cancellation is swallowed with a warning that does not include the exception type (routers/upload.py:1256-1257).

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_security_upload_hardening.py (280 lines) — TestPdfValidation pins the accept/sanitize path, the parametrised 415/400/413/422 rejections, and the page-bomb limit; TestUploadJobSecurityAndRollback pins owner-only, read-only job status and the 404 for a missing owned job; TestSqlSecurityContracts asserts the ingestion and cleanup RPCs are service-role only and that a job's owner survives account deletion
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_durable_ingestion.py (1051 lines) — the largest pin on this subsystem: `retry_at` with a provider Retry-After (line 281), the 409 on an idempotency-key content mismatch for both the single (line 600) and batch (line 975) paths
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_request_limits.py (158 lines) — the pre-authentication body ceiling and `limit_for_path`'s batch vs single allowances
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_catalog.py (349 lines) — resolve_academic_selection ownership/legacy-translation rules and the catalog router's fallbacks
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py — pins the known base-schema vs migration differences for upload_jobs and its RPCs
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_event_loop_responsiveness.py — pins that PDF parsing, hashing, and the Storage upload stay off the event loop (the `asyncio.to_thread` discipline in `_stage_and_queue_one`)
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_router_error_paths.py and tests/test_api.py — endpoint-level status mapping
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_untrusted_prompt_framing.py — pins that the manuscript text in the metadata-extraction prompt is escaped and fenced as third-party content
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_supabase_rls_integration.py — opt-in (`ALLOW_DISPOSABLE_SUPABASE_TESTS=1`) live check of the upload_jobs grants

### Gotchas

- **No malware scan on the HTTP path.** `services/malware.scan_pdf` is never called from routers/upload.py (only services/ingestion.py:115 and routers/duplication.py:196). An infected PDF is accepted, hashed, and written into the private `pdfs` bucket by the API, and is only rejected minutes later when the worker claims it. Worse, `/upload/extract-metadata` and `/upload/batch/extract-metadata` parse the bytes with PyMuPDF and ship the extracted text to Gemini with no scan at all — while `/duplication/scan`, which accepts the same file types, does scan.
- **Cancelling a job during the staging window can orphan the PDF.** `_job_status_model` advertises `can_cancel=True` for status='staging'. `request_upload_cancellation`'s non-processing branch sets `cleanup_status = CASE WHEN source_stored THEN 'pending' ELSE 'not_required' END`, but `source_stored` is false until `queue_upload_job` runs — and the API uploads to Storage *before* that. So a cancel landing between line 827 and line 842 marks cleanup 'not_required' while the object exists, and `expire_upload_jobs` then deletes the row after 30 days (`status IN ('failed','cancelled') AND source_stored = false AND cleanup_status IN ('not_required','completed')`), leaving an unreferenced private PDF. The API's own compensation path in the same window is more careful (`source_stored = not removed`), and `claim_upload_job`'s staging-timeout sweep is too (`source_stored = source_path is not null`).
- **The 600-second rung of the retry ladder is unreachable at the default config.** `workers/ingestion_worker.py:200` gates on `attempts < max_attempts`, and `claim_upload_job` post-increments `attempt_count`, so with `ingestion_max_attempts=3` the only indices ever used are 0 (30s, after attempt 1) and 1 (120s, after attempt 2); attempt 3 calls `fail_job` directly. `_RETRY_DELAYS[2]` only fires if `max_attempts >= 4`. A provider `Retry-After` can still push either rung up to 900s.
- **`/upload/tracks` is unauthenticated inside an otherwise fully-guarded prefix** (routers/upload.py:1274). Easy to miss when reasoning about the router's auth posture; it is also the only endpoint here using `rate_limit_public`.
- **`/upload/status/{id}` and `/upload/jobs` carry no explicit rate limit**, only the global 120/minute default, while the batch UI polls both. The docstring on `/jobs` argues per-job polling would 'exceed the global request limit', which is an implicit acknowledgement that the global limit is the only ceiling.
- **`_last_event_map` is an unbounded read.** `select('job_id,created_at').in_('job_id', ids).order('created_at', desc=True)` with no `.limit()` pulls *every* event row for up to 50 jobs just to keep the first per job. Each job accumulates an event per status/stage/cleanup transition via the `record_upload_job_event` trigger, so a retried job's history is fetched in full on every poll tick.
- **`/upload/extract-metadata` returns an inconsistent shape for a text-free PDF.** `_extract_one` (line 1432) returns `{'title': '', 'authors': ''}` — only two keys — while the exception path returns `_empty_metadata()` with all six and the batch path always emits the full `BatchExtractedFile`. The endpoint has no `response_model`, so nothing catches this.
- **Department code matching does not get the minimum-length guard programs got.** `_code_score` (line 268) refuses codes shorter than `_MIN_CODE_MATCH_LENGTH = 3`, but `_match_department` (line 209) has its own inline scoring with only a `\\b` word-boundary check, so a 2-letter department code literally printed on a title page can still win. Two parallel scoring implementations exist for the same job; `_match_department` also does not route through `_haystack`.
- **`_fail_staging_job` is the one transactional write in this subsystem done as a raw table UPDATE** rather than an RPC, contrary to the repo's stated Python->Postgres discipline. It works only because `supabase_setup.sql:1109` keeps `grant all on table public.upload_jobs to service_role`.
- **`resolve_effective_department` is called twice per single-file upload** — once at routers/upload.py:897 and again inside `_resolve_upload_scope` (line 662) on the already-resolved value. Idempotent, but it is a second `profiles` read (60s-cached role, uncached department validation for superadmins).
- **`cancel_upload_job` re-reads `profiles.role` directly** (line 1225) instead of using the cached `get_user_role`, creating a second source of truth for the role; a failure of that read is reported as 'Upload cancellation is temporarily unavailable' (503) rather than an auth error.
- **`UploadAccepted.status` is hard-coded `'queued'`** on the success path (line 875) even when the re-read showed the job already at `processing` or `completed`. Only the already-tracked short-circuit reports a truthful status.
- **Concurrent duplicate submissions with the same key and same bytes both succeed.** The loser of the `ON CONFLICT` sees `created=False, status='staging'`, falls through, re-uploads to the same path with `upsert=true`, and calls `queue_upload_job`; its `false` return is tolerated because the status is already 'queued'. Both return 202 with the same job_id.
- **The single-file endpoint has no idempotency protection when the header is omitted** — `_parse_idempotency_key` mints a fresh uuid4 (line 654), so a client retry after a timeout creates a second job and a second archived paper. The batch endpoint, by contrast, *requires* a per-row key.
- **`schedule_upload_retry` does not reset `stage`/`progress`.** A job that fails at `embed`/58 sits in status='retry_wait' still displaying stage 'embed' at 58%, which the UI renders as in-flight progress.
- **`cleanup_status='delegated'` rows are immortal.** `expire_upload_jobs` only deletes when cleanup_status is 'not_required' or 'completed', so a storage deletion that had to be delegated to `storage_cleanup_queue` pins its `upload_jobs` row forever.
- **Base-schema vs migration drift risk on `upload_jobs`.** `supabase_setup.sql:779` declares the inline check with only 4 statuses and later re-declares it with 7 (line ~1379); `20260724_operations_security.sql` re-creates `claim_upload_job`, `schedule_upload_retry`, `commit_upload_ingestion`, `claim_upload_cleanup` and `expire_upload_jobs` with cancellation-aware bodies. Replaying `20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql` after it silently reverts all five to cancellation-blind versions (the `create or replace` hazard CLAUDE.md warns about).
- **`UploadJobStatus`'s own stage comment is stale** (models.py:127): it lists `store | download | extract | chunk | embed | screen | index | done` and omits `queued`, `malware_scan`, `error` and `cancelled`, all of which are written.
- **Two copies of the same RPC-unwrapping helper**: `_reserved_job`/`_rpc_boolean` in routers/upload.py:539-549 duplicate `_rpc_bool` in services/upload_queue.py:18.
- **`heartbeat_upload_job_control` has a silent capability fallback.** services/upload_queue.py:56-72 catches a missing-function error (PGRST202 / 42883) and degrades to `heartbeat_upload_job`, which **always reports `cancel_requested: False`** — so on a project missing the operations migration, cooperative cancellation of a `processing` job never takes effect and the job runs to completion.
- **`_ClassificationCache` caches failures as well as successes** (line 745), keyed on `(program_id, specialization_id)`. That is deliberate, but it means a transient catalog read failure for one row is replayed as the identical rejection for every later row naming the same program in the same request.
- `routers/catalog.py::_nested_catalog` has two nested fallbacks — one dropping the `title` column, one dropping to `_legacy_catalog()` on any exception — and the legacy shape has no programs at all. `active_programs()` then returns `[]`, which the extractor treats as 'no program autofill'. A genuine Supabase outage is therefore indistinguishable from a pre-PI-04 schema on this path.
- `routers/upload.py` imports `active_programs`/`active_track_names` from `routers/catalog.py` — a router-to-router import that makes the catalog HTTP module a de facto service module.

### Open questions

- Is the staging-window cancellation leak (cancel between the Storage upload and `queue_upload_job` leaves cleanup_status='not_required' with the object present) known and accepted, or should `request_upload_cancellation` use `source_path is not null` the way `claim_upload_job`'s staging sweep does?
- Should the HTTP surface scan before staging, or at least before `/upload/extract-metadata` hands bytes to PyMuPDF and Gemini? Today an infected PDF reaches the private bucket and the LLM prompt; `/duplication/scan` scans, so the two file-accepting surfaces still have different postures despite services/filenames.py existing to fix exactly that class of divergence.
- Is `_RETRY_DELAYS[2] = 600` intended to be live? At `ingestion_max_attempts = 3` it can never be selected; either the worker gate should be `attempts <= max_attempts` or the ladder should be `(30, 120)`.
- Should `/upload/status/{id}` and `/upload/jobs` carry their own limiter given that two UIs poll them continuously and `_last_event_map` performs an unbounded event read?
- Should `/upload/tracks` move to `routers/catalog.py`, where its unauthenticated, public-limit posture would be consistent with its neighbours?
- `_match_department` and `_entry_score`/`_code_score` are two scoring implementations for the same problem, and only the program one has the minimum-code-length guard. Is the department matcher intentionally left permissive?
- Why does the base `supabase_setup.sql` add `upload_jobs.thesis_category` with a NOT NULL default and a check constraint that `reserve_upload_job` never populates, leaving `request_payload->>'thesis_category'` as the only carrier the hydrate trigger reads?

**Depends on:** dependencies/auth.py — require_upload_access (line 436), resolve_effective_department (line 105), get_user_scope (line 76), the service-role client `sb`, services/catalog.py — normalize_thesis_category, resolve_academic_selection, routers/catalog.py — active_programs, active_track_names (router-to-router import), services/filenames.py — sanitize_filename, services/cleanup.py — record_storage_cleanup, services/db_errors.py — is_invalid_identifier, services/operations.py — record_security_event, services/rate_limiting.py — limiter, services/prompts.py — metadata_extraction_prompt, services/llm_output.py — coerce_text, strip_code_fence, services/gemini_pool.py — arun, EXTRACT slot, services/request_limits.py — BodySizeLimitMiddleware (registered in main.py:180), models.py — the eight upload/batch pydantic models plus CCSICT_TRACKS, routers/openapi_responses.py — errors(), Postgres RPCs: reserve_upload_job, queue_upload_job, request_upload_cancellation; plus the worker's claim_upload_job, heartbeat_upload_job(_control), schedule_upload_retry, fail_upload_job, commit_upload_ingestion, finalize_upload_cancellation, claim_upload_cleanup, finish_upload_cleanup, expire_upload_jobs, Supabase Storage bucket `pdfs` (private), fitz (PyMuPDF) for validation and title-page text, langchain_google_genai.ChatGoogleGenerativeAI for the metadata completion

**Used by:** workers/ingestion_worker.py — consumes the rows this router reserves; `process_claimed_job` (line 173) drives schedule_retry / fail_job / finalize_cancellation, services/ingestion.py — process_ingestion_job reads `source_path`, `content_sha256`, `request_payload` and calls commit_upload_ingestion with `p_paper['id'] == job_id`, routers/maintenance.py — surfaces worker/scanner state alongside these jobs, rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:233-314 — getTracks, uploadPaper, getUploadStatus, cancelUploadJob, extractMetadata, extractMetadataBatch, uploadBatch, listUploadJobs, rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Upload.jsx and src/pages/UploadBatch.jsx — the submitting UIs and the pollers, rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/UploadHistoryTab.jsx — upload history filtered by the live track vocabulary, rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/landing/TracksMarquee.jsx and src/pages/archive/useArchiveCatalog.js — consume /upload/tracks and /catalog/departments, rag-thesis-frontend/vite.config.js:94 — the `/upload` prefix is proxied with the HTML-aware SPA bypass because /upload is also a React route

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 19 correction(s), 11 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** keyFiles: routers/upload.py is 'The whole /upload HTTP surface: 7 endpoints'
  **Actual:** There are EIGHT endpoints, and the map's own publicSurface list contains eight entries — the keyFiles summary contradicts it. Route decorators: /paper, /batch, /status/{job_id}, /jobs, /jobs/{job_id}/cancel, /tracks, /extract-metadata, /batch/extract-metadata. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:880, 996, 1142, 1188, 1212, 1274, 1455, 1483`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling: '503, five distinct messages' followed by an enumeration of five
  **Actual:** routers/upload.py raises SEVEN distinct 503 details. The enumeration omits 'Upload cancellation requires the operations migration' and 'Upload cancellation is temporarily unavailable' (both appear elsewhere in the map, so the bullet's own count is simply wrong). (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:811, 813, 839, 870, 1155, 1203, 1241, 1244`)
- **Wrong:** The map never lists 403 as reachable on POST /upload/paper, and records its declared responses as 400, 409, 413, 415, 422, 503
  **Actual:** The declared set really is 403-less, but 403 is reachable twice on that path: resolve_effective_department 403s a non-superadmin naming any department other than their own, and require_upload_access 403s a role without the upload feature. /upload/batch declares 403; /upload/paper does not, for the same two guards. That is an OpenAPI inconsistency the map should have flagged, not an accurate omission. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:113-119 and 447-450; rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:882 vs 998`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling/tests: 'TestPdfValidation pins ... the parametrised 415/400/413/422 rejections' and '413: body over max_upload_mb (handler)'
  **Actual:** The parametrised cases are 415/415/422/400 only — there is no 413 case. Worse, the handler's own 413 branch is DEAD on every HTTP path: _read_limited_upload reads limit+1 bytes and raises if len > limit, so it returns at most `limit` bytes, making `len(file_bytes) > settings.max_upload_mb * 1024 * 1024` in _validate_pdf_upload permanently false downstream. The reachable 413s are _read_limited_upload and the middleware. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_security_upload_hardening.py:59-63; rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:493 vs 515-521`)
- **Wrong:** tests: TestPdfValidation pins the accept/sanitize path and the rejections (implying the encryption rule is covered)
  **Actual:** The encrypted-PDF refusal is entirely unpinned. Nothing under rag-thesis-backend/tests/ references `needs_pass` or the string 'Encrypted or password-protected PDFs are not accepted'. It is one of the nine validation rules and the only one with zero test coverage. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:497-499 (no matching test in tests/)`)
- **Wrong:** publicSurface GET /upload/jobs: 'Max 50 ids ...; empty result set -> 422'
  **Actual:** 422 fires when NO comma-entry parses as a UUID, and also (not 413) when MORE than 50 parse. A list that parses cleanly but matches no rows returns 200 with `{"jobs": []}` — the response is built by filtering job_ids against the rows found, with no emptiness check. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1178-1181 and 1205-1210`)
- **Wrong:** dataModel: 'Indexes that shape the queue: ... upload_jobs_expiry_idx'
  **Actual:** upload_jobs_expiry_idx is partial on `where status in ('completed','failed')` — it EXCLUDES 'cancelled'. expire_upload_jobs deletes `status in ('failed','cancelled')`, so half its predicate has no index support, and because both declarations use `create index if not exists` and neither 20260724 nor any later migration recreates it, the index can never pick up the new status. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:1430-1431; rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:85-87; migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:465-481`)
- **Wrong:** controlFlow: 'commit_upload_ingestion also hard-aborts if cancel_requested_at is not null (operations_security line 431)'
  **Actual:** True but preceded by an idempotency short-circuit the map omits: `if v_job.status = 'completed' and v_job.paper_id is not null then return v_job.paper_id;` runs BEFORE the cancel check, so a re-commit of an already-committed job returns the paper id even with a cancellation pending. The cancel check is at line 429, not 431. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:421-431`)
- **Wrong:** invariants: 'heartbeat_upload_job*, schedule_upload_retry, fail_upload_job, finalize_upload_cancellation and commit_upload_ingestion all require status=processing AND lease_owner = p_worker_id AND lease_expires_at >= now()'
  **Actual:** schedule_upload_retry carries two further predicates the map omits: `attempt_count < max_attempts` AND `cancel_requested_at is null`. So a retry scheduled for a job whose cancellation landed mid-attempt is a silent no-op returning false, and the worker logs 'Retry was not scheduled because job ... no longer owns its lease' — a misleading message, since the lease was fine. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:415-417; rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:200-202`)
- **Wrong:** dataModel: 'upload_job_events — append-only lifecycle log written by the record_upload_job_event AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE trigger (migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:58-101)'
  **Actual:** Wrong anchor and wrong writer for one value. Lines 58-101 are ingestion_workers / operational_alerts / security_audit_events. The table is at 21-33, the trigger function at 98-138, the trigger itself at 140-142. `migration_snapshot` is not written by the trigger at all — it comes from a one-off backfill INSERT ... SELECT. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:21-33, 98-142, 145-156`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling: the 22P02 -> 404 conversion via services/db_errors.is_invalid_identifier is described only for /status/{job_id}
  **Actual:** cancel_upload_job does the same thing: a non-UUID job_id in the path becomes 404 'Upload job not found' rather than 503. The map's cancel bullet lists only outcome-driven 404/403 and the missing-RPC 503. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1242-1243`)
- **Wrong:** publicSurface: 'POST /upload/batch/extract-metadata ... Per-file validation failures are reported in place with their status_code'
  **Actual:** Only HTTPException failures carry error/status_code. A non-HTTPException failure produces `_extracted_file(index, filename)` — an all-blank row with error=None and status_code=None, byte-identical to the row emitted for a legitimately text-free PDF. The client cannot tell a parse crash from an image-only scan. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1508-1515`)
- **Wrong:** usedBy: 'rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:233-314 — getTracks, uploadPaper, getUploadStatus, cancelUploadJob, extractMetadata, extractMetadataBatch, uploadBatch, listUploadJobs'
  **Actual:** There is no `listUploadJobs` in api.js — the exported helper is `getUploadJobs`. `listUploadJobs` is the BACKEND handler name (routers/upload.py:1189); the map conflated the two. Range is 232-316: getTracks 232, uploadPaper 238, getUploadStatus 261, cancelUploadJob 265, extractMetadata 269, extractMetadataBatch 282, uploadBatch 298, getUploadJobs 313. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:232-316; rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/UploadBatch.jsx:11,824`)
- **Wrong:** controlFlow: 'services/upload_queue.retry_at (line 111) indexes _RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600) with min(attempt_count - 1, 2)'
  **Actual:** The function is at line 117 and the expression is `max(0, min(attempt_count - 1, len(_RETRY_DELAYS) - 1))`. Same result for a 3-rung ladder, but the map states a hard-coded 2 and drops the zero floor (which is what keeps attempt_count=0 from indexing -1 and silently selecting the 600s rung). (`rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py:117-123`)
- **Wrong:** keyFiles: 'services/filenames.py — sanitize_filename ... replaces every char outside [A-Za-z0-9._-] with '_''
  **Actual:** The regex is `[^A-Za-z0-9._-]+` with a `+`, so a RUN of unsafe characters collapses to a single underscore, not one per character. The function also `.strip('._')` before the 100-char cap, and the upload path passes `default_stem='thesis'` (not the module default 'document'). (`rag-thesis-backend/services/filenames.py:9,25; rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:480-482`)
- **Wrong:** keyFiles: 'routers/catalog.py — _nested_catalog (line 65) ... active_track_names (line 118) and active_programs (line 145)'; services/catalog.py 'resolve_academic_selection (line 53)'; request_limits 'limit_for_path (line 61)' in a '~180 lines' file
  **Actual:** _nested_catalog is at 67, active_programs at 147 (active_track_names at 118 is exact). resolve_academic_selection is at 56. limit_for_path is at 59 and services/request_limits.py is 161 lines. services/upload_queue._rpc_bool is at 16, not 18. migrations/20260723 claim_upload_job is at 172, not 175. The 7-value status check is at migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:15, not :14. migrations/20260724 is 578 lines; supabase_setup.sql is 1826 lines, not '~1400+'. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/catalog.py:67,147; services/catalog.py:56; services/request_limits.py:59; services/upload_queue.py:16; migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:172; migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:15`)
- **Wrong:** gotchas: 'UploadJobStatus's own stage comment is stale (models.py:127)'
  **Actual:** The substance is right but the anchor is off by one and points at the wrong comment. Line 127 is the `status:` field, whose comment lists all seven statuses correctly. The stale stage comment is line 128. (`rag-thesis-backend/models.py:127-128`)
- **Wrong:** Misc in-file anchors: _ClassificationCache caches failures at 745; _extract_one's two-key return at 1432; the batch 'concurrency would hold 20 x 25 MB' comment at 1043; the record_security_event swallow at 1256-1257; POST /upload/paper at 880-903
  **Actual:** 744, 1437, 1040-1042, 1262-1263, and 880-911 (decorator 880, def 885, body ends 911) respectively. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:744, 1437, 1040-1042, 1262-1263, 880-911`)
- **Wrong:** openQuestions: 'Why does the base supabase_setup.sql add upload_jobs.thesis_category with a NOT NULL default and a check constraint that reserve_upload_job never populates, leaving request_payload->>'thesis_category' as the only carrier the hydrate trigger reads?'
  **Actual:** Answered in the source at both declaration sites: 'Forward-compat mirror of papers.thesis_category; request_payload remains the authoritative carrier that the hydrate trigger reads.' It is a deliberate forward-compat column, not drift. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:1337-1339; rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql:19`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- PRIVILEGED MFA IS ABSENT FROM THE ENTIRE MAP. require_upload_access calls _require_privileged_mfa(credentials, user.id) for any admin/superadmin before returning the user, so with REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA=true — which config.validate_production_services makes mandatory under APP_ENVIRONMENT=production — every /upload endpoint except /tracks requires an aal2 token. The refusal is PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL, which the frontend string-matches. Not in publicSurface, invariants, errorHandling, configKnobs, or dependsOn. rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:436-450 and 229; rag-thesis-backend/config.py:113, 248.
- claim_upload_job's staging reaper is a 15-MINUTE window, and it deliberately SKIPS rows with cancel_requested_at set (`and cancel_requested_at is null`). That second clause is the missing half of the map's own staging-leak gotcha: a job cancelled between the Storage upload and queue_upload_job is already status='cancelled' with cleanup_status='not_required' and source_stored=false, so the sweep that WOULD have set source_stored = (source_path is not null) never touches it, claim_upload_cleanup requires source_stored=true so no cleanup is ever claimed, and expire_upload_jobs deletes the row at +30d. The leak is closed on every other path and open only on this one. rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:360-367, 449-455, 465-481.
- finish_upload_cleanup is why 'delegated' rows are immortal for TWO reasons, not one: `source_stored = case when p_delegated then source_stored else false end` leaves source_stored TRUE on a delegated cleanup, so the row fails both halves of expire_upload_jobs' failed/cancelled predicate (cleanup_status not in ('not_required','completed') AND source_stored <> false). The map names only the cleanup_status half. rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:391-410.
- _load_department_names has a hard-coded single-department fallback: when the `departments` read returns no rows it returns [{'name': 'CCSICT', 'title': ''}]. The title-page department matcher then has exactly one candidate with an empty prose title, so _department_phrases returns [] and only the 5-character whole-word code can score — a silent narrowing of the extractor's vocabulary that looks identical to a correct CCSICT match. rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1328-1344.
- routers/catalog.py::_nested_catalog(active_only: bool = True) — the active_only=False branch is DEAD. All four call sites (active_track_names, active_programs, GET /catalog/departments, GET /catalog/departments/legacy) use the default, so nothing in the codebase can read archived programs or specializations through this function. rag-thesis-backend/routers/catalog.py:67, 141, 173, 184, 190.
- GET /catalog/departments/legacy returns _nested_catalog(), NOT _legacy_catalog(). The only reachable path to _legacy_catalog is the exception fallback inside _nested_catalog. The two endpoints differ solely in the envelope ({contract_version, departments} vs a bare list) — the 'legacy' name describes the response shape, not the schema. rag-thesis-backend/routers/catalog.py:184 vs 190, and 92.
- A chunked or Content-Length-less body overrun surfaces as 400, not 413. BodyTooLarge is raised from the wrapped receive channel, and FastAPI wraps its own body read in try/except Exception and converts anything raised there into '400 There was an error parsing the body'. The middleware's own 413 only fires when it reads a declared Content-Length over the ceiling, or when the raise reaches it with nothing sent. The class docstring states this explicitly. rag-thesis-backend/services/request_limits.py:107-114, 138-161.
- BodySizeLimitMiddleware only engages for POST/PUT/PATCH, and _BATCH_PATHS is an exact-string frozenset of two paths — so '/upload/batch/' with a trailing slash falls to the single-file ceiling of max_upload_mb + 2MB, roughly 1/19th of the batch allowance. rag-thesis-backend/services/request_limits.py:38, 42, 61-66, 121.
- The base schema declares `stage text not null default 'extract'` on upload_jobs — a twelfth stage value that no RPC ever writes but that any row inserted outside reserve_upload_job would carry. The map's stage inventory ('store' first, at progress 5) misses the DDL default. rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:780.
- resolve_effective_department adds two more failure modes to POST /upload/paper and POST /upload/batch that the map's errorHandling never lists: 422 'Unknown department.' when a superadmin names a department absent from `departments`, and 503 'Department validation is unavailable.' when that lookup itself raises. The map attributes the unknown-department 422 to services/catalog.resolve_academic_selection ('Unknown or archived department.'), but for a superadmin the auth-layer check fires first with a different string. rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:121-129 vs services/catalog.py:62-66.
- The three headline gotchas all check out exactly, which is worth stating because they carry the map's weight: (a) services/malware.scan_pdf is called from exactly two places, services/ingestion.py:115 and routers/duplication.py:196, and nowhere in routers/upload.py — the HTTP surface really does write unscanned bytes into the private `pdfs` bucket and ship extracted text to Gemini; (b) the staging-window cancel really does set cleanup_status='not_required' while the object exists, because request_upload_cancellation's non-processing branch keys off source_stored, which only queue_upload_job sets; (c) the 600s rung really is unreachable at ingestion_max_attempts=3, because workers/ingestion_worker.py:200 gates on `attempts < max_attempts` against the already-incremented attempt_count, so only indices 0 and 1 are ever selected.

## 6. `ingestion-pipeline` - Durable ingestion worker and pipeline

Runs thesis-PDF ingestion out of process, so the API never blocks on parsing, OCR, embedding, or duplication screening. A separate `python -m workers.ingestion_worker` process claims leased jobs from `public.upload_jobs`, keeps the lease alive from a background thread while a pipeline thread reports real stage transitions, and runs download -> SHA-256 verify -> ClamAV -> PyMuPDF/OCR extract+clean -> 800/100 cl100k_base chunking -> Gemini embedding -> ingest-time duplication screen -> a single transactional commit RPC that writes `papers`, `paper_index_versions` and `chunks` and closes the job atomically. It also owns the queue's housekeeping: staged-source cleanup, terminal-job expiry, worker registry heartbeats, and the operations alert monitor.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py` (361 lines) - Worker process: main claim loop, LeaseHeartbeat (keep-alive thread + serialized control RPCs), per-job outcome recording (cancel / lease-lost / retry / fail), _Housekeeping cadence, ThreadPoolExecutor mode, SIGINT/SIGTERM drain.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py` (301 lines) - The pipeline itself: process_ingestion_job, the seven _require_lease checkpoints, staged-PDF validation, exception taxonomy (PermanentIngestionError / MalwareDetectedIngestionError / DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError / LeaseLostError), paper+chunk payload assembly, commit_upload_ingestion call and its ambiguous-response recovery, post-commit activity log and screening refresh.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/document_processor.py` (323 lines) - Phase 1 digitization: PyMuPDF extraction, per-page tesserocr OCR fallback, running header/footer detection, page-number and TOC-leader stripping, excluded-section removal, PII redaction, figure placeholder, 15% non-alphanumeric noise rule.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/chunker.py` (328 lines) - Phase 2 semantic indexing: lazy cl100k_base tokenizer, LangChain RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter at 800/100 tokens, cross-page splitting with character-offset chunk->page mapping, section-heading detection, chunk record validation, metadata tagging, CHUNKING_VERSION.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/cleanup.py` (34 lines) - Single function that persists a `storage_cleanup_queue` row when a private-bucket delete fails; stores only the exception class name, never text or content.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py` (184 lines) - Thin service-role wrappers over every queue RPC, plus the retry-classification policy (_RETRY_DELAYS, is_retryable_ingestion_error, _retry_after_seconds) and the cleanup drain.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/index_provenance.py` (43 lines) - PREPROCESSING_VERSION and the current_index_fingerprint() dict that commit_paper_ingestion validates field by field.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/malware.py` (61 lines) - Fail-closed ClamAV INSTREAM client; MalwareScannerUnavailable carries status_code=503 which is what makes a scanner outage retryable.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py` (55 lines) - Batches of 64 through gemini_pool.run with 3-attempt exponential backoff; 768 output dimensions.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/novelty.py` (516 lines) - screen_new_submission (one match_chunks RPC per chunk at the 0.85 threshold) and refresh_after_ingest (incremental post-commit refresh of every other paper's screening).
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py` (~380 lines) - register_worker / stop_worker / upsert_alert / notify_webhook / evaluate_operations, all called from the worker's maintenance tick.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql` (473 lines) - Original queue schema and RPC bodies (reserve/queue/claim/heartbeat/retry/fail/commit/cleanup/expire), all security definer + service_role only.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql` (~560 lines) - Cancellation-aware replacements for claim/retry/commit/cleanup/expire, plus heartbeat_upload_job_control, finalize_upload_cancellation, request_upload_cancellation, register_ingestion_worker. These five functions exist ONLY here, not in supabase_setup.sql.
- `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` (1826 lines) - Base schema. Lines 465-575 commit_paper_ingestion; 1434-1826 the queue functions, which are the pre-cancellation 20260723 bodies except commit_upload_ingestion (1665) which does carry the guard.
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py` (~1000 lines) - Producer side: _stage_and_queue_one (routers/upload.py:764-877) does reserve_upload_job -> private bucket upload -> queue_upload_job with compensation on either failure.

### Control flow

**Producer.** `routers/upload.py::_stage_and_queue_one` (764-877) reads the file under the 25 MB cap, validates the PDF, derives `job_id = uuid4()` and `source_path = uploads/{user}/{job}/{safe_filename}`, hashes the bytes off the event loop, then does a three-step two-phase commit: `reserve_upload_job` (row created `status='staging', stage='store', progress=5`), `sb.storage.from_('pdfs').upload(...)`, `queue_upload_job` (`status='queued', stage='queued', progress=8, source_stored=true`). A storage failure removes the object and calls `_fail_staging_job`; a queue-transition failure first re-reads the row and only compensates when the status has not advanced, because the PostgREST response can be lost after Postgres committed. Every blocking call is `asyncio.to_thread`. `source_stored=true` is what makes a job visible to `claim_upload_job`, so a half-staged upload can never be claimed.

**Worker loop.** `run_worker` (ingestion_worker.py:270-339) builds a service-role client, a `_Housekeeping`, and `concurrency = 1 if once else settings.ingestion_concurrency`; a `ThreadPoolExecutor` is created only when concurrency > 1, so `--once` and the default are byte-for-byte the evaluated inline path. Each iteration: reap done futures (incrementing `processed`, forcing `registry_at = 0.0`), `house.tick(one in-flight job id)`, bail to a poll sleep if `scan_state == 'unavailable'`, back off on `wait(in_flight, FIRST_COMPLETED)` when the pool is full, then `claim_job`. A claimed job is registered (`state='processing'`) and either run inline or submitted to the pool. On `stop_event` the loop returns `processed + _drain(...)`; the `finally` always calls `executor.shutdown(wait=True)` and `stop_worker`. `_Housekeeping.tick` (222-243) refreshes `scanner_status()` and writes the registry row every `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds`, and every `ingestion_maintenance_seconds` runs exactly one `process_one_cleanup`, then `expire_terminal_jobs`, then `evaluate_operations` when `OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED`.

**Leasing.** `claim_upload_job` (20260724 version, migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:351-403) first runs three sweeps inside the same call — fail `staging` rows older than 15 minutes, transition `processing` rows whose lease expired *and* that carry `cancel_requested_at` to `cancelled`, fail `processing` rows whose lease expired with `attempt_count >= max_attempts` — then selects one row where `status='queued'` OR (`retry_wait` and `next_retry_at <= now()`) OR (`processing` and `lease_expires_at < now()`), `cancel_requested_at is null`, `attempt_count < max_attempts`, `source_stored`, ordered by `coalesce(next_retry_at, created_at), created_at`, `for update skip locked limit 1`. The claiming UPDATE sets `status='processing', stage='download', progress=10`, increments `attempt_count`, writes `lease_owner`, `lease_expires_at = now() + lease_seconds`, and returns the whole row. Every later mutator re-checks `status='processing' AND lease_owner = p_worker_id AND lease_expires_at >= now()`, so a stale worker simply gets `found = false` and never corrupts a reclaimed job.

**LeaseHeartbeat.** `process_claimed_job` (173-204) wraps the pipeline in `with LeaseHeartbeat(...) as heartbeat`, and `heartbeat` is the bound `update`. Two threads touch it. The pipeline thread calls `update(stage=..., progress=..., message=...)` at each checkpoint; the daemon thread (`_run`, 149-166) calls `update()` with **no** kwargs every `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds`, so a keep-alive can never carry a stage and therefore can never rewind the progress bar. Two distinct locks do the serialization: `_state_lock` guards the `_valid`/`_cancel_requested` booleans (held only for microseconds), and `_rpc_lock` guarantees at most one `heartbeat_upload_job_control` is in flight, so a keep-alive can never land at Postgres out of order against a real stage update. They are deliberately separate — sharing one would hold a lock across a network round trip. `update` re-reads `{lease_valid, cancel_requested}` from the RPC result, raises `CancellationRequested` when the DB says cancel, and returns `lease_valid`. Any exception marks `_valid = False` and returns False.

**Pipeline stages.** `process_ingestion_job` (ingestion.py:90-301) pulls `request_payload`, `source_path`, `original_filename`, `content_sha256` off the claimed row and refuses immediately (`PermanentIngestionError`) when the source or hash is missing. Then, each preceded by `_require_lease(...)` which converts a false heartbeat into `LeaseLostError` before any side effect: (1) **download** `stage='download', progress=12` — `storage.from_('pdfs').download`, then `_validate_staged_pdf`: SHA-256 must equal the reserved digest, the bytes must start `%PDF-`, `fitz.open` must succeed, `needs_pass` must be false, and `1 <= page_count <= max_pdf_pages` (500). Every one of these is permanent. (2) **malware_scan** `progress=16` — `scan_pdf` streams the bytes to ClamAV via `zINSTREAM`; a `FOUND` reply becomes `MalwareDetectedIngestionError` (permanent, and raises a `malware:{job_id}` critical operational alert plus webhook in `process_claimed_job`), while `MalwareScannerUnavailable` (status_code 503) propagates raw and is therefore retryable. Disabled mode short-circuits. (3) **extract** `progress=20` — `extract_document` -> `extract_pdf_document`: per page, `page.get_text().strip()`; if `len(text) < 40` **and** the page has images it is routed to `_ocr_page` (200 dpi pixmap -> PNG -> `tesserocr.PyTessBaseAPI(path=tessdata.data_path(), lang='eng')`), and if OCR could not run at all the 1-based page number is appended to `unresolved_scanned_pages`; if the page has images *and* usable text, `FIGURE REDACTED FOR SEMANTIC INDEXING` is appended so the model sees that a visual was skipped. Raw page texts then feed `_detect_repeated_lines` (needs >= 4 pages; counts identical first/last non-empty lines of length 4..89 that are not chapter headings; threshold `max(3, pages//3)`), each page goes through `_clean_page` (drop page-number-only lines, drop detected running headers/footers, drop dot-leader TOC lines, strip control chars, collapse runs of 4+ identical punctuation, squeeze 3+ newlines), then `redact_pii` (six regex rules: email, PH mobile, student number, address line, participant id, signature line — counts accumulate into `redaction_stats`), and finally `_remove_excluded_sections_from_pages` drops Table of Contents / Bibliography / References / Acknowledgements / Dedication / Approval Sheet / CV / List of Figures|Tables|Appendices blocks, resuming only at the next `chapter <n>` heading. Empty text is permanent; so is any unresolved scanned page when `REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES` is true (default), with a message naming the page numbers. (4) **chunk** `progress=40` — `split_document` concatenates surviving pages into one string joined by a single space (never a newline, because '\\n\\n' and '\\n' are the strongest recursive separators and a physical page break would masquerade as a semantic boundary), recording a `(start, end, page_number)` span per page and a `(char_offset, heading_text)` pair for every line `_is_section_heading` accepts. The LangChain `RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter.from_tiktoken_encoder(encoding_name='cl100k_base', chunk_size=800, chunk_overlap=100, separators=_TOKEN_SEPARATORS, add_start_index=False)` produces the chunks; because `add_start_index` is off, `_locate_chunk` maps each chunk back to the source by predicting `previous.end_index - _shared_overlap_characters(prev, cur)` (a KMP prefix function over `right[:limit] + sentinel + left[-limit:]`, walked down the failure links until the overlap fits inside 100 tokens) and falling back to `str.find`. Pages are assigned by span intersection (`min`/`max` of overlapping page numbers), the section by `bisect_right` over the heading offsets. `validate_chunk_records` runs here, then `services.ingestion` filters `is_noise_chunk` (>15% non-alphanumeric after whitespace removal) and calls `validate_chunk_records` **again** so `chunk_index` is renumbered contiguously from 0 after removals; a `TypeError`/`ValueError` from either becomes permanent, and an empty result is permanent. (5) **embed** `progress=58` — `embed_texts` in batches of 64 through `gemini_pool.run(..., EMBED, ...)` with 3-attempt exponential backoff (0.5s base); count and per-vector dimension (768) mismatches are permanent.
