(6) **screen** `progress=72` — `screen_new_submission(embeddings, department)` issues one `match_chunks` RPC per chunk at `match_count=1`, `match_threshold=0.85`, the department, and the provenance params, aggregates into `{flagged, exact_duplicate, highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage, matched_chunk_count, total_chunks, top_paper_percentage, verdict_level, duplication_percentage, threshold, matched_papers[<=3], most_similar_paper}`, enriches titles, and stamps `screened_at`/`archive_size`/`scan_scope='at_upload'`. `exact_duplicate` (every chunk matched AND every match >= 0.999) raises `DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError` whose `public_error` names the archived thesis — the only permanent error whose text is shown to the uploader. Anything short of verbatim is committed flagged. (7) **index** `progress=88` — the paper payload is assembled with `id = str(uuid.UUID(job_id))` (paper id *is* job id), `chunk_count`, `redaction_stats`, `duplication_scan`, and `index_provenance = current_index_fingerprint()`, and each chunk row carries `build_chunk_metadata(...)` plus its embedding. One `commit_upload_ingestion` RPC then locks the job row `for update`, short-circuits when already completed, refuses when `cancel_requested_at is not null` or the lease is not this worker's, asserts `p_paper->>'id' = p_job_id`, delegates to `commit_paper_ingestion` (which validates every provenance field against the frozen constants, inserts `papers` with `ingestion_status='processing'`, inserts the `paper_index_versions` row, inserts each chunk with a 768-length check, re-counts the inserted chunks, then flips to `ready` and stamps `activated_at`), and finally marks the job `completed, stage='done', progress=100, chunks=<n>, duplication=<scan>, cleanup_status='not_required', expires_at = now()+30 days`. If the RPC response is lost, `ingestion.py:249-274` re-reads `upload_jobs` and `papers` by the deterministic id and treats `status='completed' AND ingestion_status='ready' AND chunk_count == len(chunk_rows)` as success.

**After the commit.** `log_activity` is best-effort (failure logged, never unwinds). `refresh_after_ingest(paper_id, department)` folds the new paper into every other paper's stored per-chunk match table (`duplication_scan.rescan.chunk_matches`) — incremental because each chunk retains only its closest neighbour, so only the new paper's vectors need comparing; papers with no usable table pay one full `rescan_indexed_paper`. It swallows every exception, because a stale advisory screening must not fail a committed ingestion.

**Outcome recording.** `process_claimed_job` catches `CancellationRequested` -> `finalize_cancellation` (sets `status='cancelled'`, `cleanup_status='pending'` when the source is still stored); `LeaseLostError` -> log only, leaving the row `processing` until its lease expires and another claim reclaims it; anything else -> `logger.exception`, a malware alert if applicable, then `schedule_retry` when `attempt_count < max_attempts` **and** `is_retryable_ingestion_error` (transient network markers, or a `status_code`/`code` of 408/429/5xx anywhere in the `__cause__`/`__context__` chain; `PermanentIngestionError` and `LeaseLostError` are hard-excluded), otherwise `fail_job`. `retry_at` picks `_RETRY_DELAYS[attempt_count-1]` and raises it to a provider `Retry-After` capped at 15 minutes. `fail_job` publishes `error.public_error` when present, else a fixed sentence keyed on whether the error was permanent, so a provider response can never leak to an uploader.

**Cleanup and expiry.** `process_one_cleanup` claims one `failed`/`cancelled` job with `cleanup_status='pending' AND source_stored`, deletes the staged object, and on failure writes a `storage_cleanup_queue` row (class name only) and marks the job `delegated`; if even that fails the claim is left alive so `expire_upload_jobs`'s first UPDATE (recovering `processing` cleanups with an expired lease back to `pending`) can re-offer it. `expire_upload_jobs` then deletes terminal rows past `expires_at` whose storage is confirmed gone.

### Public surface

- workers/ingestion_worker.py:56 `class CancellationRequested(RuntimeError)` - internal control signal, raised only from LeaseHeartbeat.update at a checkpoint
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:60 `class LeaseHeartbeat(client, job_id, worker_id, scanner='healthy')` - context manager; `__enter__` starts the keep-alive thread and returns the bound `update` callable; `__exit__` sets the stop event and joins with timeout `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds + 1`
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:96-114 `LeaseHeartbeat.valid` / `.cancel_requested` - lock-guarded properties with setters, kept as attributes so existing tests keep reading them
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:124 `LeaseHeartbeat.update(**updates) -> bool` - the only mutator; raises CancellationRequested, returns False on lease loss
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:149 `LeaseHeartbeat._run(scanner)` - background loop: bare `update()` then `register_worker(state='processing', current_job_id=...)`
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:169 `_worker_id() -> str` - `'{hostname}:{pid}:{uuid4hex[:8]}'`
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:173 `process_claimed_job(client, job, worker_id, scanner='healthy') -> None` - never raises; records every outcome
- workers/ingestion_worker.py:207 `class _Housekeeping(client, worker_id)` with `.tick(current_job_id=None)` and `.register_processing(job_id)`

- SQL RPC `queue_upload_job(p_job_id, p_owner_id) -> boolean` - staging -> queued, sets source_stored=true, next_retry_at=now()
- SQL RPC `claim_upload_job(p_worker_id, p_lease_seconds=120) -> setof upload_jobs` - sweeps staging>15min, cancelled-with-expired-lease, and attempt-exhausted rows, then `for update skip locked limit 1`
- SQL RPC `heartbeat_upload_job(p_job_id, p_worker_id, p_lease_seconds, p_stage, p_progress, p_message) -> boolean`
- SQL RPC `heartbeat_upload_job_control(...) -> jsonb {lease_valid, cancel_requested}` (20260724 only)
- SQL RPC `request_upload_cancellation(p_job_id, p_requester_id, p_is_superadmin, p_reason) -> jsonb {outcome,...}` (20260724 only)
- SQL RPC `finalize_upload_cancellation(p_job_id, p_worker_id) -> boolean` (20260724 only)
- SQL RPC `schedule_upload_retry(p_job_id, p_worker_id, p_retry_at, p_failure_category) -> boolean`
- SQL RPC `fail_upload_job(p_job_id, p_worker_id, p_failure_category, p_public_error) -> boolean`
- SQL RPC `commit_upload_ingestion(p_job_id, p_worker_id, p_paper, p_chunks) -> uuid`
- SQL RPC `commit_paper_ingestion(p_paper, p_chunks) -> uuid` - inner transactional insert of papers + paper_index_versions + chunks, with provenance validation and a post-insert count check
- SQL RPC `claim_upload_cleanup(p_worker_id) -> setof upload_jobs`, `finish_upload_cleanup(p_job_id, p_worker_id, p_delegated) -> boolean`, `expire_upload_jobs() -> integer`
- SQL RPC `register_ingestion_worker(p_worker_id, p_state, p_scanner_status, p_version, p_current_job_id)` / `stop_ingestion_worker(p_worker_id)` (20260724 only)
- CLI: `python -m workers.ingestion_worker [--once]`

### Invariants

- paper_id == job_id. Enforced twice: `str(uuid.UUID(job_id))` (services/ingestion.py:192) and `if nullif(p_paper->>'id','')::uuid is distinct from p_job_id then raise` inside commit_upload_ingestion. The `a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification` BEFORE INSERT trigger depends on it to find `upload_jobs.request_payload` by `new.id`.
- Content hash is bound at reservation. `reserve_upload_job` rejects a non-`^[0-9a-f]{64}$` digest and raises errcode 22000 when an idempotency key is reused with different content; the worker re-verifies SHA-256 against the downloaded bytes (services/ingestion.py:66) and treats a mismatch as permanent.
- Every mutating queue RPC requires `status='processing' AND lease_owner = p_worker_id AND lease_expires_at >= now()`. heartbeat, schedule_upload_retry, fail_upload_job, finalize_upload_cancellation and commit_upload_ingestion all re-check it, so a worker whose lease was reclaimed can never write.
- `source_stored = true` gates claimability, so a reserved-but-unstaged job is never visible to a worker.
- The background keep-alive never carries a stage, progress, or message (workers/ingestion_worker.py:152-154), so the job row's stage is written only by the pipeline thread.
- At most one control RPC per job is in flight (`_rpc_lock`, workers/ingestion_worker.py:131), so keep-alives cannot arrive out of order against stage updates.
- `ingestion_heartbeat_seconds * 2 < ingestion_lease_seconds` is a hard config validator (config.py:244); defaults 30 / 120.
- `--once` always runs a single job inline and never constructs a ThreadPoolExecutor, whatever INGESTION_CONCURRENCY says (workers/ingestion_worker.py:281, pinned by tests/test_worker_concurrency.py).
- Shutdown drains: `_drain` + the `finally` both `executor.shutdown(wait=True)`, so no lease is stranded to expire on its own.
- Screening runs strictly after embedding and strictly before the commit, so a manuscript is never compared against itself (services/ingestion.py:167-183).
- Exact-duplicate refusal requires BOTH full coverage (`len(matches) == total_chunks`) and every match >= EXACT_DUPLICATE_SIMILARITY = 0.999 (services/novelty.py:114-124). Anything less is advisory and is committed flagged.
- commit_paper_ingestion validates the index fingerprint field by field: dims == 768, preprocessing_version == 'document-v1', chunking_version == 'token-v1', tokenizer == 'cl100k_base', chunk_size 800, chunk_overlap 100, provenance_status == 'verified'. Bumping PREPROCESSING_VERSION or CHUNKING_VERSION in Python without the matching SQL edit makes every commit fail.
- `papers.chunk_count == jsonb_array_length(p_chunks)`, every embedding array is length 768, and a post-insert `count(*)` over `(paper_id, index_version)` must equal the expected count or the whole transaction aborts.
- chunk_index is contiguous from 0 after noise filtering (the second `validate_chunk_records` pass, services/ingestion.py:145), matching the unique index `chunks (paper_id, index_version, chunk_index)`.
- Every chunk's cl100k_base token count is <= settings.chunk_size_tokens (800); `validate_chunk_records` raises otherwise.
- A chunk's page range is all-or-nothing (`(page_start is None) != (page_end is None)` raises) and `page_end >= page_start`.
- Unresolved scanned pages fail the job when REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES is true; a page where OCR ran and legitimately returned nothing is NOT listed as unresolved.
- A commit is idempotent on replay: commit_upload_ingestion returns the existing paper_id when the job is already `completed`, and commit_paper_ingestion returns early when the paper is `ready` with matching chunk counts.
- Cancellation is cooperative and checkpoint-only: the pipeline is never interrupted mid-stage, and commit_upload_ingestion refuses outright if `cancel_requested_at` is set.
- All queue RPCs are `security definer`, `set search_path = public`, revoked from public/anon/authenticated and granted only to service_role; `upload_jobs` has RLS enabled and is service_role-only (pinned by tests/test_durable_ingestion.py::TestSqlQueueContracts).

### Data model

- `public.upload_jobs` — id (uuid, == future paper id), owner_id (uuid, ON DELETE SET NULL per 20260803), department, status in (staging|queued|processing|retry_wait|completed|failed|cancelled), stage, progress, message, error, idempotency_key (uuid, unique with owner_id), source_path, source_stored, original_filename, content_sha256, request_payload jsonb, thesis_category, attempt_count, max_attempts (1..10), next_retry_at, lease_owner, lease_expires_at, heartbeat_at, started_at, completed_at, expires_at, failure_category, cleanup_status in (not_required|pending|processing|completed|delegated), cancel_requested_at/_by, cancel_reason, cancelled_at, paper_id, chunks, duplication jsonb. Indexes: owner_idempotency unique, claim (status, next_retry_at, created_at) partial, lease (lease_expires_at) partial, cleanup (cleanup_status, completed_at) partial, expiry (expires_at) partial.
- `public.papers` — id, title, authors, year, abstract, track, content (never written by this path), filename, storage_path, chunk_count, duplication_scan jsonb, uploaded_by, department, active_index_version uuid, ingestion_status in (processing|ready|failed|deletion_pending), redaction_stats jsonb, thesis_category, plus program_id / specialization_id / legacy_track / classification_status filled by the hydrate trigger.
- `public.chunks` — id bigserial, paper_id, chunk_index, content, metadata jsonb, embedding vector(768), page_start, page_end, section, index_version uuid, indexed_at. Unique (paper_id, index_version, chunk_index); HNSW index on embedding with vector_cosine_ops.
- `public.paper_index_versions` — (paper_id, index_version) with embedding_model, embedding_dimensions (check = 768), preprocessing_version, chunking_version, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, provenance_status in (verified|legacy_assumed), activated_at.
- `public.ingestion_workers` — worker_id (pk), state, current_job_id, scanner_status, version, started_at, last_seen_at, stopped_at. Upserted by register_ingestion_worker.
- `public.storage_cleanup_queue` — operation, resource_path, paper_id, job_id, error_category (exception class name only), status.
- `public.operational_alerts` — dedupe_key unique, alert_type, severity, status, safe_details, occurrence_count, first/last_seen_at, last_notified_at, acknowledged_at/_by, resolved_at.
- `public.upload_job_events` — append-only audit written by the 20260724 trigger on every status / cancel transition.
- Python `ExtractedPage(page_number: int|None, text: str)` and `ExtractedDocument(pages: list[ExtractedPage], redaction_stats: dict[str,int], unresolved_scanned_pages: tuple[int,...])` with a `.text` property.
- Chunk record dict produced by `split_document`: {content, chunk_index, start_index, end_index, page_start, page_end, section} then enriched by `validate_chunk_records` with {token_count, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, chunking_version}.
- Chunk metadata jsonb from `build_chunk_metadata`: {title, author, track, year, department, page_start, page_end, section, chunk_index, token_count, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, chunking_version}.
- Index fingerprint from `current_index_fingerprint()`: {embedding_model, embedding_dimensions, preprocessing_version, chunking_version, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, provenance_status}.
- duplication_scan jsonb: {flagged, exact_duplicate, highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage, matched_chunk_count, total_chunks, top_paper_percentage, verdict_level in (clear|review_suggested|high_overlap|exact_duplicate), duplication_percentage (legacy alias), threshold, matched_papers[<=3], most_similar_paper, screened_at, archive_size, scan_scope} plus a nested `rescan` sub-object carrying `chunk_matches` after refresh_after_ingest.
- Control-RPC result: {lease_valid: bool, cancel_requested: bool}.

### Config knobs

- INGESTION_POLL_SECONDS (default 2.0, 0.2..60) — idle claim interval and the FIRST_COMPLETED wait timeout
- INGESTION_LEASE_SECONDS (default 120, 30..900) — passed to claim and every heartbeat; also validated 30..900 inside claim_upload_job
- INGESTION_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS (default 30, 5..300) — keep-alive cadence, registry cadence, and the `__exit__` join timeout; must be < half the lease
- INGESTION_MAX_ATTEMPTS (default 3, 1..10) — written to upload_jobs.max_attempts at reservation; also the fallback when the row has none
- INGESTION_MAINTENANCE_SECONDS (default 300, 30..3600) — cleanup / expiry / operations-monitor cadence
- INGESTION_CONCURRENCY (default 1, 1..8) — >1 switches to a ThreadPoolExecutor; ignored under --once
- REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES (default true) — fail rather than index a manuscript missing OCR-only pages
- MAX_PDF_PAGES (default 500, 1..2000) and MAX_UPLOAD_MB (25)
- MALWARE_SCAN_MODE ('disabled' | 'clamav'), CLAMAV_HOST/PORT/TIMEOUT_SECONDS (127.0.0.1:3310, 20 s) — clamav is mandatory under APP_ENVIRONMENT=production
- CHUNK_SIZE_TOKENS Literal[800], CHUNK_OVERLAP_TOKENS Literal[100], EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS Literal[768], DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD 0.85 — frozen; changing any of them also requires editing commit_paper_ingestion's provenance check
- OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED (default false) plus OPERATIONS_WORKER_STALE_SECONDS (90), OPERATIONS_QUEUE_AGE_SECONDS (300), OPERATIONS_QUEUE_DEPTH_THRESHOLD (10), OPERATIONS_CLEANUP_AGE_SECONDS, OPERATIONS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL/_SECRET (https + >=32 chars, both or neither)
- BACKUP_RPO_HOURS / BACKUP_RTO_HOURS — 0 disables the backup_stale condition entirely
- MAX_BATCH_FILES (20, 1..50) — producer side only
- GEMINI_EMBED_MODEL ('models/gemini-embedding-001') — part of the provenance fingerprint and of the retrieval provenance params, so changing it orphans every existing index

### Error handling

- Exception taxonomy drives everything: PermanentIngestionError (and its subclasses) and LeaseLostError are hard-excluded from retry by `is_retryable_ingestion_error`; transient network markers or a 408/429/5xx on any link of the `__cause__`/`__context__` chain are retryable; everything else fails on the first attempt.
- Permanent failures, in order of reachability: incomplete durable source, SHA-256 mismatch, not a PDF, encrypted PDF, unreadable PDF, page count outside 1..MAX_PDF_PAGES, malware detected, no extractable text, unresolved scanned pages under REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES, invalid chunk records, no clean indexable text, embedding count mismatch, embedding dimension mismatch, exact duplicate.
- Public error text is sanitized: `fail_job` publishes `error.public_error` only for DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError; every other permanent error gets 'The manuscript could not be processed safely.' and every exhausted-retry failure gets 'The thesis could not be safely indexed after the allowed retries.' (services/upload_queue.py:135-149). Log lines print `type(error).__name__`, not the message, in almost every handler.
- Malware detection additionally upserts a `malware:{job_id}` critical operational alert and fires the signed webhook; a failure to persist the alert is logged and swallowed so the job still fails cleanly (workers/ingestion_worker.py:190-199).
- Ambiguous commit responses are resolved by reading the deterministic (job_id == paper_id) pair and checking status/ingestion_status/chunk_count; if that read also fails, the original exception is re-raised (services/ingestion.py:249-274).
- Post-commit work never unwinds a commit: `log_activity` failures are logged with `logger.exception`, and `refresh_after_ingest` swallows both the archive load and every per-paper refresh (services/novelty.py:481, :510).
- LeaseLostError produces no state change at all — the job is abandoned in `processing` and recovered by the next `claim_upload_job` sweep, or failed by that sweep once attempts are exhausted.
- Storage cleanup that cannot delete falls back to a `storage_cleanup_queue` row and marks the job `delegated`; if even the queue insert fails, `logger.critical` fires and the claim is deliberately left unfinished so `expire_upload_jobs` can re-offer it.
- Housekeeping failures are non-fatal: registry heartbeat, cleanup, expiry and the operations monitor are each wrapped in a bare `except Exception` that logs the class name and continues (workers/ingestion_worker.py:232, :242, :252).
- Missing RPCs degrade rather than crash: `_missing_rpc` / the PGRST202 check in `heartbeat_job_control` detect 'pgrst202', '42883', or 'could not find the function' and fall back (or return False) instead of raising.
- The commit RPC surfaces schema violations as PostgreSQL exceptions with fixed strings that reach the operator via `failure_category`: 'Verified 768-dimensional index provenance is required', 'Paper chunk count does not match staged chunks', 'Invalid embedding dimensions', 'Inserted chunk count verification failed', 'Paper ingestion ID already exists in an incomplete state', 'Upload worker lease is no longer valid', 'Upload cancellation was requested'.

### Tests that pin it

- tests/test_durable_ingestion.py (1051 lines) — TestPipeline pins the atomic commit payload shape, exact-duplicate refusal before commit, high-overlap-still-commits, unresolved-OCR-page refusal and its escape hatch, hash-mismatch permanence, 'lost lease stops before side effects' (asserts zero RPC calls), and ambiguous-commit recovery. TestRetryPolicy pins the transient/permanent split, the bounded Retry-After, that a worker never both retries and fails the same attempt, and the exhausted-attempt path. TestCleanupRecovery pins delegated cleanup, an unpersisted cleanup staying claimable, and storage disposition. TestUploadApi / TestBatchUploadApi pin idempotency-key behaviour and staging compensation. TestSqlQueueContracts asserts the RPCs are atomic, leased and service_role-only in both SQL sources.
- tests/test_worker_concurrency.py (244 lines) — that concurrency 1 constructs no pool and runs on the calling thread, that --once never builds a pool whatever the setting, that a pooled worker genuinely runs N jobs simultaneously (threading.Barrier), and that shutdown drains.
- tests/test_chunker.py (222 lines) — the 800/100 constants, hard token limit including an unbroken 50 000-character word, determinism, cross-page overlap and section mapping, .txt chunks carrying null pages, metadata not consuming the content budget, and a parametrized accept/reject corpus for _is_section_heading including 'a rejected line inherits the heading above it'.
- tests/test_document_processor.py (157 lines) — the 15% noise rule (whitespace excluded), page-number/TOC-leader/repeated-header removal, bibliography-removal-until-next-chapter, 1-based page retention, the figure placeholder constant, and the three OCR outcomes (cannot read -> reported, recovers -> not reported, blank scan read successfully -> not a fault).
- tests/test_schema_consistency.py — pins the known supabase_setup.sql vs newest-migration drift set (match_chunks, check_topic_duplication, claim_upload_job, claim_upload_cleanup, expire_upload_jobs, schedule_upload_retry) and asserts commit_upload_ingestion keeps its cancellation guard in BOTH files.
- tests/test_security_upload_hardening.py — redact_pii rules against ExtractedDocument/ExtractedPage.
- tests/test_event_loop_responsiveness.py:452 — asserts extract_document / extract_text and friends are never called on the event loop.
- tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py — that workers/ and services/ are in the image COPY list.
- tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py — activate_paper_index grants/revokes.

### Gotchas

- A single transient heartbeat failure permanently kills the in-process lease. `LeaseHeartbeat.update` catches every exception and sets `_valid = False` with no retry (workers/ingestion_worker.py:140-144), from either thread. The DB lease is still valid for up to 120 s, but the next `_require_lease` raises LeaseLostError, `process_claimed_job` records nothing (:184), and the row sits in `processing` until the lease expires and another claim burns one of three attempts. The class docstring (:66-70) says the lock fixed exactly this — it did not; the lock only serializes access, the clearing behaviour is unchanged.
- heartbeat_upload_job_control, finalize_upload_cancellation, request_upload_cancellation, register_ingestion_worker and stop_ingestion_worker exist ONLY in migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql — supabase_setup.sql never defines them. On a base-schema-only project `heartbeat_job_control` silently degrades through the PGRST202 branch (services/upload_queue.py:62-73) to the stage-only heartbeat and always reports `cancel_requested: False`, so a mid-pipeline cancellation is invisible to the worker; the commit then raises 'Upload cancellation was requested' (supabase_setup.sql:1690), which is not a PermanentIngestionError and carries no retryable status, so the job ends `failed` instead of `cancelled`. register_worker/stop_worker return False (services/operations.py:62-74), leaving the registry empty and making /health/worker and the `worker_unavailable` alert report a dead fleet.
- supabase_setup.sql's copies of claim_upload_job (:1517), claim_upload_cleanup (:1717) and expire_upload_jobs (:1763) are the pre-cancellation 20260723 bodies: they never transition a cancelled-while-processing job, never exclude `cancel_requested_at is not null` from a claim, and never clean up or expire `cancelled` rows. tests/test_schema_consistency.py:99-107 allowlists this as 'queue reformatting', which understates a behavioural difference. Because everything is `create or replace`, re-running supabase_setup.sql after the migrations silently reverts all three.
- Only seven cancellation/lease checkpoints exist (services/ingestion.py:101, 109, 119, 139, 155, 167, 236). The two longest unchecked spans are embedding (batches of 64, each with up to 3 backoff retries) and the ingest-time screen, which issues one `match_chunks` RPC **per chunk** sequentially (services/novelty.py:350-362) — a 400-chunk thesis is 400 round trips with no cancellation point and no progress update.
- When the background keep-alive observes a cancellation it raises CancellationRequested into itself and `return`s (workers/ingestion_worker.py:160-161), so the lease stops being renewed while the pipeline keeps running to its next checkpoint. If that checkpoint is far away, the lease expires, claim_upload_job flips the row to `cancelled`, and the worker's later `finalize_cancellation` finds nothing — the 'Cancellation could not finalize because job lost its lease' warning at :183.
- `_RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600)` but the 600 s tier is unreachable at the default INGESTION_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3: `retry_at` indexes `attempt_count - 1` and retries stop once `attempt_count == max_attempts`, so only 30 s and 120 s ever fire.
- `papers.content` is in commit_paper_ingestion's insert list (`p_paper->>'content'`) but services/ingestion.py never sets it, so the column is NULL for every durably ingested paper and nothing reads it (retrieval uses chunks.content). Dead column.
- paper_data's `program_id`, `specialization_id`, `legacy_track`, `classification_status` and `thesis_category` (services/ingestion.py:197-205) are ALL inert — none is in commit_paper_ingestion's insert list; the BEFORE INSERT hydrate trigger re-reads `upload_jobs.request_payload`. The code comment at :202-205 flags only `thesis_category`, so the other four read as live fields.
- Excluded-section skipping is sticky across pages and only ends at a literal `chapter <digits|roman>` heading (services/document_processor.py:237-255 with `_CHAPTER_HEADING` at :76). A front-matter 'LIST OF TABLES', 'ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS' or 'DEDICATION' therefore deletes everything up to the first 'Chapter 1' — including ABSTRACT and INTRODUCTION when they are not spelled as chapters.
- Scanned-page detection requires `page.get_images(full=True)` to be non-empty (document_processor.py:265-266). A page whose scan is drawn as vector content, or whose image PyMuPDF does not enumerate, yields <40 chars, no OCR attempt, and no entry in `unresolved_scanned_pages` — silent data loss that REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES cannot catch, which is the exact failure mode that setting exists for.
- `_ocr_page` returns `('', False)` both when the tesserocr binding is missing and when the native call raises (document_processor.py:154-174), so an environment fault and a per-page crash are indistinguishable downstream; both fail the whole manuscript with the 'install the tesserocr runtime' message.
- `redact_pii` runs per page BEFORE excluded sections are dropped (document_processor.py:284-289), so `papers.redaction_stats` counts redactions inside text (approval sheets, CVs, signature blocks) that never reaches the index — the stat systematically overstates what was redacted from the corpus.
- The non-PDF branch of `extract_document` (:300-307) skips `_clean_page`, repeated-line detection, TOC/bibliography removal and the figure placeholder entirely, and emits a single page with `page_number=None`. Upload only accepts PDFs, but routers/duplication.py:259 shares this function, so a .txt paste is cleaned by a materially different rule set than a PDF.
- `_LazyTokenSplitter._chunk_size` / `_chunk_overlap` are CLASS attributes bound from settings at import (chunker.py:75-76) while `validate_chunk_records` reads settings live (:193). Monkeypatching chunk size at runtime desynchronizes the splitter from its validator. Harmless in production only because both are `Literal` in config.py.
- `_is_section_heading` compares against the literal string 'FIGURE REDACTED FOR SEMANTIC INDEXING' (chunker.py:162) instead of importing `document_processor.FIGURE_PLACEHOLDER` (:37). Changing the constant in one file silently stops the other from recognizing it.
- `validate_chunk_records` runs twice per job — once inside `split_document` (chunker.py:303) and again in services/ingestion.py:145 after noise filtering. Only the second run's renumbering is authoritative; the first tokenizes every chunk redundantly, so each chunk is cl100k_base-encoded twice per ingestion.
- `_locate_chunk` (chunker.py:236-248) falls back to `source.find(content, max(previous['start_index']+1, expected))` when its predicted offset misses. In a thesis with repeated boilerplate that can land on a later duplicate occurrence and attribute the chunk to the wrong pages and section; nothing checks that offsets advance by anything more than one character.
- At INGESTION_CONCURRENCY > 1 every in-flight job's LeaseHeartbeat background thread writes the same `ingestion_workers` row with its own `current_job_id` (workers/ingestion_worker.py:156-159), so the registry's `current_job_id` flaps between concurrent jobs; `_Housekeeping.tick` likewise reports only `next(iter(in_flight.values()))` (:298). The operations console shows one arbitrary job per worker.
- The frontend's 3D stage mapper (rag-thesis-frontend/src/components/three/IngestScene3D.jsx:12-20) recognizes only download/malware_scan/extract/chunk/embed/screen/index; the backend also emits `store`, `queued`, `done`, `error` and `cancelled`, all of which fall through to the `download` default — a completed or cancelled job renders as if it were still downloading.
- `claim_upload_job` resets `stage='download', progress=10` on every re-claim, so a job that failed transiently at `screen` (72%) shows its progress bar jumping backwards to 10% on retry.
- `heartbeat_upload_job_control` suppresses only `p_message` while `cancel_requested_at` is set (migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:238); `p_stage` and `p_progress` still overwrite, so a cancelling job can display 'Cancellation requested...' next to `stage='index', progress=88`.
- The migration's `expire_upload_jobs` deletes `cancelled` rows, but `upload_jobs_expiry_idx` is a partial index on `status in ('completed','failed')` — cancelled-row expiry is not index-supported.
- Cleanup drains at one job per `ingestion_maintenance_seconds` per worker (`process_one_cleanup` handles exactly one claim). A backlog of N failed uploads holding staged PDFs takes N * 5 minutes to clear on a single worker at the default.
- `MalwareScannerUnavailable` is raised both for a real outage and for a malformed ClamAV reply (services/malware.py:61); since it carries `status_code = 503`, a permanently broken scanner response is retried all three attempts before failing.
- Retry classification is attribute- and message-driven: `is_retryable_ingestion_error` walks the `__cause__`/`__context__` chain reading `status_code`/`code` and `int()`-coercing it (services/upload_queue.py:92-96). A PostgREST APIError whose `code` is a SQLSTATE string ('22000', 'P0001') coerces to 0 and is treated as non-retryable — correct by accident, and fragile to a client-library change that starts putting HTTP codes there.
- `_validate_staged_pdf` opens the manuscript with fitz to check `needs_pass`/`page_count`, then `extract_pdf_document` opens it again — the PDF is parsed twice. With `max_pdf_pages = 500`, 200-dpi OCR rasterization, and INGESTION_CONCURRENCY up to 8, this is the memory ceiling of the whole system.
- Dead / test-only exports: `document_processor.extract_pdf_text` (:295), `extract_text` (:310), `filter_noise_chunks` (:315), `_remove_excluded_sections` (:217, the page-less twin of the function that is actually used), and `chunker.split_text` (:180). `upload_queue.heartbeat_job` (:33) is reachable only through the PGRST202 fallback inside `heartbeat_job_control`.
- `services/upload_queue.py` imports LeaseLostError and PermanentIngestionError from `services.ingestion` (:8), so the queue helper depends on the pipeline module. Any future import in the other direction creates a cycle that the worker's import order would surface at start-up.
- `operations.register_worker` hardcodes `version='2.1.0'` (services/operations.py:53), duplicating the FastAPI `version='2.1.0'` literal at main.py:149. Two sources of truth for the string the operations console displays.
- `TestPipeline` in tests/test_durable_ingestion.py does not patch `refresh_after_ingest`, so those tests reach services/novelty -> the module-level `services.retriever.sb` client built against `https://test-project.supabase.co` and depend on the blanket `except` at novelty.py:481 returning `[]`. Every pipeline test pays a failing DNS/HTTP attempt.
- When the scanner reports `unavailable`, `run_worker` skips claiming entirely but keeps reaping in-flight futures and ticking housekeeping; with `MALWARE_SCAN_MODE=disabled` `scanner_status()` returns `'disabled'` (not `'unavailable'`), the worker registers as `idle` rather than `degraded`, and `scan_pdf` is a no-op — a production misconfiguration is only caught by `validate_production_services`, not by the worker.

### Open questions

- Should `LeaseHeartbeat.update` retry a failed control RPC (or tolerate N consecutive failures) before invalidating the lease? As written, one PostgREST blip costs a full attempt and a lease-expiry wait, and the class docstring already claims this was fixed.
- Is the supabase_setup.sql / 20260724 divergence in claim_upload_job, claim_upload_cleanup and expire_upload_jobs intentional for the defense freeze, or should the base file be brought forward? A fresh project built from the base file alone cannot cancel a mid-pipeline job at all (the control RPC does not exist there).
- Should the embed and screen stages emit intermediate heartbeats (per batch / per N chunks)? They are the only spans where a 400-chunk manuscript can run for minutes with no cancellation checkpoint and no progress movement.
- Is `papers.content` intended to hold anything? It is in the commit RPC's insert list, never written, and never read.
- Should scanned-page detection fall back to a text-density heuristic when `get_images(full=True)` is empty, so a vector-rendered scan is caught by REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES instead of silently contributing nothing?
- Should `redaction_stats` be computed after excluded-section removal so the number reported on the paper reflects what was actually indexed?
- Is the frontend stage mapper's fall-through to `download` for `done`/`cancelled`/`error` deliberate, or should those render a terminal state?
- Should `_RETRY_DELAYS` be trimmed to match the reachable attempt count, or should the default attempt budget be raised to 4 so the 10-minute tier is used?

**Depends on:** config.settings (pydantic-settings; frozen RAG literals and every ingestion_* knob), warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings (must run before any langchain import in both processes), supabase-py service-role client (create_client at workers/ingestion_worker.py:272; services.retriever.sb for the novelty path), PyMuPDF (fitz) for page-count validation and text/image extraction, tesserocr + tessdata + Pillow (optional; OCR_AVAILABLE gate), tiktoken cl100k_base (lazily loaded; the Dockerfile pre-warms /opt/tiktoken-cache), langchain_text_splitters.RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter, langchain_google_genai.GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings via services.gemini_pool (primary key, reserve keys on capacity errors; embeddings are never routed through the OpenAI-compatible gateway), services.malware (ClamAV), services.activity, services.novelty, services.index_provenance, services.network_retry, services.operations, services.safe_logging, PostgreSQL RPCs in supabase_setup.sql + migrations/20260718, 20260720, 20260723, 20260724(+_fixes), 20260725, 20260819, 20260829

**Used by:** routers/upload.py — the producer (`POST /upload/paper`, `POST /upload/batch`) and the admin job-status polling surface, routers/maintenance.py — `evaluate_operations` and the `ingestion_workers` listing for the superadmin operations console, main.py — `/health/worker` reads `ingestion_workers`, and a startup task calls `evaluate_operations`, services/novelty.py — rescan_indexed_paper and refresh_after_ingest reuse the same threshold, aggregation and verdict bands as the ingest-time screen, scripts/reindex_citations.py, scripts/repair_chunk_sections.py, scripts/backfill_legacy_locations.py, scripts/build_redacted_derivatives.py, scripts/seed_synthetic_corpus.py — all re-run extract_document + split_document so their output matches the indexed corpus byte for byte, routers/duplication.py — the ad-hoc novelty scanner shares extract_document / split_document / is_noise_chunk, evaluation/chunk_size_sweep.py — drives `chunker.splitter` directly, rag-thesis-frontend admin upload UI — polls job stage/progress/message and renders IngestScene3D from the `stage` string

**Additional notes returned by the reader** (fields outside the schema, preserved verbatim)


**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 16 correction(s), 6 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** `process_claimed_job(...) -> None` — never raises; records every outcome. "process_claimed_job records every outcome itself and never raises, so a finished future only needs counting."
  **Actual:** It can raise. The three outcome recorders are bare RPC calls with no try/except: `finalize_cancellation` (:180), `schedule_retry` (:201) and `fail_job` (:203). Only the malware alert is guarded (:191-199). In `run_worker` the inline call at :318 is NOT wrapped (only `claim_job` is, :307-314), so a PostgREST/transport failure while recording an outcome propagates out of the `while`, through the `finally` (shutdown + stop_worker) and out of `run_worker` -> `main()` -> process exit. At concurrency > 1 the same exception is swallowed inside the Future and the job is still counted as `processed`. The map repeats the code's own inaccurate comment. (`rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:180,201,203,293-294,318`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: "Cancellation is cooperative and checkpoint-only: the pipeline is never interrupted mid-stage."
  **Actual:** Only true for a job already in `processing`. `request_upload_cancellation` terminally cancels any other status in place — `status='cancelled', stage='cancelled', progress=100, cancelled_at/completed_at=now(), expires_at=now()+30d, cleanup_status = case when source_stored then 'pending' else 'not_required' end` — with no worker involvement, returning outcome `'cancelled'`. Only the `processing` branch sets `cancel_requested_at` and returns `'cancellation_requested'`. The map documents only the second branch. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:171-206`)
- **Wrong:** On a base-schema-only project a mid-pipeline cancellation is invisible to the worker; "the commit then raises 'Upload cancellation was requested' (supabase_setup.sql:1690) ... so the job ends `failed` instead of `cancelled`."
  **Actual:** Unreachable. `request_upload_cancellation` is also migration-only, and it is the only writer of `cancel_requested_at`. The cancel endpoint catches PGRST202 / 'could not find the function' and returns HTTP 503 'Upload cancellation requires the operations migration', so on base-only no cancellation can even be requested and the commit guard at supabase_setup.sql:1690 can never fire. (The columns themselves ARE in the base file at :1370-1373, with a comment explaining that PL/pgSQL resolves record fields at first execution.) The rest of that gotcha — degraded stage-only heartbeat, `cancel_requested: False`, empty `ingestion_workers` registry — is correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1230-1241; rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:1361-1373`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling: "The commit RPC surfaces schema violations as PostgreSQL exceptions with fixed strings that reach the operator via `failure_category`."
  **Actual:** `failure_category` is the Python exception class name, never the SQL message: `fail_job` writes `type(error).__name__` and `schedule_retry` the same (the migration's `schedule_upload_retry` additionally truncates it with `left(p_failure_category, 120)`). The public `error` column gets one of two fixed sentences. The SQL strings ('Invalid embedding dimensions', etc.) reach only the worker log traceback from `logger.exception`. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py:131,147; rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:411`)
- **Wrong:** "A PostgREST APIError whose `code` is a SQLSTATE string ('22000','P0001') coerces to 0 and is treated as non-retryable — correct by accident."
  **Actual:** `int('22000')` is 22000, not 0 (verified in the project interpreter). Only non-numeric SQLSTATEs like 'P0001' raise ValueError and become 0. Both end up non-retryable because neither value lands in {408,429} or [500,599] — the numeric range check, not a coercion to zero, is what protects it. The map also understates the first test: `is_transient_network_error` matches `winerror`/`errno` in {10035,10053,10054,10060,11001}, a `status` attribute (not just `status_code`), 14 substring markers ('timeout', 'rate limit', 'resource_exhausted', 'bad gateway', 'service unavailable', …) and a labelled-3-digit-status regex over the message — and it only recognizes 408/429/500/502/503/504, not all of 5xx. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/network_retry.py:12-42,63-81; rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py:86-99`)
- **Wrong:** "Housekeeping failures are non-fatal: registry heartbeat, cleanup, expiry and the operations monitor are each wrapped in a bare `except Exception` (workers/ingestion_worker.py:232, :242, :252)."
  **Actual:** Cleanup, expiry and the operations monitor share ONE try/except, so an exception in `process_one_cleanup` skips `expire_terminal_jobs` and `evaluate_operations` for that entire maintenance tick (and `maintenance_at` is still advanced afterwards, so the next attempt is a full `ingestion_maintenance_seconds` away). Only the registry write has its own handler (:230-233); :252 is in `register_processing`, not the maintenance path. (`rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:235-243`)
- **Wrong:** "`expire_upload_jobs` then deletes terminal rows past `expires_at` whose storage is confirmed gone."
  **Actual:** Only the failed/cancelled branch requires `source_stored = false`. A `completed` job is deleted on `cleanup_status = 'not_required'` alone, with `source_stored` still true — deliberately, because `commit_upload_ingestion` sets `cleanup_status='not_required'` and the staged object at `uploads/{user}/{job}/{file}` is written into `papers.storage_path` as the paper's permanent file. Also, `cleanup_status='delegated'` is in neither branch, so a delegated row never expires. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:470-478`)
- **Wrong:** Cleanup: "on failure writes a `storage_cleanup_queue` row (class name only) and marks the job `delegated`" — presented as the end of the worker-owned loop.
  **Actual:** Nothing in the worker or any RPC ever drains `storage_cleanup_queue`. Its rows are listed and resolved only by two superadmin HTTP endpoints, which are also the only code that flips `upload_jobs.cleanup_status` to 'completed' and `source_stored=false`. A delegated cleanup is therefore permanent manual work: the staged PDF stays in the private bucket and the job row is excluded from expiry until a human clicks retry. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:144-201; rag-thesis-backend/services/cleanup.py:19-27`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: paper_id == job_id "... The `a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification` BEFORE INSERT trigger depends on it" / gotcha: the five paper_data fields are inert because "the BEFORE INSERT hydrate trigger re-reads `upload_jobs.request_payload`".
  **Actual:** Correct about the mechanism, but both the trigger and its function exist ONLY in migrations (created 20260725:94-112, body replaced 20260819:46-63), and four of the five columns — `program_id`, `specialization_id`, `legacy_track`, `classification_status` — are not in supabase_setup.sql at all (only `thesis_category`, :261). On a base-schema-only project every durably ingested paper loses its whole academic classification silently, which is a sharper instance of the map's own base-only gotcha and is not mentioned. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260725_normalized_academic_catalog.sql:94-112; rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql:46-63; rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:261`)
- **Wrong:** Loop: "bail to a poll sleep if `scan_state == 'unavailable'`" and claim failures back off.
  **Actual:** Both branches have an `once` early return the map omits: an unavailable scanner returns `processed` immediately under `--once` (:300-301), and a `claim_job` exception also returns under `--once` (:311-312). Only the long-running mode sleeps. (`rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:299-314`)
- **Wrong:** `upload_jobs` "Indexes: owner_idempotency unique, claim, lease, cleanup, expiry".
  **Actual:** Incomplete. Also `upload_jobs_owner_idx (owner_id, created_at desc)` and `upload_jobs_status_idx (status, updated_at)` in the base file, plus the migration's `upload_jobs_cancel_idx (cancel_requested_at) where cancel_requested_at is not null and status='processing'`. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:791-792; rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:17-19`)
- **Wrong:** "routers/upload.py ... size: ~1000 lines"; "migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql ... ~560"; "the blanket `except` at novelty.py:481" / "novelty.py:510".
  **Actual:** routers/upload.py is 1545 lines; 20260724_operations_security.sql is 578; the two swallowing handlers in novelty are at :485 (archive load) and :514 (per-paper refresh). Every other anchor I checked — ingestion.py 22/26/30/34/48/56/65/85/90 and the seven `_require_lease` calls at 101/109/119/139/155/167/236, document_processor 30/35/37/40/48/123/134/147/177/193/217/237/258/295/300/310/315, chunker 21/22/24/41/46/70/100/133/161/180/184/215/236/251/306, upload_queue 13/23/33/46/76/83/103/117/126/135/152/182, worker 56/60/96-114/124/131/140-144/149/169/173/207/222/255/270/341, supabase_setup 465/1434/1501/1517/1586/1613/1639/1665/1690/1717/1742/1763, migration 157/218/238/250/275/304/351/403/421/449/465, config.py:244, IngestScene3D normalizeStageKey, and all four test line counts — was exact. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py; rag-thesis-backend/services/novelty.py:485,514`)
- **Wrong:** Permanent-failure list "in order of reachability: incomplete durable source, SHA-256 mismatch, not a PDF, …"
  **Actual:** Omits a non-permanent failure that sits between them: `_staged_pdf_bytes` raises a bare `RuntimeError('Private storage returned an invalid payload')` when the storage client returns a non-bytes payload. Because it is not a `PermanentIngestionError` and carries no status code, `is_retryable_ingestion_error` returns False and `fail_job` publishes the *retry-exhausted* sentence ('The thesis could not be safely indexed after the allowed retries.') on attempt 1. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py:60-61; rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py:139-143`)
- **Wrong:** "if the page has images *and* usable text, `FIGURE REDACTED FOR SEMANTIC INDEXING` is appended"; excluded sections are "TOC / Bibliography / References / Acknowledgements / Dedication / Approval Sheet / CV / List of Figures|Tables|Appendices".
  **Actual:** The placeholder branch is an `elif`, so it needs images AND `len(text) >= 40`: a page with images whose scan OCR successfully re-read into 20 characters gets no placeholder. The excluded-heading regex also matches `biographical sketch`, which the map's enumeration drops. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/document_processor.py:266-277,70-75`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling lists seven commit-RPC exception strings.
  **Actual:** `commit_paper_ingestion` / `commit_upload_ingestion` raise five more the map omits: 'Paper title is required', 'At least one verified chunk is required', 'Chunk content is required', 'Paper identifier must match upload job', 'Upload job not found'. The insert also silently defaults a blank department to 'CCSICT'. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:478-486,523,546-548,1673,1693-1697`)
- **Wrong:** usedBy: "main.py — /health/worker reads `ingestion_workers`, and a startup task calls `evaluate_operations`."
  **Actual:** It is a long-lived background thread (`_operations_monitor`) re-evaluating every `operations_monitor_seconds` (default 60), not a one-shot startup task — and that knob is missing from configKnobs. `/health/worker` additionally excludes `state='stopping'` rows and requires `scanner_status != 'unavailable'` within `operations_worker_stale_seconds`. (`rag-thesis-backend/main.py:60-71,289-308; rag-thesis-backend/config.py:142`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- The cancellation producer endpoint is absent from publicSurface and usedBy: `POST /upload/jobs/{job_id}/cancel` (rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1212-1272). It reads the caller's `profiles.role` to pass `p_is_superadmin`, maps the RPC's `outcome` to 404 (`not_found`) / 403 (`forbidden`) / 200 with one of three messages, converts a missing RPC into 503 'Upload cancellation requires the operations migration', and writes a `record_security_event('upload_cancellation')` audit row. Ownership is enforced inside the RPC (`owner_id is distinct from p_requester_id` unless superadmin), not in Python.
- The other two producer-side polling endpoints are unnamed: `GET /upload/status/{job_id}` (routers/upload.py:1142) and `GET /upload/jobs?ids=` (:1188), both owner-scoped and both joining `upload_job_events` through `_last_event_at`/`_last_event_map` (:1080-1115) — that is where the migration-only `upload_job_events` table is actually read.
- The idempotent-replay short circuit in the producer: when `reserve_upload_job` returns `created=false` and a status other than 'staging', `_stage_and_queue_one` returns 'This submission is already tracked. Poll its existing job for progress.' without re-uploading or re-queuing (routers/upload.py:816-822). The map describes the two-phase commit but not the replay path.
- `_LazyTokenSplitter` is a process-wide singleton whose delegate is built once on first use (chunker.py:78-100) and is shared by the API process too (routers/duplication.py:113), so at INGESTION_CONCURRENCY > 1 several pipeline threads call the same `RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter` concurrently and `_get_delegate` has no lock — two threads racing the first call each build a splitter, and the loser's instance is discarded.
- `embed_texts`' retry is not unconditional: `retry_transient` re-raises immediately unless `is_transient_network_error` accepts the exception (services/network_retry.py:98-100), so the '3-attempt exponential backoff' only applies to transient-classified failures. A deterministic embedding error costs one call, not three.
- `validate_chunk_records` enriches records but never validates `start_index`/`end_index` monotonicity or that `page_end >= page_start` when both are None-free across chunks; combined with `_locate_chunk`'s `str.find` fallback (chunker.py:245) nothing detects a chunk mapped to a later duplicate occurrence. The map flags the fallback but not that the validator is silent about offsets it is handed.

## 7. `novelty` - Novelty / duplication detection

Screens manuscripts for topic overlap against the departmental thesis archive at a fixed 0.85 cosine-similarity threshold, implementing the paper's Section 1.3 "Duplication Parameter" and Section 3.2.3 Phase 3 screen. There are three physically distinct code paths that all claim the same "85% mechanism": an ingest-time screen inside the upload worker (services/novelty.screen_new_submission), an on-demand faculty scan endpoint (POST /duplication/scan), and a query-time single-shot guard in the chat pipeline (services/retriever.check_topic_duplication). Screening flags rather than blocks — the only hard refusal is a verbatim re-upload where every chunk matches at >= 0.999. A deterministic verdict is computed from *concentration* (share of a manuscript's passages whose nearest archived neighbour sits in one single thesis), and an LLM is used only to write prose explaining that verdict, never to produce it.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/services/novelty.py` (516 lines) - All screening mathematics: verdict bands, per-chunk aggregation, exact-duplicate test, ingest-time screen, full rescan, incremental post-ingest refresh, archive sizing. No HTTP, no LLM.
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py` (608 lines) - HTTP surface: /duplication/scan (on-demand faculty scan + LLM verdict prose), /duplication/chat (follow-up Q&A over one stored scan), /duplication/history (list, delete one, clear all). Reimplements per-paper aggregation locally instead of calling novelty.aggregate_matches.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py` (check_topic_duplication = 60 lines) - check_topic_duplication() at :925-984 — the query-time guard. Owns the shared service-role Supabase client `sb` (:25) that novelty.py imports.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py` (n/a) - Pipeline call sites: screen_new_submission at :172, exact-duplicate refusal at :173-183, duplication_scan persisted into the paper row at :212, refresh_after_ingest at :298.
- `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` (n/a) - match_chunks RPC (:840-898), check_topic_duplication RPC (:920-991), scan_history table + verdict_level CHECK (:690-729), papers.duplication_scan jsonb column (:233), scan_history RLS revoke (:1210).
- `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py` (n/a) - require_novelty_access (:419-434), resolve_effective_department (:105-129), get_role_features (:334-348), _feature_enabled (:350-372).
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/rescan_duplication.py` (118 lines) - Operational CLI (python -m scripts.rescan_duplication [--apply] [--paper-id ...]) that re-screens indexed papers against the current archive and nests the result under duplication_scan.rescan.
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/backfill_screening_archive_size.py` (103 lines) - Fills duplication_scan.archive_size_estimated from upload order for records predating archive_size. Writes under a separate key so it can never be read as a measurement.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/utils.js` (n/a) - scanMetrics (:79-110), verdictLabel/verdictTone (:250-279), verdictExplanation (:223-234), currentScreening/atUploadScreening/hasRescan (:124-150), screeningIsUnchanged/screeningHasDrifted (:194-209), isScreeningFlagged (:183-185), archiveSizeLabel (:163-171), mostSimilarPaper (:243-248). Re-derives concentration client-side because the server never stores it for on-demand scans.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Novelty.jsx` (561 lines) - Faculty scan UI: dropzone, verdict ring keyed to concentration, metric tiles, top matches, markdown verdict, follow-up chat, history list.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Archive.jsx` (n/a) - ScreeningDetail / ScreeningRun / ScreeningFigures (:182-299) render the at-upload vs rescan layers of papers.duplication_scan.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/novelty/report.js` (57 lines) - buildNoveltyReport / downloadNoveltyReport — metadata-only JSON export, deliberately excluding manuscript excerpts and reviewer chat.
- `rag-thesis-backend/config.py` (n/a) - duplication_threshold (:78), embedding_dimensions Literal[768] (:21), gemini_verdict_model (:17), rate_limit_scan/followup (:97-98), max_upload_mb/max_pdf_pages (:114-115), require_ocr_for_scanned_pages (:124).

### Control flow

Ingest-time screen. services/ingestion.py chunks and embeds first, then at :172 calls screen_new_submission(embeddings, department) at stage 'screen'/progress 72, BEFORE commit_upload_ingestion — so the manuscript is never compared against itself. novelty.py:351-363 loops the embeddings and issues one `sb.rpc('match_chunks', {query_embedding, match_count: 1, match_threshold: settings.duplication_threshold, p_department: department, p_embedding_model, p_embedding_dimensions})` per chunk, keeping only {paper_id, similarity} when res.data is non-empty. The RPC (supabase_setup.sql:882-896) computes `1 - (embedding <=> query)` and applies six equality predicates after the HNSW candidate stage (active index version, ingestion_status='ready', matching embedding model and dimensions, provenance_status in verified/legacy_assumed, department, optional thesis_category), orders by distance and LIMIT 1. aggregate_matches (:126) then computes coverage = len(matches)/total*100, highest = max similarity, groups matches by paper_id into {match_count, highest_similarity}, ranks by (match_count, highest_similarity) descending, truncates to _TOP_MATCHED_PAPERS = 3, and grades on concentration = max(match_count)/total_chunks*100 via verdict_for_concentration. _enrich_matched_papers (:183) does one papers SELECT to attach title/authors/year/track/department; because most_similar_paper is the same dict object as matched_papers[0] (:179), the enrichment reaches it too. _stamp_scan (:338) adds screened_at, archive_size (a count('exact') of ready papers in the department) and scan_scope='at_upload'. Back in ingestion.py:173, if exact_duplicate is set the job raises DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError (a PermanentIngestionError) naming the archived twin and the total chunk count — the only blocking outcome in the whole subsystem. Otherwise the whole dict is written verbatim into papers.duplication_scan (:212) inside the commit_upload_ingestion RPC, and :282-284 logs duplication_flagged / matched_chunk_percentage / highest_similarity to activity_log.

Post-ingest refresh. After the commit, ingestion.py:298 calls refresh_after_ingest(paper_id, department) (novelty.py:446). It loads the new paper's own active-index chunk vectors with precomputed L2 norms (_paper_chunk_vectors, :434), then SELECTs every other ready paper in the same department with its active_index_version and duplication_scan. For each, it reads the previous per-chunk table at duplication_scan.rescan.chunk_matches. If that table is absent or its length differs from the paper's current vector count (:497), it pays for one full rescan_indexed_paper and stores it. Otherwise apply_new_paper_to_matches (:285) walks the table and, for each chunk, calls _best_archive_match against the new paper's vectors only — valid because each chunk stores only its single closest neighbour, so a new paper can change an entry only by strictly beating it (:312 keeps the incumbent on a tie). If nothing moved it skips the write; otherwise it re-aggregates, re-enriches, re-stamps with scope='rescan' and exclude_paper_id, and store_rescan nests it. Every failure path is swallowed (:485, :514) because a stale screening is advisory and the ingestion has already committed.

Full rescan. rescan_indexed_paper (:368) deliberately bypasses match_chunks. Once a paper is indexed its own chunks match themselves at 1.0 and, with the 100-token chunk overlap, its neighbours land in the 0.9s, so asking the RPC for k neighbours and discarding self returns a window full of the paper itself — measured 38.89% vs the true 97.22%. Instead _candidate_chunk_vectors (:228) mirrors the RPC's WHERE clause in Python: SELECT ready same-department papers except this one, intersect against paper_index_versions rows whose embedding_model and embedding_dimensions match this server and whose provenance_status is verified/legacy_assumed AND whose index_version equals the paper's active_index_version, then pull every chunk embedding for those papers. pgvector columns arrive over PostgREST as JSON strings, so _as_vector (:221) json.loads them. _best_archive_match (:319) is a pure-Python dot product over the candidate list divided by the two precomputed norms, argmax first, threshold gate second. The result feeds aggregate_chunk_matches, which keeps the per-chunk table alongside the totals — that table is what makes the incremental path possible.

On-demand scan. POST /duplication/scan (duplication.py:210) is an async handler that pushes every blocking step through asyncio.to_thread, because an earlier version froze the whole event loop including /health for the duration of a scan. Order: resolve_effective_department (:223) → read at most max_upload_mb+1 bytes and 413 if over (:226-229) → extension whitelist {pdf,txt} → %PDF- magic byte for pdf → plain-text MIME for txt → sanitize_filename (:242) → _reject_unsafe_scan_upload (:256, PyMuPDF open / needs_pass / page_count bounds, then a ClamAV INSTREAM scan that fails CLOSED with 503 when the scanner is unavailable) → extract_document → _clean_chunk_records (:110, split_document + is_noise_chunk + validate_chunk_records, byte-identical to ingestion.py:145-148) → embed_texts, with 502s if the count or any vector dimension is wrong → _match_chunks_against_archive (:118) which runs the same one-RPC-per-chunk loop as the ingest screen and additionally builds text_pairs carrying 320-char excerpts of both sides plus page/section locations. Aggregation is then reimplemented inline (:287-304) rather than calling aggregate_matches: coverage via compute_duplication_percentage, highest via percent(), a local paper_matches dict, concentration_percentage over its values, and verdict_for_concentration WITHOUT the exact_duplicate flag. If nothing matched, a canned '### Advisory: Clear' string is used and top_matches is empty (:308-315). Otherwise top_pids = the three highest counts, one papers SELECT for metadata; if every matched paper vanished between the vector search and that SELECT the handler logs and emits a deterministic-numbers-only advisory instead of 500ing (:345-359). Otherwise the top 5 excerpt pairs for the primary match are HTML-escaped into a prompt that states the deterministic advisory level and instructs the model not to change it, fences the excerpts as untrusted, and forbids saying the thesis is accepted or rejected; it runs through gemini_pool.arun(llm, VERDICT, ainvoke) (:405) with a static fallback string on any failure (:409-416). The row is inserted into scan_history (:433), an activity_log entry is written (:437), and if PostgREST returns no representation the handler raises 502 rather than serving an id-less scan — the client would otherwise post scan_id: undefined to the follow-up endpoint forever.

Follow-up chat. POST /duplication/chat (:463) is a SYNC def, so FastAPI runs it in a threadpool and the blocking calls are correct as written. It SELECTs only id,chat_log,matched_chunks,verdict_summary for the caller's own scan, runs services.guards.prohibited_reason on the question, and on a block logs the category and persists the refusal turn through the shared _persist_turn (:77) — the refusal path used to render a transcript it never wrote back. Otherwise it renders the newest 5 turns with prompts.fence_untrusted, re-renders the stored matched_chunks excerpts, and calls gemini_pool.run(llm, VERDICT, invoke). matched_chunks never leaves the server: it is in neither _PUBLIC_SCAN_FIELDS nor the /history field list.

Query-time guard. Unrelated to the two paths above and not in this subsystem's files: routers/chat.py:1965-1985 runs check_duplication() concurrently with search_chunks via asyncio.gather, calling retriever.check_topic_duplication with the already-computed query embedding. The RPC is LIMIT 1 over the whole department index at the same 0.85 threshold. The resulting alert_data is carried through generation (a second concurrent Gemini call writes its summary, chat.py:2713-2729), reaches the no-context early return (:2650-2657), and is persisted by save_chat_exchange as p_duplication_alert (:1610).

### Public surface

- POST /duplication/scan — routers/duplication.py:210. multipart: file (PDF or .txt, <= max_upload_mb=25 MB), optional Form field `department` (honoured only for superadmins). Guard require_novelty_access. Rate limit settings.rate_limit_scan = '5/minute'. Returns _public_scan(scan_history row): id, filename, department, duplication_percentage, highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage, matched_chunk_count, total_chunks, verdict_level, top_matches, verdict_summary, chat_log, created_at. Declared errors 400/413/415/422/502/503.
- POST /duplication/chat — routers/duplication.py:463. body DuplicationChatReq {scan_id: str 1-64 chars, question: str 1-4000 chars}. Guard require_novelty_access; row fetched with .eq('user_id', user.id) so it is owner-scoped. Rate limit rate_limit_followup = '20/minute'. Returns {answer, chat_log} where chat_log is the newest 20 entries. 404 on unknown/unowned scan, 502 on LLM failure.
- GET /duplication/history — routers/duplication.py:552. Guard require_novelty_access. NO rate-limit decorator (only the global 120/minute default). Returns the raw PostgREST rows for the caller, ordered created_at desc, with fields id,user_id,filename,department,duplication_percentage,highest_similarity,matched_chunk_percentage,matched_chunk_count,total_chunks,verdict_level,top_matches,verdict_summary,chat_log,created_at — note user_id is included here although _public_scan exists specifically to suppress it.
- DELETE /duplication/history/{scan_id} — routers/duplication.py:576. Owner-scoped on both the existence SELECT and the DELETE. rate_limit_followup. 404 for absent OR malformed uuid (via services.db_errors.is_invalid_identifier). Returns {deleted: true, id}.
- DELETE /duplication/history — routers/duplication.py:603. Deletes every scan_history row for the caller. rate_limit_followup. Returns {deleted: true}.
- services.novelty.screen_new_submission(embeddings: list[list[float]], department: str) -> dict — novelty.py:347. One match_chunks RPC per chunk at match_count=1, threshold settings.duplication_threshold, p_department=department, plus retrieval_provenance_params(). Returns the aggregate dict enriched with paper metadata and stamped with screened_at / archive_size / scan_scope='at_upload'.
- services.novelty.aggregate_matches(matches, total_chunks, threshold) -> dict — novelty.py:126. Pure. Emits flagged, exact_duplicate, highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage, matched_chunk_count, total_chunks, top_paper_percentage, verdict_level, duplication_percentage (compat alias), threshold, matched_papers (<=3), most_similar_paper.
- services.novelty.aggregate_chunk_matches(chunk_matches: list[dict|None], threshold) -> dict — novelty.py:270. Same as above plus the per-chunk table under key 'chunk_matches'.
- services.novelty.verdict_for_concentration(top_paper_percentage: float, exact_duplicate: bool = False) -> str — novelty.py:92. Returns 'exact_duplicate' | 'clear' (<25) | 'review_suggested' (25..<70) | 'high_overlap' (>=70).
- services.novelty.concentration_percentage(matched_papers, total_chunks) -> float — novelty.py:84. max(match_count)/total_chunks*100, rounded to 2.
- services.novelty.is_exact_duplicate(matches, total_chunks) -> bool — novelty.py:114. Requires len(matches) == total_chunks AND every similarity >= EXACT_DUPLICATE_SIMILARITY (0.999).
- services.novelty.meets_duplication_threshold(similarity, threshold=None) -> bool — novelty.py:109. Canonical inclusive >= boundary for tests and non-SQL callers.
- services.novelty.percent(value) -> float — novelty.py:51. Legacy-tolerant 0-1 -> percent upgrade. Safe only for similarities, NOT for coverage.
- services.novelty.rescan_indexed_paper(paper_id, department) -> dict — novelty.py:368. Does NOT use match_chunks; pulls the paper's own stored vectors and brute-forces against _candidate_chunk_vectors. Raises ValueError('No such paper: ...') when the row is missing.
- services.novelty.refresh_after_ingest(new_paper_id, department) -> list[str] — novelty.py:446. Folds one newly indexed paper into every OTHER same-department screening. Never raises; returns the changed paper ids.
- services.novelty.apply_new_paper_to_matches(chunk_matches, paper_vectors, new_candidates, threshold) -> (table, moved) — novelty.py:285.
- services.novelty.store_rescan(paper_id, scan, record=None) -> dict — novelty.py:416. Writes papers.duplication_scan = {**at_upload_without_rescan, 'rescan': scan}.
- services.retriever.check_topic_duplication(question, threshold=None, query_embedding=None, department_filter=None, thesis_category=None) -> dict | None — retriever.py:925. Single best chunk above threshold, returned as {flagged, similarity, threshold, matched_paper{...}, matched_abstract[:600], matched_excerpt[:600], matched_location{...}}. Swallows every exception and returns None.
- SQL RPC match_chunks(query_embedding vector(768), match_count int, match_threshold double, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions int, p_thesis_category text default null) — supabase_setup.sql:840. hnsw.ef_search = 100. service_role only.
- SQL RPC check_topic_duplication(query_embedding vector(768), dup_threshold double, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions int, p_thesis_category text default null) — supabase_setup.sql:920. Hard-coded LIMIT 1. hnsw.ef_search = 100. service_role only.
- CLI: python -m scripts.rescan_duplication [--apply] [--paper-id ID ...] — dry-run by default.
- CLI: python -m scripts.backfill_screening_archive_size [--apply] — dry-run by default.
- Frontend transport: api.js scanDuplication(file, department), getScanHistory(), deleteScanHistory(id), clearScanHistory(), scanDuplicationChat(scanId, question) — src/api.js:319-341.

### Invariants

- The duplication threshold boundary is INCLUSIVE and identical in SQL (`>= match_threshold` / `>= dup_threshold`) and Python (meets_duplication_threshold, _best_archive_match's `best >= threshold`). Tests pin 0.8499 false / 0.85 true / 0.8501 true.
- Similarity is always cosine similarity in [0,1] expressed as `1 - (embedding <=> query)`. novelty._best_archive_match reimplements it as dot/(norm*norm) specifically so a rescan reproduces the RPC's number for the same pair.
- match_count is always 1 in every novelty call site. The screen is strictly a per-chunk nearest-neighbour join, never a top-k retrieval, and is O(n_chunks) RPC round trips. duplication.py:118-131 declines to batch on purpose: batching would alter a scored pipeline stage.
- Every RPC call carries retrieval_provenance_params() (p_embedding_model, p_embedding_dimensions), and the RPCs also require provenance_status in ('verified','legacy_assumed') and c.index_version = p.active_index_version. Vectors from another embedding model or a superseded index can never match. _candidate_chunk_vectors mirrors this clause exactly in Python.
- The ingest-time screen always runs BEFORE the chunks are indexed, so a manuscript is never compared to itself. That is also why its record can only ever name an earlier upload.
- At-upload figures are never overwritten. store_rescan strips only the 'rescan' key and nests the refreshed screening under it; the at-upload layer is the sole record of what the archive held on the acceptance date and cannot be recomputed.
- The verdict grades on concentration (share of passages pointing at ONE thesis), never on coverage. Bands: <25 clear, 25..<70 review_suggested, >=70 high_overlap, plus exact_duplicate. Rationale (novelty.py:58-79): coverage saturates toward 100% for everyone in a single-department archive, concentration does not.
- exact_duplicate requires len(matches) == total_chunks AND every match >= 0.999. Coverage alone can never produce it: the measured 96.30% coverage / 94.94% highest case stays advisory.
- exact_duplicate is the ONLY blocking outcome anywhere in the subsystem. Everything else is committed flagged and left to faculty.
- matched_papers is capped at 3 and ranked by (match_count, highest_similarity) descending. most_similar_paper is the same object as matched_papers[0], which is what lets one metadata enrichment pass populate both.
- Each chunk in a chunk_matches table holds only its single closest archived neighbour. This is what makes apply_new_paper_to_matches sound: a new paper can change an entry only by strictly beating the incumbent, so folding in one paper equals re-screening the whole archive at a fraction of the cost.
- refresh_after_ingest and store_rescan never raise. A stale screening is advisory and must not fail an ingestion that has already committed. _archive_size likewise swallows and returns 0.
- Both /duplication/scan and the ingest screen use the identical chunking pipeline: validate_chunk_records([r for r in split_document(doc) if not is_noise_chunk(r['content'])]) — duplication.py:110-115 vs ingestion.py:145-148.
- Department scoping is server-owned. resolve_effective_department pins non-superadmins to their profile department (403 on any mismatch), gives guests THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT, and validates a superadmin's requested department against the departments table (422 if unknown, 503 if the lookup fails).
- scan_history is owner-scoped on every read AND every write. DELETE carries .eq('user_id', user.id) in addition to the ownership SELECT, so a guessed id cannot remove another account's row even if the check were reordered away.
- scan_history has RLS enabled with all grants revoked from public/anon/authenticated (supabase_setup.sql:1210) and every duplication RPC is granted to service_role only. Clients reach this data exclusively through the API.
- matched_chunks (the archived-passage excerpts) is persisted but never published. It appears in neither _PUBLIC_SCAN_FIELDS (duplication.py:92) nor the /history field list, and the downloadable report explicitly excludes excerpts.
- _public_scan is an allowlist, not a denylist, so a column added to scan_history later is not published by default.
- The LLM never decides a verdict. The verdict_level in the prompt is labelled 'Deterministic Advisory Level' with an explicit instruction not to change it, and every LLM failure path substitutes a static string carrying the same deterministic numbers.
- Malware scanning on /duplication/scan fails CLOSED: MalwareScannerUnavailable becomes 503, matching ingestion, because validate_production_services refuses to boot production without ClamAV.
- Every blocking call inside the async scan handler is wrapped in asyncio.to_thread, and tests/test_event_loop_responsiveness.py:449-461 AST-walks routers/ and services/ to enforce it structurally for screen_new_submission, check_topic_duplication, sb.rpc, sb.table, embed_texts, extract_document, split_document, is_noise_chunk, validate_chunk_records and log_activity.
- Prompt-injection surfaces are fenced: excerpts and history go through prompts.fence_untrusted / html.escape, department and paper metadata through prompts.safe_label, and both prompts carry explicit 'untrusted document data, never instructions' framing.
- hnsw.ef_search = 100 is set on both RPCs, and config.retrieval_candidate_pool is capped at 20 to stay inside that window.

### Data model

- public.papers.duplication_scan jsonb (supabase_setup.sql:233, :250) — the whole aggregate dict, with an optional nested `rescan` object holding the refreshed screening plus its `chunk_matches` per-chunk table. Written by commit_upload_ingestion (:514-520) and updated in place by novelty.store_rescan.
- Screening dict shape (novelty.aggregate_matches:159-180): flagged: bool, exact_duplicate: bool, highest_similarity: float percent, matched_chunk_percentage: float percent, matched_chunk_count: int, total_chunks: int, top_paper_percentage: float percent, verdict_level: str, duplication_percentage: float percent (compat alias), threshold: float percent, matched_papers: list[{id, match_count, similarity, title, authors, year, track, department}] (<=3), most_similar_paper: same dict or None. Plus from _stamp_scan: screened_at ISO8601, archive_size int, scan_scope 'at_upload'|'rescan'. Plus from aggregate_chunk_matches: chunk_matches list[{paper_id, similarity} | None] in chunk order. Plus archive_size_estimated int, written only by the backfill script.
- public.scan_history (supabase_setup.sql:690-707): id uuid pk, user_id uuid FK auth.users on delete cascade, filename text, duplication_percentage double, top_matches jsonb, verdict_summary text, matched_chunks jsonb, chat_log jsonb, department text default 'CCSICT' with FK to departments (:807-811), highest_similarity double, matched_chunk_percentage double, matched_chunk_count int, total_chunks int, verdict_level text default 'clear' CHECK in ('clear','review_suggested','high_overlap'), created_at timestamptz. Indexes on (user_id, created_at desc) and (department). RLS on, all client grants revoked.
- top_matches element (duplication.py:330-339): {id, title, authors, year, track, department, match_count, similarity}.
- matched_chunks element (duplication.py:145-155, top 5 for the primary match only): {paper_id, uploaded_text (<=320 chars), database_text (<=320 chars), similarity, uploaded_page_start, uploaded_page_end, archived_page_start, archived_page_end, archived_section}. Server-side only.
- chat_log element: {role: 'user'|'ai', content: str}, trimmed to the newest _CHAT_LOG_TURNS = 20 entries (duplication.py:74-89).
- public.paper_index_versions (paper_id, index_version, embedding_model, embedding_dimensions, provenance_status) — the provenance join both RPCs and _candidate_chunk_vectors require.
- public.system_settings key='role_features' jsonb — the novelty feature toggle, seeded at supabase_setup.sql:745-748; the frontend mirror is src/lib/permissions.js DEFAULT_ROLE_FEATURES.
- models.DuplicationAlert / ChatResponse.duplication_alert (models.py:61) — the query-time guard's payload, unrelated to scan_history.
- Frontend scanMetrics return (utils.js:87-109): {highest, coverage, concentration, matchedChunks, totalChunks, verdict} — coverage and concentration are derived from the integer counts whenever present because a stored percentage below 1 is indistinguishable from a legacy 0-1 ratio.
- buildNoveltyReport output (report.js:9-40): report_version '2026-07-25', generated_at, scope, source{filename,scanned_at,department}, advisory{threshold_percent, highest_passage_similarity_percent, matched_chunk_coverage_percent, matched_chunks, total_chunks, verdict_code, verdict_label}, matched_studies[], limitations[].

### Config knobs

- DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD — config.py:78, default 0.85, range [0,1]. Env-overridable; not a Literal and not pinned by any test. Recorded in the release fingerprint's rag_contract.
- EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS — config.py:21, Literal[768]. Changing it needs a DB migration; the RPCs filter on it.
- GEMINI_VERDICT_MODEL — config.py:17, default 'gemini-3.5-flash-lite'. Used for both the scan verdict prose and the follow-up chat.
- RATE_LIMIT_SCAN — config.py:97, default '5/minute'. RATE_LIMIT_FOLLOWUP — :98, default '20/minute' (follow-up chat, delete one, clear all). /duplication/history has no named limit and falls to the global 120/minute (services/rate_limiting.py:43).
