- MAX_UPLOAD_MB — config.py:114, default 25 (the frontend hard-codes the same 25 MB at Novelty.jsx:75). MAX_PDF_PAGES — :115, default 500, range [1,2000].
- MALWARE_SCAN_MODE — 'disabled' short-circuits scan_pdf entirely (services/malware.py:54), which is what keeps development and the test suite unchanged; production refuses to boot without ClamAV.
- THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT — config.py:79, default 'CCSICT'. The guest/fallback department for every RAG operation.
- system_settings.role_features['<role>']['novelty'] — the runtime novelty toggle, 60 s cached in auth.get_role_features. Fallback DEFAULT_FEATURES (routers/settings.py:18-21) grants faculty novelty:True and student False — but require_novelty_access never reads that fallback.
- REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA — config.py:113. When on, admin/superadmin calls to every /duplication route need an aal2 token.
- LLM_BASE_URL / LLM_API_KEY / LLM_GATEWAY_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS — route the VERDICT model through an OpenAI-compatible gateway. Embeddings are never routed.
- GEMINI_RESERVE_KEYS, GEMINI_KEY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS, GEMINI_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, GEMINI_MAX_RETRIES, GEMINI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS — bound every verdict call through gemini_pool.
- REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES — config.py:124, default True. Gates the ingestion pipeline only; the /duplication/scan path ignores it.
- Module constants with no env knob: novelty.EXACT_DUPLICATE_SIMILARITY = 0.999 (:48), HIGH_OVERLAP_CONCENTRATION = 70.0 (:80), REVIEW_CONCENTRATION = 25.0 (:81), _TOP_MATCHED_PAPERS = 3 (:37); duplication._CHAT_LOG_TURNS = 20 (:74), _short_excerpt limit 320 (:68), primary_pairs cap 5 (:364), top_pids cap 3 (:317).

### Error handling

- DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError (a PermanentIngestionError) — the only blocking outcome. Raised from ingestion.py:178-183 before any commit, with a public message naming the archived thesis, the total chunk count and the highest similarity. Not retried.
- /duplication/scan: 413 file over max_upload_mb; 415 wrong extension or wrong text MIME; 422 not-a-PDF magic byte, encrypted/password-protected PDF, malformed PDF, zero pages, over max_pdf_pages, or malware detected; 503 malware scanner unavailable (fail closed); 400 extraction failure, empty text, or no clean indexable chunks; 502 embedding count/dimension mismatch; 502 when scan_history insert returns no representation (deliberately loud — a response without `id` made every follow-up post scan_id: undefined and 422).
- /duplication/scan LLM failure: caught at duplication.py:409-416, replaced with a static '### Advisory explanation unavailable' block carrying coverage, highest similarity and the deterministic advisory level. Never 5xx.
- /duplication/scan all-matches-deleted race: logged as a warning and answered with a deterministic-numbers-only advisory (:345-359) rather than indexing an empty list and 500ing.
- /duplication/scan match_chunks RPC failure: NOT handled. Propagates from the asyncio.to_thread at :279 as an unhandled 500.
- /duplication/chat: 404 for a missing or unowned scan; 502 'The AI reviewer is temporarily unavailable' on LLM failure; a guards block returns HTTP 200 with REFUSAL_MESSAGE and the refusal persisted to the transcript.
- DELETE /duplication/history/{id}: a malformed uuid is read as 404 via services.db_errors.is_invalid_identifier (Postgres rejects the cast rather than returning no rows); any other database error re-raises as 500. Pinned by test_duplication_flows.py:297 and :316.
- require_novelty_access: 403 'Faculty or administrator privileges are required for this action.' With REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA on, admin/superadmin calls without an aal2 token get the frozen PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL refusal.
- resolve_effective_department: 403 on a non-superadmin requesting another department, 422 on an unknown department, 503 when the departments lookup or the profile scope read fails.
- novelty.refresh_after_ingest, store_rescan's callers and _archive_size never raise — every failure is a logger.warning and the ingestion proceeds. One unreadable paper does not stop the others (novelty.py:514).
- rescan_indexed_paper raises ValueError('No such paper: {id}') for a missing row; scripts/rescan_duplication.py:75-76 catches per paper and reports '[error] title: reason' without aborting the run.
- retriever.check_topic_duplication returns None on ANY exception, so the chat-time guard fails open with only a logger.exception. It also wraps the RPC in retry_transient.

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_novelty.py (448 lines) — pins the aggregation contract (coverage, ranking by count then similarity, the 3-paper cap, most_similar_paper identity), the inclusive 0.85 boundary, all four verdict tiers with exact band edges (24.999/25/69.999/70), is_exact_duplicate (every chunk required, 0.999 inclusive, 26-of-27 at 0.85-0.95 stays advisory), percent()'s legacy 0-1 upgrade, _best_archive_match (JSON-string and list embeddings, argmax, threshold boundary, zero vector, empty archive), _stamp_scan provenance and the fact that a failed _archive_size never breaks a screening, the incremental refresh algebra (closer paper wins, distant paper ignored, TIE LEAVES THE EARLIER PAPER IN PLACE, a new paper can flag a previously clear chunk, below-threshold changes nothing, incremental == full rescan), the short-table guard, refresh-is-never-a-gate, and the saturation argument (coverage saturates as the archive grows, a real near-duplicate still reads red, growth dilutes a scattered signal, every band from 60 to 80 selects the same three theses).
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_duplication_flows.py (334 lines) — pins the scan endpoint's ingestion-parity hardening (unopenable PDF refused before extraction, over-page-ceiling refused, malware refused and an unavailable scanner fails closed), deterministic metrics on a clear scan, the advisory AI explanation path, survival when every matched paper is deleted, extraction/empty-content failures, the follow-up chat's 404 and guards-block paths, the grounded follow-up write-back, owner-scoped history, delete-one (present / 404 / malformed id / unrelated DB error re-raised), and clear-all.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_durable_ingestion.py:160, :183, :201 — monkeypatches screen_new_submission to pin the flagged-but-committed and exact-duplicate-refused ingestion outcomes.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:440-490 — pins scripts/rescan_duplication.py: dry-run writes nothing, --apply nests under `rescan`, a per-paper exception is reported not fatal.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py:95, :189, :247 — pins the known base-vs-migration signature differences for match_chunks and check_topic_duplication and the full RPC inventory.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_retriever.py:355-379 — pins the RPC name, the alert payload shape, and the None-on-failure contract of check_topic_duplication.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_thesis_category.py:84, :117-119 — pins the conditional p_thesis_category key and the drop-old-signature migration text.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_event_loop_responsiveness.py:449-461 — AST-walks routers/, services/ and main.py and fails if screen_new_submission, check_topic_duplication, sb.rpc/table, embed_texts, extract_document, split_document, is_noise_chunk, validate_chunk_records or log_activity is called from an async def outside an await.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py:678 — monkeypatches check_topic_duplication to pin the chat-side alert.
- rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/utils.test.js (302 lines) — pins scanMetrics (counts preferred over the ambiguous stored percentage, the 0.42-vs-42 legacy case, over-100 clamping, non-object inputs), verdictLabel and verdictTone for all four levels, and the screening-layer helpers (hasRescan, currentScreening, atUploadScreening, isScreeningFlagged, archiveSizeLabel).
- rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/novelty/report.test.js (38 lines) — pins the metadata-only export shape.

### Gotchas

- check_topic_duplication is NOT part of this subsystem despite the name. Neither services/novelty.py nor routers/duplication.py calls it. It lives in services/retriever.py:925 and is reached only from routers/chat.py:1971. The novelty subsystem uses match_chunks with match_count=1. The paper, the README (:273) and docs/FIDELITY_AUDIT_2026-09-02.html (:211) present '85% on new submissions and on queries' as one mechanism; it is three independent implementations with three different aggregation rules.
- Empty-department asymmetry. screen_new_submission passes `p_department: department` straight through, so department='' makes the SQL evaluate `p.department = ''` and match NOTHING (no paper can have '' — the column defaults to CCSICT and has an FK to departments). But _candidate_chunk_vectors (:239) and _archive_size (:212) guard with `if department:` and therefore SKIP the filter, screening against the ENTIRE archive. An upload job with a missing department gets a vacuous at-upload screen and then a cross-department rescan from refresh_after_ingest.
- top_paper_percentage — the figure the verdict is actually graded on — is never persisted for on-demand scans. history_data (duplication.py:418) has no such key and scan_history has no such column, so the frontend re-derives it from top_matches[].match_count / total_chunks (utils.js:73-105). Consequence: in the all-matched-papers-deleted path (duplication.py:345) top_matches is [], the UI's concentration ring reads 0.00%, and the stored verdict_level can still be high_overlap. The ring and the badge contradict each other.
- Two different per-paper ranking implementations. novelty.aggregate_matches ranks by (match_count, highest_similarity) descending; duplication.py:317 sorts by count ONLY, so ties fall back to dict insertion order. The same match set can name a different 'primary match' — and therefore a different LLM narrative and different excerpts — depending on which path produced it.
- The on-demand scan can never report exact_duplicate: duplication.py:304 calls verdict_for_concentration without the flag. Worse, it must not — scan_history.verdict_level carries a CHECK constraint allowing only ('clear','review_suggested','high_overlap') (supabase_setup.sql:705, :723), so passing the flag would make the INSERT fail with a constraint violation. 'exact_duplicate' only ever reaches papers.duplication_scan, which is unconstrained jsonb. verdictLabel/verdictTone handle it, so the UI is ready for a value the write path cannot produce.
- Concentration quantizes uselessly on short manuscripts and there is NO minimum-chunk guard (grepped: none in novelty.py, duplication.py or ingestion.py). total_chunks=2 gives concentration in {0,50,100}, so one matching chunk is review_suggested and two are high_overlap; total_chunks=3 gives {0,33,67,100}. docs/defense/FINAL_DEFENSE_PLAN.md:24 names this exact defect and proposes suppressing the concentration verdict below 5 chunks — that suppression does not exist in the code.
- The verdict bands are outside the release fingerprint. scripts/release_fingerprint.py:51-72 hashes config.py, chat.py, prompts.py, retriever.py and question_types.py but NOT services/novelty.py, and rag_contract (:127-136) records duplication_threshold but not HIGH_OVERLAP_CONCENTRATION (70), REVIEW_CONCENTRATION (25) or EXACT_DUPLICATE_SIMILARITY (0.999). Two runs whose manifests are byte-identical can disagree on every verdict.
- duplication_threshold is Field(default=0.85, ge=0.0, le=1.0) (config.py:78), NOT Literal[0.85], unlike chunk_size_tokens: Literal[800] (:74) and embedding_dimensions: Literal[768] (:21). CLAUDE.md lists 0.85 among the frozen contracts; nothing in the type system or a test prevents DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD=0.5. supabase_setup.sql:742-743 explicitly DELETEs any system_settings row for it, so it is env-only.
- The 85% figure is hard-coded in three frontend places and therefore lies if the env value moves: report.js:19 `normalizePercent(scan?.threshold || 85)` (and `threshold` is not a scan_history column nor in _PUBLIC_SCAN_FIELDS, so the exported report's threshold_percent is ALWAYS the literal 85), the dropzone copy at Novelty.jsx:111, and the clear-scan advisory string at duplication.py:310. Archive.jsx:187/:220 does it correctly, reading screening.threshold from the stored record.
- /duplication/scan skips ingestion's OCR completeness gate. ingestion.py:126-137 fails a job when require_ocr_for_scanned_pages is on and document.unresolved_scanned_pages is non-empty (measured: 94 of 835 pages silently dropped on a host without tesserocr). The scan handler only checks `content.strip()` and never inspects unresolved_scanned_pages, so a scanned proposal on such a host produces a short, clean-looking scan that UNDERSTATES overlap. The docstring at duplication.py:159-174 claims parity with ingestion's checks; it has parity on structure and malware, not on extraction completeness.
- /duplication/history has no rate-limit decorator (only the global 120/minute) and returns user_id in its field list (duplication.py:555) — directly contradicting the _public_scan allowlist introduced at :92-107 to stop publishing user_id. Harmless today (always the caller's own) but the two response shapes for the same rows now differ.
- require_novelty_access (auth.py:419) reads get_role_features() raw and requires `role in features and features[role].get('novelty') is True`. It does NOT go through _feature_enabled, so it never reads DEFAULT_FEATURES. Two effects: (a) a transient system_settings read failure returns {} and locks faculty out entirely — intentional fail-closed, documented at :354-355; (b) if a stored role_features payload omits the faculty key, faculty is denied even though DEFAULT_FEATURES grants novelty:True. Also, if features[role] is a non-dict JSON value the `.get` raises AttributeError and the request 500s instead of 403ing (_validated_features guards the PUT path, but not rows written by hand or by an older schema).
- Tie-breaking is order-dependent and, on the full-rescan path, non-deterministic. apply_new_paper_to_matches:312 keeps the incumbent on an exact tie, so the incremental table depends on ingestion order. rescan_indexed_paper builds candidates by iterating `verified`, a Python SET (novelty.py:253, :259), and _best_archive_match uses strict `>`, so the winner of an exact tie depends on set iteration order and can differ between two runs on identical data. The docstring at :291-293 asserts the incremental path is 'exactly equivalent' to a full rescan; on ties it is not, and the full path is not even stable against itself.
- rescan_indexed_paper pulls EVERY chunk embedding in the department into the API/worker process as Python lists (768 floats each, json.loads per row) and brute-forces a pure-Python dot product with no numpy (_best_archive_match:331-334). Cost is O(paper_chunks x archive_chunks x 768) with per-chunk table SELECTs (one query per candidate paper, novelty.py:260). refresh_after_ingest runs this synchronously inside the worker for every same-department paper with no usable stored table. At a few thousand archived chunks this is hundreds of MB of Python floats and minutes of CPU per paper, post-commit, on the ingestion worker.
- store_rescan does read-modify-write on papers.duplication_scan with no optimistic concurrency (novelty.py:425-430). Two workers folding two new papers into the same third paper will clobber each other's chunk_matches table, and the loser's contribution silently disappears until the next mismatch triggers a full rescan.
- apply_new_paper_to_matches:303 does `if index >= len(updated): break`, silently truncating when the paper now has more chunks than the stored table. Unreachable from refresh_after_ingest (which guards on length equality at :497 and falls back to a full rescan), so it is effectively dead defence exercised only by tests.
- Inconsistent defensiveness inside aggregate_matches: :133 reads similarity with `float(m.get('similarity', 0.0))` but :140 reads `m['similarity']` bare, so a match dict missing the key raises KeyError from the grouping loop rather than defaulting.
- matched_papers[].similarity is `round(highest*100, 2)` with no legacy 0-1 tolerance (novelty.py:152), while the sibling top-level highest_similarity goes through percent(). duplication.py:337 uses percent() for the same field. Three fields named similarity with two different normalization rules.
- duplication_percentage is documented as a 'one-release compatibility alias' for matched_chunk_percentage (novelty.py:171-172) and is still load-bearing: it is a real scan_history column, it is written twice with the same value (duplication.py:421, :423), and routers/analytics.py:253 averages it into avg_duplication_percentage. The analytics tile therefore averages COVERAGE while the flagged_scans count beside it uses the CONCENTRATION verdict — two different quantities under one heading (the comment at analytics.py:284-287 records the previous, worse version of this bug).
- duplication.py stores percentage unrounded (compute_duplication_percentage returns a raw float, :287, written straight to two columns) while aggregate_matches rounds to 2 decimals. The same logical figure has different stored precision depending on which path produced it.
- retriever.check_topic_duplication swallows every exception and returns None (retriever.py:955-957), so the query-time guard fails OPEN and silently. By contrast screen_new_submission has no try/except at all: a transient Supabase error on chunk 400 of 500 propagates out of process_ingestion_job at stage 'screen' and the job goes through the retry ladder. And _match_chunks_against_archive has no wrapper either — an RPC failure there surfaces as an unhandled 500 from /duplication/scan, not one of its declared error codes.
- The scan endpoint never runs services.guards.prohibited_reason on the uploaded document — only the follow-up chat does. The academic-integrity refusal contract applies to questions about a report, not to what can be submitted for screening.
- /duplication/chat re-checks ownership but never re-checks department. A faculty member whose profile department changes retains full access to every scan they ran against their old department, including the archived excerpts that path feeds into the prompt.
- _archive_size returns 0 on any failure (:216-218) and archiveSizeLabel renders nothing for 0 (utils.js:168), so a failed sizing is visually indistinguishable from a pre-2026-09-14 record that never had the field.
- Both RPCs are exposed to post-filter starvation: six equality predicates and the similarity floor run AFTER HNSW has produced at most hnsw.ef_search candidates. With match_count=1 and LIMIT 1 the exposure is small but real — a genuine >0.85 duplicate in another department's slice of the index can crowd out the in-department one during a reindex rollback window. Documented at supabase_setup.sql:861-868 and migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql.
- routers/duplication.py:45-51 constructs a module-level ChatGoogleGenerativeAI at import time, so importing the router requires GEMINI_API_KEY to be set. It is passed as `primary` to gemini_pool, which prepends the gateway when LLM_BASE_URL is set (gemini_pool.py:228) and builds a gateway client with settings.gemini_verdict_model (:109) — so verdict prose IS third-party-routable while embeddings never are.
- Both RPCs are `create or replace function`, so replaying an earlier migration after a later one silently reverts the body (e.g. 20260717_security_scope_evaluation.sql:92 vs 20260819_thesis_category.sql:119 vs 20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:78 all redefine check_topic_duplication). tests/test_schema_consistency.py:95 and :189 pin the known base-vs-migration differences.
- refresh_after_ingest is same-department only (novelty.py:483), as is every screen. Cross-department duplication is invisible in both directions, by design but undocumented on the UI cards.

### Open questions

- docs/defense/FINAL_DEFENSE_PLAN.md:24 and :197 describe a capsule verdict that suppresses the concentration verdict below 5 chunks and calls a 'frozen retriever.check_topic_duplication(chunk_text, ...)' per chunk. Neither exists: there is no minimum-chunk guard anywhere, and check_topic_duplication takes an embedding, not chunk text. Is that plan pending work, or stale?
- Is the three-way split intentional? The ingest screen and /duplication/scan are two separate implementations of the same per-chunk aggregation (novelty.aggregate_matches vs duplication.py:287-304) with different tie-breaks and different persistence, and the chat guard is a third mechanism entirely. Consolidating /duplication/scan onto aggregate_matches would remove the ranking divergence — but it would change a scored pipeline stage, which the frozen-contract policy forbids.
- Should top_paper_percentage be a scan_history column? Today the graded figure is thrown away on write and re-derived client-side, which is why the deleted-paper path shows 0.00% concentration beside a high_overlap badge.
- Should scan_history.verdict_level's CHECK constraint be widened to include 'exact_duplicate'? The frontend already renders that level, and papers.duplication_scan already stores it, but an on-demand scan physically cannot write it.
- Should services/novelty.py be added to scripts/release_fingerprint.py's hashed inputs, or the three band constants added to rag_contract? As it stands the verdict bands can change without moving the fingerprint the paper's reproducibility claim rests on.
- Should duplication_threshold become Literal[0.85] to match the other frozen RAG constants, given CLAUDE.md lists it as frozen and no test pins it?
- refresh_after_ingest's full-rescan fallback is O(archive) pure-Python per paper, synchronous inside the worker after commit. At what corpus size does this stop being acceptable, and should it move to a queued job or numpy/pgvector-side scoring?
- store_rescan has no optimistic concurrency. Is a single ingestion worker a hard deployment invariant, or does this need an if-version guard?
- Should /duplication/scan enforce the OCR completeness gate that ingestion does? Its own docstring claims parity with ingestion's checks, and a silently short scan understates overlap in exactly the direction that matters.
- Should /duplication/history drop user_id and take a rate limit, to match _public_scan and the other three routes on the same router?

**Depends on:** services/retriever.py (the shared service-role Supabase client `sb`, and check_topic_duplication for the chat path), services/index_provenance.py (retrieval_provenance_params, PROVENANCE_STATUS_VERIFIED/LEGACY, current_index_fingerprint), services/embedder.py (embed_texts / embed_text), services/chunker.py (split_document, validate_chunk_records), services/document_processor.py (extract_document, is_noise_chunk), services/malware.py (scan_pdf, MalwareDetected, MalwareScannerUnavailable), services/gemini_pool.py (VERDICT kind, run/arun, gateway + reserve-key chain), services/llm_output.py (coerce_text), services/prompts.py (safe_label, fence_untrusted), services/guards.py (prohibited_reason, REFUSAL_MESSAGE), services/activity.py (log_activity), services/filenames.py (sanitize_filename), services/db_errors.py (is_invalid_identifier), services/rate_limiting.py (limiter), dependencies/auth.py (require_novelty_access, resolve_effective_department, its own `sb`), config.py (settings), Postgres RPCs match_chunks and check_topic_duplication; tables papers, chunks, paper_index_versions, scan_history, profiles, system_settings, departments, activity_log, PyMuPDF (fitz) for the scan-path structural checks, langchain_google_genai.ChatGoogleGenerativeAI (module-level client in routers/duplication.py)

**Used by:** services/ingestion.py — screen_new_submission (:172), the exact-duplicate refusal (:173), refresh_after_ingest (:298), routers/chat.py — check_topic_duplication (:1971) and the duplication_alert path (:2639-2729, persisted at :1610), routers/analytics.py — novelty_scans, avg_duplication_percentage from scan_history.duplication_percentage (:252-257), flagged_scans from verdict_level == 'high_overlap' (:288-290), routers/papers.py — serves papers.duplication_scan in both the normalized (:58) and legacy (:83) archive field lists, scripts/rescan_duplication.py, scripts/backfill_screening_archive_size.py, Frontend: src/pages/Novelty.jsx (scan + follow-up + history), src/pages/Archive.jsx ScreeningDetail (:239-299) and the card badge (:355), src/pages/novelty/report.js (export), src/lib/utils.js helpers, src/lib/permissions.js (canScan mirror), src/context/AuthContext (canScan gate), src/api.js:319-341, evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:269-289 (the dated 2026-09-08 evidence entry for most_similar_paper and the 0.999 band), docs/FIDELITY_AUDIT_2026-09-02.html:211

**Additional notes returned by the reader** (fields outside the schema, preserved verbatim)

- `controlFlowNote`: n/a
- `publicSurfaceNote`: n/a

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 7 correction(s), 6 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "The 85% figure is hard-coded in three frontend places and therefore lies if the env value moves: ... and the clear-scan advisory string at duplication.py:310."
  **Actual:** duplication.py does NOT hard-code 85. The clear-scan advisory interpolates the setting: `f'No chunk reached the {settings.duplication_threshold * 100:.0f}% cosine similarity '` (rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py:311), and the verdict prompt does the same at :379. Both track DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD correctly. duplication.py is also not a frontend file. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py:311, :379`)
- **Wrong:** "duplication_threshold is Field(default=0.85, ge=0.0, le=1.0) ... NOT pinned by any test" / configKnobs: "Env-overridable; not a Literal and not pinned by any test" / openQuestion premised on "no test pins it".
  **Actual:** Two tests assert it. tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:138 pins it inside `test_frozen_rag_contract_is_untouched`, in the same assertion block as chunk_size_tokens 800/100, retrieval_threshold 0.30, retrieval_match_count 5 and embedding_dimensions 768. tests/test_chat_logic.py:408 asserts `settings.duplication_threshold == 0.85 # paper-mandated 85%`. tests/conftest.py:19-44 does not clear DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD, so a developer .env setting it to 0.5 turns both tests red. The type is indeed a Field rather than a Literal, but the enforcement claim is false. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:138, tests/test_chat_logic.py:408, tests/conftest.py:19`)
- **Wrong:** Empty-department asymmetry: "An upload job with a missing department gets a vacuous at-upload screen and then a cross-department rescan from refresh_after_ingest."
  **Actual:** Half wrong. refresh_after_ingest has NO `if department:` guard — it filters unconditionally with `.eq('department', department)` (services/novelty.py:483). With department='' that matches zero rows, so `others` is empty and the function returns [] immediately. There is no cross-department rescan; _candidate_chunk_vectors' `if department:` (:239) is never reached on that path because rescan_indexed_paper is only called from inside that loop. The map also misses that commit_upload_ingestion coerces the empty string: `coalesce(nullif(p_paper ->> 'department', ''), 'CCSICT')` (supabase_setup.sql:522), so the paper row lands in CCSICT regardless. The real artifact is narrower and different: _stamp_scan calls _archive_size, whose `if department:` guard IS at novelty.py:211, so the card is stamped with a count of every ready paper in every department beside a screening that compared against nothing. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/novelty.py:483, :239, :211; rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:522`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: "scan_history is owner-scoped on every read AND every write."
  **Actual:** False for one write. `_persist_turn` updates the transcript with `sb.table('scan_history').update({'chat_log': updated}).eq('id', scan_id).execute()` — id only, no user_id filter (routers/duplication.py:88). It is not exploitable today because the only caller is duplication_chat, which fetched the row with `.eq('user_id', user.id)` first (:476), but it is exactly the belt-and-braces the map praises DELETE for having and the follow-up write lacks. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py:88 vs :476, :598`)
- **Wrong:** "The paper, the README (:273) and docs/FIDELITY_AUDIT_2026-09-02.html (:211) present '85% on new submissions and on queries' as one mechanism."
  **Actual:** The README does the opposite. README.md:31 reads: "**85% cosine similarity duplication threshold** (`DUPLICATION_THRESHOLD=0.85`) — enforced three ways: automatically on every new submission during upload ingestion ..., on demand at scan time (`/duplication/scan`), and at query time (chat duplication guard)." It explicitly enumerates the three paths. README.md:273 is a bullet about the optional `p_thesis_category` RPC parameter, not about the 85% claim. Only the FIDELITY_AUDIT line survives. (`README.md:31, README.md:273`)
- **Wrong:** "retriever.check_topic_duplication swallows every exception and returns None" / "returns None on ANY exception".
  **Actual:** The try/except covers only the embedding call and the RPC (services/retriever.py:938-957). The payload construction at :962-983 sits outside it and reads `match['similarity']` and `match['paper_id']` bare, so a malformed RPC row raises KeyError out of the function rather than returning None. The fail-open characterisation is right for transport failures, wrong as stated. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:938-957 vs :962-983`)
- **Wrong:** Anchor drift (checked, all off by one or two): history_data writes duplication_percentage/matched_chunk_percentage "at :421, :423"; "duplication.py:337 uses percent()"; "_archive_size (:212)" for the department guard; "ingestion.py:126-137" for the OCR gate.
  **Actual:** Actual lines: routers/duplication.py:422 and :424 (both write the raw `percentage`); routers/duplication.py:338 for `'similarity': percent(...)`; services/novelty.py:211 for `if department:` (the function starts at :202, the except at :216); services/ingestion.py:127-137 for the OCR refusal. The substance of each claim holds. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py:422, :424, :338; services/novelty.py:211; services/ingestion.py:127`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- STALE EVIDENCE ARTIFACT the map cites as supporting but never checks: docs/FIDELITY_AUDIT_2026-09-02.html:212 asserts "Two figures reported, coverage verdict clear / review_suggested < 50 / high_overlap >= 50, advisory only (1.3)" with citation `services/novelty.py:31-36` and a green "Matches" chip. The code grades on CONCENTRATION at 25/70 (services/novelty.py:80-81, :92-106), and novelty.py:31-36 is now the module's import block. A dated fidelity audit is asserting a band the code abandoned and pointing at lines that no longer contain it — a larger defense exposure than the 'one mechanism' wording the map does flag on :211.
- MISSING CONSUMER: routers/departments.py:186 counts scan_history rows for a department and refuses the delete with 409 if any exist (`references` dict at :179-198). scan_history is therefore a referential blocker on department lifecycle, not just an analytics source. Absent from the map's usedBy list.
- MISSING AUDIT-TRAIL BUG: the scan handler calls `log_activity(user.id, 'novelty_scan', {...})` with no `department=` kwarg (routers/duplication.py:437-445). services/activity.py:19-22 then falls back to `detail['department']` (absent) and finally to a profiles lookup on the caller. So a superadmin scanning department X writes an activity_log row stamped with the superadmin's OWN profile department, not `effective_department`. Every other department-scoped figure in this subsystem is server-resolved; this one silently is not, and routers/analytics.py filters activity_log by department for non-superadmins.
- FOURTH hard-coded 85 in the frontend, missed by the map's list: src/pages/Novelty.jsx:405 — "Validate proposed topics against the archive at the paper-mandated 85% similarity threshold." The map lists only :111. With duplication.py:311 removed (it is dynamic), the true set of literals is report.js:19, Novelty.jsx:111 and Novelty.jsx:405.
- The map's framing that `_reject_unsafe_scan_upload`'s docstring "claims parity with ingestion's checks" overstates it: the docstring at routers/duplication.py:160-161 scopes itself precisely to "the ingestion pipeline's structural and malware checks". The underlying finding — that /duplication/scan never inspects `document.unresolved_scanned_pages` while ingestion.py:127 refuses on it — is correct and I confirmed no minimum-chunk or OCR-completeness guard exists anywhere in novelty.py, duplication.py or the scan path.
- Cosmetic divergence not noted: routers/duplication.py:294 seeds `{'count': 0, 'highest_similarity': 0}` with an int where services/novelty.py:138 uses 0.0 — another small symptom of the two reimplemented aggregators the map correctly identifies.

## 8. `authz` - Authentication, authorization and abuse controls

The backend holds the Supabase service-role key, so Postgres RLS is bypassed on every query and *all* identity, role, department and feature enforcement happens in Python. `dependencies/auth.py` is that enforcement point: it validates JWTs against Supabase Auth, rejects unapproved profiles, resolves roles through a 60 s in-process cache, and exposes seven guard dependencies plus `resolve_effective_department`, the single department boundary for RAG operations. A second, largely independent layer bounds abuse: an ASGI body-size ceiling that runs before authentication, slowapi rate limits keyed on a verified user id or an IP-bound guest hash, a Cloudflare Turnstile challenge for guest chat, a global UTC-day guest token budget, and a global logging filter that redacts credentials from messages and tracebacks.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py` (451 lines) - Supabase client singleton (`sb`, service-role), JWT validation, approval check, role cache, AAL/MFA check, all seven guards, `resolve_effective_department`
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/settings.py` (80 lines) - `DEFAULT_FEATURES` (the fallback matrix), GET/PUT `/settings/features`, GET `/settings/public`
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/rate_limiting.py` (45 lines) - `rate_limit_key`, `ip_rate_limit_key`, the shared `Limiter` with `default_limits=['120/minute']`
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/request_limits.py` (161 lines) - `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` — pure-ASGI body ceiling that interposes on the receive channel before FastAPI spools multipart to disk
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/turnstile.py` (137 lines) - Cloudflare siteverify, action/hostname context check, in-process verified-guest TTL cache, `ensure_guest_chat_verification`
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/guest_budget.py` (146 lines) - UTC-day token ceiling over the `limits` storage URI; `estimate_charge`, `charge`, `is_exhausted`
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/safe_logging.py` (54 lines) - `redact_log_text`, `PrivacyFilter`, `configure_safe_logging`
- `rag-thesis-backend/config.py` (~270 lines of settings) - `Settings` incl. rate-limit strings, `require_privileged_mfa`, `supabase_jwt_secret`, Turnstile knobs, `guest_daily_token_budget`, and `validate_production_services`
- `rag-thesis-backend/main.py` (~335 lines) - Middleware registration order (SlowAPI, GZip, security headers, BodySizeLimit, CORS), `configure_safe_logging()` call, unauthenticated `/health` `/ready` `/health/worker`
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/permissions.js` (28 lines) - `DEFAULT_ROLE_FEATURES` mirror of `DEFAULT_FEATURES`; `canUseFeature`
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/privilegedMfa.js` (89 lines) - Matches the MFA refusal on the lowercase prefix `multi-factor authentication is required`
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js` (~220 lines read) - Sole transport: Bearer attach, `X-Guest-ID` mint (sessionStorage UUID), one silent refresh on 401, `X-Turnstile-Token` on chat
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_auth_authorization.py` (271 lines) - Pins the MFA string, negative-cache policy, department rules, guard matrix

### Control flow

An authenticated request enters through five middleware layers registered in `main.py:154-200` and applied in reverse: CORS (outermost, so 413s and 429s stay readable to the browser), `BodySizeLimitMiddleware`, the `security_headers` http middleware, GZip, then `SlowAPIMiddleware` (innermost), then the router. `BodySizeLimitMiddleware.__call__` (services/request_limits.py:120) only engages for POST/PUT/PATCH; it computes `limit_for_path` (batch paths get `max_batch_files * max_upload_mb MB + 8 MB`, everything else `max_upload_mb MB + 2 MB`, i.e. 27 MB by default), refuses a declared `Content-Length` over that without reading a byte, and otherwise wraps `receive` so an undeclared/lying chunked body raises `BodyTooLarge` at the ceiling. This deliberately sits *outside* authentication because FastAPI runs `await request.form()` before `solve_dependencies`, spooling every part past 1 MB to container disk — an unauthenticated caller could previously fill the disk against `/upload/paper` and take a 401 afterwards. `SlowAPIMiddleware.dispatch` then applies the global `120/minute` default, but only to handlers with **no** `@limiter.limit` decorator (`_should_exempt` returns True for any name in `_route_limits`); decorated routes are left entirely to their decorators.

Routing resolves dependencies next. `get_current_user` (dependencies/auth.py:263) calls `sb.auth.get_user(token)` — a real network round trip to Supabase Auth on every request, no cache — then `_ensure_approved_account` (:141), a second round trip selecting `profiles.status`, which 403s anything not `'approved'` and 503s on a lookup error. Guards layered on top add `get_user_role` (:50): a lock-guarded read of `_ROLE_CACHE` keyed by user id, else a `profiles.select('role,status')` read. A non-`approved` status or an unrecognised role string coerces to `student` (:59-61); a *lookup exception* returns `student` and is deliberately **not** cached (:62-68), so one transient Supabase failure cannot pin an admin to `student` for the full 60 s TTL. Successful results are written under the lock after `_prune_role_cache` (:36) drops expired keys and then oldest-insertion-first while `len >= 2048`. `resolve_effective_department` (:105) is a *third* profile read via the uncached `get_user_scope` (:76); a guest short-circuits to `settings.thesis_evaluation_department`, a non-superadmin is pinned to the profile department and gets 403 if `requested` differs, and a superadmin's `requested` is validated against `departments` (422 unknown, 503 on failure). A superadmin chat turn therefore costs `auth.get_user` + status read + role read (cached) + scope read + departments read; `routers/chat.py:2026-2032` resolves the department once in the route wrapper and threads it into `_chat_impl` as `resolved_department` specifically to avoid paying twice.

MFA is checked after the role check, never before. `_require_privileged_mfa` (:229) is a no-op unless `settings.require_privileged_mfa`; otherwise it demands `_token_aal(credentials, user.id) == 'aal2'`. `_token_aal` (:158) reads the unverified JOSE header to pick a branch: with a configured `supabase_jwt_secret` **and** `alg == HS256` it verifies the signature locally with `audience='authenticated'`; otherwise, only when an `expected_user_id` is supplied (which means `get_current_user` already had Supabase validate this exact token), it decodes with `verify_signature=False` and discards the result unless `claims['sub'] == expected_user_id`. Any `InvalidTokenError`/`DecodeError`, a non-string credential, or no `expected_user_id` degrades to `'aal1'`, i.e. fails closed. Because `require_admin`/`require_superadmin`/`require_novelty_access`/`require_upload_access` all declare `credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials = Security(security)` using the *same* module-level `HTTPBearer` instance as `get_current_user`, FastAPI's per-request dependency cache guarantees the bytes `_token_aal` decodes are the bytes Supabase validated. The 403 body is exactly `PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL`; `src/api.js:148` funnels every response through `isPrivilegedMfaRefusal` (403 + lowercase substring `multi-factor authentication is required`) and fires `reportPrivilegedMfaRequired()` so the shell can offer TOTP enrolment instead of a Retry button.

The feature matrix has two *different* enforcement paths. `require_chat_access`/`require_archive_access` go through `_require_feature` → `_feature_enabled` (:350), which returns admin/superadmin early, prefers an explicitly stored boolean from `system_settings.role_features`, and otherwise falls back to `routers.settings.DEFAULT_FEATURES` — so a transient `system_settings` outage cannot take chat or the archive offline. `require_novelty_access` (:419) and `require_upload_access` (:436) do **not** use that helper: they inline `role in features and features[role].get(...) is True` against the raw `get_role_features()` result, with no `DEFAULT_FEATURES` fallback and no isinstance guard. `get_role_features` (:334) caches for 60 s, returns `{}` on both an exception *and* a missing row without caching either, and is invalidated only by `PUT /settings/features` (routers/settings.py:78).

Guest chat is a separate stack. The browser mints a `crypto.randomUUID()` per browser session into `sessionStorage['iskai_guest_rate_id']` (`src/api.js:25-39`) and sends it as `X-Guest-ID` only when there is no Supabase session. On the server, `rate_limit_key` (services/rate_limiting.py:12) prefers `user:<sub>` but **only** when `supabase_jwt_secret` is configured and the token verifies as HS256 with `aud=authenticated`; a forged, wrong-audience or unparseable token silently falls back rather than minting a fresh bucket. A well-formed guest id (16–128 chars, `[A-Za-z0-9\-_]`) is hashed together with the client IP into `guest:<sha256(...)[:32]>`, so rotating ids still moves within one IP's namespace; `ip_rate_limit_key` is the rotation-proof ceiling stacked on `/chat` at 300/minute. Because slowapi's `limit()` decorator wraps the *endpoint function*, both `/chat` limits evaluate after dependency resolution (so the Supabase auth round trips are already spent) but before the body of `chat()`, and both fire from a single `_check_request_limit` call — the outer wrapper sets `request.state._rate_limiting_complete = True` and the inner one skips, while `__evaluate_limits` walks all limits registered under `routers.chat.chat`, each with its own `key_func`. `ensure_guest_chat_verification` (services/turnstile.py:111) then runs first in the route body: authenticated users and a blank `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` short-circuit; a guest with a fresh entry in the in-process `_verified_guests` cache passes; otherwise a missing `X-Guest-ID` **or** missing `X-Turnstile-Token` is 403 + `X-Guest-Verification: required`, and a token Cloudflare rejects — or one whose echoed `action` != `turnstile_expected_action` (`guest_chat`, matched by `src/pages/Chat.jsx:208`) or whose `hostname` is outside `TURNSTILE_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES` when that list is non-empty — is 403 + `failed`. Network/JSON errors from siteverify return False, i.e. fail closed.

Finally the guest token budget. `routers/chat.py:2218` calls `guest_budget.is_exhausted()` before any embedding or model call and returns a `kind='notice'` HTTP 200 carrying `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`. `charge()` (services/guest_budget.py:130) does an atomic `incr` on `guest-token-budget:YYYY-MM-DD` in a `limits` storage built from the same `RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI`, expiring just past UTC midnight (+60 s slack), and reports `allowed = spent <= budget`. The charge is booked *before* generation and is a deliberate over-estimate: measured prompt tokens (via the chunker's `cl100k_base` proxy) plus `gemini_max_output_tokens`. A refused request still increments, because check-then-increment would reintroduce the race the atomic op exists to avoid. `_charge_guest_extra` (routers/chat.py:1378) books the turn's *additional* generation calls — follow-up rewrite (:2531), query translation (:2578), duplication summary (:2717), citation repair (:2783) and coverage repair (:2843) — but only the pre-generation ones can still refuse; post-hoc repairs book and proceed so the day's counter stays honest for the next caller. Both `spent_today` and `charge` fail **open** on storage errors. `guest_daily_token_budget == 0` means unlimited, and `config.validate_production_services` is what forbids that (along with `memory://` storage, `require_privileged_mfa=False`, and a non-ClamAV scan mode) under `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production`.

Logging is redacted globally by `configure_safe_logging()` (main.py:39, workers/ingestion_worker.py:349), which installs `PrivacyFilter` on the root logger *and* on each of the root's handlers. The handler-level copy is the one that matters: Python calls only the emitting logger's own filters, so a record from `routers.chat` reaches root's *handlers* without ever passing root's logger filters. The filter renders `record.getMessage()` once, replaces `record.msg` with the redacted text, blanks `record.args` so no `%`-formatting re-runs, and pre-renders `exc_info` into a redacted `exc_text` so `logger.exception()` tracebacks (including frame text) go through the same four regexes.

### Public surface

- dependencies/auth.py:14 `sb = create_client(settings.supabase_url, settings.supabase_key)` — module-level service-role client imported by every router, `services/activity.py`, `main._verify_database_contract` and the operations monitor. Monkeypatched wholesale in tests.
- dependencies/auth.py:15-16 `security = HTTPBearer()` / `optional_security = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)`. FastAPI 0.127.0 raises **401** `Not authenticated` on a missing/non-bearer header (older FastAPI raised 403).
- dependencies/auth.py:18-21 `ROLE_STUDENT='student'`, `ROLE_FACULTY='faculty'`, `ROLE_ADMIN='admin'`, `ROLE_SUPERADMIN='superadmin'` — the only accepted role vocabulary; anything else coerces to student.
- dependencies/auth.py:50 `get_user_role(user_id) -> str` — cached 60 s, capped at 2048 entries, lock-guarded.
- dependencies/auth.py:76 `get_user_scope(user_id) -> {'role','department'}` — **uncached**; 403 when the profile is missing or not approved, 503 on lookup failure.
- dependencies/auth.py:105 `resolve_effective_department(user, requested=None) -> str` — the department boundary; guest → `settings.thesis_evaluation_department`.
- dependencies/auth.py:132 `invalidate_role_cache(user_id=None)` — called from `routers/analytics.py:353,405,461` after role/detail/delete writes.
- dependencies/auth.py:141 `_ensure_approved_account(user_id)` — 403 `'This account is pending approval or has been rejected.'`, 503 `'Account validation is temporarily unavailable.'`.
- dependencies/auth.py:158 `_token_aal(credentials, expected_user_id=None) -> 'aal1'|'aal2'`.
- dependencies/auth.py:224 `PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL = 'Multi-factor authentication is required for privileged access.'` (exact, trailing period included).
- dependencies/auth.py:229 `_require_privileged_mfa(credentials, expected_user_id)`.
- dependencies/auth.py:243 `get_optional_user(credentials=Security(optional_security))` — returns `None` with no credentials; a *supplied but invalid* token is 401, never downgraded to guest.
- dependencies/auth.py:263 `get_current_user(credentials=Security(security))` — 401 `'Invalid or expired authentication token'` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`.
- dependencies/auth.py:282 `require_admin` — admin|superadmin, then MFA. Refusal: `'Administrator privileges are required for this action.'`
- dependencies/auth.py:296 `require_faculty_or_admin` — faculty|admin|superadmin; MFA only for admin/superadmin. **Wired to zero routes** (dead outside tests).
- dependencies/auth.py:311 `require_superadmin` — superadmin only, then MFA. Refusal: `'Superadmin privileges are required for this action.'`
- dependencies/auth.py:329 `invalidate_features_cache()` / :334 `get_role_features() -> dict` (60 s cache, no lock).
- dependencies/auth.py:350 `_feature_enabled(role, feature) -> bool` — reads through to `routers.settings.DEFAULT_FEATURES` (function-level import to break the cycle).
- dependencies/auth.py:389 `_require_feature(user, feature, refusal)` — admin/superadmin always pass, **no MFA** by deliberate design.
- dependencies/auth.py:398 `require_chat_access` (optional auth; guest → None) refusal `'Research chat is not enabled for your role.'` — used only by `routers/chat.py:75`.
- dependencies/auth.py:412 `require_archive_access` refusal `'Archive access is not enabled for your role.'` — used only by `routers/papers.py:26`.
- dependencies/auth.py:419 `require_novelty_access` — admin/superadmin + MFA, else `features[role]['novelty'] is True`. Refusal reuses the *faculty* wording: `'Faculty or administrator privileges are required for this action.'`
- dependencies/auth.py:436 `require_upload_access` — admin/superadmin + MFA, else `features[role]['upload'] is True`. Refusal: `'Administrator privileges are required for this action.'`
- routers/settings.py:18-21 `DEFAULT_FEATURES = {'student': {chat:T, archive:T, novelty:F, upload:F}, 'faculty': {chat:T, archive:T, novelty:T, upload:F}}` — only two roles; admin/superadmin are never in the matrix.
- routers/settings.py:22-23 `_FEATURE_ROLES={'student','faculty'}`, `_FEATURE_NAMES={'chat','archive','novelty','upload'}`.
- routers/settings.py:39 `GET /settings/public` — unauthenticated, no explicit limit, returns `{'evaluation_department': ...}`.
- routers/settings.py:45 `GET /settings/features` — any approved user; read-only, returns `DEFAULT_FEATURES` when the row is absent (it used to INSERT, which raced).
- routers/settings.py:63 `PUT /settings/features` — superadmin; `_validated_features` requires exactly `{student,faculty}` × exactly the four boolean feature names, else 422; then `invalidate_features_cache()` + `log_activity(..., 'settings_update', ...)`.
- services/rate_limiting.py:12 `rate_limit_key(request) -> 'user:<sub>' | 'guest:<sha256(ip:guest_id)[:32]>' | 'ip:<addr>'`.
- services/rate_limiting.py:36 `ip_rate_limit_key(request) -> 'ip:<addr>'`.
- services/rate_limiting.py:41 `limiter = Limiter(key_func=rate_limit_key, default_limits=['120/minute'], storage_uri=settings.rate_limit_storage_uri)`.
- services/request_limits.py:59 `limit_for_path(path) -> int`; :95 `BodySizeLimitMiddleware`; :55 `BodyTooLarge`.
- services/turnstile.py:27-30 `VERIFY_URL`, `VERIFICATION_HEADER='X-Guest-Verification'`, `REQUIRED_MESSAGE`, `FAILED_MESSAGE`; :36 `guard_enabled()`; :47 `is_guest_verified`; :54 `mark_guest_verified`; :61 `reset_guest_verifications()` (test hook); :93 `verify_turnstile_token`; :111 `ensure_guest_chat_verification(request, user)`.
- services/guest_budget.py:49 `KEY_PREFIX='guest-token-budget'`; :51 `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`; :57 `BudgetDecision(allowed, charged, spent, budget)`; :66 `ALLOWED_UNLIMITED`; :95 `is_enabled()`; :99 `estimate_charge(*texts)`; :109 `spent_today()`; :123 `is_exhausted()`; :130 `charge(tokens)`.
- services/safe_logging.py:15 `redact_log_text(value) -> str`; :24 `PrivacyFilter`; :45 `configure_safe_logging()`.
- Route-level limits actually declared: `routers/chat.py:2008-2009` (`300/minute` IP + `30/minute` key), `routers/upload.py:884,1000,1216,1456,1487` (`10/minute`), `routers/upload.py:1275` (`30/minute` public), `routers/duplication.py:211` (`5/minute`), `routers/duplication.py:464,577,604` (`20/minute`), `routers/analytics.py:137`, `routers/catalog.py:182,188`, `routers/departments.py:61` (`30/minute` public). Everything else gets the global `120/minute`.

### Invariants

- RLS is bypassed: `sb` is built from the service-role key (dependencies/auth.py:14), so *every* role and department check must exist in Python. There is no database-side backstop for these routes.
- The refusal string `'Multi-factor authentication is required for privileged access.'` (dependencies/auth.py:224) is a cross-repo contract. Pinned literally by tests/test_auth_authorization.py:200-202, asserted identical across all five privileged guards at :210-223, matched as a lowercase prefix by rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/privilegedMfa.js:16. Rewording it strands the frontend's TOTP recovery path.
- A role-lookup *failure* must never be cached (dependencies/auth.py:62-68, pinned by tests/test_auth_authorization.py:63-71). Caching the `student` fallback would 403 an admin for the full 60 s with no way to clear it.
- `_token_aal` must never return `'aal2'` from an unverified decode unless `expected_user_id` is supplied and `claims['sub']` matches it (dependencies/auth.py:186-212). The unverified branch is safe only because `get_current_user` already had Supabase validate the identical token in the same request, via the same cached `Security(security)` dependency.
- `get_optional_user` must 401 a supplied-but-invalid token rather than degrade to guest (dependencies/auth.py:256-260). Silently becoming a guest would let an expired admin token quietly read the guest-scoped evaluation department.
- `resolve_effective_department` is the only department boundary for RAG: non-superadmin `requested != assigned` is 403, guest is always `settings.thesis_evaluation_department`, superadmin `requested` must exist in `departments`.
- `rate_limit_key` must fall back to IP rather than trust an unverified `sub` (services/rate_limiting.py:17-27, pinned by tests/test_rate_limit.py:40-57). Trusting it would let a forged token mint an unlimited supply of fresh buckets.
- The guest-id hash is IP-bound (`sha256(f'{address}:{guest_id}')`), and `/chat` always stacks `ip_rate_limit_key` at 300/min on top, so rotating `X-Guest-ID` cannot escape a per-IP ceiling.
- `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` must stay registered *after* the header middleware and *before* CORS (main.py:180) so it ends up outside every body-touching layer and inside CORS. Moving it inside SlowAPI or outside CORS breaks either the disk-spool protection or the browser's ability to read the 413.
- Turnstile and the guest budget both fail in their documented directions: Turnstile fails **closed** (any siteverify error returns False, services/turnstile.py:103-105), the budget fails **open** (storage errors return 0 / ALLOWED_UNLIMITED, services/guest_budget.py:117-120,141-143).
- `charge()` increments before deciding; a refused request still consumes budget (documented at services/guest_budget.py:26-29). `is_exhausted` uses `>=` while `charge` allows `spent <= budget`, so landing exactly on the budget is allowed and the *next* request is refused (tests/test_guest_budget.py:85-87).
- `_feature_enabled` must read through to `DEFAULT_FEATURES` for `chat` and `archive` (dependencies/auth.py:350-372). Failing those closed on a `system_settings` outage would take the library offline for every student and faculty member.
- `DEFAULT_FEATURES` (routers/settings.py:18-21), `DEFAULT_ROLE_FEATURES` (src/lib/permissions.js:10-13) and the seed row in `supabase_setup.sql:746` are three copies of the same matrix and must move together.
- `PrivacyFilter` must be installed on root *handlers*, not just the root logger — child loggers bypass ancestor logger filters entirely. `configure_safe_logging` does both (services/safe_logging.py:48-52).
- `config.validate_production_services` refuses to construct `Settings` under `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production` without Redis storage, `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA=true`, ClamAV, and `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET > 0` (config.py:246-253).
- `_validated_features` accepts exactly `{student, faculty}` × exactly `{chat, archive, novelty, upload}` all-boolean (routers/settings.py:26-36); admin and superadmin can never be given a matrix entry.

### Data model

- `profiles(id uuid pk, role text, department text, status text, full_name, email)` — the authorization source of truth. `role` ∈ {student, faculty, admin, superadmin}; anything else coerces to student. `status` must equal `'approved'` or the account is 403'd at the boundary.
- `system_settings(key text pk, value jsonb, description text)` — row `key='role_features'`, seeded at supabase_setup.sql:746 with exactly the `DEFAULT_FEATURES` payload. Shape: `{role: {chat,archive,novelty,upload: bool}}` for `student` and `faculty` only.
- `departments(id, name, code, created_at, ...)` — `name` is the department key compared against `profiles.department`; `resolve_effective_department` validates a superadmin's requested name against it.
- `activity_log(user_id nullable, action, department nullable, detail jsonb)` — written by `services/activity.log_activity`; guest rows carry `user_id = NULL`.
- In-process `_ROLE_CACHE: dict[str, tuple[str, float]]` — user id → (role, monotonic expiry). TTL 60 s, max 2048, insertion-ordered eviction.
- In-process `_FEATURES_CACHE: {'features': dict, 'expires_at': float}` — single slot, 60 s.
- In-process `turnstile._verified_guests: dict[str, float]` — guest id → monotonic expiry, TTL `turnstile_guest_ttl_seconds` (default 3600).
- Shared counter `guest-token-budget:YYYY-MM-DD` in the `limits` storage named by `RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI`, TTL `max(60, seconds_to_utc_midnight + 60)`.
- slowapi buckets: `LIMITER/<key>/<module>.<function>/<limit>` in the same storage URI (a separate client instance from the guest budget's `lru_cache`d one).
- `BudgetDecision = NamedTuple(allowed: bool, charged: int, spent: int, budget: int)`.
- Frontend `AuthContext` derives `{canChat, canArchive, canScan, canUpload}` from `canUseFeature(role, features, …)` (src/context/AuthContext.jsx:262-265); `features` stays `null` on fetch failure so `DEFAULT_ROLE_FEATURES` applies.
- Browser `sessionStorage['iskai_guest_rate_id']` — a `crypto.randomUUID()` per browser session, sent as `X-Guest-ID`.

### Config knobs

- `APP_ENVIRONMENT` (development|test|production) — production turns on the `validate_production_services` gate at config.py:246-253.
- `SUPABASE_URL` / `SUPABASE_KEY` — the service-role key; RLS is bypassed with it.
- `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` — optional legacy HS256 secret. Enables per-user rate-limit buckets *and* local signature verification in `_token_aal`. Empty by default.
- `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` (default false; mandatory in production) — arms the `aal2` check in every privileged guard.
- `THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT` (default `CCSICT`) — the guest department and the scope of `/settings/public`, `/analytics/summary`, `/upload/tracks`.
- `RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI` (default `memory://`; must be Redis in production) — backs both slowapi and the guest token budget.
- `RATE_LIMIT_CHAT` 30/minute, `RATE_LIMIT_CHAT_IP` 300/minute, `RATE_LIMIT_UPLOAD` 10/minute, `RATE_LIMIT_SCAN` 5/minute, `RATE_LIMIT_FOLLOWUP` 20/minute, `RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` 30/minute, plus the hard-coded `default_limits=['120/minute']` in services/rate_limiting.py:43.
- `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET` (default 0 = unlimited; must be > 0 in production).
- `GEMINI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` — the per-call over-estimate added by `estimate_charge`.
- `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` (empty = guard off), `TURNSTILE_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (5.0), `TURNSTILE_GUEST_TTL_SECONDS` (3600), `TURNSTILE_EXPECTED_ACTION` (`guest_chat`; empty disables the action check), `TURNSTILE_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES` (empty disables the hostname check — the default, so localhost works).
- `MAX_UPLOAD_MB` (25) and `MAX_BATCH_FILES` (20) — feed `limit_for_path`; the effective ceilings are 27 MB single / 508 MB batch.
- `CORS_ORIGINS` — explicit allow-list with `allow_credentials=True`; `allow_headers` must list `X-Guest-ID`, `Idempotency-Key`, `X-Turnstile-Token` and `expose_headers` must list `X-Guest-Verification` (main.py:198-202).
- Frontend: `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` gates `turnstileEnabled` (src/components/security/turnstileConfig.js:11) — it must be set whenever the backend secret is, or guests get a 403 with no widget to solve.

### Error handling

- 401 `'Invalid or expired authentication token'` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` — `get_current_user` / `get_optional_user` on any Supabase validation failure (dependencies/auth.py:256, 275). `src/api.js:122-140` performs exactly one silent `refreshSession()` + replay before signing out and redirecting to `loginPathWithNext`.
- 401 `'Not authenticated'` — FastAPI's `HTTPBearer` for a missing or non-Bearer header on a required-auth route (fastapi 0.127.0 returns 401, not the legacy 403).
- 403 `'This account is pending approval or has been rejected.'` — `_ensure_approved_account`, on every authenticated request.
- 403 `'Multi-factor authentication is required for privileged access.'` — the only 403 the browser can act on; triggers `reportPrivilegedMfaRequired()` and the TOTP enrolment gate.
- 403 role refusals, worded per guard: `'Administrator privileges are required for this action.'` (require_admin, require_upload_access), `'Superadmin privileges are required for this action.'`, `'Faculty or administrator privileges are required for this action.'` (require_faculty_or_admin **and** require_novelty_access), `'Research chat is not enabled for your role.'`, `'Archive access is not enabled for your role.'`
- 403 `'You can only search your assigned department.'` — non-superadmin department mismatch in `resolve_effective_department`.
- 403 + `X-Guest-Verification: required|failed` — Turnstile. The header is in the CORS `expose_headers` list (main.py:200) so the browser can read it and re-render the widget.
- 422 `'Unknown department.'` — superadmin requested a department not in `departments`. 422 from `_validated_features` for a malformed feature payload.
- 413 `{'detail': 'Request body exceeds the N MB limit'}` — `BodySizeLimitMiddleware`, sent directly from the middleware without touching the app. A chunked overrun degrades to FastAPI's 400 `There was an error parsing the body`.
- 429 via slowapi's `_rate_limit_exceeded_handler` (main.py:154), with `Retry-After` and `X-RateLimit-*` headers injected by `_inject_headers`.
- 503 `'Account validation is temporarily unavailable.'` / `'User department validation is unavailable.'` / `'Department validation is unavailable.'` — Supabase read failures in the auth path. `src/api.js:16-17` deliberately does *not* retry 503 (only 502/504, GET only, twice).
- HTTP 200 + `kind='notice'` carrying `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE` — the guest budget never raises; it returns a normal `ChatResponse` so the transcript renders it like a system notice. Same shape as the capacity cooldown.
- Swallowed-and-logged: `get_user_role` lookup failures (→ student, not cached), `get_role_features` failures (→ `{}`), `spent_today`/`charge` storage failures (→ fail open), `verify_turnstile_token` HTTP/JSON failures (→ fail closed), `log_activity` insert failures (never breaks the request).

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_auth_authorization.py — pins the exact MFA string, that all five privileged guards use it, that a role refusal does *not*, the negative-cache policy, `_token_aal`'s four branches (valid HS256, forged HS256, no secret, asymmetric + sub match), guest/ordinary/superadmin department rules with 403/422/503, and the feature-guard matrix.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_rate_limit.py — `rate_limit_key` for verified token, forged signature, wrong audience, garbage token, anonymous, and that guest ids are IP-bound while `ip_rate_limit_key` cannot be rotated.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_guest_turnstile.py — guard off by default, authenticated never challenged, missing token/guest-id 403 with the `required` header, rejected token with the `failed` header and no cache entry, TTL expiry, fail-closed on network error.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_guest_budget.py — shipped default is 0/unlimited and touches no storage, accumulation across charges, exact-budget landing allowed then exhausted, per-UTC-day keys, storage failure behaviour.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_request_limits.py — declared over-ceiling refused unread, within-ceiling passes, unparseable length falls through to the streaming guard, undeclared body stopped at the ceiling, GET never intercepted, non-http scope passthrough, batch vs single ceilings, extraction endpoints bounded.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_operations_security.py:189-218 — `redact_log_text` on the three canonical shapes, `PrivacyFilter` blanking `record.args`, and traceback redaction via `exc_text` while preserving `RuntimeError`/`Traceback`.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:151-198 — role-cache prune on write, the 2048 ceiling, unexpired survival; `GET /settings/features` returning defaults without writing and a present row verbatim; :289-295 that each public endpoint declares a limit stricter than the global default.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py:19-44 — forces `APP_ENVIRONMENT=test`, `memory://`, `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA=false`, `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY=''`, no gateway, no reserve keys, so none of these controls inherit a developer's `.env`.

### Gotchas

- **`require_novelty_access` and `require_upload_access` bypass `DEFAULT_FEATURES` entirely** (dependencies/auth.py:428-430, 445-447). They read `get_role_features()` raw. Because `get_role_features` returns `{}` when the `system_settings` row is missing *or* unreachable, a faculty member is 403'd from novelty screening during any transient outage — even though `DEFAULT_FEATURES['faculty']['novelty'] is True`, `GET /settings/features` serves that default to the browser, and `canUseFeature` shows the nav item. The frontend says yes, the API says no. `_feature_enabled` exists and would fix this in one line; the two guards predate it and were never migrated.
- **Same two guards have no type guard on the stored value.** `features[role].get('novelty')` raises `AttributeError` → unhandled 500 if the `role_features` jsonb holds a non-object for a role. `_feature_enabled` checks `isinstance(configured, dict)`; these do not. `PUT /settings/features` validates, but the row is also seeded by SQL and editable directly in Supabase.
- **`require_faculty_or_admin` is dead code.** Defined at dependencies/auth.py:296, exercised at tests/test_auth_authorization.py:213 and :239, referenced by no router. It also calls `get_user_role` twice (:301, :306).
- **Decorated routes are rate-limited *after* authentication.** slowapi's `limit()` wraps the endpoint function (.venv/.../slowapi/extension.py:723-736), and FastAPI resolves dependencies before calling it. So `POST /chat` at 30/min still pays `sb.auth.get_user` + the `profiles.status` read on every request, including the ones it is about to 429. Undecorated routes are the opposite: `SlowAPIMiddleware` checks the 120/min default before routing. `_should_exempt` (.venv/.../slowapi/middleware.py:110) also means a decorated route is *excluded* from the global default — `/chat` is bounded only by 30/min + 300/min, never by 120/min.
- **With the shipped default `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET=''`, every authenticated user shares one bucket per IP.** `rate_limit_key` falls straight to `ip:<addr>` (services/rate_limiting.py:28-33), and `src/api.js:79-87` sends `X-Guest-ID` *only* when unauthenticated, so there is no per-user discriminator at all. A whole campus behind one NAT then shares 30 chat/min, 10 upload/min and 5 scan/min. config.py:181-186 acknowledges the trade-off but the default leaves it unmitigated.
- **The role cache, the feature cache and the Turnstile verified-guest cache are all in-process.** `invalidate_role_cache` after a role change (routers/analytics.py:353,405,461) and `invalidate_features_cache` after a settings write only clear the replica that served the write; other replicas serve stale authorization for up to 60 s. services/turnstile.py:11-13 states the multi-replica limitation for the guest cache but nothing enforces single-replica deployment.
- **`_FEATURES_CACHE` has no lock** (dependencies/auth.py:324-327) where `_ROLE_CACHE` has `_role_cache_lock`. Benign under the GIL but an inconsistency. `get_role_features` also returns a *shallow copy* on a cache hit (:338) and the *raw cached object* on a fresh fetch (:345), so the miss path hands callers a mutable reference to cached state.
- **A missing `role_features` row is never negatively cached.** `get_role_features` only writes the cache inside `if res.data:` (:341-344), so an absent row (or a failing read) means one `system_settings` round trip per `_feature_enabled` call — i.e. per `GET /papers` and per `POST /chat`.
- **`get_user_scope` is not cached at all** while `get_user_role` is. A superadmin chat turn does five auth-related round trips; a student four.
- **Four unreconciled implementations of 'what role/department is this caller'.** `get_user_role`, `get_user_scope`/`resolve_effective_department`, `analytics._admin_scope` (routers/analytics.py:41), and inline `sb.table('profiles').select('role,department')` reads in `papers.list_papers:51`, `papers.delete_paper:113`, `analytics.update_user_role:333`, `delete_user:373`, `update_user_details:419`. Two of them hard-code the department fallback as the literal `'CCSICT'` (papers.py:55, analytics.py:320) instead of `settings.thesis_evaluation_department`.
- **`GET /papers` silently coerces the department instead of refusing it.** papers.py:54-55 overwrites a non-superadmin's `?department=` with their own; `resolve_effective_department` would 403. A superadmin's `?department=` is never validated against `departments`, so a typo returns an empty archive rather than 422.
- **The `status != 'approved' → student` branch in `get_user_role` (:59) is effectively unreachable from HTTP.** `_ensure_approved_account` already 403'd such a caller inside `get_current_user`. Same for the 403 in `get_user_scope:82-86`. Defensive, but it means the approval check costs two extra reads that can only disagree in a race.
- **Admin and superadmin are outside the feature matrix by construction**, and `_require_feature` returns early for them (:391-392) *without* an MFA check — deliberate (dependencies/auth.py:384-388), so enabling `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` does not lock an admin out of chat or the archive. But it also means an admin at `aal1` reads the whole archive while being refused `/upload` and `/duplication/scan`.
- **`/sessions` is not gated by `chat`.** Every session endpoint uses bare `get_current_user` (routers/sessions.py:15), so a role with `chat` turned off can still create, rename, list and delete chat sessions.
- **Turnstile caches the raw client-supplied guest id with no format validation and no size cap.** `services/turnstile.py:119` strips and uses whatever arrives; `_verified_guests` is pruned by expiry only (:41-45), unlike `_ROLE_CACHE` which is explicitly capped at 2048. `rate_limit_key` *does* validate the id (16–128 chars) — the two disagree, so a 3-char id gets a Turnstile cache slot but falls back to the bare `ip:` rate-limit bucket.
- **Uvicorn's access log is not redacted.** `configure_safe_logging` only touches the root logger and the handlers present at call time; uvicorn installs `uvicorn.access`/`uvicorn.error` with their own handlers and `propagate=False`, and nothing in the repo passes a `log_config` (only `logging.basicConfig` at main.py:35 and workers/ingestion_worker.py:345). Query strings in the request line therefore reach stdout unredacted.
- **`redact_log_text` covers four shapes only** (services/safe_logging.py:9-12): `bearer <token>`, `key: value` for authorization/apikey/token/secret/password, `eyJ…` JWTs, and URL query strings. Not covered: Gemini `AIza…` keys standing alone, `postgres://user:password@host` credentials (no `?`, no `key=` form), email addresses, and user UUIDs.
- **Fast-path chat activity rows carry no department.** The greeting/identity/capability logs (routers/chat.py:2127, 2143, 2162, 2176, 2194) omit `'department'` from the detail dict, and `log_activity` only back-fills from the profile when `user_id` is set (services/activity.py:20-29) — so guest fast-path turns insert `department = NULL` and are excluded from `public_summary.total_queries`, which filters on `department = CCSICT` (routers/analytics.py:161-164). The landing-page query counter undercounts guest traffic.
- **`_BATCH_PATHS` is exact-string** (services/request_limits.py:42). `POST /upload/batch/` with a trailing slash falls to the single-file ceiling (27 MB) instead of the batch ceiling. Conversely the single-file ceiling applies to *every* other POST including `/chat`, so a 27 MB JSON chat body passes the middleware and is only stopped by pydantic's `max_length=4000` on `question` after parsing.
- **A chunked overrun surfaces as 400, not 413.** `BodyTooLarge` raised from `limited_receive` is swallowed by FastAPI's own body-read `try/except` and reported as `400 There was an error parsing the body`; the middleware's own 413 only lands when the raise reaches it with nothing sent (services/request_limits.py:107-114, 159-161).
- **`_declared_length` accepts a negative `Content-Length`** (`int('-5')` succeeds, `-5 > limit` is False) — harmless, because the streaming counter still enforces the ceiling, but the fast-refuse path is skipped.
- **`ALLOWED_UNLIMITED` is returned on storage failure**, so the log line at routers/chat.py:2683 can report `0/0 tokens booked` for a request that was allowed by a failure rather than by a measurement.
- **`PUT /settings/features` has an update-then-insert race** (routers/settings.py:67-76): two concurrent superadmin writes to a missing row both fall to `insert` and the second hits the primary key. Superadmin-only, so low exposure, but it is the same shape that was already removed from `GET /settings/features`.
- **`.env.example` omits `RATE_LIMIT_UPLOAD` and `RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC`** even though both are live settings (config.py:96, 103) with route decorators; only chat/scan/followup/storage are documented at .env.example:96-101.
- **`get_optional_user` treats a non-Bearer `Authorization` header as a guest**, not a 401, because `optional_security` has `auto_error=False` and returns `None` for a wrong scheme (fastapi/security/http.py:100-101). Only a *Bearer* token that Supabase rejects becomes 401.

### Open questions

- Should `require_novelty_access` / `require_upload_access` be migrated onto `_require_feature`? Doing so would make faculty novelty access survive a `system_settings` outage (matching `DEFAULT_FEATURES`, the frontend and `GET /settings/features`) but would also change `upload` from fail-closed to default-closed-by-table — the same outcome, since both default to False for both roles. The only behaviour that actually changes is faculty novelty, and that is currently a frontend/backend disagreement rather than a policy decision.
- Is `require_faculty_or_admin` intended for a route that was never wired, or should it be deleted? It is tested but unreachable.
- `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` is mandatory in production, but `_token_aal` falls back to an unverified decode whenever `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` is unset. Is the production deployment expected to set the legacy HS256 secret, or to rely on the Supabase-validated + `sub`-match binding? The README/evidence should state which, because only the first gives local signature verification.
- Is the deployment single-replica? The role cache, feature cache and Turnstile verified-guest cache are all per-process, and `invalidate_role_cache` after a role change clears only the replica that served the write.
- Should uvicorn's access logger be brought under `PrivacyFilter` (a `log_config` or `propagate=True` + handler removal), or is the request line considered free of secrets by policy?
- Should `papers.list_papers` use `resolve_effective_department` instead of its own profile read, so a non-superadmin's `?department=` is refused rather than silently rewritten and a superadmin's is validated?

**Depends on:** Supabase Auth (`sb.auth.get_user`) — one network round trip per authenticated request, uncached, Supabase Postgres tables `profiles`, `system_settings`, `departments`, `activity_log`, `config.settings` (pydantic-settings, validated at import), `limits` / `slowapi` — shared storage URI for rate limits and the guest budget, `services/chunker.count_tokens` — the `cl100k_base` proxy used by `estimate_charge`, `PyJWT` — header inspection and HS256 verification in `_token_aal` and `rate_limit_key`, `httpx` — Cloudflare siteverify, `services/activity.log_activity` — audit rows for role changes, settings updates, budget exhaustion, Cloudflare Turnstile (external)

**Used by:** routers/chat.py (require_chat_access, resolve_effective_department, guest budget, Turnstile, the only stacked dual rate limit), routers/upload.py (require_upload_access on 7 endpoints, resolve_effective_department, rate_limit_upload/public), routers/duplication.py (require_novelty_access on 5 endpoints, resolve_effective_department, rate_limit_scan/followup), routers/papers.py (require_archive_access on GET, require_admin on DELETE), routers/analytics.py (require_admin on 6 endpoints, get_current_user on /me, invalidate_role_cache on 3 write paths, rate_limit_public on /summary), routers/sessions.py (get_current_user + get_user_scope — no feature gate), routers/settings.py, routers/catalog.py, routers/departments.py, routers/maintenance.py (require_superadmin), main.py (middleware stack, configure_safe_logging, unauthenticated /health, /ready, /health/worker), workers/ingestion_worker.py (configure_safe_logging, and `sb` transitively), rag-thesis-frontend: src/api.js, src/context/AuthContext.jsx, src/lib/permissions.js, src/lib/privilegedMfa.js, src/components/security/*, src/App.jsx ProtectedRoute

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 12 correction(s), 6 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "FastAPI 0.127.0 raises 401 `Not authenticated` on a missing/non-bearer header (older FastAPI raised 403)." — stated twice (publicSurface, errorHandling).
  **Actual:** The pinned version is fastapi==0.139.2 (rag-thesis-backend/requirements.txt:4), and .venv/Lib/site-packages/fastapi-0.139.2.dist-info confirms that is what is installed. The 401 part is right (fastapi/security/http.py:86-92 `make_not_authenticated_error` -> HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`), the version is not. (`rag-thesis-backend/requirements.txt:4`)
- **Wrong:** Gotcha: "`get_optional_user` treats a non-Bearer `Authorization` header as a guest, not a 401, because `optional_security` has `auto_error=False` and returns `None` for a wrong scheme (fastapi/security/http.py:100-101)." And, correspondingly, that the required `HTTPBearer` raises `Not authenticated` on a "non-bearer header".
  **Actual:** This FastAPI version's `HTTPBase.__call__` performs NO scheme check at all: `scheme, credentials = get_authorization_scheme_param(authorization); if not (authorization and scheme and credentials): ...; return HTTPAuthorizationCredentials(scheme=scheme, credentials=credentials)`. `Authorization: Basic abc` therefore yields credentials with scheme='Basic', and `get_optional_user` hands 'abc' to `sb.auth.get_user`, which fails -> 401 'Invalid or expired authentication token'. It is NOT downgraded to guest. Only a missing header, or a header with no parameter (`Authorization: Bearer`), returns None/guest. Symmetrically, the required `security` dependency does not emit 'Not authenticated' for a wrong scheme either — it passes the credential through and the 401 comes from `get_current_user` with the other wording.
