(`rag-thesis-backend/.venv/Lib/site-packages/fastapi/security/http.py:93-102`)
- **Wrong:** `resolve_effective_department`: "a superadmin's `requested` is validated against `departments` (422 unknown, 503 on failure)" — phrased throughout as if only a supplied `requested` triggers the lookup.
  **Actual:** `selected = requested or assigned or settings.thesis_evaluation_department` and the `departments` query runs unconditionally on the superadmin branch. A superadmin who sends no `department` still pays that round trip, and gets 422 'Unknown department.' if their own `profiles.department` is not a row in `departments`. This also means the map's cost model ("superadmin chat turn ... + departments read") is correct but for a different reason than stated. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:121-128`)
- **Wrong:** usedBy: "routers/analytics.py (require_admin on 6 endpoints, get_current_user on /me ...)".
  **Actual:** `AdminUser` gates 7 endpoints: overview (:212), recent_activity (:296), list_users (:312), update_user_role (:328), delete_user (:368), update_user_details (:411), get_system_logs (:472). `CurrentUser` gates two endpoints, GET /me (:512) and PUT /me (:533), not one. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/analytics.py:212,296,312,328,368,411,472,512,533`)
- **Wrong:** errorHandling enumerates the auth-path 403/503 strings but lists only `_ensure_approved_account`'s 'This account is pending approval or has been rejected.' for approval failures.
  **Actual:** `get_user_scope` raises two further, differently-worded 403s that the map never quotes: 'This account is not approved for application access.' (:83-86) and 'A valid user profile is required for department-scoped access.' (:99-102). These are reachable from `/sessions` POST (routers/sessions.py:59) and from every `resolve_effective_department` caller, so they are live API wording, not dead defence. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:83-86,99-102`)
- **Wrong:** `_token_aal`: "it decodes with `verify_signature=False`"; and the invariant "must never return 'aal2' from an unverified decode unless `expected_user_id` is supplied and `claims['sub']` matches it."
  **Actual:** The unverified branch passes `options={'verify_signature': False, 'verify_aud': False}` — audience verification is disabled too (:207). Separately, the HS256 branch does not require `expected_user_id` at all: `_token_aal(credentials)` with no expected id and a configured secret returns the token's `aal` claim directly, because the `sub` check at :211 is itself gated on `expected_user_id`. That is safe (the signature was verified) but the invariant as written implies the sub-match always gates aal2. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:179-213`)
- **Wrong:** "A well-formed guest id (16–128 chars, `[A-Za-z0-9\-_]`) is hashed together with the client IP."
  **Actual:** The check is `all(char.isalnum() or char in '-_' for char in guest_id)`. `str.isalnum()` is Unicode-aware, so a 16-character id of accented letters, CJK characters or Arabic-Indic digits passes and gets its own `guest:` bucket. Also the surrounding `try` catches only `jwt.InvalidTokenError`; `jwt.InvalidKeyError` is a sibling under `PyJWTError` and would propagate out of the key function. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/rate_limiting.py:26-32`)
- **Wrong:** `configure_safe_logging` is described purely as installing `PrivacyFilter` on the root logger and its handlers.
  **Actual:** It also does `logging.getLogger('httpx').setLevel(WARNING)` and the same for `httpcore` (:53-54). That is materially part of the redaction story: it is what stops the per-request httpx INFO lines (which carry Supabase/Gemini/Cloudflare URLs) from reaching stdout in the first place — a bigger lever than the four regexes for URL leakage. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/safe_logging.py:53-54`)
- **Wrong:** keyFiles and the MFA invariant say privilegedMfa.js matches "on the lowercase prefix `multi-factor authentication is required`" (controlFlow says substring).
  **Actual:** The matcher is `detail.toLowerCase().includes(PRIVILEGED_MFA_MARKER)` — a substring anywhere, not a prefix. The practical latitude is wider than the map states: the sentence may also gain a LEADING clause, not only a trailing one. (The file's own comment says 'prefix'; the code does not.) (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/privilegedMfa.js:16,22`)
- **Wrong:** "`verify_turnstile_token` ... Network/JSON errors from siteverify return False, i.e. fail closed" / invariant "Turnstile fails closed (any siteverify error returns False)".
  **Actual:** The except clause is `(httpx.HTTPError, ValueError)` only. If Cloudflare returns valid JSON that is not an object (e.g. a list or a bare string), `result.get('success')` raises `AttributeError`, which escapes `ensure_guest_chat_verification` and surfaces as an unhandled 500 rather than a 403 — neither open nor closed. 'Any error' is an overstatement. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/turnstile.py:98-108`)
- **Wrong:** "`.env.example` omits `RATE_LIMIT_UPLOAD` and `RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` ... only chat/scan/followup/storage are documented at .env.example:96-101."
  **Actual:** The omission claim is correct, but the enumeration is not: `RATE_LIMIT_CHAT_IP` is documented too (:99). The documented set is storage (:96), chat (:98), chat_ip (:99), scan (:100), followup (:101). (`rag-thesis-backend/.env.example:96-101`)
- **Wrong:** Anchor drift on five citations that a reader would chase.
  **Actual:** `src/api.js:148 isPrivilegedMfaRefusal` -> actually :154. `routers/analytics.py:161-164` for the `total_queries` department filter -> actually :164-167. `tests/test_operations_security.py:189-218` -> actually :185-216. `slowapi/middleware.py:110 _should_exempt` -> defined at :98, the `_route_limits` branch is :109-111. `src/pages/Chat.jsx:208` for `action="guest_chat"` -> :207. Everything else I spot-checked (auth.py's 24 anchors, settings.py, rate_limiting.py, request_limits.py, turnstile.py, guest_budget.py, safe_logging.py, main.py:39/154/155/158/180/187/198/200, config.py:246-253, all 16 `@limiter.limit` sites, chat.py:2008/2009/2016/2028/2113/2218/1378/2531/2578/2683/2717/2783/2843, papers.py:51/54-55/113, analytics.py:41/320/333/353/405/461, sessions.py:15, supabase_setup.sql:746, permissions.js:10-13, conftest.py:19-42, test_auth_authorization.py:63-71/200-202/210-223/239, test_guest_budget.py:85-87, test_hardening_fixes.py:151-198/289-295) resolved exactly. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:154`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- `GET /upload/tracks` is a fully UNAUTHENTICATED endpoint living on the upload router — `def list_tracks(request: Request)` with no user dependency at all, bounded only by `@limiter.limit(settings.rate_limit_public)`. The map enumerates the unauthenticated surface (`/settings/public`, `/health*`, `/analytics/summary`, departments/catalog) and never flags that one of the seven `/upload/*` routes is not behind `require_upload_access`. rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1274-1276
- The main pre-generation guest charge is never anchored. The map cites `_charge_guest_extra` (:1378) and the four repair sites, and the exhaustion log line (:2683), but the actual single charge that can refuse a turn is `_charge_guest_generation(context, req.question, effective_question, chat_history_str)` at rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2674-2678, with the refusal at :2682-2691 logging `stage='pre_generation'`. Note the pre-retrieval check at :2218 logs `stage='pre_retrieval'` — two distinct activity rows, only one of which the map describes.
- A guest supplying `session_id` is refused 401 'Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.' inside `_chat_impl_unstamped`, before any fast path. That is a guest-boundary refusal absent from errorHandling. rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2116-2117
- `get_user_role` treats a MISSING `status` key as approved: `profile.get('status', 'approved') == 'approved'` (auth.py:59). The map describes this branch as 'a non-`approved` status ... coerces to student'; a row selected without the column defaults to approved instead. Only reachable through a fake client today (the real select asks for `role,status`), but it is the opposite default from `_ensure_approved_account`, which 403s when `status` is absent (auth.py:151).
- `guest_budget.charge` clamps `amount = max(1, int(tokens))` (services/guest_budget.py:135), so a zero or negative estimate still books one token. Minor, but it means `BudgetDecision.charged` is never 0 on the enabled path, which the map's 'reports 0/0 tokens booked' gotcha does not distinguish from the `ALLOWED_UNLIMITED` storage-failure case.
- `_token_context_is_expected` (services/turnstile.py:67-90) is missing from the publicSurface enumeration even though it is the function that implements the action/hostname checks the controlFlow section describes, and it is the only place the `turnstile_allowed_hostname_list` computed property (config.py:225-226) is consumed.

## 9. `other-routers` - Analytics, papers, departments, maintenance and operations

This is the institutional-administration half of the API: the landing-page and admin dashboard statistics, user/role administration and the audit log (`routers/analytics.py`), archive metadata listing and paper deletion (`routers/papers.py`), superadmin department CRUD (`routers/departments.py`), and the superadmin operations console covering worker registry, upload-job queue, deduplicated alerts, retention and private-storage cleanup (`routers/maintenance.py` + `services/operations.py`). Because the backend holds the Supabase service-role key and bypasses RLS, every department and role boundary in this subsystem is enforced in Python by re-reading the caller's `profiles` row. Two cross-cutting helpers live here: `services/activity.py` (best-effort audit insert that must never break a primary request) and `services/db_errors.py` (SQLSTATE classification so opaque driver errors become documented statuses instead of 500s).

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/analytics.py` (578 lines) - Public summary, admin overview, audit activity, user management, system logs, own-profile read/update. Contains the PostgREST row-cap paging helper `_fetch_all` and the two count contracts (`_exact_count` strict, `_count` tolerant).
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/papers.py` (164 lines) - GET /papers archive metadata (indirect-access model: no file paths, no URLs) and DELETE /papers/{id} with storage-then-row ordering and a cleanup-queue fallback.
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/departments.py` (206 lines) - Superadmin department CRUD plus an unauthenticated list. Owns `_derive_code` (mirrors the 20260725 backfill) and the six-table reference check that blocks deletion.
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py` (201 lines) - Superadmin operations console HTTP surface: operations summary, worker registry (hashed ids), upload-job listing, alert list/acknowledge, retention dry-run/apply, storage-cleanup list/retry.
- `rag-thesis-backend/routers/openapi_responses.py` (28 lines) - `errors(*codes)` -> OpenAPI `responses` mapping from a fixed 11-entry description table. Documentation only; no runtime effect.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py` (375 lines) - Worker registration RPCs, deduplicated alert upsert/resolve, signed HMAC webhook with bounded retry, backup-staleness check, `evaluate_operations` condition engine, retention RPC wrapper, security-audit insert.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/activity.py` (41 lines) - `log_activity(user_id, action, detail, department)`: resolves department from detail or the actor's profile, inserts into `activity_log`, swallows every exception.
- `rag-thesis-backend/services/db_errors.py` (87 lines) - SQLSTATE probes for 23505 / 42703 / 22P02 across `code`, `pgcode` and message text, plus the `identifier_not_found` context manager that turns a malformed UUID into the route's documented 404/422.
- `rag-thesis-backend/main.py` (~345 lines relevant) - Registers all routers (lines 204-213); owns the operations-monitor thread lifecycle (61-139) and `/health`, `/health/worker`, `/ready`.
- `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py` (~470 lines) - `sb` service-role client (line 14), `require_admin` (282), `require_superadmin` (311), `require_archive_access` (412), `invalidate_role_cache` (132), `_require_privileged_mfa`.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql` (~575 lines) - DDL for ingestion_workers / operational_alerts / security_audit_events, the worker + retention + alert RPCs, and the service_role grant matrix (lines 525-562).
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260804_backup_runs.sql` (103 lines) - `backup_runs` table and the `record_backup_run` security-definer RPC the PowerShell backup wrapper calls.
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/storage_backup.py` (249 lines) - AES-GCM + scrypt encrypted Storage backup / verify / local-only restore CLI. Restore refuses any non-localhost target (line 209).
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/scheduled_backup.ps1` (71 lines) - Nightly wrapper: DPAPI passphrase, backup_system.ps1, manifest check, best-effort record_backup_run.ps1, optional pruning.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_router_crud.py` (960 lines) - Pins department CRUD/code derivation, paper listing + delete cleanup, the PostgREST row-cap paging contract, public-summary decline-vs-invent, user management scope, storage-cleanup retry.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_operations_security.py` (945 lines) - Pins alert dedup/resolution, webhook signing and bounded retry, scanner fail-closed, monitor single-instance restart, maintenance route fail-closed, encrypted backup round-trip and restore guards.

### Control flow

Every route in this subsystem starts from the same trust reset. `dependencies/auth.py:14` creates one module-level service-role Supabase client `sb`, which bypasses RLS entirely, so the guard chain is the only boundary: `get_current_user` validates the bearer token with `sb.auth.get_user`, `_ensure_approved_account` re-reads `profiles.status` (non-'approved' -> 403, read failure -> 503), then `require_admin`/`require_superadmin` consult `get_user_role` (60s cache) and call `_require_privileged_mfa`, which demands an `aal2` token when `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` is on. `require_archive_access` (auth.py:412) deliberately does NOT enforce MFA, because reading the archive is an ordinary user feature and an aal2 requirement would lock admins out of the library. Having passed the guard, almost every handler re-reads `profiles.role,department` for itself — `_admin_scope` in analytics, an inline select in `list_users`/`update_user_*`/`get_system_logs`/`list_papers`/`delete_paper` — rather than trusting the cached role. That is six or seven distinct code paths doing the same query with slightly different fallbacks, and the divergence matters: `_admin_scope` raises 403 when the profile is missing, `list_users` and `list_papers` silently fall back to the literal string `'CCSICT'`, and `update_user_role`/`delete_user`/`update_user_details` fall back to `{}` — which makes `current_profile.get('role') != 'superadmin'` true and so fails closed into the restrictive branch.

Analytics aggregation is built around one hard constraint: PostgREST caps a single response at `db-max-rows` (1000 by default on Supabase). `_fetch_all` (analytics.py:76-109) therefore takes a *builder factory*, not a builder, because each page must be a fresh query rather than a re-ranged one; it loops `range(len(rows), len(rows)+999)`, takes `count='exact'` from the first response as the authoritative total, and stops on an empty chunk, on `len(rows) >= total`, or at a 50-page / 50,000-row backstop that logs a warning (totals stay exact; only the `Counter()` distributions are then computed over a prefix). Two count contracts sit beside each other on purpose: `_exact_count` propagates a failure, `_count` swallows it and returns 0. `public_summary` and the three paged reads in `overview` use the strict path and answer 503 rather than publish a fabricated zero — but `overview`'s `usage.chat_queries` and `usage.chat_sessions` keep the tolerant `_count`, on the reasoning that one blank tile on a dashboard of a dozen figures is better than a blank page. Note that `_admin_scope(user)` at analytics.py:214 runs *before* the try block at 226, so a transient Supabase failure while reading the caller's own profile escapes the whole 503 contract as an unhandled 500. `_overview_papers` narrows its exception handling to `is_missing_column` only, because catching everything previously retried a transient read against the same connection and then 500'd anyway.

Paper deletion (papers.py:110-164) is ordered so that no step can leave an orphan reachable. First the row is flipped to `ingestion_status='deletion_pending'`, which immediately removes it from every listing (all reads filter `='ready'`); then the private object is removed from the `pdfs` bucket; then the row is deleted, which cascades `chunks` and `paper_index_versions` via `on delete cascade`. If storage removal fails, `record_storage_cleanup` persists a `storage_cleanup_queue` row carrying only `type(error).__name__` (never exception text), an audit event is written, and the caller gets 503 with an explicit "retrying is safe". If the row delete fails after the object is gone, the caller gets a second 503 — but nothing is enqueued for that half, so the row sits at `deletion_pending` with a `storage_path` pointing at a deleted object until an operator retries the DELETE.

The operations engine is `evaluate_operations` (operations.py:265-370). It reads at most 50 `ingestion_workers` rows and at most 500 `upload_jobs` rows whose status is in {queued, retry_wait, processing, failed, cancelled}, ordered by `created_at` ASC. Workers are 'active' when `state != 'stopping'` and `last_seen_at >= now - OPERATIONS_WORKER_STALE_SECONDS`; `_timestamp` maps any unparseable value to `datetime.min`, so malformed timestamps fail stale. 'healthy' additionally requires `scanner_status` in an allowed set that contains `'healthy'` plus `'disabled'` only when `MALWARE_SCAN_MODE == 'disabled'` — so `'unknown'` always counts as `scanner_unavailable`, a critical condition. Queue depth counts `queued` plus `retry_wait` rows whose `next_retry_at` has already passed; staleness is measured against `next_retry_at` for retry_wait rows and `created_at` otherwise. `exhausted` is every row with status `failed`, with no attempt-budget test and no time window — deliberately, because `fail_upload_job` is the only writer of that status and a malware-flagged or unreadable manuscript is failed on attempt 1 of 3, so requiring the budget to be exhausted silently hid the entire permanent-failure class. `_backup_condition` returns `None` when `BACKUP_RPO_HOURS <= 0`, so the check adds no condition and resolves nothing; otherwise a missing `backup_runs` row is treated as the most severe form of staleness. The seven conditions are then reconciled in one pass: active ones go to `upsert_alert` (atomic `upsert_operational_alert` RPC, falling back to read-modify-write only when the RPC is absent), and if `_notify_due` says the last notification is older than 15 minutes, `notify_webhook` fires synchronously — three attempts with 0 / 0.25 / 1.0s sleeps and a per-attempt httpx timeout. Inactive conditions get an unconditional `resolve_alert` UPDATE every cycle. This whole routine is driven both by the background thread in `main.py` (only when `OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED`, every `OPERATIONS_MONITOR_SECONDS`) and, on every request, by the superadmin `GET /maintenance/operations/summary`.

The monitor thread's lifecycle (main.py:61-139) is worth knowing: each generation gets its *own* `threading.Event` passed as an argument rather than read from module scope, because a shared event meant a restart called `clear()` on the event the previous thread was still watching — un-cancelling it — and then started a second thread, and the two would race on `upsert_alert`/`resolve_alert` and flap alerts between open and resolved. `stop_operations_monitor` joins with a 2-second timeout and, if the thread is still inside a cycle, deliberately *keeps* the handle so the next `start()` declines instead of racing. The worker process runs the same evaluation on its own heartbeat (`workers/ingestion_worker.py:239-240`).

`db_errors` exists because the Supabase SDK raises opaque exceptions whose SQLSTATE may sit on `.code`, on `.pgcode`, or only inside the rendered message; `_sqlstate_matches` probes all three, and each classifier adds a message-text fallback ('duplicate key value', 'invalid input syntax for type uuid', 'column ... does not exist'). The `identifier_not_found` context manager exists because Postgres cannot distinguish "no such row" from "that is not a UUID" — the first returns empty, the second raises 22P02 — so the malformed case is given the same status the route already documents for the empty one. `analytics._is_reference_conflict` is a near-duplicate of this machinery for 23503, living in the router instead of the shared module.

### Public surface

- GET /analytics/summary — UNAUTHENTICATED, @limiter.limit(settings.rate_limit_public) (default '30/minute'), responses=errors(503). Pages `papers` (id,track,year) filtered ingestion_status='ready' AND department=settings.thesis_evaluation_department via `_fetch_all`, then `_exact_count('activity_log', {action:'chat_query', department:<eval dept>})`. Returns {total_papers, total_tracks (distinct non-'Uncategorized' tracks), year_range {from,to} or null, total_queries}. ANY read failure -> 503 'Archive statistics are temporarily unavailable.' — it declines rather than publishing a fabricated 0 (analytics.py:154-183).
- GET /analytics/overview — require_admin (admin|superadmin, + aal2 when REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA), responses=errors(403,503). `_admin_scope` re-reads profiles.role/department; superadmin => scope None (global), otherwise pinned to the profile department. Pages papers (+thesis_category, with a 42703-only retry to a legacy column list), profiles(role), scan_history(duplication_percentage,verdict_level,created_at). Returns {papers:{total, per_track, per_year, per_category, total_chunks}, users:{total, per_role}, usage:{chat_queries, chat_sessions, novelty_scans, avg_duplication_percentage, flagged_scans}}. The three paged reads are wrapped -> 503; the two `usage` counts use tolerant `_count` and become 0 on failure (analytics.py:211-292).
- GET /analytics/activity?limit=25 — require_admin, errors(403). limit clamped to [1,100]. Selects `_ACTIVITY_FIELDS` from activity_log, .eq('department', <profile dept>) unless superadmin, order created_at desc. Returns a bare JSON array (analytics.py:295-304).
- GET /analytics/users — require_admin, NO `responses=` declared. Selects `_PROFILE_FIELDS` from profiles ordered created_at desc; non-superadmin adds .eq('department', profile.department or 'CCSICT') and .neq('role','superadmin'). Unbounded: no limit, no paging (analytics.py:311-324).
- PUT /analytics/users/{user_id}/role — require_admin, body RoleUpdate{role: ^(student|faculty|admin|superadmin)$, status?: ^(pending|approved|rejected)$}, errors(400,403,404). 400 on self-target; 404 (via identifier_not_found) on missing/malformed id; admin (non-superadmin) gets 403 cross-department and 403 for touching or assigning superadmin. Writes {role, status: body.status or 'approved'}, calls invalidate_role_cache, logs 'role_change'. Returns {id, role, status} (analytics.py:327-360).
- DELETE /analytics/users/{user_id} — require_admin, errors(400,403,404,409,500). Same self/scope/superadmin guards, then `sb.auth.admin.delete_user(user_id)`. A 23503 FK violation (detected by the local `_is_reference_conflict`) becomes 409; anything else 500 'The user could not be deleted safely'. Then invalidate_role_cache + 'user_delete' audit. Returns {deleted:true} (analytics.py:367-407).
- PUT /analytics/users/{user_id}/details — require_admin, body UserUpdate{full_name required 1..120, role required pattern, department?, status?}, errors(400,403,404,422). 400 on self-target (added because the role endpoint had the guard and this one did not, letting a superadmin demote themselves). Admin cannot cross department, cannot touch/assign superadmin, cannot reassign department. A supplied department is validated against `departments.name` -> 422 'Unknown department'. Updates full_name+role (+department/status when supplied), invalidate_role_cache, logs 'role_change'. Returns the raw updated profile row (analytics.py:410-468).
- GET /analytics/logs/system?limit=200 — require_admin, NO `responses=`. limit clamped to [1,1000]. activity_log `_ACTIVITY_FIELDS`, department-scoped unless superadmin, then a single .in_() profile lookup (id,email,full_name,department) attached as log['user']. Returns an array (analytics.py:471-504).
- GET /analytics/me — get_current_user (any approved account), errors(404). Selects id,email,full_name,role,department,status,created_at,avatar_url,program_id,specialization_id; on ANY exception retries without program_id/specialization_id. 404 'Profile not found' when empty (analytics.py:511-529).
- PUT /analytics/me — get_current_user, body ProfileUpdate{full_name?<=120, avatar_url?<=512, program_id?, specialization_id?}, errors(422,500,503). full_name stripped, empty -> 422. avatar_url is keyed on `model_fields_set` (so an explicit null clears it) and must start with '<user.id>/' or 422. program/specialization go through `resolve_academic_selection` against the caller's own department with require_program=True for students; a non-HTTPException failure -> 503 'Academic program updates are unavailable until the catalog migration is applied.'. Empty patch returns {'status':'no changes'}; empty update result -> 500 (analytics.py:532-578).
- GET /papers?department=&program_id=&specialization_id=&thesis_category= — require_archive_access (feature-matrix `archive`; admins bypass and are NOT asked for MFA), response_model=list[PaperOut], errors(503). thesis_category normalized -> 422 if not student|faculty. `department` is honoured only for superadmin; everyone else is forced to profile.department or literal 'CCSICT'. Always .eq('ingestion_status','ready'), order created_at desc. On read failure: if any normalized filter was supplied -> 503; otherwise logs and retries with a legacy column set. Uploader names resolved with one .in_() lookup, missing -> 'Unknown / System'. No limit, no pagination (papers.py:40-107).
- DELETE /papers/{paper_id} — require_admin, errors(403,404,503). identifier_not_found wraps the lookup (uuid column, so a mistyped id raises 22P02). 403 when the paper's department differs and the caller is not superadmin. Order: set ingestion_status='deletion_pending' -> `sb.storage.from_('pdfs').remove([storage_path])` -> delete the row. Storage failure enqueues `storage_cleanup_queue` via record_storage_cleanup, logs 'paper_delete_pending' and returns 503; row-delete failure returns 503 with the object already gone. Success logs 'paper_delete' and returns {deleted: paper_id} (papers.py:110-164).
- GET /departments/ — UNAUTHENTICATED, @limiter.limit(rate_limit_public), response_model=list[DepartmentOut], no `responses=`. Selects `_DEPARTMENT_COLUMNS` = id,name,code,track_label,tracks,created_at ordered created_at asc. No `active` filter (departments.py:60-74).
- POST /departments/ — require_superadmin, body DepartmentCreate{name 1..100, code? 2..24 ^[A-Z0-9-]+$, track_label default 'Academic track', tracks list<=50}, response_model=DepartmentOut, responses=errors(400,409,422) but can also emit 502. Pre-checks name uniqueness -> 400. `_resolve_code` uses the explicit code or `upper(strip non-alnum)`; underivable or >24 chars -> 422. A 23505 on insert -> 409 naming the code; other exceptions re-raise. Empty representation -> 502 (departments.py:77-111).
- PUT /departments/{department_id} — require_superadmin, body DepartmentUpdate{name?, track_label?, tracks?}, response_model=DepartmentOut, errors(400,404,409,422) and can emit 502. identifier_not_found on the lookup; empty name -> 422; renaming settings.thesis_evaluation_department -> 409; name collision -> 400. Empty patch returns the existing row unchanged. `code` is not updatable through any endpoint (departments.py:114-163).
- DELETE /departments/{department_id} — require_superadmin, errors(404,409). 409 for the formal evaluation department. Runs six count='exact' probes (profiles, papers, scan_history, chat_sessions, upload_jobs, activity_log) keyed on `department = <name>`; any non-zero -> 409. Otherwise deletes and returns {'message':'Department deleted successfully'} (departments.py:166-206).
- GET /maintenance/operations/summary — require_superadmin, errors(503). Calls `evaluate_operations(sb)`, which both computes and MUTATES alert state (upserts/resolves, may fire webhooks). Returns {healthy_workers, registered_workers, queued_jobs, stale_jobs, pending_cleanups, failed_jobs, scanner_unavailable, backup_monitored, backup_stale, status} (maintenance.py:46-51).
- GET /maintenance/workers — require_superadmin, errors(503). ingestion_workers with last_seen_at >= now-7d, order desc, limit 100, projected through `_worker_view`: worker_id replaced by sha256(worker_id)[:12]. Returns {workers:[...]} (maintenance.py:54-64).
- GET /maintenance/upload-jobs?limit=100 — require_superadmin, errors(503). limit clamped [1,250]. Fixed 14-column projection of upload_jobs ordered created_at desc, NOT department-scoped. Returns {jobs:[...]} (maintenance.py:67-82).
- GET /maintenance/alerts?limit=100 — require_superadmin, errors(503). operational_alerts `ALERT_FIELDS` ordered last_seen_at desc, limit clamped [1,250]. Returns {alerts:[...]} (maintenance.py:85-94).
- POST /maintenance/alerts/{alert_id}/acknowledge — require_superadmin, errors(404,503). UPDATE status='acknowledged', acknowledged_at/by/updated_at WHERE id=? AND status<>'resolved'; empty result -> 404 'Open operational alert not found'. Then `record_security_event('operational_alert_acknowledged')`. Returns {id, status:'acknowledged'} (maintenance.py:97-118).
- POST /maintenance/retention/run?apply=false — require_superadmin, errors(409,503). `apply=true` requires settings.retention_enforcement_enabled or 409. Calls the `apply_operations_retention(p_apply)` RPC (purges upload_job_events >90d, resolved operational_alerts >90d, security_audit_events >1y) and records a 'retention_run' security event. Returns the RPC's jsonb counts (maintenance.py:121-133).
- GET /maintenance/retention/report — require_superadmin, errors(503). Same RPC with apply=false; no security event (maintenance.py:136-141).
- GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup — require_superadmin, NO `responses=` and NO try/except. storage_cleanup_queue where status='pending', projection id,operation,paper_id,job_id,error_category,attempts,created_at, ordered created_at asc. Returns {tasks:[...]} (maintenance.py:144-153).
- POST /maintenance/storage-cleanup/{task_id}/retry — require_superadmin, task_id is int (non-int -> FastAPI 422), errors(404,503). Reads with .limit(1) (deliberately not .single(), which would raise before the 404 guard). Already-completed task returns early. Otherwise removes the storage object, deletes the paper row when operation=='delete_paper', marks the queue row completed and flips the owning upload_job to cleanup_status='completed', source_stored=false. Any failure bumps `attempts`, stores `error_category=type(exc).__name__` and returns 503. Success logs 'storage_cleanup_completed' (maintenance.py:156-201).
- services/operations.register_worker(client, worker_id, *, state, scanner, current_job_id=None, version='2.1.0') -> bool — RPC `register_ingestion_worker`; returns False (not raise) when the RPC is absent (PGRST202 / 42883).
- services/operations.stop_worker(client, worker_id) -> bool — RPC `stop_ingestion_worker`; same missing-RPC tolerance.
- services/operations.record_security_event(client, event_type, *, severity='info', actor_id=None, department=None, details=None) -> None — direct insert into security_audit_events with event_type truncated to 120 chars. Not exception-tolerant: it propagates.
- services/operations.upsert_alert(client, dedupe_key, alert_type, severity, details) -> dict|None — prefers the atomic `upsert_operational_alert` RPC; falls back to a read-modify-write that preserves acknowledgement while active and reopens a resolved alert as a new occurrence.
- services/operations.resolve_alert(client, dedupe_key) -> None — UPDATE ... SET status='resolved' WHERE dedupe_key=? AND status<>'resolved'.
- services/operations.notify_webhook(client, alert) -> bool — no-op returning False when OPERATIONS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL is unset. Otherwise POSTs a six-field sanitized payload with `X-ISU-Signature: sha256=<hmac>` over the canonical JSON, retrying on delays (0, 0.25, 1.0) for timeouts/network/5xx/408/429 and stopping immediately on other 4xx. Failure raises a `webhook_degraded` alert; success resolves it and stamps last_notified_at.
- services/operations.newest_backup(client) -> dict|None — newest backup_runs row; tolerates a missing table by returning None.
- services/operations.evaluate_operations(client) -> dict — the condition engine (see controlFlow).
- services/operations.retention_report(client, *, apply=False) -> dict — thin wrapper over the `apply_operations_retention` RPC.
- services/operations.ALERT_FIELDS / WORKER_FIELDS — the pinned column sets shared between the internal fallback read and the client-facing listings.
- services/activity.log_activity(user_id, action, detail=None, department=None) -> None — never raises.
- services/db_errors.is_unique_violation / is_invalid_identifier / is_missing_column (bool) and identifier_not_found(detail, status_code=404) context manager.
- routers/openapi_responses.errors(*codes) -> dict[int, {'description': str}] — codes must be in {400,401,403,404,409,413,415,422,500,502,503}.

### Invariants

- The backend holds the service-role key and bypasses RLS: every department and role boundary in this subsystem is Python-side. Removing a `profiles` re-read removes the boundary.
- Non-superadmin scope is never taken from the request. `department` on GET /papers is overwritten from the profile; `_admin_scope`, `list_users`, `get_system_logs`, `update_user_*` and `delete_user` all re-derive it server-side.
- A missing/unreadable current profile must fail closed. `current_profile = {}` makes `role != 'superadmin'` true, which is the restrictive branch in every user-management handler.
- Neither `update_user_role` nor `update_user_details` may target the caller (400). This was added to `details` after the fact — a superadmin could otherwise demote or reject their own account and lock the only unrestricted role out of the system.
- An administrator (non-superadmin) may never read, modify, delete, or assign the superadmin role, and may never move a user across departments.
- `_PROFILE_FIELDS`, `_ACTIVITY_FIELDS`, `_DEPARTMENT_COLUMNS`, `ALERT_FIELDS`, `WORKER_FIELDS` and the inline paper/job projections replace `select('*')` so a future column cannot reach a client by default. Adding a column to a payload must be a deliberate edit.
- Archive reads always filter `ingestion_status = 'ready'` (papers.py:34, analytics.py:222). A paper mid-ingestion or mid-deletion is invisible everywhere.
- GET /papers never exposes `storage_path`, a signed URL, or full text — the indirect-access model. Enforced twice: by the pinned field list and by `response_model=list[PaperOut]`.
- Paper deletion order is status -> storage -> row. Never delete the row first; the object would be unreferenced and unrecoverable by the cleanup queue.
- A figure published to anonymous visitors as fact must be measured or declined. `public_summary` uses `_exact_count`/`_fetch_all` and answers 503; only `overview`'s two usage tiles may report a tolerant 0.
- Totals come from `count='exact'`, never `len()`. Distributions come from paged reads. The two must not be mixed in one response without the paging helper.
- `flagged_scans` counts the stored `verdict_level == 'high_overlap'`, never a re-derived percentage band. `services/novelty.verdict_for_concentration` is the single definition; the previous `duplication_percentage >= 50` test made the tile's number disagree with its own label.
- The formal evaluation department (`settings.thesis_evaluation_department`, default 'CCSICT') can be neither renamed (409) nor deleted (409).
- A department with any institutional record in profiles/papers/scan_history/chat_sessions/upload_jobs/activity_log cannot be deleted (409).
- `departments.code` is NOT NULL with no default since 20260725, and is derived as `upper(regexp_replace(name,'[^A-Za-z0-9]+','','g'))` to match that migration's backfill exactly. It is immutable through the API.
- Worker identities are never published: `_worker_view` replaces `worker_id` with `sha256(worker_id)[:12]`, matching how `record_backup_run.ps1` hashes the machine fingerprint.
- Alerts and security events carry only `safe_details` — counts and ids, never user content, manuscript text, file paths or hostnames. `record_storage_cleanup` stores `type(error).__name__`, never the exception text.
- One alert per condition: `dedupe_key` is UNIQUE and equals the condition key. Acknowledgement survives while the condition is active; only a resolve-then-reopen increments `occurrence_count` and clears the acknowledgement.
- Retention `apply=true` is refused (409) unless `RETENTION_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` is set on the server — institutional approval is a server setting, not a request parameter.
- `log_activity` must never raise. Analytics failing cannot be allowed to fail the primary request.
- Restore is local-only by construction: `storage_backup.restore` rejects any host outside {localhost, 127.0.0.1, host.docker.internal} before creating a client, and `restore_database_local.ps1` applies the same check to the PG URI.
- A recorded backup must have at least one artifact and a `sha256-manifest.json`; `record_backup_run` raises on `artifact_count <= 0` so an empty run cannot silence the staleness alert.
- `errors()` is documentation only — it never changes status codes, authorization, or bodies.
- Only `is_missing_column` (42703) justifies a reduced-column retry. Catching every exception turns a transient failure into a duplicate attempt and then a 500.

### Data model

- `profiles` (supabase_setup.sql:31) — id uuid PK references auth.users ON DELETE CASCADE, email, full_name, avatar_url, role CHECK(student|faculty|admin|superadmin) default 'student', department text NOT NULL default 'CCSICT', status CHECK(pending|approved|rejected) default 'approved', created_at, updated_at. Later migrations add program_id / specialization_id. `_PROFILE_FIELDS` is the pinned read projection.
- `papers` (supabase_setup.sql:~230) — uploaded_by references auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL; ingestion_status CHECK(processing|ready|failed|deletion_pending) (widened by 20260718); department, track, year, chunk_count, abstract, duplication_scan jsonb, storage_path, active_index_version, program_id, specialization_id, legacy_track, classification_status, thesis_category. `chunks` and `paper_index_versions` cascade from it.
- `activity_log` (supabase_setup.sql:754) — id bigserial, user_id references auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL, action text NOT NULL (unconstrained), department text, detail jsonb default '{}', created_at. Indexed on (action, created_at desc) and (department, created_at desc). Observed actions: chat_query, chat_query_blocked, chat_query_budget_exhausted, paper_upload, paper_delete, paper_delete_pending, role_change, user_delete, settings_update, scan_history_delete, scan_history_clear, duplication_chat_blocked, storage_cleanup_completed.
- `departments` — id, name (the short institutional code; the FK target for papers/profiles/chat_sessions/scan_history/upload_jobs/activity_log by *name*), code NOT NULL (20260725), active bool NOT NULL default true (20260725), title text (20260819), track_label, tracks jsonb NOT NULL default '[]', created_at. `programs.department_id -> departments.id ON DELETE RESTRICT`.
- `scan_history` (supabase_setup.sql:690) — user_id CASCADE, duplication_percentage, verdict_level CHECK(clear|review_suggested|high_overlap), matched_chunk_percentage/count, total_chunks, highest_similarity, department NOT NULL default 'CCSICT', top_matches/matched_chunks/chat_log jsonb.
- `chat_sessions` (supabase_setup.sql:582) — user_id CASCADE, title, department NOT NULL default 'CCSICT', created_at.
- `ingestion_workers` (20260724:48) — worker_id text PK, state CHECK(starting|idle|processing|degraded|stopping), current_job_id uuid, scanner_status CHECK(unknown|disabled|healthy|unavailable), version, started_at, last_seen_at, stopped_at. service_role has SELECT only; writes go through the security-definer register/stop RPCs.
- `operational_alerts` (20260724:64) — id uuid, dedupe_key text UNIQUE, alert_type, severity CHECK(info|warning|critical), status CHECK(open|acknowledged|resolved), safe_details jsonb, occurrence_count > 0, first/last_seen_at, last_notified_at, acknowledged_at/by, resolved_at, created_at, updated_at. service_role: SELECT + UPDATE only (no INSERT).
- `security_audit_events` (20260724:85) — id bigint identity, actor_id uuid (no FK), event_type, severity CHECK, department, safe_details jsonb, created_at. service_role: SELECT + INSERT.
- `storage_cleanup_queue` (20260718:9) — id bigserial, operation CHECK(rollback_upload|delete_paper), resource_path, paper_id references papers ON DELETE SET NULL, job_id text, error_category NOT NULL, status CHECK(pending|completed), attempts, created_at, updated_at. service_role: ALL.
- `backup_runs` (20260804:26) — id uuid, backup_id text UNIQUE (the yyyy-MM-dd-HHmmss folder stamp), completed_at, artifact_count >= 0, total_bytes >= 0, manifest_sha256, host_fingerprint, created_at. service_role: SELECT only; the writer is the `record_backup_run` security-definer RPC.
- RPCs used by this subsystem: `register_ingestion_worker(text,text,text,text,uuid)`, `stop_ingestion_worker(text)`, `upsert_operational_alert(text,text,text,jsonb)` (replaced by 20260724_operations_security_fixes.sql), `apply_operations_retention(boolean)`, `record_backup_run(text,timestamptz,integer,bigint,text,text)`. All security definer, all granted to service_role only.
- Pydantic models (models.py): `PaperOut` (94), `RoleUpdate` (242), `ProfileUpdate` (246), `UserUpdate` (265), `DepartmentOut` (272), `DepartmentCreate` (281), `DepartmentUpdate` (295).

### Config knobs

- THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT (config.py:79, default 'CCSICT') — the department `public_summary` counts, the guest scope, and the department that cannot be renamed or deleted.
- RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC (config.py:103, default '30/minute') — applied to GET /analytics/summary and GET /departments/.
- OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED (141, default false) — gates the background thread in both the API and the worker. The HTTP summary route evaluates regardless.
- OPERATIONS_MONITOR_SECONDS (142, 15..3600, default 60) — monitor cycle period.
- OPERATIONS_WORKER_STALE_SECONDS (143, 30..900, default 90) — worker liveness window; also used by /health/worker.
- OPERATIONS_QUEUE_AGE_SECONDS (144, 60..86400, default 300) — queue_age threshold.
- OPERATIONS_QUEUE_DEPTH_THRESHOLD (145, 1..10000, default 10) — queue_depth threshold (strict >).
- OPERATIONS_CLEANUP_AGE_SECONDS (146, 60..86400, default 600) — cleanup_delayed threshold.
- BACKUP_RPO_HOURS (153, 0..8760, default 0) — 0 disables the backup_stale check entirely and sets backup_monitored=false.
- BACKUP_RTO_HOURS (156, 1..8760, default 4) — reported in the alert details only.
- OPERATIONS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL (157) — must start with https:// when set; empty disables notification.
- OPERATIONS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_SECRET (158) — HMAC-SHA256 key; validated to be >= 32 chars and must be set together with the URL.
- OPERATIONS_ALERT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS (159, 1.0..30.0, default 5.0) — per-attempt httpx timeout inside the three-attempt retry.
- RETENTION_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED (160, default false) — gates POST /maintenance/retention/run?apply=true (409 otherwise).
- MALWARE_SCAN_MODE (161, 'disabled'|'clamav') — when 'disabled', a worker reporting scanner_status='disabled' still counts as healthy; production refuses to start unless 'clamav'.
- REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA — makes require_admin / require_superadmin demand an aal2 token. Deliberately not applied to require_archive_access.
- BACKUP_PASSPHRASE (scripts/storage_backup.py:86) — min 12 chars; set by scheduled_backup.ps1 from a DPAPI-protected file for the run's duration.
- SUPABASE_BACKUP_KEY (storage_backup.py:144, 211) — service key for backup/restore, passed via the environment rather than argv.

### Error handling

- 503 is the house style for 'a backing service is unavailable': public_summary, overview (the three paged reads), PUT /analytics/me catalog resolution, GET /papers with normalized filters, paper delete (both halves), and every maintenance route except GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup.
- 500 is reserved for 'the write was accepted but not confirmed': PUT /analytics/me empty update result, DELETE /analytics/users for a non-FK failure.
- 502 is used by the departments router for 'submitted but the database did not confirm it' (empty PostgREST representation) — and is undocumented in both routes' `responses=`.
- 409 covers three distinct conflicts: a dependent-record FK violation on user delete, the formal-department rename/delete refusal, a department that still has institutional records, a duplicate department code, and a retention apply without server enablement.
- 404 is produced both by an empty result and, through `identifier_not_found`, by SQLSTATE 22P02 on a malformed UUID. Without that wrapper a mistyped id reaches the caller as a 500.
- 422 comes from pydantic validation, from `normalize_thesis_category`, from an unknown department on user details, from an avatar path outside the caller's own prefix, and from an underivable/overlong department code. `retry_storage_cleanup` also 422s on a non-integer task_id via path coercion.
- 403 comes from the guards (role, MFA, feature matrix) and from the in-handler cross-department and superadmin-protection checks.
- `_count` converts a read failure into 0 and logs a warning; `_exact_count` propagates. Mixing them in one payload is intentional and documented in the docstrings.
- `log_activity` catches everything and logs `action` + exception class name only. An audit write can never fail a request — and can never be relied on either.
- `register_worker` / `stop_worker` / `upsert_alert` return False or take a fallback on a missing RPC (`_missing_rpc` matches PGRST202, 42883, 'could not find the function') and re-raise anything else.
- `notify_webhook` stops immediately on a 4xx other than 408/429, retries timeouts / network errors / 5xx / 408 / 429 twice more, and on total failure raises a `webhook_degraded` alert whose own persistence failure is only logged.
- `newest_backup` swallows a missing `backup_runs` table so the whole operational view is not taken down by a feature that is off by default.
- `_timestamp` maps any unparseable timestamp to `datetime.min` (UTC) so malformed data fails stale rather than fresh.
- `_fetch_all` never raises on truncation; exceeding 50 pages logs a warning and returns exact totals with prefix distributions.
- The operations monitor thread catches every exception per cycle and logs the class name, so a bad cycle cannot kill the thread.

### Tests that pin it

- tests/test_router_crud.py — TestDepartmentsAndSettings (145-304): department CRUD, code derivation from name, punctuation stripping, explicit code precedence, underivable/overlong -> 422, duplicate code -> 409 not 500, unrelated insert failures not swallowed, formal department rename/delete refusal, referenced-department 409.
- tests/test_router_crud.py:306-523 TestPapersAndAnalytics — paper listing + delete cleanup, pre-catalog-migration fallback, storage failure keeps the paper pending and retryable, `test_overview_aggregates_past_the_postgrest_row_cap` (373) which stubs a PostgREST server that truncates every response at 1000 rows and asserts exact totals, full distributions, and contiguous ranges [0,1000,2000].
- tests/test_router_crud.py:525-584 TestPublicSummaryDeclinesRatherThanInvents — either unreadable source is a 503, a measured zero is still published, and the admin dashboard keeps its tolerant count.
- tests/test_router_crud.py:586-848 TestAnalyticsAdministration — department scoping of activity/user lists, role-change scope + audit write, superadmin delete and detail update, dependent-records 409, `_is_reference_conflict` shape matrix, fail-closed behaviour on profile read failure, system-log profile join, server-resolved normalized profile selection.
- tests/test_router_crud.py:850-903 TestStorageCleanupMaintenance — list + complete, retrying an expunged task is 404 not 500, retry failure leaves the task pending.
- tests/test_router_crud.py:905-960 TestFlaggedScansFollowTheVerdict — flagged_scans counts `verdict_level`, not a re-derived coverage band.
- tests/test_operations_security.py — migration is additive/private/cancellation-safe (46); alert dedup and resolution of inactive conditions (321); unavailable and unknown scanner states fail closed (340, 356); a future retry_wait is neither queued nor stale (372); all active conditions dedupe (392); cancelled job with delayed cleanup alerts (422); webhook signing + bounded transient retry (129) and bounded failure persistence (778); upsert prefers the atomic RPC (171) with a fallback that preserves acknowledgement (761); retention dry run never changes the apply flag (178); superadmin operations routes (602) and maintenance routes fail closed (799); acknowledging a missing alert is 404 (835); monitor lifecycle (653), one bounded evaluation (842), and `test_a_restart_cannot_run_two_monitors` (857); worker health is generic (639) and handles registry failure (901); encrypted storage round-trip, wrong passphrase, path-traversal and duplicate-manifest rejection (219-265, 910); restore scripts' local-only guards (231, 941); the backup wrapper loads the backend environment without prompting for a secret (287).
- tests/test_activity.py — log_activity inserts the expected row shape and returns None (never raises) on failure.
- tests/test_export_openapi.py — exercises scripts/export_openapi.py, which serializes the schema the `errors()` mappings feed.
- tests/test_schema_consistency.py — pins the known base-schema vs migration differences for the RPCs above.

### Gotchas

- `evaluate_operations` MUTATES on a GET. `GET /maintenance/operations/summary` upserts alerts, resolves alerts, and can fire the webhook inline — three attempts with 0.25s + 1.0s sleeps and a per-attempt httpx timeout (default 5s), for each of up to seven conditions. A worst-case dashboard refresh can block a threadpool worker for well over a minute. Any load test or uptime probe pointed at this route also writes alert rows.
- `retries_exhausted` effectively never clears. `exhausted = [row for row in jobs if row.get('status') == 'failed']` (operations.py:317) has no time window, and `apply_operations_retention` purges `upload_job_events` — not `upload_jobs`. One permanently failed upload pins a `critical` alert open and keeps `failed_jobs` non-zero forever, and the `backup_stale`-style severity means it also re-notifies every 15 minutes.
- The job window is capped at 500 rows ordered `created_at` ASC (operations.py:276-279). Past 500 open/failed/cancelled jobs the *newest* ones are invisible, so `queued_jobs`, `stale_jobs` and `pending_cleanups` silently undercount exactly when the queue is in trouble.
- `evaluate_operations` returns `status: 'healthy'` based only on `healthy_workers and not scanner_unavailable`. A system with a 10,000-deep stale queue, delayed cleanups, and a two-week-old backup still reports `healthy`.
- `delete_department` checks six name-keyed tables but NOT `programs`, whose FK is `department_id ... on delete restrict` (migrations/20260725_normalized_academic_catalog.sql:14). Every seeded department has programs (20260819_isu_academic_catalog.sql:64), so deleting one that passed the six checks raises a 23503 the handler does not catch — an unhandled 500 where the route documents 409. `specializations` hang off programs and are equally unconsidered.
- `GET /departments/` publishes all ten colleges, including the nine that 20260819 seeded with `active = false` specifically so nothing could be addressed to them. `_DEPARTMENT_COLUMNS` omits `active`, and `DepartmentOut` has no `active` field, so a consumer cannot tell. `routers/catalog.py::_nested_catalog` filters active-only; these two department listings disagree. Nothing in the frontend calls this route (`src/api.js:422` uses `/catalog/departments/legacy`), which is presumably why it went unnoticed.
- `GET /departments/` has no fallback and `DepartmentOut` requires `code: str`. On a project without 20260725 the select raises 42703 -> unhandled 500; on a partially backfilled project a NULL code is a ResponseValidationError -> 500. Every sibling read in the codebase has a legacy-column fallback; this one does not.
- `create_department` and `update_department` document `errors(400,409,422)` / `errors(400,404,409,422)` but both can return 502 (departments.py:106, 158). The OpenAPI schema is wrong for those two routes.
- No department mutation writes to `activity_log`. Create, update and delete are unaudited, while paper deletion, role changes, user deletion, settings changes and cleanup retries all call `log_activity`.
- `create_department`'s name-uniqueness pre-check is a TOCTOU: two concurrent creates both pass, and the second hits `departments_name` uniqueness — which `is_unique_violation` catches and reports as "code ... already in use", naming the wrong column.
- `_derive_code` can produce a 1-character code (name 'A'), bypassing the `min_length=2` the model enforces on an explicit code.
- `_admin_scope(user)` runs outside the try in `overview` (analytics.py:214 vs 226). A transient Supabase failure reading the caller's own profile returns an unhandled 500, defeating the explicit "decline rather than fabricate" 503 contract the rest of the handler implements.
- Several handlers pass a possibly-NULL department into `.eq('department', department)`. postgrest-py stringifies the value, producing `department=eq.None`, which matches nothing. That is fail-closed (empty list), but it silently looks like "no activity" rather than "broken profile". `list_users` and `list_papers` instead hardcode `or 'CCSICT'` rather than using `settings.thesis_evaluation_department` — three different behaviours for the same missing field.
- `update_user_role` defaults `status` to `'approved'` whenever the body omits it (analytics.py:350). Changing a pending user's role silently approves their account.
- `update_user_role` never checks `result.data`, so updating a row that vanished between the existence check and the write returns HTTP 200 with a synthesized body. `update_user_details` does check and returns 404.
- The PUT handlers return the raw PostgREST representation (`result.data[0]` at analytics.py:468 and 577), i.e. every profile column, while the GET handlers carefully pin `_PROFILE_FIELDS`. The column-pinning discipline is only half applied on this router. (`DepartmentOut` saves the departments router from the same leak.)
- `analytics._is_reference_conflict` (23503) duplicates `db_errors._sqlstate_matches`/`_error_text` instead of living in `services/db_errors.py` with the other three SQLSTATEs. It is also close to dead: the only `on delete restrict` FK to `auth.users` was `upload_jobs.owner_id`, and 20260803_upload_jobs_owner_on_delete_set_null.sql changed it to `set null`. On a fully migrated project the 409 path is unreachable.
- `upsert_alert`'s non-RPC fallback INSERTs into `operational_alerts` (operations.py:129), but migration 20260724 revokes all and grants only SELECT + UPDATE to `service_role` (lines 532-536). If that path is ever reached against a real database it fails with a permission error, not the intended degraded write. It is only exercised by a stub in `tests/test_operations_security.py:761`.
- `acknowledge_alert` and `run_retention` write first and record the security event second, inside the same try. If `record_security_event` fails, the caller gets 503 although the acknowledgement (or, worse, the retention DELETEs) already committed. `routers/upload.py:1257` wraps the same call in its own try/except precisely to avoid this.
- `GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup` is the only route on that router with no try/except and no `responses=`; an unavailable database surfaces as a bare 500 while every sibling answers 503.
- `retry_storage_cleanup` never re-checks that the paper is still `deletion_pending` before `sb.table('papers').delete()`, and never updates `storage_cleanup_queue.updated_at` (there is no trigger), so the queue's own age column is frozen at insert time even though `evaluate_operations` measures cleanup delay from `upload_jobs.updated_at`.
- `GET /analytics/users` and `GET /papers` are both unbounded — no limit, no paging, no `_fetch_all`. Ironically the *inner* profile lookups in those same handlers were deliberately narrowed for scale (papers.py:88-93, analytics.py:484-493) while the outer reads were left unpaged, and PostgREST will silently truncate them at `db-max-rows`, reintroducing exactly the bug `_fetch_all` exists to prevent.
- The conversational fast paths in chat (`routers/chat.py:2127, 2143, 2162, 2176, 2194`) log `chat_query` WITHOUT `'department'` in the detail. For a guest there is no `user_id` either, so `activity.log_activity` writes `department = NULL` and the row is excluded from `public_summary.total_queries`, which filters on the evaluation department. Guest greetings/identity/capability turns are counted by the admin superadmin tile (unfiltered) but not by the public one. For signed-in users the same omission costs an extra `profiles` SELECT per logged event.
- `errors()` does `_DESCRIPTIONS[code]` with no `.get`, so documenting an unlisted code (429 is absent, and every `@limiter.limit` route can emit one) raises KeyError at import time. Consequently no route documents its rate-limit response.
- `errors()` emits descriptions only — no `model`/`content`, so the generated OpenAPI shows no error body schema anywhere despite FastAPI always returning `{'detail': ...}`.
- `_fetch_all`'s `count` is read only from the first page. Against a client or PostgREST build that omits it, the helper silently degrades to the short-page heuristic, which is wrong if the server's `db-max-rows` is smaller than `_AGGREGATE_PAGE_SIZE` — it would stop after one page believing it saw everything. Safe today because both are 1000.
- `notify_webhook` indexes `alert['id']` unguarded at operations.py:203 after a successful delivery; a stub or RPC variant returning a row without `id` raises KeyError inside the monitor loop (caught in `main._operations_monitor`, but a 503 on the HTTP route).
- `resolve_alert` issues an unconditional UPDATE for every inactive condition on every cycle — six to seven writes per minute against `operational_alerts` on a completely healthy system.
- `_backup_condition` returning `None` means the summary reports `backup_stale: false` and `backup_monitored: false` when `BACKUP_RPO_HOURS = 0` (the default). Out of the box, nothing monitors backups; the check is opt-in.
- `check_backup_freshness.ps1` and the `backup_stale` alert answer the same question from different data (local folder mtime vs the `backup_runs` table) and can disagree — e.g. after a `-SkipRecording` rehearsal run, which deliberately writes a folder without resetting the staleness clock.
- `scheduled_backup.ps1` prunes with `-KeepLast` only after a successful run, but `record_backup_run.ps1` failures are swallowed with a warning (scheduled_backup.ps1:53). A long-running recording outage leaves backups on disk while the alert fires — correct, but the operator sees a `backup_stale` critical for backups that exist.
- `backup_system.ps1` passes the plaintext service-role key through `$env:SUPABASE_BACKUP_KEY` for the duration of the Python subprocess; `record_backup_run.ps1` reads `SUPABASE_KEY` out of the backend `.env` by regex. Both clear the variable in a `finally`, but the key transits the process environment.
- `GET /maintenance/upload-jobs` is not department-scoped. That is fine for superadmin-only, but it is the one operations listing whose rows carry a `department` column and do not filter on it — worth remembering if the guard is ever relaxed.
- `/ready` is not in the Vite dev proxy list (`rag-thesis-frontend/vite.config.js:93-105`) although `/health` is.
- `update_department` cannot change `code`, `title`, or `active`. There is no API path to deactivate a department; only a direct SQL `update departments set active = ...` (which 20260819's header documents as the intended activation mechanism).

### Open questions

- Is `delete_department`'s missing `programs` check known? Every department seeded by 20260819 has programs with `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so the six-table check can pass and the delete still 500s. Adding `programs` (id-keyed, not name-keyed) to the reference map would close it.
- Should `retries_exhausted` be time-bounded (e.g. failed within the retention window) or should `apply_operations_retention` also prune terminal `upload_jobs`? As written the critical alert is permanent after the first hard failure.
- Should `GET /maintenance/operations/summary` be read-only, with mutation left to the monitor thread? Today an unauthenticated-looking dashboard refresh can drive webhook traffic and alert writes, and blocks for the retry backoff.
- `GET /departments/` is unauthenticated, unfiltered by `active`, and has no frontend caller. Is it dead surface that should be removed, or should it gain the active filter and the legacy-column fallback the catalog route has?
- Should the fast-path `chat_query` audit events carry `effective_department` so guest conversational turns appear in `public_summary.total_queries`? Right now the public and admin query counters disagree for guests.
- Should `analytics._is_reference_conflict` (23503) move into `services/db_errors.py`, and is the 409 path still reachable at all after 20260803 relaxed the last RESTRICT FK?
- Should `GET /papers` and `GET /analytics/users` use `_fetch_all` or gain explicit pagination? Both are silently truncated at `db-max-rows`, the exact failure mode the helper exists to prevent.

**Depends on:** dependencies/auth.py — the `sb` service-role client and every guard (`get_current_user`, `require_admin`, `require_superadmin`, `require_archive_access`, `invalidate_role_cache`, `resolve_effective_department`)., services/db_errors.py — SQLSTATE classification, used by analytics, papers, departments, maintenance and catalog., services/activity.py — audit writes from analytics, papers, maintenance, settings, duplication and ingestion., services/catalog.py — `resolve_academic_selection` (PUT /analytics/me) and `normalize_thesis_category` (GET /papers)., services/cleanup.py — `record_storage_cleanup`, the durable fallback for a failed private-object delete., services/rate_limiting.py — the slowapi `limiter` applied to the two unauthenticated routes., services/operations.py — consumed by routers/maintenance.py, workers/ingestion_worker.py, main.py and routers/upload.py (`record_security_event`)., routers/openapi_responses.py — imported by all ten routers., routers/settings.py — `DEFAULT_FEATURES`, read by `_feature_enabled` behind `require_archive_access`., config.py — the operations/backup/retention/rate-limit settings block., Postgres RPCs: register_ingestion_worker, stop_ingestion_worker, upsert_operational_alert, apply_operations_retention, record_backup_run., External: httpx (webhook), cryptography (scrypt + AES-GCM in storage_backup.py), the Supabase Python SDK (PostgREST + Storage + Auth admin).

**Used by:** rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:344-437 — getPublicSummary, getAnalyticsOverview, getRecentActivity, updateMyProfile, listUsers, updateUserRole, deleteUser, updateUserDetails, getSystemLogs, getOperationsSummary, getIngestionWorkers, getOperationalJobs, getOperationalAlerts, acknowledgeOperationalAlert, getRetentionReport, createDepartment, updateDepartment, deleteDepartment. Operations responses pass through `requireOperationsObject`, which throws a TypeError on a non-object / missing-array shape., rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx, OperationsTab.jsx, OverviewCharts.jsx, SystemManagementTab.jsx, UploadHistoryTab.jsx — the admin console., rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/archive/useArchiveCatalog.js and the archive page — GET /papers., main.py:204-213 (router registration) and main.py:61-139 (the operations monitor driving evaluate_operations)., workers/ingestion_worker.py:35-39, 156, 192-197, 231, 239-240, 247, 337 — register_worker, stop_worker, upsert_alert, notify_webhook, and its own evaluate_operations pass., routers/upload.py:58, 1257 — record_security_event for upload cancellation., scripts/record_backup_run.ps1 -> the record_backup_run RPC -> newest_backup -> the backup_stale condition., Operator-only, with no frontend caller: GET /departments/, POST /maintenance/retention/run, GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup, POST /maintenance/storage-cleanup/{id}/retry, GET /analytics/me.

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 8 correction(s), 7 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** GET /papers: 'thesis_category normalized -> 422 if not student|faculty'
  **Actual:** normalize_thesis_category coerces falsy input to 'student' before the membership test: `category = (value or 'student').strip().lower()`. An empty query string (`?thesis_category=`) is not None, so papers.py:49 enters the branch, normalize returns 'student' (no 422), and papers.py:68 then applies `.eq('thesis_category','student')`. An empty param silently becomes a student-only filter instead of no filter or a rejection. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/catalog.py:21-26, rag-thesis-backend/routers/papers.py:49-50,68-69`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: 'A recorded backup must have at least one artifact and a sha256-manifest.json; record_backup_run raises on artifact_count <= 0 so an empty run cannot silence the staleness alert.'
  **Actual:** Only half is enforced by the RPC. `manifest_sha256 text` is nullable with no check, and the parameter is `p_manifest_sha256 text default null` — the function body raises only for a blank backup_id, a null completed_at, and artifact_count <= 0. The manifest requirement lives entirely in the PowerShell wrapper, which throws before calling the RPC. Any direct RPC call with a null manifest is accepted and resets the staleness clock. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260804_backup_runs.sql:34,51,63-74 vs rag-thesis-backend/scripts/record_backup_run.ps1:33-35`)
- **Wrong:** POST /maintenance/alerts/{alert_id}/acknowledge: 'UPDATE status=acknowledged ... WHERE id=? AND status<>resolved; empty result -> 404'
  **Actual:** Incomplete: the UPDATE is wrapped in `identifier_not_found('Open operational alert not found')`, so a malformed (non-UUID) alert_id raises 22P02 and is converted to the same 404 rather than falling into the outer `except Exception` and becoming the documented 503. The map describes identifier_not_found on every other route that uses it but omits it here, which is the only place on this router it appears. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:101-109`)
- **Wrong:** DELETE /papers/{id}: 'Order: set ingestion_status=deletion_pending -> sb.storage.from_(pdfs).remove([storage_path]) -> delete the row.'
  **Actual:** The storage step is conditional: `if paper.get('storage_path'):`. A row with a null/empty storage_path (e.g. a failed ingestion never staged, or a row already half-deleted by a prior retry) skips storage removal entirely and goes straight from `deletion_pending` to the row delete, with no cleanup-queue entry and no 503. The map presents the three steps as unconditional. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/papers.py:133-136`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant/controlFlow: "'healthy' additionally requires scanner_status in an allowed set ... so 'unknown' always counts as scanner_unavailable, a critical condition."
  **Actual:** True for evaluate_operations, but the map presents it as a system-wide invariant while the uptime probe on the same data contradicts it. `/health/worker` computes `healthy = bool(rows and rows[0].get('scanner_status') != 'unavailable')` — 'unknown' and 'starting'-state scanners report HTTP 200 'healthy' there, while evaluate_operations simultaneously raises a critical `scanner_unavailable` alert for the same worker. The two health definitions disagree, and the map's configKnobs entry only notes that they share OPERATIONS_WORKER_STALE_SECONDS. (`rag-thesis-backend/main.py:289-308 vs rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py:284-289,318-320`)
- **Wrong:** gotcha: "`_fetch_all`'s `count` is read only from the first page."
  **Actual:** It is read from the first page that reports one: `if total is None: total = getattr(result,'count',None)` runs on every iteration until a non-None count is seen. The practical conclusion the map draws (degradation to the short-page heuristic when no page ever reports a count) is right; the mechanism as stated is not. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/analytics.py:90-102`)
- **Wrong:** Invariant: "_worker_view replaces worker_id with sha256(worker_id)[:12], matching how record_backup_run.ps1 hashes the machine fingerprint."
  **Actual:** Both hash with SHA-256 and truncate, but not to the same width: the worker view takes 12 hex characters, record_backup_run.ps1 takes 16 (`.Substring(0, 16)` over `"$env:COMPUTERNAME|$env:USERDOMAIN"`). 'Matching' overstates it. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:33 vs rag-thesis-backend/scripts/record_backup_run.ps1:89-94`)
- **Wrong:** dataModel: "papers ... ingestion_status CHECK(processing|ready|failed|deletion_pending) (widened by 20260718)"
  **Actual:** The base schema already ships the four-value check inline in the CREATE TABLE, so a fresh project does not depend on 20260718 for it; 20260718 re-applies it as a drop/add for already-installed projects. Minor, but it is exactly the kind of base-vs-migration divergence tests/test_schema_consistency.py exists to pin. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:237-238 vs rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260718_transactional_ingestion_cleanup.sql:5-7`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- The shared 502 description is semantically wrong for the only routes that emit 502. `_DESCRIPTIONS[502] = 'An upstream AI service was unavailable or returned an error.'` but the departments router's 502 means 'PostgREST returned no representation for a write'. The map notes the two routes fail to declare 502 at all; it does not note that declaring it would produce a misleading description (rag-thesis-backend/routers/openapi_responses.py:21 vs routers/departments.py:106-110,157-162).
- The chat fast-path `chat_query` audit writes are FastAPI BackgroundTasks (`background_tasks.add_task(log_activity, ...)`), not inline calls. This changes the map's stated cost: the extra `profiles` SELECT per logged event happens after the response is sent, not on the request path. The undercount in public_summary.total_queries that the map describes is still real (rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2127,2143,2162,2176,2194).
- `services/novelty.verdict_for_concentration` has a fourth return value, 'exact_duplicate', which is NOT in the `scan_history_verdict_level_check` constraint (clear|review_suggested|high_overlap). Today the constraint holds only because routers/duplication.py:304 calls the function without the `exact_duplicate` argument, while the ingest-time screen (services/novelty.py:170) passes it and writes to `papers.duplication_scan` jsonb instead. If the scan route ever passed the flag, the insert would fail the check — and `flagged_scans` (analytics.py:288-290) counts only 'high_overlap', so an exact duplicate would not be flagged either. The map states verdict_for_concentration is 'the single definition' without noting its range exceeds both the constraint and the tile's test (rag-thesis-backend/services/novelty.py:92-106, supabase_setup.sql:705, routers/analytics.py:288-290).
- `upsert_operational_alert` (as replaced by the fixes migration) truncates its inputs: `left(p_dedupe_key, 160)` and `left(p_alert_type, 120)`. The map documents the 120-char truncation in record_security_event but not this pair, and the atomic RPC path therefore differs from the read-modify-write fallback, which writes both values untruncated (rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security_fixes.sql:22 vs services/operations.py:129-134).
- `DepartmentOut` requires `track_label: str` and `tracks: list[str]` as non-optional in addition to `code: str`. The map's 42703/NULL-code gotcha for GET /departments/ is right but narrower than reality: a row with a NULL `track_label` (the column has no NOT NULL in the base schema path) is equally a ResponseValidationError -> 500 on an unauthenticated route (rag-thesis-backend/models.py:272-279).
- `update_department`'s no-op branch returns `existing.data[0]`, which was selected with `_DEPARTMENT_COLUMNS` — so the empty-patch response is column-pinned, while the non-empty path returns the raw PostgREST representation at line 163. The map credits `DepartmentOut` with saving the router from the analytics-style leak, which is correct, but the two return paths do differ in what reaches the serializer (rag-thesis-backend/routers/departments.py:121-122,153-154,163).
- `/health/worker` picks its single sample row with `.limit(1)` and no `.order(...)` after the `gte`/`neq` filters, so which worker determines the whole system's reported health is whatever PostgREST returns first — unlike `evaluate_operations`, which reads up to 50 rows ordered `last_seen_at desc` (rag-thesis-backend/main.py:299-305).

## 10. `database` - Database schema, RPC contract and migrations

Defines the entire persistence and transactional contract for the RAG thesis library: 20 tables, 31 functions (7 trigger functions + 24 callable RPCs), one HNSW vector index, and the RLS posture that makes the backend's service-role key the only write path. Every transactional operation (ingest commit, queue lease, chat persistence, vector search, duplication screen, index activation) crosses Python→Postgres through a `security definer` RPC rather than a table write, so business invariants live in PL/pgSQL, not in the API. `supabase_setup.sql` is the hand-maintained base schema for a fresh project; `migrations/*.sql` (27 files, 22 forward + 5 rollback) apply on top in filename order and are individually replayable — which is also the subsystem's main hazard, since `create or replace function` silently reverts a body when an older file is replayed.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` (1826 lines / 74 KB) - Base schema for a fresh project. One `begin;`…`commit;` transaction (line 17 → 1826). Sections numbered 0,1,1A,2–12,15 (13 and 14 do not exist). Contains 12 of the 20 tables, 22 of the 31 functions, all RLS, both storage buckets, and a fail-fast contract assertion at 1284–1315. Section 15 (1319→1826) is a verbatim-ish backport of 20260723 + selective bits of 20260724.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260717_rag_items_9_16.sql` (246 lines) - Oldest forward migration. Creates `departments`, adds index_version/page columns to chunks, and defines match_chunks/check_topic_duplication with plain `create function` (4-arg / 3-arg, no provenance join). Header line 2 says 'This file is NOT executed by the application', which conflicts in tone with README step 2 ('apply every file').
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260718_transactional_ingestion_cleanup.sql` (129 lines) - `storage_cleanup_queue` + the pre-provenance `commit_paper_ingestion`. This is the file the README explicitly warns never to replay after 20260720.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260719_production_hardening.sql` (243 lines) - `upload_jobs` v1, the six NOT VALID department FKs, save_chat_exchange (8-arg), avatars bucket, pdfs restrictive policy, profile triggers.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql` (395 lines) - `paper_index_versions` + the composite `chunks_index_provenance_fkey` (ON DELETE RESTRICT). Rewrites commit_paper_ingestion to require a verified fingerprint, and converts both retrieval RPCs to `language sql stable` with the provenance join.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql` (473 lines) - The leased queue: 20 columns on upload_jobs, 5 partial indexes, and 10 RPCs (reserve/queue/claim/heartbeat/retry/fail/commit/cleanup-claim/cleanup-finish/expire). Its status CHECK has no 'cancelled' and its owner FK is ON DELETE RESTRICT — both superseded later.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql` (578 lines) - Cancellation + observability. Adds 4 tables (upload_job_events, ingestion_workers, operational_alerts, security_audit_events), the record_upload_job_event AFTER trigger, and cancellation-aware replacements for claim_upload_job / schedule_upload_retry / commit_upload_ingestion / claim_upload_cleanup / expire_upload_jobs. Also *narrows* service_role on its four new tables to SELECT (+UPDATE on alerts, +INSERT on audit).
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260725_normalized_academic_catalog.sql` (147 lines) - PI-04 catalog: departments.code/active, `programs`, `specializations`, program_id/specialization_id on six tables, and the hydrate + validate triggers on papers/profiles.
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql` (163 lines) - Adds papers.thesis_category (+ a never-written mirror on upload_jobs) and appends the defaulted `p_thesis_category` param to both retrieval RPCs (7-arg / 6-arg).
- `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql` (122 lines) - Newest migration. Re-emits both retrieval RPCs with `set hnsw.ef_search = 100` and nothing else changed. This file, not 20260819, is the authoritative definition of the evaluated retrieval path.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py` (272 lines) - The drift guard. Parses `create or replace function` blocks from both sources, diffs base-vs-newest-migration, pins a 6-entry KNOWN_BASE_SCHEMA_DRIFT allowlist, pins the 14-function cross-migration redefinition inventory, and asserts three must-hold behaviours in both files.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py` (~140 lines (first class only read)) - Static SQL applicability: no stray '+' diff markers, balanced `$$` pairs, plus text assertions on 20260717/20260719/20260720/20260829 and supabase_setup.sql.
- `rag-thesis-backend/main.py` (n/a) - `_verify_database_contract` at :263 — four `.select().limit(1)` probes (profiles, departments, papers, paper_index_versions). `/health` caches it 15 s (:241); `/ready` at :325 does not.
- `supabase/.temp/` (9 small files) - Supabase CLI link state, gitignored (.gitignore:75). Records the live stack: Postgres 17.6.1.155, PostgREST v14.5, GoTrue v2.195.0, Storage v1.71.0, CLI v2.116.0.

### Control flow
