Three flows matter. **(1) Ingestion.** `routers/upload.py:786` builds `request_payload` (title, authors, year, abstract, program_id, specialization_id, legacy_track, classification_status, thesis_category, department, uploader_id) and calls `reserve_upload_job` with a client-supplied or generated Idempotency-Key. The row lands in `status='staging'` and is invisible to `claim_upload_job` because that predicate requires `source_stored = true`. Only after the PDF is written to `pdfs/uploads/<user>/<job>/<file>` does `routers/upload.py:609` call `queue_upload_job`, flipping to `queued` and setting `source_stored`. The worker's `claim_upload_job` performs three housekeeping sweeps before its `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` pick, so a single call both reaps stale staging/lease-expired jobs and claims work — meaning the queue self-heals only while a worker is polling. `services/ingestion.process_ingestion_job` then drives `heartbeat_upload_job_control` at each stage boundary; that RPC returns `{lease_valid, cancel_requested}`, which is the only channel by which an admin's `request_upload_cancellation` reaches a mid-flight pipeline. At the end `commit_upload_ingestion` takes `FOR UPDATE` on the job and, inside one transaction, re-checks cancellation → lease → paper-id-equals-job-id, delegates to `commit_paper_ingestion`, and marks the job complete. Because paper id == job id, `a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification` can look the request payload back up during the papers INSERT — that trigger, not the RPC's column list, is what actually populates `papers.thesis_category`/`program_id`/`specialization_id`/`legacy_track`/`classification_status`. `services/ingestion.py:248` also handles the lost-response case by re-reading `upload_jobs.status` and `papers.chunk_count` and treating a matching pair as a successful commit, which is safe precisely because `commit_paper_ingestion` short-circuits on an already-ready paper. On failure, `fail_upload_job` sets `cleanup_status='pending'`; a later `claim_upload_cleanup` → storage delete → `finish_upload_cleanup` removes the staged PDF, and if the storage delete throws, `services/cleanup.record_storage_cleanup` writes a `storage_cleanup_queue` row and the job is marked `delegated` instead of `completed`, which deliberately keeps `expire_upload_jobs` from ever deleting it.

**(2) Retrieval.** `services/retriever.search_chunks` embeds the query, then calls `match_chunks` with `match_count = settings.retrieval_candidate_pool` (15, not 5), `match_threshold = 0.30`, the resolved department, and `retrieval_provenance_params()` = `{p_embedding_model: settings.gemini_embed_model, p_embedding_dimensions: 768}`. `p_thesis_category` is omitted entirely unless a category scope was requested, so PostgREST resolves the call through the parameter default and the evaluated pipeline is byte-identical to the pre-20260819 shape. Inside the RPC the HNSW index supplies candidates ordered by `embedding <=> query_embedding`; six equality predicates and the similarity floor are applied *after* that, which is exactly why `SET hnsw.ef_search = 100` is attached to the function — a default-40 candidate list can be exhausted by the filters and return a short context with no error. Selection (hybrid rerank, per-paper cap, final 5 blocks) all happens in Python; the RPC is a pure candidate source. `check_topic_duplication` is the same query with `LIMIT 1` at the 0.85 threshold, and `routers/duplication.py:135` / `services/novelty.py:353` reuse `match_chunks` at `match_count=1, match_threshold=0.85` per chunk in a serial loop — one round trip per chunk, deliberately not batched because batching would alter a scored pipeline stage.

**(3) Reindex and index lifecycle.** Every paper carries `active_index_version`, and every (paper, version) pair carries exactly one immutable `paper_index_versions` row. `scripts/reindex_citations.apply_paper` stages a new version by *direct table writes* (`paper_index_versions` insert, then `chunks` inserts in batches of 100) — this is the one place that bypasses an RPC, which is why `grant all on paper_index_versions to service_role` exists. It then re-reads the staged rows, verifies `metadata.tokenizer`, `chunking_version`, per-chunk `token_count <= 800`, and that the stored fingerprint equals `current_index_fingerprint()` exactly, and only then calls `activate_paper_index`, which is the sole atomic cutover (it flips `active_index_version` and recomputes `chunk_count` in the same statement pair). If anything before activation throws, the script deletes the staged chunks and then the staged version row — in that order, because `chunks_index_provenance_fkey` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`. Old versions linger as rollback material until `prune_inactive_indexes` removes them, and `match_chunks` never sees them because of the `c.index_version = p.active_index_version` predicate.

### Public surface

- TABLE public.profiles — id uuid PK → auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE; email, full_name, avatar_url text; role text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'student' CHECK IN (student|faculty|admin|superadmin); department text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CCSICT' → departments(name) ON UPDATE CASCADE ON DELETE RESTRICT (NOT VALID); status text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'approved' CHECK IN (pending|approved|rejected); created_at, updated_at timestamptz; program_id/specialization_id uuid (20260725). Indexes: profiles_department_idx(department), profiles_role_status_idx(role,status), profiles_program_specialization_idx(program_id,specialization_id). RLS ON. Policies: profiles_select_own (SELECT USING auth.uid()=id), profiles_update_own_name (UPDATE USING+WITH CHECK auth.uid()=id). Grants: SELECT to authenticated; UPDATE only on columns (full_name, avatar_url) to authenticated (supabase_setup.sql:1174-1175). supabase_setup.sql:31.
- TABLE public.departments — id uuid PK; name text UNIQUE (the FK target used everywhere, i.e. the *code* string, not the prose title); track_label text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Academic track'; tracks jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'; created_at; title text (20260819); code text NOT NULL UNIQUE via departments_code_uq (20260725:10); active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true. RLS ON, zero policies, all privileges revoked from anon/authenticated → deny-all for clients. supabase_setup.sql:151.
- TABLE public.papers — id uuid PK; title NOT NULL; authors, year int, abstract, track, content, filename, storage_path; chunk_count int DEFAULT 0; duplication_scan jsonb; uploaded_by uuid → auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL; department NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CCSICT' (FK NOT VALID); active_index_version uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(); ingestion_status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ready' CHECK IN (processing|ready|failed|deletion_pending); redaction_stats jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'; thesis_category NOT NULL DEFAULT 'student' CHECK IN (student|faculty); program_id/specialization_id uuid → programs/specializations ON DELETE RESTRICT; legacy_track text; classification_status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unclassified' CHECK IN (classified|needs_review|unclassified); created_at. **No updated_at column.** Indexes: papers_track_idx, papers_year_idx, papers_department_idx, papers_thesis_category_idx(department,thesis_category) WHERE ingestion_status='ready', papers_program_specialization_idx(department,program_id,specialization_id) WHERE ingestion_status='ready'. RLS ON, no policies. supabase_setup.sql:222.
- TABLE public.chunks — id bigserial PK; paper_id uuid → papers ON DELETE CASCADE; chunk_index int NOT NULL; content text NOT NULL; metadata jsonb DEFAULT '{}'; embedding vector(768); page_start/page_end int; section text; index_version uuid NOT NULL; indexed_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(). Composite FK chunks_index_provenance_fkey (paper_id,index_version) → paper_index_versions ON DELETE RESTRICT. Indexes: chunks_paper_id_idx(paper_id) [redundant with the next index's prefix], chunks_embedding_idx USING hnsw (embedding vector_cosine_ops) with NO WITH clause → pgvector defaults m=16, ef_construction=64 (supabase_setup.sql:311-312), UNIQUE chunks_version_position_idx(paper_id,index_version,chunk_index). RLS ON, no policies. supabase_setup.sql:284.
- TABLE public.paper_index_versions — PK (paper_id,index_version); embedding_model text NOT NULL; embedding_dimensions int NOT NULL CHECK (=768); preprocessing_version, chunking_version text NOT NULL; tokenizer text; chunk_size_tokens int CHECK (null or >0); chunk_overlap_tokens int CHECK (null or >=0); provenance_status text NOT NULL CHECK IN (verified|legacy_assumed); CONSTRAINT verified_index_provenance_is_current CHECK (status='legacy_assumed' OR (preprocessing_version='document-v1' AND chunking_version='token-v1' AND tokenizer='cl100k_base' AND chunk_size_tokens=800 AND chunk_overlap_tokens=100)); created_at, activated_at. RLS ON, no policies; `grant all` to service_role (the reindex script inserts directly). supabase_setup.sql:317 / 20260720:6.
- TABLE public.chat_sessions — id uuid PK; user_id → auth.users ON DELETE CASCADE; title NOT NULL DEFAULT 'New conversation'; department NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CCSICT' (FK NOT VALID); created_at; program_id/specialization_id (never written). Indexes: chat_sessions_user_idx(user_id,created_at desc), chat_sessions_department_idx(department,created_at desc). RLS ON, all client policies dropped. supabase_setup.sql:582.
- TABLE public.chat_messages — id uuid PK; session_id → chat_sessions ON DELETE CASCADE; question, answer text NOT NULL; sources jsonb DEFAULT '[]'; duplication_alert jsonb; kind text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'answer' CHECK IN (answer|notice); created_at. Indexes: chat_messages_session_idx(session_id,created_at), partial chat_messages_session_answer_idx(session_id,created_at desc) WHERE kind='answer'. RLS ON, no policies. supabase_setup.sql:592.
- TABLE public.scan_history — id uuid PK; user_id → auth.users CASCADE; filename NOT NULL; duplication_percentage float8 NOT NULL DEFAULT 0; top_matches/matched_chunks/chat_log jsonb DEFAULT '[]'; verdict_summary text; department NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CCSICT' (FK NOT VALID); highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage float8; matched_chunk_count, total_chunks int; verdict_level NOT NULL DEFAULT 'clear' CHECK IN (clear|review_suggested|high_overlap); created_at; program_id/specialization_id (never written). Indexes: scan_history_user_idx(user_id,created_at desc), scan_history_department_idx(department). RLS ON, no policies, all privileges revoked. supabase_setup.sql:690.
- TABLE public.system_settings — key text PK; value jsonb NOT NULL; description text; updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(). Seeded with exactly one row, `role_features`, whose default is {"student":{chat,archive:true,novelty,upload:false},"faculty":{chat,archive,novelty:true,upload:false}} — mirrored in routers/settings.py:18 DEFAULT_FEATURES. The setup script also DELETEs any legacy duplication_threshold / retrieval_threshold / retrieval_match_count rows (supabase_setup.sql:742) because those constants are now frozen in config.py. RLS ON, no policies.
- TABLE public.activity_log — id bigserial PK; user_id → auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL; action text NOT NULL (free text: chat_query, paper_upload, novelty_scan, paper_delete, paper_delete_pending, role_change, …); department text (FK NOT VALID); detail jsonb DEFAULT '{}'; created_at; program_id/specialization_id (never written). Indexes: activity_log_action_idx(action,created_at desc), activity_log_department_idx(department,created_at desc). RLS ON, no policies.
- TABLE public.storage_cleanup_queue — id bigserial PK; operation text NOT NULL CHECK IN (rollback_upload|delete_paper); resource_path text NOT NULL; paper_id → papers ON DELETE SET NULL; job_id text; error_category text NOT NULL (an exception class name, never the message); status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending' CHECK IN (pending|completed); attempts int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0; created_at, updated_at. Partial index storage_cleanup_pending_idx(status,created_at). RLS ON, no policies. supabase_setup.sql:406.
- TABLE public.upload_jobs — 39 columns. Core: id uuid PK (== the eventual papers.id); owner_id uuid NULL → auth.users ON DELETE SET NULL (20260803); department NOT NULL (FK NOT VALID); status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued' CHECK IN (staging|queued|processing|retry_wait|completed|failed|cancelled); stage text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'extract' — **no CHECK constraint** (live values: store, queued, download, extract, malware_scan, chunk, embed, screen, index, done, error, cancelled); progress int CHECK BETWEEN 0 AND 100; message, error text; paper_id → papers ON DELETE SET NULL; chunks int; duplication jsonb. Durability: idempotency_key uuid NOT NULL, source_path, source_stored bool, original_filename, content_sha256, request_payload jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}', attempt_count CHECK >=0, max_attempts CHECK BETWEEN 1 AND 10, next_retry_at, lease_owner text, lease_expires_at, heartbeat_at, started_at, completed_at, expires_at, failure_category, cleanup_status CHECK IN (not_required|pending|processing|completed|delegated). Cancellation: cancel_requested_at, cancel_requested_by uuid (no FK), cancel_reason, cancelled_at. Dead mirrors: thesis_category, program_id, specialization_id, legacy_track, classification_status. Indexes: UNIQUE upload_jobs_owner_idempotency_uidx(owner_id,idempotency_key); upload_jobs_owner_idx; upload_jobs_status_idx; partial upload_jobs_claim_idx(status,next_retry_at,created_at) WHERE status IN (queued,retry_wait,processing); partial upload_jobs_lease_idx(lease_expires_at) WHERE status='processing'; partial upload_jobs_cleanup_idx(cleanup_status,completed_at) WHERE cleanup_status='pending'; partial upload_jobs_expiry_idx(expires_at) WHERE status IN (completed,failed); partial upload_jobs_cancel_idx(cancel_requested_at) WHERE cancel_requested_at IS NOT NULL AND status='processing' (migration only). RLS ON, no policies.
- TABLE public.programs (20260725:12) — id uuid PK; department_id → departments(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT; code text CHECK (~'^[A-Z0-9-]+$'); name text CHECK (length(btrim(name)) BETWEEN 2 AND 120); active bool NOT NULL DEFAULT true; created_at, updated_at; UNIQUE (department_id, code). Seeded: 5 CCSICT programs with pinned UUIDs 10000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000000{1..5} (BSCS, BSIT, BSDSA, BSIS, BLIS) plus 32 standby-college programs from 20260819. RLS ON, no policies.
- TABLE public.specializations (20260725:23) — id uuid PK; program_id → programs ON DELETE RESTRICT; code/name with the same CHECKs; active; UNIQUE (program_id, code). Seeded: 3 CCSICT specializations with pinned UUIDs 20000000-…-0000000{1,2,3} (DM under BSCS; WMAD, NETSEC under BSIT) plus 9 majors under CBAPA/CED from 20260819. RLS ON, no policies.
- TABLE public.upload_job_events (20260724:21) — id bigint GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY PK; job_id uuid → upload_jobs ON DELETE SET NULL (deliberately, so 90-day events outlive 30-day jobs); owner_id, department, event_type NOT NULL, status NOT NULL, stage, attempt_count, worker_id, details jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}', created_at. Indexes: (job_id,created_at desc), (created_at). RLS ON; service_role has SELECT only.
- TABLE public.ingestion_workers (20260724:48) — worker_id text PK; state CHECK IN (starting|idle|processing|degraded|stopping); current_job_id uuid (no FK); scanner_status CHECK IN (unknown|disabled|healthy|unavailable); version text; started_at, last_seen_at, stopped_at. Index (last_seen_at desc). RLS ON; SELECT only for service_role — writes go through register_ingestion_worker / stop_ingestion_worker.
- TABLE public.operational_alerts (20260724:64) — id uuid PK; dedupe_key text NOT NULL UNIQUE; alert_type; severity CHECK IN (info|warning|critical); status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'open' CHECK IN (open|acknowledged|resolved); safe_details jsonb; occurrence_count int CHECK >0; first_seen_at, last_seen_at, last_notified_at, acknowledged_at, acknowledged_by uuid, resolved_at, created_at, updated_at. Index (status,severity,last_seen_at desc). RLS ON; service_role has SELECT + UPDATE only — **no INSERT**.
- TABLE public.security_audit_events (20260724:85) — id bigint identity PK; actor_id uuid; event_type NOT NULL; severity CHECK IN (info|warning|critical); department; safe_details jsonb; created_at. Index (created_at desc). RLS ON; service_role has SELECT + INSERT and USAGE on security_audit_events_id_seq.
- TABLE public.backup_runs (20260804_backup_runs.sql:26) — id uuid PK; backup_id text NOT NULL UNIQUE (the yyyy-MM-dd-HHmmss folder stamp); completed_at NOT NULL; artifact_count int CHECK >=0; total_bytes bigint CHECK >=0; manifest_sha256, host_fingerprint text (opaque, no hostnames/paths); created_at. Index (completed_at desc). RLS ON; service_role SELECT only.
- STORAGE — bucket `pdfs`: private (public=false), forced on conflict. bucket `avatars`: public=true, file_size_limit 2097152 (2 MiB), allowed_mime_types {image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp}. Policies on storage.objects: `pdfs_indirect_access_only` RESTRICTIVE FOR ALL TO anon,authenticated USING/WITH CHECK (bucket_id <> 'pdfs'); `avatars_public_read` SELECT TO public; `avatars_owner_insert|update|delete` TO authenticated requiring (storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text. supabase_setup.sql:1222-1261.
- RPC public.match_chunks(query_embedding vector(768), match_count integer, match_threshold double precision, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions integer, p_thesis_category text DEFAULT null) RETURNS TABLE(id bigint, paper_id uuid, chunk_index integer, content text, metadata jsonb, page_start integer, page_end integer, section text, similarity double precision) — authoritative definition: migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:35, `language sql STABLE`, `SET hnsw.ef_search = 100`, NOT security definer. Joins chunks→papers→paper_index_versions; predicates: c.index_version = p.active_index_version AND p.ingestion_status='ready' AND piv.embedding_model = p_embedding_model AND piv.embedding_dimensions = p_embedding_dimensions AND piv.provenance_status IN ('verified','legacy_assumed') AND 1-(embedding<=>q) >= match_threshold AND (p_department IS NULL OR p.department = p_department) AND (p_thesis_category IS NULL OR p.thesis_category = p_thesis_category); ORDER BY embedding <=> q LIMIT match_count. Revoked from public/anon/authenticated, EXECUTE to service_role. Callers pass match_count = retrieval_candidate_pool (15), not 5 (services/retriever.py:783); the duplication/novelty callers pass match_count=1 with match_threshold=0.85.
- RPC public.check_topic_duplication(query_embedding vector(768), dup_threshold double precision, p_department text, p_embedding_model text, p_embedding_dimensions integer, p_thesis_category text DEFAULT null) RETURNS TABLE(chunk_id bigint, paper_id uuid, title text, authors text, year integer, track text, abstract text, chunk_content text, chunk_index integer, department text, page_start integer, page_end integer, section text, similarity double precision) — migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:78. Identical predicate set, hard `LIMIT 1`. service_role only.
- RPC public.commit_paper_ingestion(p_paper jsonb, p_chunks jsonb) RETURNS uuid — supabase_setup.sql:465 / 20260720:106. plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public. Whole-function transactional. Validates: non-blank title; p_chunks is a non-empty array; p_paper->'index_provenance' is an object whose embedding_dimensions=768, preprocessing_version='document-v1', chunking_version='token-v1', tokenizer='cl100k_base', chunk_size_tokens=800, chunk_overlap_tokens=100, provenance_status='verified' (any deviation → 'Verified 768-dimensional index provenance is required'); p_paper->>'chunk_count' == jsonb_array_length(p_chunks). Idempotent short-circuit: if a papers row with this id is already 'ready' with matching chunk_count and matching COUNT(chunks) it returns the id unchanged; if the id exists in any other state it raises 'Paper ingestion ID already exists in an incomplete state'. Then inserts papers (ingestion_status='processing'), one paper_index_versions row (provenance_status hard-coded 'verified'), each chunk (rejecting any embedding array whose length ≠ 768 or empty content), re-counts, and only then flips ingestion_status='ready' and stamps activated_at. Note the insert column list has no thesis_category/program_id/specialization_id — those arrive via the BEFORE INSERT hydrate trigger, and `content` is passed as p_paper->>'content' which the application never supplies, so papers.content is always NULL.
- RPC public.commit_upload_ingestion(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text, p_paper jsonb, p_chunks jsonb) RETURNS uuid — 20260724:421 (authoritative) / supabase_setup.sql:1665. plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER. `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the job row, then in order: not-found → raise; already completed with a paper_id → return it (idempotent); **cancel_requested_at IS NOT NULL → raise 'Upload cancellation was requested'**; status ≠ 'processing' OR lease_owner ≠ p_worker_id OR lease_expires_at < now() → raise 'Upload worker lease is no longer valid'; p_paper->>'id' ≠ p_job_id → raise. Calls commit_paper_ingestion, then marks the job completed/done/100 with chunks, duplication, cleanup_status='not_required', expires_at = now()+30 days, lease cleared. The whole thing is one transaction, so a failed paper commit rolls the job update back too.
- RPC public.save_chat_exchange(p_user_id uuid, p_session_id uuid, p_title text, p_question text, p_answer text, p_sources jsonb, p_duplication_alert jsonb, p_department text, p_kind text DEFAULT 'answer') RETURNS uuid — supabase_setup.sql:626 / 20260804_chat_message_kind.sql:65. plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER. Rejects p_kind outside {answer,notice}. NULL session → creates a chat_sessions row with left(p_title,120) and p_department; non-NULL session → must exist AND be owned by p_user_id AND match p_department, else 'Session not found, not owned by user, or belongs to another department'. Inserts the message and returns the session id. The 8-argument variant is explicitly DROPped (supabase_setup.sql:679) because PostgreSQL treats it as a separate function, not an overload.
- RPC public.activate_paper_index(p_paper_id uuid, p_index_version uuid) RETURNS void — migrations/20260829_reindex_updates_chunk_count.sql:4 (authoritative) / supabase_setup.sql:995. plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER. COUNTs chunks for (paper,version); 0 → raise 'Cannot activate an empty or missing index version'; requires a paper_index_versions row with provenance_status IN (verified, legacy_assumed) else 'Cannot activate an index without compatible provenance'. Then atomically sets papers.active_index_version AND papers.chunk_count = the counted value, and stamps paper_index_versions.activated_at. The chunk_count update is the 20260829 addition; the 20260717 and 20260720 bodies do not have it.
- RPC public.prune_inactive_indexes(p_older_than_days integer DEFAULT 7) RETURNS integer — supabase_setup.sql:1039 / 20260720:344. plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER. Raises if p_older_than_days < 1. Builds a TEMP TABLE provenance_prune_versions (ON COMMIT DROP) of versions that are (a) not the active one, (b) older than the window by paper_index_versions.created_at, and (c) have another non-active version created after them. Deletes chunks first (RESTRICT FK requires that order), captures ROW_COUNT as the return value (chunks removed, not versions), then deletes the paper_index_versions rows.
- RPC public.reserve_upload_job(p_job_id uuid, p_owner_id uuid, p_department text, p_idempotency_key uuid, p_source_path text, p_original_filename text, p_content_sha256 text, p_request_payload jsonb, p_max_attempts integer DEFAULT 3) RETURNS TABLE(job_id uuid, job_status text, stored_source_path text, stored_content_sha256 text, created boolean) — supabase_setup.sql:1434 / 20260723:89. SECURITY DEFINER. Rejects NULL owner or key, any digest not matching '^[0-9a-f]{64}$', and max_attempts outside 1..10. INSERT … ON CONFLICT (owner_id, idempotency_key) DO NOTHING, then re-SELECTs the row; if the stored content_sha256 differs from the supplied one it raises with SQLSTATE 22000 and message 'Idempotency key was already used for different content' (routers/upload.py:809 matches on the substring 'different content' → HTTP 409). Row lands in status='staging'.
- RPC public.queue_upload_job(p_job_id uuid, p_owner_id uuid) RETURNS boolean — transitions staging → queued, sets source_stored=true, progress=8, next_retry_at=now(). Returns FOUND; only fires while status='staging'.
- RPC public.claim_upload_job(p_worker_id text, p_lease_seconds integer DEFAULT 120) RETURNS SETOF public.upload_jobs — 20260724:351 (authoritative) / supabase_setup.sql:1517 (older 20260723 body). Rejects blank worker id or lease outside 30..900. Three sweeps first: (1) staging rows untouched for >15 min → failed/'staging_timeout' (the 20260724 version skips rows with cancel_requested_at); (2) processing rows with expired lease AND cancel_requested_at → 'cancelled' (20260724 only); (3) processing rows with expired lease AND attempt_count >= max_attempts → failed/'worker_lease_expired'. Then SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED the oldest eligible job (queued, or retry_wait whose next_retry_at has passed, or processing with an expired lease) that has attempt_count < max_attempts AND source_stored, ORDER BY coalesce(next_retry_at, created_at), created_at, LIMIT 1 — the 20260724 version additionally requires cancel_requested_at IS NULL. Claims it: status='processing', stage='download', progress=10, attempt_count+1, lease_owner, lease_expires_at = now()+lease, heartbeat_at, started_at (first time only).
- RPC public.heartbeat_upload_job(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text, p_lease_seconds integer DEFAULT 120, p_stage text DEFAULT null, p_progress integer DEFAULT null, p_message text DEFAULT null) RETURNS boolean — extends the lease and COALESCE-updates stage/progress/message only while status='processing' AND lease_owner matches AND lease_expires_at >= now(). Used only as a fallback when the control variant is missing (services/upload_queue.py:61-71 catches PGRST202 / 42883).
- RPC public.heartbeat_upload_job_control(same 6 args) RETURNS jsonb {lease_valid: bool, cancel_requested: bool} — 20260724:218. Same lease predicate, but refuses to overwrite `message` once cancel_requested_at is set, and returns the cancellation flag so the pipeline can stop at its next checkpoint. Migration-only; absent from supabase_setup.sql.
- RPC public.schedule_upload_retry(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text, p_retry_at timestamptz, p_failure_category text) RETURNS boolean — 20260724:403 (authoritative). Requires a live lease and attempt_count < max_attempts AND cancel_requested_at IS NULL. Sets status='retry_wait', clears error, failure_category = left(p_failure_category,120), next_retry_at = greatest(p_retry_at, now()), clears the lease. Python supplies p_retry_at from services/upload_queue.py:_RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600) seconds, raised to any provider Retry-After up to 15 min.
- RPC public.fail_upload_job(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text, p_failure_category text, p_public_error text) RETURNS boolean — terminal failure under a live lease: status='failed', stage='error', progress=100, error=p_public_error, cleanup_status='pending' iff source_stored, completed_at=now(), expires_at=now()+30 days, lease cleared. Defined identically in both sources (not in the drift allowlist).
- RPC public.claim_upload_cleanup(p_worker_id text) RETURNS SETOF public.upload_jobs — 20260724:449 (authoritative) picks status IN ('failed','cancelled') AND cleanup_status='pending' AND source_stored, FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, and stamps cleanup_status='processing' with a fixed 120-second lease. The supabase_setup.sql:1717 copy only considers 'failed'.
- RPC public.finish_upload_cleanup(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text, p_delegated boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS boolean — sets cleanup_status to 'delegated' (keeping source_stored) or 'completed' (clearing source_stored), and releases the lease. 'delegated' means the storage delete failed and a storage_cleanup_queue row was written instead.
- RPC public.expire_upload_jobs() RETURNS integer — 20260724:465 (authoritative). First recovers cleanup claims whose lease expired (processing → pending). Then DELETEs rows past expires_at that are either completed with cleanup_status='not_required', or (failed|cancelled) with source_stored=false and cleanup_status IN (not_required, completed). Returns the delete count. The supabase_setup.sql:1763 copy omits 'cancelled' from the second branch.
- RPC public.request_upload_cancellation(p_job_id uuid, p_requester_id uuid, p_is_superadmin boolean DEFAULT false, p_reason text DEFAULT null) RETURNS jsonb — 20260724:157, migration only. FOR UPDATE on the job. Returns {'outcome':'not_found'} / {'outcome':'forbidden'} when not superadmin and owner_id ≠ requester / {'outcome':'already_terminal','status':…} for completed|failed|cancelled. For status='processing' it only *requests* (sets cancel_requested_at/by/reason, message changed) → outcome 'cancellation_requested'; for any other live status it cancels immediately → outcome 'cancelled', cleanup_status='pending' iff source_stored. Reason is truncated to 200 chars.
- RPC public.finalize_upload_cancellation(p_job_id uuid, p_worker_id text) RETURNS boolean — 20260724:250, migration only. The worker's own confirmation after it stops at a checkpoint: requires a live lease AND cancel_requested_at IS NOT NULL, then sets status='cancelled', cleanup_status='pending' iff source_stored.
- RPC public.register_ingestion_worker(p_worker_id text, p_state text, p_scanner_status text, p_version text, p_current_job_id uuid DEFAULT null) RETURNS boolean — 20260724:275. UPSERT on worker_id, version truncated to 80 chars, clears stopped_at. RPC public.stop_ingestion_worker(p_worker_id text) RETURNS boolean — sets state='stopping', clears current_job_id, stamps stopped_at.
- RPC public.apply_operations_retention(p_apply boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS jsonb — 20260724:318. Dry-run by default: counts upload_job_events older than 90 days, resolved operational_alerts resolved >90 days ago, and security_audit_events older than 1 year; deletes them only when p_apply. Returns {applied, upload_job_events, resolved_operational_alerts, security_audit_events}.
- RPC public.upsert_operational_alert(p_dedupe_key text, p_alert_type text, p_severity text, p_safe_details jsonb DEFAULT '{}') RETURNS SETOF public.operational_alerts — 20260724_operations_security_fixes.sql:7 (authoritative; identical body to 20260724:483). `language sql` SECURITY DEFINER. INSERT … ON CONFLICT (dedupe_key) DO UPDATE: reopens a resolved alert, increments occurrence_count only on reopen, preserves acknowledged_at/by while the alert is still active, always clears resolved_at. dedupe_key truncated to 160, alert_type to 120.
- RPC public.record_backup_run(p_backup_id text, p_completed_at timestamptz, p_artifact_count integer, p_total_bytes bigint, p_manifest_sha256 text DEFAULT null, p_host_fingerprint text DEFAULT null) RETURNS uuid — 20260804_backup_runs.sql:47. SECURITY DEFINER. Rejects blank backup_id, NULL completion time, and artifact_count <= 0 ('A completed backup must record at least one artifact'). UPSERT on backup_id. Called over raw REST by scripts/record_backup_run.ps1:108.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.handle_new_user() → trigger on_auth_user_created AFTER INSERT ON auth.users. SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, EXECUTE granted to supabase_auth_admin. Authoritative body = 20260828_allow_any_email_signup.sql:10: role = 'faculty' iff raw_user_meta_data->>'requested_role'='faculty' else 'student'; department hard-coded 'CCSICT'; status = 'pending' for faculty else 'approved'; ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.sync_profile_email() → on_auth_user_email_changed AFTER UPDATE OF email ON auth.users WHEN (new.email IS DISTINCT FROM old.email). Mirrors the address into profiles and bumps updated_at.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.validate_editable_profile_fields() → validate_editable_profile_fields BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF full_name, avatar_url ON profiles. Rejects whitespace-only full_name; requires avatar_url to be prefixed with '<profile id>/' AND to exist as a row in storage.objects for bucket 'avatars'.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.protect_profile_security_fields() → protect_profile_security_fields BEFORE UPDATE ON profiles. Raises 'Protected profile fields may only be changed by the backend' when auth.role()='authenticated' AND auth.uid()=old.id AND any of role/status/department/email changed. Defence in depth behind the column-level UPDATE grant.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.record_upload_job_event() → record_upload_job_event AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE ON upload_jobs (20260724:98/140). SECURITY DEFINER. Emits exactly one upload_job_events row per statement, classified by the first matching rule: INSERT→'reserved'; new cancel_requested_at→'cancellation_requested'; status change→'status_changed'; stage change→'stage_changed'; cleanup_status change→'cleanup_changed'; otherwise no event. details = jsonb_strip_nulls({progress, cleanup_status, failure_category, cancel_requested}).
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.hydrate_paper_academic_classification() → a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification BEFORE INSERT ON papers (20260725:94/111; body superseded by 20260819_thesis_category.sql:47). NOT security definer, search_path=public. Reads upload_jobs.request_payload WHERE id = new.id and overwrites new.program_id, new.specialization_id, new.legacy_track, new.classification_status (default 'unclassified'), new.thesis_category (default 'student'). The 'a_' name prefix is load-bearing: same-timing triggers fire alphabetically, so it must sort before papers_validate_academic_classification.
- TRIGGER FUNCTION public.validate_academic_classification() → papers_validate_academic_classification and profiles_validate_academic_classification, BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF program_id, specialization_id (20260725:114/139/143). NULL program with non-NULL specialization → 'Specialization requires a program'; program must exist and be active; specialization must belong to that program and be active; program code IN ('BSCS','BSIT') requires a specialization; every other program code forbids one.

### Invariants

- Apply order is `supabase_setup.sql` then every `migrations/*.sql` (excluding `.rollback.sql`) in **filename order**. There is no migration ledger table, no checksum, and no application-side runner — order is an operator instruction enforced only by tests/test_schema_consistency.py::test_every_redefinition_chain_ends_at_the_newest_file.
- Fourteen functions are redefined across migrations and the set is pinned: activate_paper_index, check_topic_duplication, claim_upload_cleanup, claim_upload_job, commit_paper_ingestion, commit_upload_ingestion, expire_upload_jobs, handle_new_user, hydrate_paper_academic_classification, match_chunks, prune_inactive_indexes, save_chat_exchange, schedule_upload_retry, upsert_operational_alert (tests/test_schema_consistency.py:236).
- Exactly six functions may differ between supabase_setup.sql and the newest migration: match_chunks, check_topic_duplication, claim_upload_job, claim_upload_cleanup, expire_upload_jobs, schedule_upload_retry (KNOWN_BASE_SCHEMA_DRIFT, tests/test_schema_consistency.py:88). Adding a seventh fails test_no_undeclared_drift; resolving one without deleting its entry fails test_no_stale_allowlist_entries.
- Both retrieval RPCs must carry `set hnsw.ef_search = <n>` with n > 40, in **both** source files (tests/test_schema_consistency.py:186). Current value 100 = 20× retrieval_match_count.
- commit_upload_ingestion must contain `cancel_requested_at is not null` and the literal 'Upload cancellation was requested' in both source files (tests/test_schema_consistency.py:160).
- commit_paper_ingestion must mention paper_index_versions and 'provenance' in both source files — the pre-20260720 body writes no provenance row and every subsequent chunk insert then violates chunks_index_provenance_fkey (tests/test_schema_consistency.py:196).
- Embedding dimension 768 is enforced in four independent places: the `vector(768)` column type, `check (embedding_dimensions = 768)` on paper_index_versions, the RPC parameter type `vector(768)`, and a per-chunk `jsonb_array_length(embedding) <> 768` raise inside commit_paper_ingestion.
- Chunking parameters 800/100 with tokenizer cl100k_base and preprocessing_version document-v1 / chunking_version token-v1 are hard-coded twice: in the `verified_index_provenance_is_current` table constraint and again in commit_paper_ingestion's provenance validation. Changing config.py's Literal[800]/Literal[100] without a migration makes every new ingest raise.
- A `verified` paper_index_versions row can only ever describe the current frozen pipeline; anything else must be `legacy_assumed`.
- Only one index version per paper is searchable: `c.index_version = p.active_index_version` appears in both retrieval RPCs, so staged and rollback versions are invisible to retrieval by construction.
- Retrieval is embedding-space-safe: `piv.embedding_model = p_embedding_model AND piv.embedding_dimensions = p_embedding_dimensions`, with the values supplied by services/index_provenance.retrieval_provenance_params(). Vectors built by a different model can never match.
- The `pdfs` bucket is private and covered by a RESTRICTIVE storage policy denying anon/authenticated all operations; no application role can obtain a signed URL. The setup script's final DO block aborts the whole transaction if the bucket is missing or public (supabase_setup.sql:1304).
- Every mutating RPC is `security definer` with `set search_path = public`, revoked from public/anon/authenticated, and granted EXECUTE to service_role only. The two retrieval RPCs are the exception: invoker-rights, no search_path, but still service_role-only EXECUTE.
- Chunk deletion must precede paper_index_versions deletion wherever both happen (prune_inactive_indexes, the reindex rollback path) because of the ON DELETE RESTRICT composite FK. tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py pins this ordering for the migration text.
- upload_jobs dedupe is (owner_id, idempotency_key) UNIQUE; NULL owner_id (after an account delete) is treated as distinct by Postgres, so orphaned rows are historical and never deduplicated against.
- Worker mutations of a job require a live lease: status='processing' AND lease_owner = p_worker_id AND lease_expires_at >= now(). Every heartbeat/retry/fail/commit/finalize RPC repeats that triple.
- A 15-minute staging timeout and a 30-day terminal retention are baked into claim_upload_job and every terminal-transition RPC as literal intervals; they are not configurable from Python.
- Operations tables are write-protected even from service_role: upload_job_events and ingestion_workers are SELECT-only, operational_alerts is SELECT+UPDATE, security_audit_events is SELECT+INSERT (20260724:531-537). All other writes must go through their SECURITY DEFINER RPCs.
- Trigger firing order on papers INSERT depends on alphabetical trigger names: `a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification` must sort before `papers_validate_academic_classification`, otherwise validation runs against the pre-hydration (NULL) classification.

### Data model

- papers (1) ←→ (N) chunks via paper_id ON DELETE CASCADE, and (1) ←→ (N) paper_index_versions via paper_id ON DELETE CASCADE; chunks additionally references paper_index_versions on the composite (paper_id, index_version) with ON DELETE RESTRICT
- papers.active_index_version is a plain uuid column with NO foreign key to paper_index_versions — nothing at the schema level stops it pointing at a version row that does not exist; only activate_paper_index enforces that
- upload_jobs.id is deliberately reused as papers.id, which is how the hydrate trigger finds request_payload and how commit_upload_ingestion's `p_paper->>'id' = p_job_id` check works
- request_payload jsonb is the authoritative carrier for academic classification and thesis_category; the same-named upload_jobs columns are never written by any code path
- departments.name (the short code string, e.g. 'CCSICT') — not departments.id — is the FK target for profiles, papers, chat_sessions, scan_history, upload_jobs and activity_log, all ON UPDATE CASCADE / ON DELETE RESTRICT and all created NOT VALID
- departments.code + departments.id are the catalog-side keys used by programs.department_id; so departments carries two parallel identity schemes (name-as-code for scoping, id for the catalog tree)
- programs/specializations seeded with pinned UUID literals 10000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000000{1..5} and 20000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000000{1..3}; code paths and the 20260725 backfill CASE statements depend on those exact values
- system_settings holds exactly one live key, role_features, shaped {student|faculty: {chat, archive, novelty, upload: bool}} — admin/superadmin are not representable and are hard-coded in dependencies/auth.py
- chat_messages.kind ∈ {answer, notice} is the structural replacement for the earlier text-prefix matching; services/chat_notices.is_stored_non_answer still runs as a second, text-based filter after the SQL kind filter
- paper_index_versions is append-only in practice: no RPC updates it except activate_paper_index stamping activated_at

### Config knobs

- config.py:18 `gemini_embed_model` (default 'models/gemini-embedding-001') — flows into both retrieval RPCs as p_embedding_model and into every new paper_index_versions row. Changing it makes the whole existing index unreachable until a full reindex.
- config.py:21 `embedding_dimensions` Literal[768] — must match the vector(768) column and the `check (embedding_dimensions = 768)` constraint; changing it needs a DB migration.
- config.py:74-75 `chunk_size_tokens` Literal[800] / `chunk_overlap_tokens` Literal[100] — validated verbatim by commit_paper_ingestion and by verified_index_provenance_is_current.
- config.py:76 `retrieval_threshold` 0.30 → match_threshold; config.py:78 `duplication_threshold` 0.85 → dup_threshold and the per-chunk novelty screen.
- config.py:77 `retrieval_match_count` 5 (final context blocks, applied in Python) vs config.py:88 `retrieval_candidate_pool` 15 (what is actually sent as match_count) and config.py:89 `retrieval_per_paper_cap` 3.
- `hnsw.ef_search = 100` — a per-function SET clause, not an environment variable. Changing it means editing both RPCs in both files; 20260903_hnsw_ef_search.rollback.sql resets it to the server default of 40 via `alter function … reset`.
- `app.institutional_email_domain` Postgres GUC — read by the 20260803 handle_new_user body only (`alter database postgres set app.institutional_email_domain = 'isu.edu.ph'`). Inert under the current 20260828 body.
- `system_settings.role_features` jsonb — the only runtime-mutable policy in the database; edited via PUT /settings/features (superadmin), falls back to routers/settings.py:18 DEFAULT_FEATURES.
- `departments.active` — the single switch that scales a college up; 20260819 seeds nine colleges inactive and CCSICT active.
- `settings.ingestion_max_attempts` → reserve_upload_job's p_max_attempts (constrained 1..10 by both the RPC and a CHECK).
- `settings.operations_worker_stale_seconds` → the ingestion_workers.last_seen_at cutoff in main.py:295.
- `ALLOW_DISPOSABLE_SUPABASE_TESTS=1` — gates tests/test_supabase_rls_integration.py.
- `BACKUP_RPO_HOURS` — must be set to 0 before running 20260804_backup_runs.rollback.sql, or the operations monitor queries a dropped table.

### Error handling

- Every RPC signals failure by RAISE EXCEPTION, which PostgREST surfaces as HTTP 400/500 with the message in the JSON body; the Python layer string-matches. routers/upload.py:809 matches 'different content' → HTTP 409; services/upload_queue.py:63 matches 'pgrst202' / '42883' / 'could not find the function' to detect an unmigrated database and fall back from heartbeat_upload_job_control to heartbeat_upload_job.
- reserve_upload_job deliberately raises with `errcode = '22000'` for the idempotency-key-reuse case so it is distinguishable from a generic error.
- Lease-loss is not an exception path in the queue RPCs: heartbeat/retry/fail/finish all return `found` as a boolean, and the caller (`services/upload_queue._rpc_bool`) turns a false into a LeaseLostError. Only commit_upload_ingestion raises on a lost lease, because it has already done the work.
- commit_paper_ingestion's idempotent short-circuit is the error-recovery mechanism for a lost commit response: services/ingestion.py:248 re-reads upload_jobs.status + papers.ingestion_status/chunk_count and treats a matching pair as success rather than retrying.
- storage-delete failures never abort the flow: services/cleanup.record_storage_cleanup writes a storage_cleanup_queue row and returns True; finish_upload_cleanup is then called with p_delegated=true, which keeps source_stored set so expire_upload_jobs will not delete the job row. If even the queue insert fails, it logs CRITICAL and returns False and the cleanup claim is simply not finished — the 120-second cleanup lease expires and expire_upload_jobs resets it to 'pending'.
- routers/papers.py:133 returns HTTP 503 ('Private-file deletion is pending') after marking the paper `deletion_pending`, so a storage outage leaves a hidden-but-present row rather than an orphaned PDF.
- The setup script is one transaction and ends with a DO block (supabase_setup.sql:1284-1315) that raises if any of the 12 required tables, profiles.avatar_url, the CCSICT department seed, the private pdfs bucket, or the public avatars bucket is missing — so a partial apply rolls back entirely.
- Catalog reads degrade rather than fail: routers/catalog.py:86 catches any exception from the programs/specializations queries and falls back to `_legacy_catalog()`, and there is a nested fallback at :77 for projects that have PI-04 but not the `title` column.
- RLS denial does not produce an error for anon/authenticated reads of policy-less tables — it returns an empty set, which is why every one of those tables also has its privileges REVOKEd (privilege denial does raise 42501).

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py — the primary guard. TestTheScannerWorks (parser sanity: ≥15 base functions, ≥25 migration functions, rollback files excluded, filename order is sorted); TestBaseSchemaMatchesNewestMigration (no undeclared base-vs-migration drift; no stale allowlist entries); TestGuaranteesThatMustHoldInBothFiles (commit_upload_ingestion cancellation guard; hnsw.ef_search > 40 on both retrieval RPCs; commit_paper_ingestion mentions paper_index_versions + provenance); TestEarlierMigrationsCannotOverwriteLaterOnes (the exact 14-function redefinition set, and that the newest definition always lives in the highest filename).
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py — TestSqlFilesAreApplicable (no line starts with '+', `$$` count is even, across supabase_setup.sql and all 27 migration files); TestMigrationContract (text assertions on 20260717's threshold/department/active-version predicates and grants, 20260719's upload_jobs + save_chat_exchange + storage contracts, 20260720's provenance table/backfill/FK/activation guard, the prune ordering, and 20260829's chunk_count recomputation before the active_index_version write); TestReindexDryRun (zero external calls, 768 dims, token-v1, cl100k_base, token_count <= 800, overlap <= 100).
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_supabase_rls_integration.py — opt-in via ALLOW_DISPOSABLE_SUPABASE_TESTS=1; exercises real RLS against a disposable project (creates users, inserts a paper_index_versions row with embedding_model 'models/gemini-embedding-2' at :271, deletes upload_jobs rows and users). No cascade-delete-of-papers coverage.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_index_provenance.py — compatibility matrix for is_embedding_compatible, including 'models/gemini-embedding-2' at 3072 and 768 dims.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_readme_accuracy.py — pins the README's factual claims, which include the migration-order warnings and the seven-migration-only-tables table at README.md:52-58.

### Gotchas

- **The legacy provenance backfill writes a model name that retrieval can never match.** supabase_setup.sql:354 and migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:48 stamp every pre-provenance index with `'models/gemini-embedding-2'`, while config.py:18 sets `gemini_embed_model = 'models/gemini-embedding-001'` and both retrieval RPCs filter `piv.embedding_model = p_embedding_model`. Any paper whose provenance row came from that backfill is permanently invisible to /chat retrieval and to duplication screening, silently — the query succeeds and returns nothing. Documented in docs/FIDELITY_AUDIT_2026-09-02.html:313 and evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:679, and pinned as expected text by tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:95, so the literal cannot be corrected without also editing that test.
- **prune_inactive_indexes never prunes the first rollback version.** Its `exists (… newer.index_version <> p.active_index_version and newer.index_version <> piv.index_version and newer.created_at > piv.created_at)` clause requires a *third* version. After a single reindex a paper has exactly two versions (old + active) and the old one is retained forever regardless of `p_older_than_days`. The '7-day rollback window' only starts behaving as described from the third index build onward.
- **supabase_setup.sql ships the pre-cancellation bodies of four queue RPCs.** `claim_upload_job` (:1517), `claim_upload_cleanup` (:1717), `expire_upload_jobs` (:1763) and `schedule_upload_retry` (:1613) are the 20260723 bodies, not the cancellation-aware 20260724 ones — even though the base file *does* add the cancel_* columns (:1367-1371) and *does* allow `'cancelled'` in the status CHECK (:1378), and even though `commit_upload_ingestion` (:1665) *was* backported with its cancellation guard. On a base-only project a cancelled job is never cleaned up (claim_upload_cleanup only looks at 'failed'), never expired (expire_upload_jobs omits 'cancelled'), and a cancel-requested job can still be claimed and retried. The allowlist entry calls this 'reformatting', which understates it. In a correct full install the 20260724 bodies win, so this only bites base-only installs and out-of-order replays.
- **The operational_alerts fallback path is unreachable-by-construction dead code.** services/operations.py:129 does `client.table('operational_alerts').insert(...)` when the `upsert_operational_alert` RPC is missing, but 20260724:531-534 revokes ALL from service_role and grants only SELECT and UPDATE on that table. If the RPC is missing the table does not exist either; if the table exists the insert is a guaranteed 42501. Same for the UPDATE-on-existing branch, which does work (UPDATE is granted).
- **Six department foreign keys were created NOT VALID and are never validated.** supabase_setup.sql:800-825 and 20260719:118-143 add profiles/papers/scan_history/chat_sessions/upload_jobs/activity_log → departments(name) with `not valid`, and no migration ever issues `ALTER TABLE … VALIDATE CONSTRAINT`. New and updated rows are checked; pre-existing violating rows persist indefinitely and will not surface until someone tries to validate or dump/restore.
- **Deleting a paper depends on undocumented RI-trigger ordering.** `papers → chunks` and `papers → paper_index_versions` are both ON DELETE CASCADE, while `chunks → paper_index_versions` is ON DELETE RESTRICT. Postgres fires same-timing constraint triggers alphabetically by name (`RI_ConstraintTrigger_a_<oid>`), so the chunks cascade happens first only because the chunks FK has the lower OID. It works today (routers/papers.py:156 and routers/maintenance.py:181 both DELETE from papers) but nothing pins it, and tests/test_supabase_rls_integration.py has no cascade-delete coverage. Recreating the chunks FK later would flip the order and start raising 'update or delete on table paper_index_versions violates foreign key constraint'.
- **Five upload_jobs columns and six program/specialization columns are dead.** `upload_jobs.thesis_category`, `.program_id`, `.specialization_id`, `.legacy_track`, `.classification_status` are described as 'forward-compat mirrors'; `reserve_upload_job` does not write them and nothing else does, so they sit at their defaults forever. Likewise `chat_sessions.program_id/specialization_id`, `scan_history.program_id/specialization_id` and `activity_log.program_id/specialization_id` added by 20260725 are never read or written by any code path.
- **Replaying 20260803_institutional_signup_domain.sql silently re-enables the institutional email gate.** 20260828_allow_any_email_signup.sql is the authoritative `handle_new_user`; the 20260803 body puts every off-`isu.edu.ph` signup into `status='pending'`, read from the `app.institutional_email_domain` GUC. Both files end with `drop trigger … create trigger`, so the last one applied wins. The base file agrees with 20260828.
- **20260803_upload_jobs_owner_on_delete_set_null.sql references a function name that does not exist.** Its header justifies the change with 'stage_upload_job rejects a null p_owner_id'; the function is `reserve_upload_job`. Harmless, but it will send a reader looking for the wrong object.
- **20260717_rag_items_9_16.sql:2 says 'This file is NOT executed by the application', while README.md:50 says to apply every file in migrations/.** It also defines `match_chunks` and `check_topic_duplication` with plain `create function` (not `create or replace`), which means tests/test_schema_consistency.py's `_DEFINITION` regex does not see them at all — those two 20260717 definitions are invisible to the redefinition inventory, and a replay of that file after 20260720 would fail with 'function already exists' rather than silently reverting (a lucky accident, not a designed one).
- **upload_jobs.stage has no CHECK constraint.** The lifecycle values (store, queued, download, extract, malware_scan, chunk, embed, screen, index, done, error, cancelled) are convention only, split between the queue RPCs' literals and services/ingestion.py. `failure_category` is likewise unconstrained; `schedule_upload_retry` truncates it to 120 chars but `fail_upload_job` does not truncate at all.
- **supabase_setup.sql's match_chunks/check_topic_duplication are `language plpgsql` with no volatility marker — i.e. VOLATILE — while the migrations define them `language sql STABLE`.** Same rows, but different planner treatment and different PostgREST semantics (a VOLATILE function cannot be reached by GET). This is the largest of the six allowlisted drifts and is deliberately deferred until after the defense because reconciling it edits the frozen evaluated path.
- **`_verify_database_contract` (main.py:263) probes only four tables** — profiles, departments, papers, paper_index_versions. A deployment missing programs, specializations, upload_job_events, ingestion_workers, operational_alerts, security_audit_events or backup_runs reports `/health` and `/ready` as fully OK and then fails at the feature boundary (routers/catalog.py:86 catches this and silently drops to `_legacy_catalog()`, which publishes `active: true` for every department).
- **`system_settings.updated_at` is never maintained.** routers/settings.py:67 updates only `value`, and there is no BEFORE UPDATE trigger on the table. Same for `storage_cleanup_queue.updated_at` (services/cleanup.py never updates a row), `programs.updated_at` and `specializations.updated_at`. Only upload_jobs (via its RPCs) and profiles (via sync_profile_email) keep the column honest.
- **papers has no updated_at column at all**, so 'when was this thesis row last touched' is unanswerable; `paper_index_versions.activated_at` is the closest proxy.
- **`prune_inactive_indexes` creates a TEMP TABLE inside a `SET search_path = public` SECURITY DEFINER function** and then references it unqualified. It works because Postgres searches pg_temp first for relations whether or not it is listed, but the pattern is fragile and would break if the function were ever changed to `search_path = pg_catalog, public`.
- **The `avatars` bucket is public with a `to public` SELECT policy**, so any avatar object URL is world-readable once known. The ownership checks only constrain who can write. This is intentional but sits next to the strictly-private pdfs bucket and is easy to misread.
- **`chunks_paper_id_idx` is fully redundant** with the leading column of the unique `chunks_version_position_idx(paper_id, index_version, chunk_index)`. It costs write throughput on the hottest insert path (100-row batches during reindex, N-row loops during commit) for no read benefit.
- **The HNSW index is built with no tuning parameters and inside the setup transaction.** `create index … using hnsw (embedding vector_cosine_ops)` at supabase_setup.sql:311 takes pgvector defaults (m=16, ef_construction=64) and holds an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock for the duration of a full build; on a populated project this is a blocking operation with no CONCURRENTLY variant offered anywhere.
- **Section numbers 13 and 14 do not exist in supabase_setup.sql** — it jumps from '12. SEED / OPERATIONS' to '15. DURABLE, LEASED INGESTION QUEUE'. Cosmetic, but it reads like two sections were lost.
- **`upsert_operational_alert` is defined twice with identical bodies** (20260724:483 and 20260724_operations_security_fixes.sql:7). The 'fixes' file was written for projects that had already applied the first version, and then the fix was folded back into 20260724 — so the two now agree and the second file is a no-op. It still counts toward the pinned redefinition inventory.

### Open questions

- The deployed pgvector extension version is still unrecorded in the repository. 20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql's header says the cleaner fix (`hnsw.iterative_scan`) needs pgvector 0.8.0 and that the live version is unknown — but supabase/.temp/postgres-version records Postgres 17.6.1.155, which narrows it considerably (Supabase PG17 images ship pgvector 0.8.x). A single `select extversion from pg_extension where extname='vector'` would settle whether ef_search can be replaced by iterative scan.
- Nothing reconciles the legacy `'models/gemini-embedding-2'` provenance rows with the configured `gemini-embedding-001`. Are there any legacy_assumed rows in the live project, or is the corpus entirely post-provenance? If any exist they are silently excluded from retrieval and from duplication screening, and the fix is either a one-line UPDATE or a reindex — but the literal is pinned by tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:95, so the test must change too.
- The four queue RPCs in supabase_setup.sql are two migrations behind their 20260724 counterparts and the allowlist reason ('reformatting') does not match the actual behavioural gap (cancelled jobs are never cleaned up or expired, and cancel-requested jobs remain claimable). Was the base file intentionally left at 20260723 for those four, or was only commit_upload_ingestion backported because that was the one the 2026-08-04 incident exposed?
- papers.active_index_version has no FK to paper_index_versions. Is that deliberate (to allow the initial gen_random_uuid() default before any version row exists) or an omission? The default means a freshly INSERTed paper row always points at a nonexistent version for the duration of commit_paper_ingestion.
- No migration ever runs `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` on the six NOT VALID department FKs. Is there a known set of violating legacy rows, or can they now be validated safely?
- The cascade-vs-restrict ordering on DELETE FROM papers is unverified by any test. Worth one integration test in the disposable project before the defense, since paper deletion is an admin-facing path.
- 20260717_rag_items_9_16.sql's 'This file is NOT executed by the application' header contradicts README.md:50's instruction to apply every migration file. Which is current?

**Depends on:** pgvector (extension `vector`) — vector(768) column + HNSW cosine index, pgcrypto — gen_random_uuid(), Supabase Auth (`auth.users`) — FK target for profiles.id, papers.uploaded_by, chat_sessions.user_id, scan_history.user_id, activity_log.user_id, upload_jobs.owner_id; also the trigger site for handle_new_user / sync_profile_email, Supabase Storage (`storage.objects`, `storage.buckets`) — private `pdfs` bucket, public `avatars` bucket, and the avatar-ownership check inside validate_editable_profile_fields, PostgREST — every RPC is reached as POST /rest/v1/rpc/<name> with named arguments; defaulted trailing params are how older builds stay compatible, config.py frozen constants — Literal[768] dimensions, Literal[800]/Literal[100] chunking, 0.30 retrieval threshold, 0.85 duplication threshold, gemini_embed_model, services/index_provenance.py — supplies p_embedding_model/p_embedding_dimensions and the verified fingerprint the commit RPC validates

**Used by:** rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:798 (match_chunks), :951 (check_topic_duplication), rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1603 (save_chat_exchange), rag-thesis-backend/routers/duplication.py:135 and services/novelty.py:353 (match_chunks at match_count=1, 0.85 threshold), rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:597 (reserve_upload_job), :609 (queue_upload_job), :1230 (request_upload_cancellation), rag-thesis-backend/services/upload_queue.py:24 claim_upload_job, :39 heartbeat_upload_job, :58 heartbeat_upload_job_control, :77 finalize_upload_cancellation, :122 schedule_upload_retry, :139 fail_upload_job, :153 claim_upload_cleanup, :167 finish_upload_cleanup, :183 expire_upload_jobs, rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py:242 (commit_upload_ingestion), rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py:55 register_ingestion_worker, :70 stop_ingestion_worker, :93 upsert_operational_alert, :374 apply_operations_retention, rag-thesis-backend/scripts/reindex_citations.py:235 (activate_paper_index), :303 (prune_inactive_indexes), rag-thesis-backend/scripts/seed_synthetic_corpus.py:603 (commit_paper_ingestion), rag-thesis-backend/scripts/record_backup_run.ps1:108 (record_backup_run over raw REST), rag-thesis-backend/main.py:263 _verify_database_contract — reads profiles, departments, papers, paper_index_versions, rag-thesis-frontend — only via the API; the anon key sees profiles (own row) and the public avatars bucket, nothing else

(`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260717_rag_items_9_16.sql:95-101,131-137`)
- **Wrong:** record_upload_job_event 'Emits exactly one upload_job_events row per statement'
  **Actual:** It is a FOR EACH ROW trigger, so it emits one event row per *row* changed, not per statement. `create trigger record_upload_job_event after insert or update on public.upload_jobs for each row execute function public.record_upload_job_event();`. This matters for the three bulk housekeeping UPDATEs inside claim_upload_job, each of which can sweep many rows in one statement and will emit one event per swept job. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:140-142`)
- **Wrong:** 20260723 is described as '20 columns on upload_jobs, 5 partial indexes, and 10 RPCs'
  **Actual:** It adds 17 columns in one ALTER (idempotency_key, source_path, source_stored, original_filename, content_sha256, request_payload, attempt_count, max_attempts, next_retry_at, lease_owner, lease_expires_at, heartbeat_at, started_at, completed_at, expires_at, failure_category, cleanup_status), not 20. It creates 5 indexes of which only 4 are partial — upload_jobs_owner_idempotency_uidx is a plain UNIQUE index with no WHERE clause. The 10-RPC count is correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:4-21,74-87`)
- **Wrong:** Programs/specializations seeding: 'plus 32 standby-college programs from 20260819'
  **Actual:** 33 standby-college program rows are seeded, not 32. Counting the `('CODE', 'CODE', 'Name')` tuples in the VALUES list at 20260819_isu_academic_catalog.sql:64-114 gives 33. The 9-specialization and 10-department (9 inactive + CCSICT active) counts are correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260819_isu_academic_catalog.sql:32-63,64-114,115-131`)
- **Wrong:** tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py listed as '~140 lines (first class only read)' with three classes: TestSqlFilesAreApplicable, TestMigrationContract, TestReindexDryRun.
  **Actual:** The file is 633 lines with six classes. The three the map missed are TestRepairChunkSections (:259), TestRescanDuplication (:381), TestBackfillScreeningArchiveSize (:496) and TestRepairReadsOnlyTheActiveIndex (:550) — that last one specifically pins that repair paths read only the active index version, which is a schema-contract assertion the map's 'tests' section does not cover. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_migration_and_reindex.py:17,46,124,259,381,496,550`)
- **Wrong:** Three test anchors in the invariants section: commit_upload_ingestion guard at test_schema_consistency.py:160, hnsw.ef_search at :186, commit_paper_ingestion provenance at :196.
  **Actual:** All three point into the wrong test. :160 is inside test_no_stale_allowlist_entries; the cancellation-guard test is test_commit_upload_ingestion_refuses_a_cancelled_job at :175. :186 is the last assert of that same test; the ef_search test is test_retrieval_rpcs_raise_ef_search_above_the_default at :190. :196 is inside that test's docstring; the provenance test is test_commit_paper_ingestion_requires_verified_provenance at :213. The KNOWN_BASE_SCHEMA_DRIFT anchor (:88) and the redefinition-inventory anchor (:236, assertion at :246) are correct, and the 14-function set is correct verbatim. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py:175,190,213`)
- **Wrong:** 'Grants: SELECT to authenticated; UPDATE only on columns (full_name, avatar_url) to authenticated (supabase_setup.sql:1174-1175)'
  **Actual:** Wrong lines. `grant select on table public.profiles to authenticated;` is at :1149 and `grant update (full_name, avatar_url) on public.profiles to authenticated;` is at :1160 (preceded by `revoke update on public.profiles from authenticated;` at :1159). Line 1174 falls inside the protect_profile_security_fields body. The substance of the claim is correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:1148-1160`)
- **Wrong:** The invariants section says apply order 'is an operator instruction enforced only by tests', but says nothing about per-file atomicity.
  **Actual:** Nine of the twenty forward migrations have no `begin;`/`commit;` at all and therefore apply statement-by-statement in autocommit: 20260717_security_scope_evaluation, 20260718_transactional_ingestion_cleanup, 20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs (473 lines, the entire leased queue), 20260725_normalized_academic_catalog (147 lines, the whole PI-04 catalog incl. the `alter column code set not null` and the backfill), 20260804_backup_runs, 20260804_chat_message_kind, 20260819_isu_academic_catalog, 20260819_thesis_category, 20260829_reindex_updates_chunk_count. A failure partway through any of these leaves the schema half-applied with no rollback, which is a materially different risk profile from supabase_setup.sql's single all-or-nothing transaction that the map does describe. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:1 (no begin), 20260725_normalized_academic_catalog.sql:1, 20260819_thesis_category.sql:17`)
- **Wrong:** record_upload_job_event's event_type taxonomy is given as exactly five values: reserved, cancellation_requested, status_changed, stage_changed, cleanup_changed.
  **Actual:** A sixth value exists in the table: 20260724:145-155 backfills one `'migration_snapshot'` row per pre-existing job at migration time. It is not produced by the trigger, but any consumer reading upload_job_events.event_type must handle it. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:145-155`)
- **Wrong:** upload_jobs.owner_id FK history is given as '20260723 ON DELETE RESTRICT … superseded later' and '20260803 → SET NULL'.
  **Actual:** Incomplete: 20260719 created the column as `owner_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete CASCADE`. The FK went CASCADE (20260719:89) → RESTRICT (20260723:33) → SET NULL (20260803:27). The CASCADE generation would have silently deleted a user's entire ingestion job history on account deletion, which is the opposite of the provenance-retention rationale 20260803 gives — worth noting because the 20260803 header asserts the column 'was NOT NULL referencing auth.users ON DELETE RESTRICT', which is only true from 20260723 onward. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260719_production_hardening.sql:87-89; 20260723_durable_ingestion_jobs.sql:30-33; 20260803_upload_jobs_owner_on_delete_set_null.sql:22-27`)
- **Wrong:** 'services/cleanup.py never updates a row' (used to justify storage_cleanup_queue.updated_at being unmaintained)
  **Actual:** The conclusion holds but the reason is wrong: routers/maintenance.py:182-186 DOES update storage_cleanup_queue rows (status, attempts, error_category). It simply never sets updated_at, and there is no BEFORE UPDATE trigger on the table. So updated_at is stale despite the table being written to. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:182-186`)
- **Wrong:** Several column specs omit NOT NULL / DEFAULT that the DDL actually carries.
  **Actual:** Concretely: upload_jobs.message is `text not null default ''` and progress is `not null default 0` (supabase_setup.sql:781-782); scan_history.highest_similarity, matched_chunk_percentage, matched_chunk_count and total_chunks are all `not null default 0` (:699-702); ingestion_workers.state defaults 'idle', scanner_status defaults 'unknown' and version is `not null default 'unknown'` (20260724:50-55); backup_runs.artifact_count and total_bytes are `not null default 0` (20260804_backup_runs.sql:32-33); operational_alerts.safe_details and security_audit_events.safe_details are `not null default '{}'`. Also chunks.index_version is declared nullable in the CREATE TABLE and only promoted by a later `alter column … set not null` (supabase_setup.sql:293,306). (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:781-782,699-702,293-306`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- migrations/20260717_security_scope_evaluation.sql (118 lines) is absent from the map entirely — it is the file that first adds `p.ingestion_status = 'ready'` to both retrieval RPCs, first installs protect_profile_security_fields, and is the first of three handle_new_user definers.
- Nine forward migrations are non-transactional (no begin/commit), including the 473-line 20260723 durable-queue migration and the 147-line 20260725 PI-04 catalog. supabase_setup.sql is the only source with all-or-nothing semantics.
- GET /health/worker (rag-thesis-backend/main.py:287-306) reads `ingestion_workers` directly with a last_seen_at cutoff and returns HTTP 503 when no non-'stopping' worker with scanner_status != 'unavailable' has checked in. It is a schema dependency on a migration-only table that `_verify_database_contract` does not probe — the exact failure the map flags for programs/specializations applies here too, and here it degrades an uptime probe rather than a feature.
- services/operations.py:77-86 `record_security_event` writes security_audit_events by direct `.table().insert()`, not through an RPC. That is the only operations table with a live direct-write path, and it works precisely because 20260724:537 grants INSERT on it. The map notes the grant but not the caller, and its 'All other writes must go through their SECURITY DEFINER RPCs' invariant is therefore false for this table.
- 20260819_isu_academic_catalog.sql:56-58 upserts departments with `on conflict (name) do update set code = excluded.code, title = excluded.title` — it deliberately does NOT update `active`, so re-running it cannot deactivate a college that was scaled up. This is the load-bearing detail behind the map's `departments.active` configKnob and is unstated.
- 20260803_institutional_signup_domain.sql:63 revokes handle_new_user from public/anon/authenticated but, unlike supabase_setup.sql:74 and 20260828:37, never re-grants EXECUTE to supabase_auth_admin. It survives only because CREATE OR REPLACE preserves the existing ACL; applying 20260803 to a project that never had that grant would break signup.
- commit_paper_ingestion writes papers with a fixed column list that includes `content` but no thesis_category/program_id/specialization_id (supabase_setup.sql:483-497) — the map states this — but the corollary it misses is that services/ingestion.py:192-214 builds paper_data WITH thesis_category/program_id/specialization_id/legacy_track/classification_status keys that the RPC silently ignores (the code comments say so at :202-204). The values reach papers only via the hydrate trigger's second read of upload_jobs.request_payload, so a job row deleted between queue and commit would produce an unclassified paper.
- scripts/seed_synthetic_corpus.py:602 calls commit_paper_ingestion directly with no upload_jobs row, so `a_papers_hydrate_academic_classification` finds no request_payload, leaves new.* untouched, and every synthetic corpus paper lands at classification_status='unclassified', program_id=NULL, thesis_category='student' (the column defaults). The PI-08 evaluation corpus therefore cannot be filtered by program.
